# NICEOPS code review: finding details (companion to the report)

Recovered from the partial workflow run `wf_79c5f1ad-97a`. Each entry is one reviewing agent's finding as reported, with its own words. Reviewers worked on `84462d7`. Evidence tags and caveats are in the report, section 0. Where an independent verifier agent also ran, its reasoning follows the finding. Treat claims tagged S as unverified.


## Output files overwritten across trajectories

### main-setup/A.r1.1: With several trajectories, -i and --band_history write the same filenames for each one, so earlier histories are silently overwritten

- Finder's severity medium, category correctness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.97, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: R V2 C×6**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:1717`, `src/niceops.cpp:1728`, `src/niceops.cpp:778`
- **Claim.** Time-history and band-history files are named options.time_history_prefix + "_i" + std::to_string(index) + ".csv" (and the band_history_prefix equivalent) with no per-trajectory component. The observer index restarts at 0 for each trajectory, so trajectory N+1 overwrites trajectory N's files. Every other per-trajectory output is disambiguated (-o and -S via expand_per_path, --powerfile via job.count > 1 and the track stem at 778-782). These two were missed.
- **Evidence given.** `niceops -t tracks/S-76D/197401AC.csv -t tracks/S-76D/197402AC.csv --gridstep 984 -i timehistory/h -o integrated/multi.csv` exits 0. grep -c 'Writing time history to timehistory/h_i0.csv' log/niceops.log gives 2, and `ls timehistory | wc -l` gives 121 files for the two runs, one set only. The first trajectory's histories are gone.
- **Scenario.** A batch comparing two flights' level histories at the same microphones with -i or --band_history keeps only the last flight's files. Nothing in the log or exit status says so.
- **Proposed change.** When job.count > 1, insert the track stem: `<prefix>_<stem>_i<N>.csv`, as the power file already does. Keep the current name for a single trajectory so nothing existing moves.
- **Expected impact.** Removes silent data loss in multi-trajectory history runs.
- **Verifier (trace): confirmed, confidence 0.95.** I re-read the code and reproduced the overwrite with the shipped Release build from a scratch directory. Both history writers build the filename as `prefix + "_i" + std::to_string(index) + ".csv"`. `index` is the observer index, which restarts at 0 for each trajectory. Nothing in `run()` or `process_trajectory` adds a per-trajectory part: `-i` and `--band_history` are single `std::string` options and never go through `expand_per_path`. `PathJob` has `trajectory` and `count` but no index. The `--powerfile` path does disambiguate, in the `job.count == 1 ? ... : stem + "_power.csv"` branch at 778-782. No CTest case or unit test combines several `-t` with `-i` or `--band_history`, so no test pins the current behavior. The README's several-trajectories section lists `-d`, `-v`, `-o` and `-S` as per-trajectory and says nothing about histories, so the collision is undocumented. The D original was single-trajectory, so there is no port-fidelity constraint on the multi-trajectory case. Goldens use a single trajectory and are untouched.

The claim holds for both options. Two small corrections to the report: the log does print the same filename twice at info level (so it is visible if you look, but nothing warns), and `-S` with one value is copied to every trajectory by `expand_per_path` rather than disambiguated, so `-S` has the same collision. That second point is adjacent and not part of this finding. Severity is medium at most, because it needs the uncommon combination of several trajectories and a history option, but the loss is silent and the exit status is 0.
  - Refined claim: With `-t` repeated, `-i` and `--band_history` write `PREFIX_i<N>.csv` with `N` the observer index, restarting at 0 for each trajectory. Each later trajectory overwrites the earlier one's files, so only the last trajectory's histories survive. The run exits 0 and logs the same filename once per trajectory with no warning. Reproduced for both options with the shipped build. `--powerfile` was the only per-trajectory output that got a stem-based name, and `-S` has the same collision because a single value is copied to every trajectory.
  - Verifier's fix: Give the two history writers a per-trajectory component when `job.count > 1`, keeping `PREFIX_i<N>.csv` for a single trajectory so the CTest band_history cases (which read `integrated/band_history/refracted_i3.csv`) and the existing outputs do not move. Either mirror the power-file rule (`PREFIX_<stem>_i<N>.csv`) or add a `path_index` to `PathJob` and use it to avoid stem collisions. Add a CTest case with two `-t` and `-i` that checks the file count. Optionally make `-S` behave the same way, or document that a single `-S` value is shared. Also document `-i` and `--band_history` in README's multi-trajectory section.
- **Verifier (context): confirmed, confidence 0.95.** I reproduced it with the shipped Release binary in a scratch directory. The bug is real and not deliberate.

Code. src/niceops.cpp:1717 builds `options.time_history_prefix + "_i" + std::to_string(index) + ".csv"`. Lines 1728-1731 build `options.band_history_prefix + "_i" + std::to_string(index) + ".csv"`. `index` is the observer index and restarts at 0 for every trajectory. Neither name uses `job`, which is in scope because the parallel_chunks lambda at 1424 captures by reference. The power file at 778-782 does disambiguate, with `job.count == 1 ? options.power_file : parent/(stem + "_power.csv")`. The -o names are derived from the track stem at 2205-2214.

Intent. Commit 6879da0 introduced multi-trajectory support. Its message says "with several tracks and no -o, each output is named after its own track rather than overwriting one file". Overwriting was what the change set out to avoid, so the history files were missed rather than chosen. `-i` and `--band_history` predate that commit and were not revisited. README.md lines 77-82 and 143-144 document `PREFIX_i<N>.csv` with no mention of several trajectories. CLAUDE.md says nothing on the point. This is not a documented limitation, not a port-fidelity item, and not on the rejected-optimizations list.

Experiments (scratch run directory, /Users/evg5332/.claude/jobs/8792c8f6/tmp/scratch/verif_hist_r11/run).
1. The reporter's command, `-t 197401AC -t 197402AC --gridstep 984 -i timehistory/h -o integrated/multi.csv`, exits 0 and leaves 121 files (11x11 observers). The log line 'Writing time history to timehistory/h_i0.csv' appears twice.
2. I then ran each track alone. The two-track h_i0.csv is byte-identical to the track-2-only h_i0.csv and differs from the track-1-only one (`cmp` differs at char 121, line 3). Track 1's histories are gone.
3. `--band_history` with the ctest band_history_straight options and both tracks gives 8 files, `bh/multi/p_i0..p_i7.csv`. All 8 are byte-identical to the track-2-only run (`cmp`). Exit status is 0.

Output effect. The fix would only change names when `job.count > 1`. A single-trajectory run keeps its current names, so the goldens are untouched and the numeric output is bit-identical. The ctest cases `band_history_refracted_is_straight` and `band_history_leaves_footprint` read fixed single-trajectory names, `integrated/band_history/refracted_i3.csv` and `.../straight_i3.csv`, and would keep passing. No ctest case uses `-i` or repeats `-t`, so nothing pins the multi-track naming and the change is free to make. The output effect is changes-behavior, limited to multi-trajectory runs with `-i` or `--band_history`.
  - Refined claim: With `-t` repeated, `-i` (src/niceops.cpp:1717) and `--band_history` (src/niceops.cpp:1728-1731) write `<prefix>_i<N>.csv` with `N` the per-trajectory observer index. Every trajectory therefore writes the same files, the last one wins, and the run exits 0. Demonstrated for both options. The reporter's contrast is slightly wrong: `-S` is not disambiguated when given a single value. README line 208 documents one shared value for `-d`, `-v`, `-o` and `-S`. `expand_per_path` then copies that one `-S` name to every trajectory (2229-2230, 2552). I confirmed `-S state/s.csv` with two tracks logs 'Writing state data to state/s.csv' twice and leaves one file. So `-S` is a sibling silent-overwrite (state files, also opt-in) on a different code path, and the README describes it as shared. It is worth a separate look but is not part of this finding. Only `-o` (stem-derived at 2205-2214) and `--powerfile` (778-782) are actually disambiguated.
  - Verifier's fix: The reporter's fix is right in kind. Improve it in three ways.

1. Compute the effective prefix once per trajectory, before the parallel_chunks observer loop. Do not rebuild it per observer inside the worker. For example, `const auto stem = std::filesystem::path(job.trajectory).stem().string(); const std::string history_prefix = job.count == 1 ? options.time_history_prefix : options.time_history_prefix + "_" + stem;`, and the same for `band_history_prefix`. Use one small helper for all three call sites (power file, history, band history) so the stem rule lives in one place.
2. Put the stem in the filename part of the prefix, not in a new directory. The `create_directories` calls at 813-822 take `parent_path()` of the prefix, so they then keep working unchanged.
3. Keep the current name when `job.count == 1`. `band_history_refracted_is_straight` depends on `_i3.csv`.

Add a ctest case that runs two tracks with `-i` and asserts both sets of files exist. No current case covers multi-track `-i` or `--band_history`.

Residual limitation to state in the README: two `-t` paths with the same stem, such as the same track twice for a DNL identity check or the same basename in different directories, still collide. `-o` and `--powerfile` already share that limitation. Options are to reject duplicate stems at parse time or to fall back to the trajectory index. Do not expand scope beyond this unless asked.

### main-loop/A.r1.3: Per-observer history files (-i, --band_history) and a single -S are shared by all trajectories in a run, so later trajectories silently overwrite earlier ones

- Finder's severity medium, category correctness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.97, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: R C×6**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:1714`, `src/niceops.cpp:2042`, `src/niceops.cpp:2200`, `src/niceops.cpp:776`
- **Claim.** The history file name is `prefix + "_i" + index + ".csv"` with no trajectory component, and expand_per_path broadcasts a lone -S to every trajectory (help text says "Once per trajectory"). --powerfile handles job.count > 1 by deriving a per-track name; -o does too; -i, --band_history and -S do not.
- **Evidence given.** `niceops -t tracks/S-76D/197401AC.csv -t tracks/S-76D/197402AC.csv -o integrated/two.csv --gridstep 984 -i th2/y -L warn` exit=0, `ls th2 | wc -l` = 121 (one set for 121 observers). Runs of each track alone into thA/ and thB/: `cmp th2/y_i10.csv thB/y_i10.csv` identical, `cmp th2/y_i10.csv thA/y_i10.csv` differs at line 3. No warning in the log. `-S state/two.csv` with the same two tracks leaves one 1.49 MB file holding only the second track (LAE outputs are separate: 197401AC_LAE.csv, 197402AC_LAE.csv).
- **Scenario.** A batch of several flights with -i or --band_history (the stated use is comparing with measured spectra frame by frame) writes N trajectories of files that all land on the same names; only the last flight's histories survive and the run exits 0.
- **Proposed change.** For path_count > 1 derive `<prefix>_<track stem>_i<N>.csv` (as the power file does at lines 778-781) and likewise per-track state names when a single -S is given; or reject a single -i/--band_history/-S with several trajectories. Keep the single-trajectory names unchanged.
- **Expected impact.** No silent loss of per-observer outputs in multi-trajectory runs.

### main-io-sel/A.r1.2: -i / --band_history file names ignore the trajectory, and a single -S is shared by every trajectory: multi-trajectory runs silently overwrite earlier trajectories' files

- Finder's severity medium, category correctness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.95, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: R C×6**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:1714`, `src/niceops.cpp:2198`, `src/niceops.cpp:776`
- **Claim.** Per-observer history files are named `options.time_history_prefix + "_i" + index + ".csv"` (and the band-history equivalent) from RunOptions, which is shared by all trajectories, so with N > 1 trajectories trajectory k overwrites trajectory k-1's files for the same observer index. `-S` is treated the same way: expand_per_path copies a single name to every trajectory, so each one overwrites the state file. The same function already knows how to avoid this: `-o` with one name and several tracks derives `<dir>/<stem>_LAE.csv` (lines 2214-2221) and --powerfile uses `<stem>_power.csv` when job.count > 1 (lines 778-782). README documents -S as 'either one value, shared by every trajectory, or one per trajectory' and says nothing about -i.
- **Evidence given.** `niceops -t tracks/S-76D/197401AC.csv tracks/S-76D/197402AC.csv -o m1.csv m2.csv --gridstep 1500 -i hist2/p -j 2` (exit 0, log empty): hist2/p_i0.csv has 5053 lines = 197402's 5051 states + 2 header lines; 197401's 4351 states are gone. `niceops -t 197401AC.csv 197402AC.csv -S state/s2.csv` -> exit 0, state/s2.csv has 5052 lines (197402 only).
- **Scenario.** A user computes histories for a morning and an evening operation in one invocation to compare a microphone's time history against measurements and finds only the last operation's files, with no warning.
- **Proposed change.** When job.count > 1, put the trajectory stem into the name (`prefix_<stem>_i<N>.csv`, mirroring the power file), do the same for --band_history, and derive per-trajectory names for a single -S as -o does; failing that, refuse duplicate output names up front.
- **Expected impact.** Removes silent data loss in the documented multi-trajectory workflow.

### main-io-sel/B.r1.2: -i and --band_history silently overwrite each other when several trajectories are run

- Finder's severity medium, category correctness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.95, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: R V2 C×6**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:1714`, `src/niceops.cpp:778`
- **Claim.** Per-observer history files are named `<prefix>_i<index>.csv` with no trajectory component, so with `-t A -t B` the second trajectory overwrites the first's files without warning and the run exits 0. Every other per-trajectory output copes with this: -o and -S are per trajectory, and --powerfile derives `<stem>_power.csv` when job.count > 1.
- **Evidence given.** `niceops -t tracks/S-76D/197421AC.csv -t tracks/S-76D/197403AC.csv --gridstep 1640 -i hist/h -o integrated/m1.csv -o integrated/m2.csv -L warn` -> exit=0, `ls hist | wc -l` = 49 (one 7x7 set, not two). `hist/h_i5.csv` is byte-identical to the file produced by running the second track alone, and differs from the first track's. README.md documents per-trajectory handling for -d, -v, -o and -S (README L206-210) and says nothing for -i or --band_history.
- **Scenario.** A user batches several flights with -i or --band_history to compare time histories per flight and gets only the last flight's files, with no diagnostic.
- **Proposed change.** When job.count > 1, build the prefix as `<prefix>_<track stem>` (as the --powerfile branch does with the stem) for both time_history_prefix and band_history_prefix at the two file-name sites. About 8 LOC. Add a sentence to README's 'Several trajectories' section.
- **Expected impact.** No silent loss of diagnostic output in multi-trajectory runs. Single-trajectory runs keep their current file names.
- **Verifier (trace): confirmed, confidence 0.96.** Reproduced independently on the shipped build from a scratch runenv, for both -i and --band_history. The code has no trajectory component in either file name. Two trajectories in one invocation leave one file set, byte-identical to the second track's solo run. Exit status is 0 and stdout is empty. Nothing upstream prevents it: `expand_per_path` (niceops.cpp ~2200-2216) is applied to database, vehicle, output and state names, but never to the two history prefixes. No CTest case combines several -t with -i or --band_history (grep of CMakeLists.txt found none), so nothing pins the behavior. The README says "-d, -v, -o and -S" are per trajectory (L206-208) and is silent on -i and --band_history. This is a real silent-overwrite defect in a documented multi-trajectory mode, not a design choice. A user cannot avoid it in one invocation, because a single prefix cannot be given per trajectory. Severity is medium-low: diagnostic output only, and the goldens and level files are untouched.
  - Refined claim: Confirmed as stated. `-i` and `--band_history` file names are `<prefix>_i<index>.csv` with no trajectory component. With N greater than 1 trajectories, each trajectory's files are overwritten by the next one's. The run exits 0 and logs only an info-level "Writing time history" line per file. Only the last trajectory's per-observer histories survive. The claim that "-o and -S cope" holds only when the user gives them per trajectory, or, for -o, when auto-naming applies. Their shared-value paths are not protected in the same way, but a user can avoid the collision there, whereas no invocation can give -i or --band_history a per-trajectory prefix.
  - Verifier's fix: When `job.count > 1`, insert the track stem into both file names, e.g. `<prefix>_<stem>_i<index>.csv`, using the same `std::filesystem::path(job.trajectory).stem()` idiom as the --powerfile branch at L778-782. Single-trajectory names stay unchanged. Add a sentence to README "Several trajectories". Two tracks with the same stem in different directories would still collide. `-o` auto-naming (L2214-2222) and `--powerfile` have that limit too, so it is consistent with the existing convention. A cheaper alternative is to `input_error` when `path_count > 1` and either prefix is set. A one-trajectory-per-invocation regression test would also be worth adding, since no test combines several -t with -i or --band_history.
- **Verifier (context): confirmed, confidence 0.9.** Reproduced independently on both paths. (1) `-i`: two `-t`, `--gridstep 1640`, `-i hist/h`, exit 0, `ls hist | wc -l` = 49. I then ran each track alone into hist1/ and hist2/. All 49 files in hist/ are byte-identical to the second track's, and none match the first's, so the first flight's histories are silently lost. (2) `--band_history`: with the B407 spectral database and the ctest `band_history_straight` flags on `tests/data/mixed_flush.csv`, two `-t` gave 8 files (`x_i0..x_i7`) and exit 0. `x_i0`, `x_i3` and `x_i5` are identical to the second track run alone and differ from the first's.

The code matches. `src/niceops.cpp` L1717 builds `options.time_history_prefix + "_i" + std::to_string(index) + ".csv"` and L1730 builds `options.band_history_prefix + "_i" + ...`. Neither uses `job`. Trajectories run one after another (L2540-2560), so the last write wins. `job` (with `job.trajectory` and `job.count`) is in scope in the `[&]` lambda. No warning or overwrite check exists anywhere.

Not deliberate. `-i` comes from the D original, which handled a single trajectory. Commit 6879da0 added multi-trajectory runs and says "with several tracks and no -o, each output is named after its own track rather than overwriting one file". Commit 4302efa gave `--powerfile` the same `<stem>_power.csv` treatment when `job.count > 1`. `-i` was missed in both, and `--band_history` (84462d7) copied `-i`'s naming. The README's per-trajectory list (L206-207: "`-d`, `-v`, `-o` and `-S`") and the Outputs section (L74, L79) say nothing about `-i` or `--band_history` under several trajectories. Nothing in CLAUDE.md, README or code comments makes last-wins intentional.

The fix does not touch the hot path or the rejected list. File-name construction happens once per observer, after the SEL is computed. Single-trajectory names stay unchanged, so `band_history_refracted_is_straight`, which reads `integrated/band_history/refracted_i3.csv`, and the goldens are unaffected. No ctest uses more than one `-t`, and none pins multi-trajectory history names.

Overstated points in the report:
- "Every other per-trajectory output copes" is not accurate. `-S` given once is expanded by `expand_per_path` (L2229-2230) into one shared file. I ran two `-t` with a single `-S st/s.csv` and got exit 0 and one file, `s.csv`. README documents that as "shared". The real difference is that `-i` and `--band_history` are single `std::string` options with no per-trajectory form, so a user has no way around the collision within one invocation. `-o` (L2214-2224) and `--powerfile` are the only outputs that derive per-track names, and they are the precedent for the fix.
- "medium" is generous. Only diagnostic time-history files are lost. The SEL and DNL outputs are intact and the exit code is 0. The maintainer's own validation harness (`niceops-validation`) invokes one track per run with a unique prefix, so no known workflow hits it.

The scenario is still plausible: `-t run1 -t run2 ... -b mics.csv --band_history P` for flight-test microphones.
  - Refined claim: With more than one `-t`, `-i PREFIX` and `--band_history PREFIX` write `PREFIX_i<N>.csv` (src/niceops.cpp L1717 and L1730) with no trajectory component. Each later trajectory silently overwrites the earlier trajectories' files, and the run exits 0. Only the last flight's histories survive. `-o` (when given once) and `--powerfile` derive per-track names when more than one trajectory is run. `-i` and `--band_history` take a single string, so there is no per-trajectory form and no in-invocation workaround. A single shared `-S` also last-wins, but README documents that as "shared" and `-S` can be given per trajectory.
  - Verifier's fix: The direction is right: follow the `--powerfile` and `-o` precedent and change nothing when `job.count == 1`. Improvements on the proposal:
1. Add one helper in `process_trajectory`, near L805-822 where the history directories are created. It returns the prefix as `job.count == 1 ? prefix : prefix + "_" + std::filesystem::path(job.trajectory).stem().string()`. Compute it once per trajectory, before the parallel loop, for both `time_history_prefix` and `band_history_prefix`, and use it at L1717 and L1730. Names then look like `hist/h_197421AC_i5.csv`.
2. Do not copy `--powerfile`'s `parent_path() / (stem + suffix)` form. That replaces the user's file stem, but here the prefix is a filename prefix (`hist/h`), not a path. Appending the stem to the prefix keeps `parent_path()` unchanged, so the `create_directories` calls at L813 and L820 need no change.
3. Document it: add `-i` and `--band_history` to README's "Several trajectories" section (L206-207) and the Outputs bullets (L74, L79). Say that with several trajectories the track's stem is inserted before `_i<N>`.
4. Add a ctest that runs two `-t` with `--band_history` or `-i` (for example `tests/data/mixed_flush.csv` and the B407 flags from `band_history_straight`). It should assert that both stems' files exist, for example by `golden_compare`-ing `<prefix>_<stem>_i3.csv` with a single-track run. No ctest currently uses more than one `-t`.
5. Residual: two tracks in different directories with the same basename would still collide. `-o` and `--powerfile` already share that limitation. If it matters, a single up-front check for unique stems when `path_count > 1` would cover all three.
6. A cheaper alternative, `input_error` when `path_count > 1` and either option is given, is safe but removes a useful feature. Derived names are better.

### main-cli/A.r1.1: Multi-trajectory runs silently overwrite -S, -i and --band_history outputs; derived -o names collide on equal stems

- Finder's severity medium, category correctness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.95, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: R C×6**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:2214`, `src/niceops.cpp:1717`, `src/niceops.cpp:1728`, `src/niceops.cpp:1785`, `src/niceops.cpp:778`
- **Claim.** With several -t, only -o (single value) and --powerfile are turned into per-trajectory names. expand_per_path copies a single -S value to every trajectory, and the -i and --band_history prefixes are used verbatim (prefix + '_i<index>.csv'), so trajectory N+1 overwrites trajectory N's state file and every per-observer history file. The derived -o name is directory/(stem + '_LAE.csv'), so two tracks with the same file stem in different directories also write one file. No warning, exit 0. README.md:208 states that -S may take 'one value, shared by every trajectory'.
- **Evidence given.** Two tracks, one shared -S and -i (bash, scratch dir):
$ niceops -t tracks/S-76D/197421AC.csv -t tracks/S-76D/197402AC.csv --gridstep 492 -S state/shared.csv -i timehistory/h -o integrated/x.csv  -> exit 0
log: 'Writing state data to state/shared.csv' appears twice; 'Writing time history to timehistory/h_i0.csv' appears twice; state/ holds one file, timehistory/ holds 441 files (the second trajectory's); grep -i warn log -> nothing.
Stem collision: a/f.csv and b/f.csv (symlinks to two different tracks) with -o integrated/out.csv -> 'Writing output to integrated/f_LAE.csv' twice, integrated/ contains a single f_LAE.csv, exit 0.
Precedent in the same file: --powerfile is already made per-trajectory (778-782) and -m appends with a per-track casename. No add_test in CMakeLists.txt passes -t twice, so multi-trajectory naming, skipping and the shared-database cache have no end-to-end test.
- **Scenario.** A day's operations are run as one DNL job: niceops -t a.csv -t b.csv -t c.csv -S state/day.csv -i th/run -D dnl.csv. The DNL file is right, but state/day.csv and th/run_i*.csv contain only trajectory c; a and b were computed and silently discarded. Same for an archive laid out as 2020-01/flight1.csv, 2020-02/flight1.csv, whose footprints land in one integrated/flight1_LAE.csv.
- **Proposed change.** When path_count > 1: derive -S names the way -o is derived (directory of the given name + stem + '_state.csv'); insert the track stem into the -i and --band_history prefixes (prefix_<stem>_i<index>.csv); after derivation, reject duplicate output/state names with input_error before any work starts. Keep single-trajectory names unchanged so CI, goldens and scripts are untouched. Add a two-track ctest that checks both output files exist and differ.
- **Expected impact.** Removes silent loss of per-trajectory outputs in the exact workflow the DNL feature targets; the collision check turns a silent overwrite into an up-front error.
- **Conflicts with docs.** README.md:208 says -S may be shared by every trajectory, which is what makes this a data-loss path.

### main-cli/B.r1.1: Output names are not derived per trajectory: several runs collapse onto one file

- Finder's severity medium, category correctness, effort M, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: C×6**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:1928`, `src/niceops.cpp:2023`, `src/niceops.cpp:2214`, `src/niceops.cpp:1717`, `src/niceops.cpp:778`
- **Claim.** Only -o (when given once for several tracks) and --powerfile get a per-trajectory name. Four other cases silently write different runs to the same path. (a) A single `-t X` with no -o writes `integrated/197421_LAE.csv`, the demo's name and the file regression_golden reads, although the help says 'derived from the track name if omitted'. (b) A single `-S` is broadcast to every trajectory by expand_per_path (2229), so each trajectory overwrites the previous state file. (c) `-i` and `--band_history` name files `prefix + "_i" + index + ".csv"` (1717, 1730), with no trajectory component, so trajectory N overwrites trajectory N-1's per-observer files. (d) The derived -o is `directory / (stem + "_LAE.csv")` (2222-2223), so `dayA/t.csv` and `dayB/t.csv` collide.
- **Evidence given.** All run against the shipped build in scratch dirs. (a) `niceops -t tracks/S-76D/197403AC.csv --gridstep 492` leaves only `integrated/197421_LAE.csv`, exit 0. (b) and (c) `niceops -t ..197403AC.csv -t ..197404AC.csv -S state/s.csv -i timehistory/h --gridstep 1600` leaves one state/s.csv and one set of 49 timehistory files. The log has `Writing state data to state/s.csv` twice and `Writing time history to timehistory/h_i0.csv` twice, exit 0. (d) `-t dayA/t.csv -t dayB/t.csv` logs `Writing output to integrated/t_LAE.csv` twice, exit 0. The code already has two separate copies of the derive-a-name logic (2218-2223 and 778-783). README says -S may be 'one value, shared by every trajectory', which for an output means last-writer-wins.
- **Scenario.** Someone processes a day of flights with `-t a -t b -t c -S state/day.csv -i th/h`, or a loop over one track per invocation without -o. The results of every trajectory but the last are silently lost or replaced, and the exit status is 0. A single-track run without -o also silently clobbers the file the golden test compares.
- **Proposed change.** Add one helper, `derived_path(given, track, suffix)`, that replaces the two existing copies. Use it for -o, --powerfile, -S, and the -i/--band_history prefixes whenever path_count > 1. Change the default -o so that it is derived from the track whenever -t was given; keep `integrated/197421_LAE.csv` only when neither -t nor -o is given, so CTest and the golden path are untouched. After derivation, input_error if two trajectories map to the same path, naming both tracks. Add a two-track CTest that checks the number of files.
- **Expected impact.** Removes silent data loss in the multi-trajectory feature the README advertises, and stops single-track runs from overwriting the demo output. About +15 LOC net.


## Exception in a worker thread terminates the process

### main-setup/A.r1.3: An exception thrown inside the observer loop on a worker thread terminates the whole process instead of skipping the trajectory

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.95, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: R V1 C×6**
- Locations: `src/util/parallel.hpp:16`, `src/util/parallel.hpp:78`, `src/niceops.cpp:66`, `src/niceops.cpp:1714`
- **Claim.** parallel_chunks documents that 'an exception escaping the body terminates the process ... nothing here throws'. That is no longer true. open_output() calls input_error (which throws std::runtime_error) and ensure_parent_directory can throw filesystem_error; both are reached from the body via write_time_history_file and write_band_history_file. The body runs in std::thread workers, and also on the calling thread while the pool is still joinable. Either way the exception reaches std::terminate. process_trajectory's stated contract ('Anything that throws in here condemns this trajectory alone; the caller catches and moves on') and the batch loop's per-trajectory catch do not hold with more than one thread.
- **Evidence given.** `mkdir ro; chmod 555 ro; niceops --gridstep 984 -j 4 -i ro/h -o integrated/ro4.csv` prints 'libc++abi: terminating' and exits 134 (SIGABRT). The same command with -j 1 exits 1 with the log line 'Skipping tracks/S-76D/197421AC.csv: Unable to open file for writing: ro/h_i0.csv'. The directory exists, so the create_directories at niceops.cpp:813-815 on the main thread does not trip.
- **Scenario.** A multi-trajectory batch with -i or --band_history to a directory that is unwritable, full, or holds a directory named like an output file. At the default thread count the process aborts, losing the rest of the batch and the DNL, where -j 1 would skip one trajectory.
- **Proposed change.** In parallel_chunks, wrap body() in try/catch inside `pull`, store the first std::exception_ptr in a shared slot, make other workers stop pulling chunks, and rethrow on the calling thread after joining. Also join the pool before rethrowing when the calling thread's own pull() throws. Fix the header comment. Add a unit test with a body that throws at threads=1 and threads=4.
- **Expected impact.** The per-trajectory error contract holds at any thread count. No change to results.
- **Verifier (trace): confirmed, confidence 0.93.** I re-read the code and reproduced the crash with the shipped build from a scratch directory.

Code path:
- parallel.hpp:16-17 says "an exception escaping the body terminates the process ... nothing here throws". `pull` (parallel.hpp:73-81) calls `body` with no try/catch. It runs on `std::thread` workers (parallel.hpp:85-86) and on the calling thread (parallel.hpp:87) while `pool` is still joinable.
- The observer-loop body (niceops.cpp:1424) reaches `write_time_history_file` (niceops.cpp:1718) and `write_band_history_file` (niceops.cpp:1728). Both call `open_output` (niceops.cpp:66-74), which calls `ensure_parent_directory` (can throw `filesystem_error`) and `input_error("Unable to open file for writing: ", path)`. `input_error` (logger.hpp:265-272) logs and then does `throw std::runtime_error`.
- `process_trajectory`'s contract (niceops.cpp:668-669) says "Anything that throws in here condemns this trajectory alone; the caller catches and moves on". The loop in `run()` (niceops.cpp:2557-2561) catches `std::exception` and logs "Skipping ...". That catch is on the main thread, so a throw on a worker never reaches it.
- The pre-loop `create_directories` (niceops.cpp:806-822) runs on the main thread and only covers a missing directory. It does not cover an existing directory that cannot be written to.

Reproduction: `chmod 555 ro`, then `niceops -S state/ci_state.csv --gridstep 984 -i ro/h -o integrated/roN.csv`.
- `-j 1`: exit 1. The log has "[error] Skipping tracks/S-76D/197421AC.csv: Unable to open file for writing: ro/h_i0.csv". This is the intended behavior.
- `-j 4`: exit 134 (SIGABRT), with "libc++abi: terminating" printed. No "Skipping" line is produced.
- Default thread count (no `-j`): exit 134, with "libc++abi: terminating due to uncaught exception of type std::runtime_error: Unable to open file for writing: ro/h_i0.csv".

No test covers `parallel_chunks` or an exception thrown in its body (grep of tests/, tools/ and CMakeLists.txt).

The finding is real and its evidence reproduces. Its impact is narrower than "medium" suggests. It needs an unwritable or otherwise non-openable output location, and a missing directory is already handled. It is an abort in place of a skip, not a wrong result.
  - Refined claim: The finding holds as stated. Any exception escaping the `parallel_chunks` body aborts the process whenever more than one thread is used, which is the default (`default_thread_count()`, 14 on this machine). The header comment "nothing here throws" has been false since `open_output` switched from `fatal()` to the throwing `input_error`. Commit fb0211e, "Simplify: remove vestigial layers", is the one that last touched the message. The abort is reachable through `-i` and `--band_history`.

Two caveats. First, other `parallel_chunks` callers share the hazard: src/refraction.cpp:604 (shadow-table build, whose helpers document that they throw past |q| = 6) and src/pe.cpp:431 (`solve_pe` throws `invalid_argument`). The proposed fix in `parallel_chunks` would cover all three. Second, `-j 1` skipping is not fully clean either. Observers already processed before the throw leave their `integrated_levels` and `dnl_energy[index] +=` contributions in place. That is deterministic at `-j 1` but would depend on scheduling if the multi-thread path also rethrew.
  - Verifier's fix: The proposed change is sound. In `pull`, catch around `body`, store the first `std::exception_ptr` under an atomic flag or mutex, and stop handing out chunks once it is set. Run `pull()` on the calling thread inside its own try/catch. Join all workers, then rethrow on the calling thread, so a throw on the calling thread cannot leave a joinable `std::thread` to hit `std::terminate` in `~thread`. Update the header comment. Add a Catch2 case with a throwing body at threads=1 and threads=4. Consider also deciding what a skipped trajectory should leave in `dnl_energy`, since partial per-observer accumulation before the throw already exists at `-j 1`.

### main-io-sel/A.r1.1: An exception thrown inside an observer-loop worker terminates the process; the header comment says nothing throws, and process_trajectory promises per-trajectory containment

- Finder's severity medium, category correctness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: R C×6**
- Locations: `src/util/parallel.hpp:16`, `src/util/parallel.hpp:73`, `src/niceops.cpp:66`, `src/niceops.cpp:1714`, `src/niceops.cpp:2545`, `src/util/logger.hpp:265`, `CMakeLists.txt:203`
- **Claim.** parallel_chunks runs `pull` on std::threads with no try/catch: `// Note that an exception escaping the body terminates the process. That matches what already happens: fatal() aborts, and nothing here throws.` That is no longer true. input_error() throws std::runtime_error (logger.hpp:265-272), and the body calls open_output (input_error on a file that cannot be opened) and ensure_parent_directory (std::filesystem_error) for -i and --band_history from inside the workers. run() catches std::exception per trajectory ('Skipping ...', exit 1) but only the calling thread's exception can reach it: at -j 1 (or when the caller thread happens to hit it) the trajectory is skipped and the batch continues, at -j >= 2 a worker throws and std::terminate aborts the whole run.
- **Evidence given.** `mkdir -p hist/p_i3.csv` (a directory where observer 3's file should go), then
`niceops -S state/a.csv --gridstep 984 -i hist/p -L warn -o h.csv -j 1` -> exit 1, log: `Skipping tracks/S-76D/197421AC.csv: Unable to open file for writing: hist/p_i3.csv`.
Same with `-j 4` -> `libc++abi: terminating due to uncaught exception of type std::runtime_error: Unable to open file for writing: hist/p_i3.csv`, exit 134 (SIGABRT), nothing further processed. Also: pool.emplace_back(pull) (line 86) can throw std::system_error (thread limit / huge -j) and unwinds with joinable threads in `pool`, which is another std::terminate. Separately, parallel_chunks is used by library code (src/refraction.cpp:604, src/pe.cpp:431) that lives in niceops_core, but Threads::Threads is linked only to `niceops` and `niceops_benchmark` (CMakeLists.txt:204,245); niceops_unit_tests, niceops_pe_compare and niceops_pe_field get no -pthread, which is harmless on glibc >= 2.34 (CI) but on older glibc with libstdc++ std::thread fails at run time ('Enable multithreading to use std::thread'). Not reproducible on this machine.
- **Scenario.** `niceops -t a.csv -t b.csv -t c.csv -i out/hist -D dnl.csv` on a full disk, a read-only prefix directory or a name collision: with -j 1 track b is skipped and c still runs; with the default thread count the process aborts, c never runs and the DNL file is never written. Mid-loop failure at -j 1 also leaves dnl_energy partially accumulated for the skipped trajectory (observers before the failing one got the += , the rest did not), and that partial sum is written to the -D file.
- **Proposed change.** In `pull`, wrap body() in try/catch, store the first std::exception_ptr (mutex or atomic_flag), set `next` to `count` so the other workers stop being dealt chunks, join everything, then std::rethrow_exception on the calling thread. Wrap the emplace_back loop so already-started workers are stopped and joined if thread creation fails. Fix the header comment. Add `target_link_libraries(niceops_core PUBLIC Threads::Threads)`. Optionally make a failed trajectory subtract nothing from dnl_energy (accumulate into a per-trajectory buffer and add on success).
- **Expected impact.** Failure behavior becomes independent of -j; batch runs survive a bad output path; no output change on successful runs.

### main-io-sel/B.r1.1: Exceptions in parallel_chunks workers call std::terminate, defeating the per-trajectory error contract (and leave a partial DNL at -j 1)

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: R V2 C×6**
- Locations: `src/util/parallel.hpp:16`, `src/util/parallel.hpp:72`, `src/niceops.cpp:1704`, `src/niceops.cpp:66`, `src/ground_effect.cpp:299`
- **Claim.** parallel.hpp says 'an exception escaping the body terminates the process... nothing here throws'. That is no longer true. The observer-loop body throws through input_error: open_output when a time-history or band-history file cannot be opened, and excess_ground_attenuation for a non-positive magnitude. The other two callers throw too: ShadowTransition builds in refraction.cpp:604 (impedance_airy_zeros runtime_error) and solve_pe_bands in pe.cpp:431 (invalid_argument). process_trajectory's documented contract is 'anything that throws in here condemns this trajectory alone; the caller catches and moves on', and run() keeps a per-trajectory try/catch. That holds only at -j 1.
- **Evidence given.** Read-only experiment with the shipped binary, unwritable history directory `ro` (chmod 555), 7x7 grid:
`niceops -S state/ci_state.csv --gridstep 1640 -j 1 -i ro/h -o integrated/x_j1.csv -L warn` -> exit=1, trajectory skipped with a log line.
`... -j 4 ...` -> `libc++abi: terminating due to uncaught exception of type std::runtime_error: Unable to open file for writing: ro/h_i2.csv` / `libc++abi: terminating`, exit=134 (SIGABRT). Every later trajectory in a batch is lost.
Partial-state consequence at -j 1: dnl_energy[index] is incremented (niceops.cpp:1710) before write_time_history_file (1718) can throw. Running `niceops -S state/g.csv --gridstep 1640 -j 1 -i ro/h -D integrated/dnl_partial.csv -o ...` exits 1 but still writes the DNL file, whose first data row is `64.619797735286468,-inf,-inf,-inf,-inf,-inf,-inf`: observer 0 carries the failed trajectory's energy and the rest do not.
No test covers parallel_chunks: grep of tests/ finds no use of it.
- **Scenario.** A batch of trajectories with -i or --band_history onto a full or read-only disk, or a ground-impedance model that cancels exactly, or an Airy-zero failure in the shadow-transition build. At -j >= 2 the process aborts instead of skipping that trajectory. At -j 1 it skips it but writes a DNL file with a partial contribution from the failed one.
- **Proposed change.** In parallel_chunks, wrap body() in try/catch inside `pull`. Store the first std::exception_ptr under a mutex, set `next` to `count` so the other workers stop, join every thread, then rethrow on the caller. Guard the emplace_back loop so a failed thread creation joins the ones already started. In process_trajectory, stage the DNL contribution in a local vector and add it into dnl_energy only after the loop succeeds. Fix the header comment. Add a Catch2 case: coverage of [0,count) exactly once for threads 0, 1, N and N > count, and exception propagation with no leaked threads.
- **Expected impact.** A failing trajectory is skipped with the logged reason at any thread count, and the rest of the batch runs. DNL no longer includes a partial trajectory. No hot-path cost: one try block per chunk.
- **Verifier (trace): confirmed, confidence 0.9.** Confirmed for the observer loop, which is the caller that matters. I re-read the code and ran the shipped binary. parallel_chunks (src/util/parallel.hpp:56-90) has no try/catch. At threads<=1 it calls body inline, so an exception reaches run()'s per-trajectory catch (niceops.cpp ~2557). At threads>=2 the exception escapes a std::thread body, or unwinds the caller with joinable threads in `pool`, and either way std::terminate runs. The header comment at parallel.hpp:16-17 ("nothing here throws") is stale. Commit 7036c1a ("Decide what a failure costs in one place") turned open_output's fatal() into input_error, which throws, and did not touch parallel.hpp. The observer-loop body does throw. write_time_history_file and write_band_history_file go through open_output (niceops.cpp:66-74), and excess_ground_attenuation calls input_error at ground_effect.cpp:301. The thread count defaults to 0, which means all hardware threads (niceops.cpp:804), so the aborting path is the default configuration. No test uses parallel_chunks: grep of tests/ finds nothing. Two of the finding's five locations are weak. The ShadowTransition body at refraction.cpp:604 wraps upward_pressure, the only impedance_airy_zeros caller, in try/catch (lines 646-651), so Airy failures do not escape. Its other throws (588, 590) are argument checks before parallel_chunks. solve_pe_bands (pe.cpp:431) is called only from tools/pe_compare.cpp and tools/pe_field.cpp, where terminating on an invalid setup is tolerable. The partial-DNL claim is also confirmed. At -j 1 the failed trajectory's contribution stays in dnl_energy, which is written at the end of run() even though the trajectory was reported as skipped.
  - Refined claim: In the observer loop, parallel_chunks does not honor process_trajectory's per-trajectory error contract. Any input_error thrown from the body (an unwritable -i or --band_history file, or a non-positive ground-attenuation magnitude) is caught and skipped at -j 1 but calls std::terminate (SIGABRT, exit 134) at -j >= 2, which is the default. Terminate discards the rest of a batch and its DNL output. At -j 1 the failed trajectory's partial energy stays in dnl_energy and reaches the DNL file. The parallel.hpp header comment is stale since 7036c1a. The refraction.cpp:604 and pe.cpp:431 callers are not real instances: the first catches its Airy failures locally, and the second is used only by dev tools.
  - Verifier's fix: Wrap body() in try/catch inside `pull`. Keep the first std::exception_ptr under a mutex or atomic flag, set `next` to `count` so the other workers stop, join every thread, then rethrow on the caller. Guard the emplace_back loop so a failed thread creation joins the threads already started. Stage or snapshot the DNL energy in process_trajectory so a failed trajectory contributes nothing. For example, take a copy of dnl_energy before the loop and restore it in a catch-and-rethrow, or add into it only after the loop succeeds. Fix the header comment. Add a Catch2 case covering exactly-once coverage of [0,count) and exception propagation without leaked threads. Drop the refraction.cpp:604 and pe.cpp:431 sites from the justification; they do not throw in normal use. The success path stays bit-identical and the hot path pays one try block per chunk.
- **Verifier (context): confirmed, confidence 0.85.** Reproduced with the shipped binary, then fixed and re-tested in a scratch copy.

What holds:
- Shipped build, unwritable history dir (chmod 555 ro), 7x7 grid, `niceops -S state/ci_state.csv --gridstep 1640 -i ro/h -o ...`. At -j 1 it exits 1 with 'Skipping tracks/S-76D/197421AC.csv: Unable to open file for writing: ro/h_i0.csv' in log/niceops.log. At -j 4 it prints 'libc++abi: terminating due to uncaught exception of type std::runtime_error: Unable to open file for writing: ro/h_i2.csv' and exits 134.
- src/util/parallel.hpp lines 16-17 still say 'nothing here throws'. process_trajectory's comment (src/niceops.cpp ~668-669) promises 'Anything that throws in here condemns this trajectory alone'. `pull` (parallel.hpp 73-81) has no try/catch and the worker threads sit in std::thread frames, so the exception calls std::terminate.
- Partial DNL confirmed. At -j 1 with `-D`, integrated/dnl_partial.csv has the first data row `64.619797735286468,-inf,-inf,...`. src/niceops.cpp:1710 does `dnl_energy[index] += std::pow(...)` before write_time_history_file can throw. The failed trajectory leaks its energy into observer 0.
- Not deliberate. `git log -S` shows open_output was `fatal()` until 7036c1a ('Decide what a failure costs in one place'), so 'nothing here throws' was true when parallel.hpp was written. 7036c1a converted the site to input_error but never touched parallel.hpp or the workers. No CLAUDE.md, README or rejected-list entry covers it.

What the reporter overstated:
- Two of the four cited callers do not lose the per-trajectory contract.
  - The ShadowTransition build (refraction.cpp:604) wraps the `upward_pressure` call, which is where impedance_airy_zeros throws, in `catch (const std::exception&) { continue; }` (line 652). The only throws in that file are outside the body (lines 288-291, 511, 588, 590).
  - solve_pe_bands (pe.cpp:431) is called only from tools/pe_field.cpp, and that tool has no try/catch. An uncaught exception terminates at -j 1 too, so there is no thread-count difference and no per-trajectory contract to break.
- Only the observer loop in niceops.cpp is really affected. Its throw sites are open_output, `std::filesystem::create_directories` in ensure_parent_directory (via both history writers), and ground_effect.cpp:301, the non-positive-magnitude `input_error`. I did not trigger the ground_effect case. It needs exact cancellation or a NaN, so it looks rare, but nothing prevents it.
- It is not a regression. Before 7036c1a the same failures were fatal() aborts at any -j. At -j >= 2 nothing got worse; the intended 'skip this trajectory' improvement was never carried across the thread boundary.
- 'Every later trajectory in a batch is lost' adds little for the write-failure scenario. `-i` and `--band_history` use one prefix (`prefix_i<N>.csv`) for every trajectory, so a persistent permission or path problem fails all of them anyway. The real differences at -j >= 2 are SIGABRT, a possible core dump and exit 134 instead of a clean exit 1, no DNL file, and no continuation past a trajectory-specific failure such as the ground-effect one.
- Severity is more like low-to-medium than medium. It is a robustness gap, not wrong numbers on any success path.

The proposed fix works. I patched a scratch copy only, at /Users/evg5332/.claude/jobs/8792c8f6/tmp/scratch/verif_parallel_fix/tree, and left the repo untouched. The patch is a try/catch inside `pull` that stores the first exception_ptr under a mutex and sets `next` to `count`, then rethrows after join, plus a dnl_stage vector added to dnl_energy after the loop.
- The unwritable-directory command now exits 1 with exactly one 'Skipping' line at -j 1, 4 and 14.
- With `-D` the DNL is all -inf, with no partial row.
- Success paths are byte-identical to the shipped binary: the demo output (also byte-identical to golden/197421_LAE.csv), and a two-trajectory `-j 3 -D` run's two SEL files and its DNL file.
- A standalone TSAN harness over the patched header gave exactly-once coverage for threads {0,1,2,7,64} against counts {0,1,5,100,12345}, and the exception rethrown on every threaded run (4 and 64 workers).
  - Refined claim: In the observer loop of src/niceops.cpp, an input_error thrown from the loop body (open_output or ensure_parent_directory in the -i and --band_history writers, or the non-positive-magnitude check at src/ground_effect.cpp:301) skips only that trajectory at -j 1. At -j >= 2 it calls std::terminate (SIGABRT, exit 134), because `parallel_chunks` neither catches in the workers nor propagates to the caller. The header comment at parallel.hpp:16-17 ('nothing here throws') has been false since 7036c1a. Separately, at any -j, a failing trajectory leaves a partial contribution in `dnl_energy` (niceops.cpp:1710 runs before the throwing writes), so the DNL written after an exit-1 run carries a half-counted trajectory. The ShadowTransition and solve_pe_bands callers are not affected: the first catches its own exceptions and the second is only used by tools that have no handler.
  - Verifier's fix: Do the parallel.hpp change as proposed. Wrap `body()` in try/catch inside `pull`, keep the first `std::exception_ptr` under a mutex, `next.store(count)` so the other workers stop at their next chunk, join every thread, and rethrow on the caller. Update the header comment. For thread-creation failure, do not rethrow. Catch `std::system_error` around the `emplace_back` loop and carry on with the workers that started. Chunks are dealt on demand and the caller thread pulls too, so fewer workers only means a slower run, and the result is byte-identical. That is simpler and more robust than joining and rethrowing.

In process_trajectory, stage the DNL contribution in a per-trajectory `std::vector<double>` (`dnl_stage`, sized only when `want_dnl`). The loop writes `dnl_stage[index] = std::pow(...)`, and `dnl_energy[i] += dnl_stage[i]` runs once after `parallel_chunks` returns, before the log lines and `write_levels`. Each addend is the same double, so the result is bit-identical. Do not commit the staging at the very end of process_trajectory. A later write_levels failure would then drop a fully computed trajectory from the DNL, which differs from today's behavior.

Add a Catch2 case for parallel_chunks: exactly-once coverage for threads 0, 1, N and N > count, and count 0; propagation of a thrown exception at threads > 1 with all workers joined. Optionally add a CTest that runs `niceops -j 4 -i <unwritable>/h` and expects exit 1 rather than a signal.

### main-cli/A.r1.3: Some per-trajectory failures abort or terminate the whole process instead of skipping the trajectory (worker-thread exceptions, fatal in write_state)

- Finder's severity medium, category correctness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: R C×6**
- Locations: `src/util/parallel.hpp:16`, `src/util/parallel.hpp:73`, `src/niceops.cpp:1424`, `src/niceops.cpp:1717`, `src/niceops.cpp:66`, `src/ground_effect.cpp:301`, `src/trajectory.cpp:650`, `src/niceops.cpp:1785`
- **Claim.** parallel_chunks documents that 'nothing here throws' and lets an exception from the body terminate the process, but the observer-loop body can throw: write_time_history_file and write_band_history_file go through open_output, whose input_error (niceops.cpp:71) is a std::runtime_error, and excess_ground_attenuation throws input_error at ground_effect.cpp:301 from inside the per-pair loop. A throw on a worker thread hits std::terminate; a throw on the calling thread unwinds past a vector of joinable std::threads, which also terminates. The identical failure with -j 1 runs the body on the main thread and is caught by the per-trajectory try/catch (exit 1, trajectory skipped), so the behavior depends on the thread count. Separately, Trajectory::write_state calls fatal() for an unwritable -S path (trajectory.cpp:650, invoked at niceops.cpp:1787), which aborts the batch after the first trajectory's output.
- **Evidence given.** mkdir timehistory/x_i3.csv (a directory where a history file must go), then:
$ niceops --gridstep 492 -i timehistory/x -j 4 -o integrated/w.csv
libc++abi: terminating due to uncaught exception of type std::runtime_error: Unable to open file for writing: timehistory/x_i3.csv
exit=134, integrated/ empty (the footprint was never written).
$ niceops --gridstep 492 -i timehistory/x -j 1 -o integrated/w1.csv
exit=1, log: 'Skipping tracks/S-76D/197421AC.csv: Unable to open file for writing: timehistory/x_i3.csv'
Batch with -S to an unwritable path:
$ niceops -t 197421AC.csv -t 197402AC.csv --gridstep 492 -j 2 -S state/  -> Abort trap: 6, exit 134; only 197421AC_LAE.csv exists, 197402AC never processed; log '[fatal] Unable to open state file for writing: state/'.
README.md:222-223 promises 'one bad file in a batch costs that file alone'.
- **Scenario.** Re-running into a shared results directory that contains a read-only or directory-shadowed history file, a full quota, or a mistyped -S path, in a multi-hour batch. With -j > 1 the process dies with SIGABRT, nothing on the terminal, and no outputs from the current trajectory; with -S it also kills every later trajectory.
- **Proposed change.** In parallel_chunks catch std::exception_ptr per worker (first one wins, set a stop flag so remaining chunks are skipped), join every thread, then rethrow on the calling thread; that also makes the throw thread-count independent. Change trajectory.cpp:650 from fatal() to input_error() (the comment above it already documents the problem). Update the parallel.hpp comment.
- **Expected impact.** Failures cost one trajectory, as documented, at any -j; no effect on successful runs, whose output stays byte-identical because the work split is unchanged.
- **Conflicts with docs.** parallel.hpp:16-18 ('nothing here throws') is contradicted by open_output and ground_effect.cpp:301; README.md:222-223.

### main-loop/A.r1.1: An exception thrown inside the observer loop kills the whole process (std::terminate) instead of skipping the trajectory, and -j 1 behaves differently

- Finder's severity medium, category correctness, effort M, finder's confidence 0.95, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: R C×6**
- Locations: `src/util/parallel.hpp:16`, `src/util/parallel.hpp:73`, `src/niceops.cpp:66`, `src/niceops.cpp:1714`, `src/niceops.cpp:667`, `src/ground_effect.cpp:297`
- **Claim.** parallel.hpp says "nothing here throws", but the body calls open_output() (input_error -> std::runtime_error) for every -i and --band_history file, and excess_ground_attenuation() input_errors on a non-positive magnitude, all from worker threads. process_trajectory's contract ("Anything that throws in here condemns this trajectory alone; the caller catches and moves on") and run()'s per-trajectory try/catch only hold when the loop runs on the calling thread (-j 1). With the default thread count the exception escapes a std::thread, or the calling thread's pull() with joinable threads alive, and terminates the process.
- **Evidence given.** mkdir th; mkdir th/x_i5.csv (a directory where observer 5's history file goes), or a read-only output directory:
  $ niceops -S state/a.csv --gridstep 492 -i th/x -o integrated/a.csv -L warn
  libc++abi: terminating due to uncaught exception of type std::runtime_error: Unable to open file for writing: th/x_i5.csv   exit=134, no "Skipping" line in log, no -o file
  $ ... -j 1  -> log: "Skipping tracks/S-76D/197421AC.csv: Unable to open file for writing: th/x_i5.csv", exit=1
  chmod 555 ro; niceops --gridstep 984 -i ro/x -o integrated/ro.csv: default threads exit=134 with the same terminate message; -j 1 exit=1.
Second consequence, visible with -j 1: integrated_levels/dnl_energy are written observer by observer, so a mid-loop failure leaves partial state that is still output. `niceops --gridstep 984 -j 1 -i th/x -D integrated/pdnl.csv -o integrated/pl.csv` exits 1, writes no LAE file, but writes integrated/pdnl.csv with finite DNL for observers 0-5 and -inf for the rest (the failed trajectory's partial energy).
- **Scenario.** A batch `-t a -t b -t c -D dnl.csv -i hist/x` where hist/ is not writable, or a ground-effect run where one exact-ground pair cancels to magnitude <= 0: at default -j the first failure aborts the process, later trajectories and the DNL file are lost, and no error line reaches the log summary; at -j 1 the run continues and may publish a partially accumulated DNL.
- **Proposed change.** In parallel_chunks::pull wrap body() in try/catch, keep the first std::exception_ptr (atomic flag so other workers stop taking chunks), join every thread, then rethrow on the calling thread; fix the header comment. In process_trajectory accumulate into a per-trajectory local buffer for integrated_levels and dnl_energy and commit only after the loop succeeds, so a failed trajectory contributes nothing. Optionally check the ofstream state after writing in the three writers.
- **Expected impact.** Per-trajectory failure handling becomes identical at every -j; no aborts, no partial DNL.

### main-loop/B.r1.2: Any exception thrown inside the observer loop terminates the process when threads > 1, though process_trajectory promises a per-trajectory skip

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: R C×6**
- Locations: `src/util/parallel.hpp:16`, `src/util/parallel.hpp:73`, `src/niceops.cpp:66`, `src/niceops.cpp:669`, `src/niceops.cpp:1718`
- **Claim.** parallel_chunks runs `body(...)` on std::thread workers with no try/catch, and its header comment says 'nothing here throws'. That is no longer true. The per-observer writers reach open_output -> input_error (throws std::runtime_error) at :1718 and :1728, and excess_ground_attenuation throws input_error (ground_effect.cpp:301) from the per-pair loop. Any throw in a worker calls std::terminate. process_trajectory's contract (:669) and run()'s per-trajectory catch never see it. With threads <= 1 the body runs on the caller and the same error is handled cleanly, so behavior depends on -j.
- **Evidence given.** In a scratch run dir: `mkdir ro_dir; chmod 555 ro_dir; niceops -S state/ci_state.csv --gridstep 984 -i $PWD/ro_dir/th -j 1 ...` gives exit 1 and the log line '[error] Skipping tracks/S-76D/197421AC.csv: Unable to open file for writing: .../th_i0.csv'. The same command with -j 2 (or the default 14 threads) gives exit 134 and 'libc++abi: terminating due to uncaught exception of type std::runtime_error: Unable to open file for writing: .../th_i0.csv'. No footprint file is written (integrated/ro.csv absent). `--band_history $PWD/ro_dir/bh` on the B407 array case aborts the same way (exit 134). A two-trajectory run (`-t 197421AC.csv -t 197401AC.csv -o b1.csv -o b2.csv -i ro_dir/th`) aborts and writes neither b1.csv nor b2.csv. Main-thread create_directories (:~742) only proves the directory exists, not that it is writable, so it does not catch this.
- **Scenario.** A batch of the 84 shipped tracks with --band_history or -i pointed at a read-only or full location: the first trajectory kills the whole batch (SIGABRT) and any DNL accumulated so far is lost, where -j 1 would log the error and continue with the rest.
- **Proposed change.** In parallel_chunks' `pull`, wrap `body(begin, end)` in try/catch(...). Record the first std::exception_ptr under a mutex, set an atomic stop flag that the other workers check before pulling another chunk, and after joining rethrow it on the caller thread. About 15 lines, and it makes threads>1 behave like threads<=1. Update the header comment. Optionally add a Catch2 case: a body that throws for one chunk must propagate out of parallel_chunks(…, 4, …). None exists today; parallel_chunks has no unit test.
- **Expected impact.** Turns a process abort into the documented 'Skipping <track>: <reason>' and exit 1, for every thread count. No cost on the normal path.


## Errors and rejections never reach the terminal

### main-cli/A.r1.0: User-facing errors never reach the terminal once the log file is open (exit 1/2, empty stderr)

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.95, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: R C×4**
- Locations: `src/util/logger.hpp:157`, `src/util/logger.hpp:265`, `src/niceops.cpp:2259`, `src/niceops.cpp:2560`, `src/niceops.cpp:2618`, `CMakeLists.txt:296`
- **Claim.** Logger::write_line writes to the log file when it is open and to std::cerr only when it is not. run() opens log/niceops.log at niceops.cpp:2259, so every input_error/error after that point (all flag-combination checks at 2401-2448, --path_smoothing/--origin/--latlon, --plate_mic_azimuth, the atmosphere conflicts, the 'Skipping <track>' line at 2560, main()'s 'Stopping' at 2620, the plotting-off refusal) goes only to the log file. The process then exits 1 or 2 with nothing on stdout or stderr. Errors raised before line 2259 (--gridstep, --grid, per-trajectory list-size errors) do print, so whether a user sees a message depends on which check fires.
- **Evidence given.** Release build in a scratch run dir:
$ niceops --ground_effect --no-spectral --gridstep 492   -> exit=2, stdout empty, stderr empty; log tail: '[error] --ground_effect needs the spectral path: ...' and '[error] Stopping: ...'
$ niceops -t nonexist.csv --gridstep 492   -> exit=1, stderr empty; log: '[error] Skipping nonexist.csv: Unable to open CSV file: nonexist.csv'
$ niceops --gridstep -1 2>&1   -> prints '[error] --gridstep must be positive, got -1.' (checked at 2236, before set_file)
$ niceops --path_smoothing -1 2>&1   -> prints nothing, exit 2 (checked at 2271, after set_file)
README.md:1078 promises -p/-f/-F 'are then refused with a message rather than silently doing nothing' and README.md:222-223 says a bad trajectory 'is logged and skipped'; both are true only of the log file. CLAUDE.md and trajectory.cpp:645 record this silence for fatal() only. Only four call sites use error()/warn() directly (niceops.cpp:2560, 2620, plotting_disabled.cpp:28, trajectory.cpp:516) besides input_error, so mirroring is cheap. The two WILL_FAIL ctest cases (speed_reference_air_needs_wind, band_history_needs_spectral) pass on any nonzero exit, including a mistyped database path, so they do not pin the reason.
- **Scenario.** A user (or a batch script) runs niceops with --refraction linear and forgets --atmosphere, or points -t at a misspelt file. The tool exits 2 or 1 and prints nothing; the reason is 40 lines into log/niceops.log, which is easy to miss and which -L error/-L fatal suppress entirely (write_line filters on level, so with -L fatal even the 'Stopping' text is dropped).
- **Proposed change.** In Logger::write_line, additionally std::cerr the line when level >= error (and warn) even when a file is open, independent of -L for the terminal copy of error/fatal. That also makes fatal()'s abort visible. Then give the two WILL_FAIL tests PASS_REGULAR_EXPRESSION on their messages so they pin the reason, and drop the 'dump the log on failure' workaround's necessity in ci.yml.
- **Expected impact.** Every misconfiguration and every skipped trajectory becomes visible at the terminal; ctest error-path cases stop passing for the wrong reason. No effect on computed output.
- **Conflicts with docs.** README.md:1078 and 223 claim messages/logging that only exist in the log file; CLAUDE.md treats stderr silence as a fatal()-only property and calls input_error's path 'reported'.

### main-cli/B.r1.2: Errors and rejections never reach the terminal (exit 1 or 2 with empty stderr)

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: R C×4**
- Locations: `src/util/logger.hpp:157`, `src/niceops.cpp:2557`, `src/niceops.cpp:2605`
- **Claim.** Logger::write_line writes to std::cerr only when no log file is open (line 170). run() opens log/niceops.log first, so every input_error, every 'Skipping <track>' and the 'Stopping:' line in main() go to the file only. A rejected invocation exits with a bare status code and no message. CLAUDE.md documents this for fatal() (CI dumps the log on failure). The same design has already cost the maintainers once, in trajectory.cpp:643-646, and it affects every error path.
- **Evidence given.** In a scratch dir with the shipped build, `niceops --refraction linear` exited 2 and printed nothing. The reason ('--refraction linear needs --atmosphere') is at log/niceops.log:1338. `niceops --ground_effect --no-spectral` exited 2 with nothing printed. `niceops -t nonexist.csv` exited 1 with nothing printed. `niceops -d nonexist.nod` exited 2 with nothing printed. Only CLI11's own parse errors print (e.g. exit 109 'The following argument was not expected'). warn/error calls in the tree: 1 warn (trajectory.cpp:516) and 3 error, so an echo would be quiet. Results-affecting conditions such as the tip-speed mismatch at niceops.cpp:~683 are logged at info and are equally invisible.
- **Scenario.** A user or a script (or a CI job that does not dump the log) sees a nonzero exit with nothing to explain it and has to know to open log/niceops.log, which is relative to the cwd.
- **Proposed change.** In Logger::write_line, when the level is warn or above and a log file is open, also print the message to std::cerr (it is already under the mutex). Optionally change the tip-speed mismatch at niceops.cpp:~683 from log() to warn(). No change to the CSV outputs or exit codes.
- **Expected impact.** Every rejected invocation says why on the terminal. ctest output for a failing test carries the reason, which makes the 'Show the log on failure' CI step a convenience rather than a necessity. About +4 LOC.
- **Conflicts with docs.** CLAUDE.md ('Note what such a failure looks like: fatal() logs its reason to log/niceops.log ... nothing reaches stdout or stderr') and the logger.hpp header describe the current behavior. They do not present it as a design goal, and the trajectory.cpp:643-646 comment treats it as a pitfall.

### spline-util/A.r1.3: Almost every input error is invisible to the person running the tool: after set_file the message goes only to the log, and -L error/fatal drops even that

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: R C×4**
- Locations: `src/util/logger.hpp:157`, `src/util/logger.hpp:265`, `src/niceops.cpp:2259`, `src/niceops.cpp:2557`, `src/niceops.cpp:2618`
- **Claim.** write_line() sends output to std::cerr only when no log file is open (`if (file_.is_open()) { file_ << line; flush } else { std::cerr << line; }`). run() opens the log at niceops.cpp:2259, before most validation (path_smoothing, origin, ground-effect/atmosphere combinations, database and trajectory loading). From then on input_error(), the per-trajectory 'Skipping ...' handler, main()'s 'Stopping: ...' handler and fatal() reach the log file only. The comment on main() says such failures are 'reported', but the process exits with status 2 (or 1, or SIGABRT for fatal) and prints nothing. In addition all of it goes through log_enabled(), so with -L error or -L fatal an input_error is not even written to the file.
- **Evidence given.** `niceops --gridstep -1` (validated before set_file) prints '[error] --gridstep must be positive, got -1.' to the terminal, exit 2. `niceops --path_smoothing -5 -S state/x.csv >out 2>err`: exit=2, stdout 0 bytes, stderr 0 bytes, log/niceops.log holds '[error] --path_smoothing must not be negative, got -5.' and '[error] Stopping: ...'. `niceops --database /nonexistent.nod`: exit=2, 0 bytes on both streams, message only in log. `niceops -L fatal --path_smoothing -5`: exit=2, 0 bytes on stdout, stderr and in the log file. The geodetic failure in the previous finding likewise appears only as '[error] Skipping ...' in the log with exit 1. Every message also appears twice in the log (input_error logs, then main logs 'Stopping: ' + what()).
- **Scenario.** A user mistypes the database path or combines flags illegally and the tool exits silently; from a script or CI job the only signal is the code. CLAUDE.md records the fatal() variant as a trap (CTest shows 'Subprocess aborted' with empty output; CI dumps the log), but the same applies to the ordinary, recoverable input_error paths that were meant to fix that.
- **Proposed change.** Echo anything at level error or above to std::cerr as well when the sink is a file, independent of the -L threshold (or do it in main()'s catch, the 'Skipping' handler and fatal(), which are the only three places that end a run). Keep the file line. Optionally make write_line() for input_error skip the second 'Stopping:' duplicate.
- **Expected impact.** Failures become self-explanatory at the terminal and in CTest output; no change to stdout, output files or goldens.
- **Conflicts with docs.** CLAUDE.md documents fatal()'s log-only behavior as a known trait ('nothing reaches stdout or stderr ... CI dumps the log'); it does not say the input_error/'Stopping:'/'Skipping' paths are equally silent, and main()'s comment says they are 'reported'. The three WILL_FAIL ctest cases only check the exit status, so a stderr echo does not affect them.

### spline-util/B.r1.0: warn/error/fatal reach only log/niceops.log: failed runs exit 1, 2 or abort with empty stdout and stderr

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: R C×4**
- Locations: `src/util/logger.hpp:157`, `src/niceops.cpp:2557`, `src/niceops.cpp:2618`, `src/util/logger.hpp:264`
- **Claim.** Logger::write_line writes to std::cerr only when the log file failed to open (`if (file_.is_open()) { file_ << line; file_.flush(); } else { std::cerr << line; }`). Once set_file() has succeeded, every level, including warn, error and fatal, goes to the file alone. CLAUDE.md records this for fatal() as an observation and works around it in CI. It is equally true of every ordinary user-facing failure: input_error() throws, main() catches it and calls `error("Stopping: ", ...)` then `return 2`; a bad trajectory is `error("Skipping ", ...)` then exit 1; warn() data diagnostics (trajectory.cpp:516) never reach the terminal.
- **Evidence given.** Ran the shipped Release binary in a scratch dir:
  niceops --path_smoothing -1 -l log/err.log   -> exit=2, stdout empty, stderr empty; log/err.log holds '[error] --path_smoothing must not be negative, got -1.' and '[error] Stopping: ...'
  niceops -t nonexistent.csv -l log/err2.log   -> exit=1, stdout empty, stderr empty; log: '[error] Skipping nonexistent.csv: Unable to open CSV file: nonexistent.csv'
No CTest test depends on stderr content: the only WILL_FAIL tests are speed_reference_air_needs_wind and band_history_needs_spectral (CMakeLists.txt:299, 358) and there is no *_REGULAR_EXPRESSION property. tools/pe_field.cpp and pe_compare.cpp already print progress to stderr, so stderr is not off-limits.
- **Scenario.** A user mistypes a track or database path, or passes an option combination the tool refuses (e.g. --ground_effect with --no-spectral). The command returns 1 or 2 and prints nothing; the reason is in a file whose default location (log/niceops.log, relative to the cwd) they may not know to look in. Combined with the unvalidated atmosphere overrides below, even a run that goes wrong only 'says so' in the log.
- **Proposed change.** In Logger::write_line, after the file write, add `if (level >= LogLevel::warn) std::cerr << line;` under the same lock (about 3 lines). Keep the file write so the log stays complete. The CI log-dump step can stay as belt and braces, or be trimmed.
- **Expected impact.** Every refusal, skipped trajectory and data warning becomes visible at the terminal; a CTest 'Subprocess aborted' failure would now carry its reason. Cold path only, so no timing effect on the loop.
- **Conflicts with docs.** CLAUDE.md ('Note what such a failure looks like: fatal() logs its reason to log/niceops.log and calls abort(), so nothing reaches stdout or stderr. CTest reports only Subprocess aborted ... CI dumps the log on failure for this reason') documents the fatal() case as a hazard to work around. New here: the identical silence applies to input_error/main's exit 1 and 2 and to warn(), i.e. to normal user errors, and one three-line change removes the root cause. All output files stay byte-identical; only stderr changes.


## Non-increasing or zero time step gives -inf/NaN footprint

### main-setup/B.r1.3: A trajectory whose time stamps do not increase produces an all -inf or all NaN footprint and exit 0

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.85, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: R V2 C×3**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:720`, `src/util/sel.hpp:102`
- **Claim.** The SEL time step comes from uniform_sample_interval, the mean spacing (t_last - t_first)/(n-1). Nothing checks that it is positive. With a constant or descending time column the step is 0 or negative, the log says only 'Integrating with the trajectory's sample interval of 0 s.' at info level, and every cell is written as -inf or nan. The run exits 0 and writes a plausible-looking file. CLAUDE.md already treats the 'yields nothing but exits 0' pattern as a bug for observer files.
- **Evidence given.** Demo track with column 2 rewritten (awk) to a constant 0.0: `niceops -t exp_flat_time.csv --gridstep 492` exit 0, log 'Integrating with the trajectory's sample interval of 0 s.', level rows all '-inf'. With time = -0.02*k: interval -0.02 s, rows all 'nan', exit 0. Checked all 84 shipped tracks (python): every one has strictly increasing time, so a check cannot reject a shipped input.
- **Scenario.** A track exported with a broken or repeated time column (or descending time) runs to completion and writes an all -inf or NaN footprint. A batch script sees exit 0 and archives it.
- **Proposed change.** After computing integration_step (niceops.cpp:733), if nt >= 2 and !(integration_step > 0.0), throw via input_error naming the file and the first non-increasing sample. It is caught per trajectory, like the other bad-input cases. Optionally reject in the Trajectory constructor so every consumer benefits.
- **Expected impact.** Turns silent garbage into a per-trajectory 'Skipping ...' error and a non-zero exit.
- **Verifier (trace): confirmed, confidence 0.88.** I re-read the code and reproduced the claim with the shipped Release build, run from a scratch directory.

Code path. `src/niceops.cpp:720-741` takes `integration_step = uniform_sample_interval(sample_times, &relative_spread)` and only logs it at info level. `src/util/sel.hpp:102-121` returns `(t_back - t_front)/(n-1)` with no sign check. When `mean <= 0` the `if (mean > 0.0)` guard skips the spread loop, so `relative_spread` stays 0 and the "sampling is not uniform" warning never fires either. Nothing else rejects the value. `tests/test_peak_range.cpp:70-87` covers only the positive, empty and single-sample cases.

Upstream guards. The AC (27-column) branch of the `Trajectory` constructor (`src/trajectory.cpp:565-601`) copies `data[1]` into `now.t` with no monotonicity check. `correct_vertical_speed_sign` even has `if (!(dt > 0.0)) continue;` at line 499, so bad time is tolerated there. The geodetic reader does check, at `src/trajectory.cpp:222-227` (`if (!(time[row] > time[row - 1])) input_error("Time does not increase between data rows ...")`). `tests/test_trajectory.cpp:299` has a `backwards` REQUIRE_THROWS for it. So the finding is a real gap: the AC format is unchecked, the WGS84 format is checked.

Reproduction on the shipped build. I took the demo track and rewrote column 2 with awk.
- Constant time 0.0: `niceops -t state/flat_time.csv --gridstep 492` exits 0. The log line is "Integrating with the trajectory's sample interval of 0 s." All 441 level cells are `-inf`.
- Descending time (-0.02*k): exit 0, the log says "sample interval of -0.02 s", and all 441 cells are `nan`.
- stdout and stderr are empty in both runs (0 bytes) and no warning appears in the log.

Control. A geodetic track with constant time exits 1. The log reads "[error] Skipping state/geo_flat.csv: Time does not increase between data rows 1 and 2 of state/geo_flat.csv: 0 then 0." and "1 of 1 trajectories could not be processed."

Safety of a fix. I scanned all 84 AC-format files under `tracks/` and `tests/data` with python. Every one has strictly increasing time, so a check cannot reject a shipped input. Valid-input output is unaffected, so the goldens and the demo bytes do not change.

Severity. Real but modest: the trigger is a corrupt time column, which no shipped input has. I would rate it low-to-medium, not a defect that touches valid runs.
  - Refined claim: The AC (27-column) trajectory reader never checks that the time column increases, although the WGS84 reader does (`src/trajectory.cpp:222-227`, tested at `tests/test_trajectory.cpp:299`). With a constant or descending time column, `uniform_sample_interval` returns 0 or a negative mean.
