The run then writes an all `-inf` or all `nan` footprint, exits 0, prints nothing to stdout/stderr, and leaves only an info-level log line.
  - Verifier's fix: Better than the proposed `!(integration_step > 0)` check in `niceops.cpp`: add the same strictly-increasing loop to the AC branch of the `Trajectory` constructor. Put it after the states are parsed at `src/trajectory.cpp:~601`, or as a shared helper with the check at line 222. It should use `input_error` and name the row numbers, so it is caught per trajectory as "Skipping ..." and gives exit 1. The mean-step check alone would miss a track whose time resets partway (positive mean, large spread, only a log-level warning). The strict check catches that too. But it also rejects duplicate timestamps that currently run with only a log warning, so the maintainer should decide whether that is wanted. If the narrower behavior is preferred, a `!(integration_step > 0)` guard in `process_trajectory` after line 733 catches only the 0 and negative-mean cases. Add a REQUIRE_THROWS in `tests/test_trajectory.cpp` for an AC file with a repeated or descending time, mirroring the `backwards` case at line 299.
- **Verifier (context): confirmed, confidence 0.85.** Reproduced independently, and nothing in the docs or history makes it deliberate.

Reproduction with the shipped Release binary in a scratch dir, on the demo AC track with column 2 rewritten:
- Constant time 0.0: `niceops -t flat.csv --gridstep 492 -o integrated/flat.csv` gave exit 0. The log says `[info] Integrating with the trajectory's sample interval of 0 s.` All 441 cells are `-inf`.
- Descending time (-0.02*k): exit 0, `[info] ... interval of -0.02 s.`, all 441 cells `nan`.

Deliberateness:
- `26f4c0c` introduced `uniform_sample_interval`. Its header comment says "Mean spacing of a monotonically increasing time series", so the precondition is stated but enforced nowhere on the AC path.
- The unit test covers only the empty and single-sample cases, which return 0.
- CLAUDE.md and the README say nothing that favours accepting such input.
- The geodetic reader already rejects it. `src/trajectory.cpp:222-225` throws `input_error("Time does not increase between data rows ...")`, and `SmoothingSpline::validate` (`src/smoothing_spline.cpp:115-118`) rejects non-increasing x. The 27-column AC path (`src/trajectory.cpp:~570`, `now.t = data[1]`) has no equivalent, so this is a real asymmetry.

Fit with existing conventions:
- The fix mechanism works at this point in the code. I ran the demo with `--speed_reference air` (an existing `input_error` inside `process_trajectory`, line 646, before the integration-step block). It gave "Skipping <path>: ...", "1 of 1 trajectories could not be processed", exit 1, and no output file. So a throw at ~line 735 gets per-trajectory handling and a non-zero exit through the existing `failed_paths` logic (niceops.cpp:2560, 2602).
- Real inputs are unaffected. All 84 shipped AC tracks have strictly increasing time (my own check, not the reporter's). So do all 281 27-column CSVs under ~/Code/Python/niceops-validation. Unit tests use the real tracks, or writers with real times.

Corrections to the report:
- The output is not "plausible-looking". It is an obvious file of all `-inf` or all `nan`. The real harm is the exit code, since a batch script that checks only status archives it.
- Trigger likelihood is low. It needs a corrupted or degenerate time column, and no shipped or validation input has one.
- Severity is therefore low to medium, not medium. It is worth fixing because the fix is about 4 lines, has no side effects on valid input, and matches the geodetic reader.
  - Refined claim: On the 27-column AC path, a time column with zero or negative mean spacing makes `uniform_sample_interval` return 0 or a negative step. `process_trajectory` (src/niceops.cpp:733-734) logs it at info level and integrates anyway. Every cell comes out `-inf` (step 0) or `nan` (step < 0), and the run exits 0. The geodetic reader already rejects the same defect with "Time does not increase between data rows", so only the AC path lacks the guard. Reproduced: constant time gives 441/441 `-inf`, descending time gives 441/441 `nan`, both exit 0.
  - Verifier's fix: Recommended: a minimal guard right after the `uniform_sample_interval` call in `process_trajectory`, inside the `nt >= 2` block, for example `if (!(integration_step > 0.0) || !std::isfinite(integration_step)) input_error("Trajectory ", job.trajectory, ": the time column does not increase (first ", state_time0, " s, last ", state_time_last, " s over ", nt, " states), so there is no sample interval to integrate over.");`.

Why this form:
- It rejects only mean-step <= 0 or non-finite input, which is exactly the set that provably yields all `-inf` or `nan`.
- It does not reject files that currently run correctly. A stricter per-row check (as the geodetic reader has) would also reject an AC file with a duplicated or coarsely quantized timestamp but a positive mean. Such a file runs today with a "not uniform" warning and a usable result.
- The strict check is a behavior change the maintainer should choose deliberately. If wanted, put it in the AC parse or the `Trajectory` constructor next to `trajectory.cpp:222` so both formats share one message. Do not make it the default fix.

Add a test: a small AC file with constant or descending time should make `Trajectory` or `process_trajectory` throw.

### main-loop/A.r1.5: AC-format tracks with a constant or decreasing time column are accepted and produce an all -inf or all NaN footprint with exit 0

- Finder's severity low, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.95, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: R C×3**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:725`, `src/util/sel.hpp:102`, `src/trajectory.cpp:224`
- **Claim.** integration_step is the mean of the time column and is used unchecked as the SEL weight. The geodetic reader rejects non-increasing time (trajectory.cpp:224), the 27-column reader does not. A zero mean gives 10*log10(0) = -inf; a negative mean gives log10 of a negative = NaN; the only trace is an info-level line "Integrating with the trajectory's sample interval of 0 s".
- **Evidence given.** hover_t0.csv (500 rows, time column all 0): `niceops -t hover_t0.csv -b obs_hover.csv` exit=0, levels `-inf,-inf,...`, log "sample interval of 0 s". hover_rev.csv (time decreasing by 0.02): exit=0, levels `nan`, log "sample interval of -0.02 s". The non-uniform warning is skipped when the mean is not positive because `worst` stays 0 (sel.hpp:114).
- **Scenario.** Concatenated or hand-edited AC files whose time column was reset, zeroed or reversed yield a footprint of -inf/NaN cells that plots as nothing, with exit status 0 in a batch script.
- **Proposed change.** After uniform_sample_interval, `if (!(integration_step > 0.0)) input_error(...)` naming the file and the interval; optionally check strict monotonicity of state.t in the AC reader as the geodetic one does.
- **Expected impact.** Malformed time columns fail loudly per trajectory.

### main-io-sel/A.r1.4: A non-positive trajectory sample interval gives an all-NaN or all -inf footprint with exit 0 and no diagnostic; result-invalidating conditions are logged at info

- Finder's severity low, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.85, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: R C×3**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:726`, `src/util/sel.hpp:102`, `src/util/sel.hpp:148`, `src/niceops.cpp:685`
- **Claim.** integration_step = uniform_sample_interval(times) is the mean spacing. When the time column decreases or is constant the mean is <= 0, the spread check is skipped by design (`if (mean > 0.0)`), nothing validates the step, and sel_over_window returns 10*log10(T*sum) = NaN (T<0) or -inf (T=0) for every observer. The only trace is the info line 'Integrating with the trajectory's sample interval of -0.02 s.' Separately, the two other messages that say the numbers are wrong ('Main rotor tip speed disagrees ... mu and CT will index the wrong conditions', 'sampling is not uniform') go through log() = info, so `-L warn`/`-L error` (used by every ctest and any quiet batch run) drops them; warn() exists and is used elsewhere.
- **Evidence given.** Track copy with column 1 negated: `niceops -t neg_t.csv ... -L info` -> exit 0, 'sample interval of -0.02 s', every output cell `nan`. Column 1 all zero -> 'sample interval of 0 s', every cell `-inf`. Vehicle JSON with tip speed 600 instead of 220 (S-76D.json edited): with `-L warn` the log is empty and the run exits 0; the footprint is shifted by mean -0.40 dB, min -2.80, max +1.99 dB against the correct run (gridstep 984). At info the message is only in log/niceops.log, as with all logging.
- **Scenario.** A concatenated or time-reset AC file, or a mis-scaled vehicle file, produces a plausible-looking or all-NaN footprint with a zero exit status; a batch script that runs with -L warn to keep logs small never sees why.
- **Proposed change.** After computing it, `if (!(integration_step > 0.0)) input_error(...)` naming the file; emit the tip-speed mismatch and non-uniform-sampling messages with warn() (or make the tip-speed mismatch an error unless overridden).
- **Expected impact.** Bad time columns and mismatched vehicle files are caught at the source instead of downstream.


## Non-finite or malformed input accepted silently

### trajectory-geodetic/B.r1.1: parse_numeric_row accepts nan/inf, so one NaN field in a track or observer file silently turns most of the footprint into NaN (exit 0)

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.95, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: R**
- Locations: `src/util/csv.hpp:41`, `src/trajectory.cpp:567`
- **Claim.** parse_numeric_row treats a field as numeric if `std::stod` consumes all of it (csv.hpp:54-58: `const double value = std::stod(field, &pos); if (pos != field.size()) return false; row.push_back(value);`). stod accepts `nan`, `NaN`, `inf` and `infinity`, so a row containing one is kept as data instead of being dropped like every other non-numeric row. The header comment says 'Lines that contain a non-numeric field ... are skipped', which is not what happens for NaN. Flight-test exports very commonly write NaN for a dropped measurement. For the AC format the NaN goes straight into derive_flight_parameters (LF, CT, fpa_e become NaN) and from there into the sphere blend. Nothing warns, and no shipped data file contains a non-finite value, so tightening this does not touch the goldens.
- **Evidence given.** Copy of tracks/S-76D/197421AC.csv with the `ax` field of data row 3000 replaced by `NaN`:
$ niceops -t nan_track.csv --gridstep 492 -o integrated/nan_out.csv   -> exit 0, no warn/error line in log/niceops.log
The 441-cell output contains 298 NaN cells (68%). The rest are finite (min 62.08 dB).
The same reader feeds observer files (read_numeric_csv, niceops.cpp:408) and atmosphere profiles (read_labeled_csv, atmosphere_profile.cpp:126), so NaN/inf coordinates or humidity also pass.
- **Scenario.** An AC-format or observer CSV exported with a `NaN` for one missing sample. The run completes with exit 0 and writes a footprint that is mostly NaN. Because exit is 0, a batch script cannot tell.
- **Proposed change.** Reject non-finite values in parse_numeric_row (`if (!std::isfinite(value)) return false;`) so the row is dropped like any non-numeric row. Better, count dropped data rows in LabeledCsv / read_numeric_csv and have the trajectory and observer loaders warn or fail when a data row (not the header or a units line) was dropped, because a dropped row is a hole in the time series. Add a test with a `nan` field.
- **Expected impact.** Turns a silent all-NaN footprint into either a dropped row plus a non-uniform-sampling warning, or a clear error. No effect on any shipped input.

### trajectory-geodetic/A.r1.2: CSV readers accept nan/inf and silently drop malformed rows, so one bad field yields a NaN footprint with exit 0

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: R**
- Locations: `src/util/csv.hpp:41`, `src/util/csv.hpp:163`, `src/trajectory.cpp:173`, `src/trajectory.cpp:565`
- **Claim.** parse_numeric_row uses std::stod, which accepts `nan`, `inf` and `infinity`, so a non-finite field is valid data. Nothing in the AC branch or in read_geodetic_states checks finiteness (geodetic checks only lat/lon range; time monotonicity fails only for NaN time). The other direction is also silent: an empty field (`1,,3`), or a value stod reports as out_of_range (an overflow, or a denormal such as `1e-320`), makes the catch/return false path drop the whole row. Nothing counts or reports rows dropped after the first data row. Only the coarse 'sampling is not uniform' info line, in the log file, hints at it.
- **Evidence given.** Demo AC track with x of row 3000 set to `NaN`: `niceops -t nan_x.csv ... --gridstep 492 --no-spectral` exits 0, logs `Parsed 6551 trajectory states.` with no warning, and 298 of 441 output cells are `nan` (same for a NaN az). An empty x field or `1e-320` in one row: `Parsed 6550 trajectory states.`, exit 0, and only `Trajectory sampling is not uniform (worst interval deviates by 99.9695% ...)`. A geodetic file with one `NaN` altitude in 100 rows: 100 of 100 states have non-finite z/CT, because the NaN spreads through the spline solve. A vehicle JSON with `"tip speed": "0"` also loads (mu=inf, CT=inf), and a missing key throws `type must be number, but is null` without naming the key or the file.
- **Scenario.** A pandas or Excel export writes `NaN` for a missing sensor sample, or drops a value. The footprint is silently partly or wholly NaN, or a row vanishes and shifts the record. Nothing in the run output says which file or row.
- **Proposed change.** In parse_numeric_row reject non-finite values. Have both readers count and report (warn, with the first line number) any non-numeric line that comes after the first numeric row, leaving leading header or units lines free. Give read_geodetic_states and the AC branch an explicit `input_error` on a non-finite value that names the row and column. Optionally validate the vehicle JSON (positive radius, tip speed, rho, weight; name the missing key).
- **Expected impact.** Turns silent NaN output and silently shortened tracks into a diagnosable error. No effect on clean input.

### spline-util/A.r1.0: A NaN/inf altitude (or any non-finite y) sails through SmoothingSpline and the geodetic reader: every footprint cell becomes NaN, exit 0, no diagnostic

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.95, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/smoothing_spline.cpp:107`, `src/trajectory.cpp:173`
- **Claim.** validate() checks x ordering, sizes and weights, but never that y (or x, or the weights) are finite. read_geodetic_states range-checks latitude/longitude with fabs(..)<=90/180 (which rejects NaN) but reads the altitude and velocity columns unchecked (trajectory.cpp:199-200 `data[columns.altitude] * unit.to_meters - input.ground_elevation`). std::stod accepts 'nan', so one bad altitude poisons the spline. within_tolerance then does nothing sensible with a NaN residual: `upper_residual < tolerance` is false, `lower_residual > tolerance` is false, and the bisection's `residual_at(...) > tolerance` is always false, so it silently returns fit(exp(log_lower)) whose fitted values, curvature and every derived state are NaN.
- **Evidence given.** Made a geodetic CSV (time,lat,lon,alt) from tracks/S-76D/197421AC.csv and set row 3000's alt to 'nan'. `niceops -t geo_nan.csv -o integrated/out_geo_nan.csv -j 4 --origin 39.8104276 -74.4263086 --frame_bearing 210 --ground_elevation 54.2651` -> exit=0, stdout 0 bytes, stderr 0 bytes; the log ends with '[info] Evaluated 94334400 of 94334400 (100%)' and '[info] Gridded output.' with no warn/error line. Output file: 14400 cells, 14400 of them 'nan'. The identical file without the NaN row runs normally (exit 0, finite levels). Root cause visible at src/smoothing_spline.cpp:344 (`while (upper_residual < tolerance && ...)`), :365 (`while (lower_residual > tolerance && ...)`) and :379 (`if (residual_at(std::exp(log_mid)) > tolerance)`): all three comparisons are false for NaN.
- **Scenario.** A flight-test export with a dropped altitude sample written as NaN (common from pandas/MATLAB dumps) is fed to a WGS84 run. The user gets a full-size, all-NaN levels file and a zero exit code; a batch script cannot tell it failed. The precedent in the code base (an observer file that yields nothing is an error, CLAUDE.md) is the opposite policy.
- **Proposed change.** In SmoothingSpline validate(), reject non-finite x, y and weights with the offending index (input_error), so the existing per-trajectory catch in run() skips the file with a named reason. Optionally also check the altitude/velocity columns in read_geodetic_states with the row number (better message than a spline index), and consider treating an output grid that is entirely non-finite as an error. Add a test next to 'Unusable geodetic input is rejected where it can be named' in tests/test_trajectory.cpp.
- **Expected impact.** Turns a silent all-NaN result into a named, skippable input error (exit 1). No effect on valid input.

### main-setup/A.r1.4: AC-format trajectory rows are never validated: one NaN cell or a non-increasing time column gives a NaN or -inf footprint with exit 0

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: R**
- Locations: `src/trajectory.cpp:574`, `src/niceops.cpp:725`, `src/util/sel.hpp:102`
- **Claim.** The geodetic reader checks finiteness and strictly increasing time (trajectory.cpp:170-180, 222-226). The 27-column AC reader does neither. A NaN in any row propagates through LF, the normal and CT into the sphere lookup. A time column that is not increasing makes uniform_sample_interval return a mean of zero or a negative number, which process_trajectory uses as the integration step T. The log says only 'Integrating with the trajectory's sample interval of -0.02 s.' (the non-uniformity warning is skipped when mean <= 0).
- **Evidence given.** Modified copies of tracks/S-76D/197421AC.csv, run as `niceops -t X.csv --gridstep 984`. (a) time column negated: rc=0, log 'sample interval of -0.02 s', every output cell 'nan'. (b) time column constant 5.0: rc=0, 'sample interval of 0 s', every cell '-inf'. (c) column 21 (ax) of row 3000 set to 'nan': rc=0, 79 of 132 numeric fields in the output are nan, and there is no diagnostic. (A blank cell instead drops the row: 'Parsed 6550 trajectory states' at info level only.)
- **Scenario.** A concatenated or corrupted flight-test file, or a track with a dropped or garbled sample. A single bad row destroys most of the footprint and the run still reports success.
- **Proposed change.** After the AC states are built (or in process_trajectory), require every field of every State to be finite. Require time to be strictly increasing, as read_geodetic_states does, and fail with the row number. Also raise input_error when integration_step is not > 0.
- **Expected impact.** Converts silent NaN or -inf output into a legible per-trajectory error.

### refraction-profile/A.r1.0: read_atmosphere_profile accepts NaN/inf cells and silently drops any level with an unparseable cell; a NaN temperature turns the whole footprint into nan at exit 0

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.95, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/atmosphere_profile.cpp:312`, `src/atmosphere_profile.cpp:355`, `src/util/csv.hpp:41`
- **Claim.** The profile reader never checks that the values it keeps are finite. parse_numeric_row uses std::stod, which accepts 'nan' and 'inf', so those cells are stored. std::minmax_element skips NaN, so the temperature, humidity and pressure range checks pass. The wind-component check catches NaN only through hypot(u,v)<=150; a NaN wind direction passes, and an 'inf' height passes the strict-ascent check. A blank, 'NA' or '--' cell is the opposite failure: parse_numeric_row returns false and read_labeled_csv drops that entire level with no message, so the profile quietly loses a level (and its top).
- **Evidence given.** Experiment 1, reader only (/Users/evg5332/.claude/jobs/8792c8f6/tmp/scratch/r1_refr/atm_nan.cpp). Results: 'nan-T-mid: 3 levels; T=288 nan 286' (accepted); 'nan-RH' accepted; 'nan-p' accepted; 'nan-dir: wind_east=2 nan 6' accepted; 'inf-height' logged as '3 levels from 0 to inf m above the observers'; 'blank-row-cell: 2 levels' and 'NA-top: 2 levels from 0 to 100 m' (the 200 m level vanished, no warning). Only 'nan-wind-mid' (NaN in u_mps) was rejected.
Experiment 2, end to end: B407_fixed_load_factor.nod, demo track, example_windy.csv with one cell changed, `niceops ... --gridstep 492 --atmosphere atm_X.csv --refraction linear --frame_bearing 210 -j 4`.
- T_K of the 500 ft row = nan: exit 0, 439 of 462 grid values in the output are the literal 'nan'. The log has no warning, only '[info] absorption layered ... nan dB/m at the top' and 'the gradient is nan /s toward +x'.
- wind_direction of that row = nan: the same 439 of 462 nan.
- RH of that row = nan: no nan in the output, but cells differ from the clean run by up to 36.2 dB, silently. NaN bands appear to fall out of the band sum.
- That row's wind_speed cell blank: the level is dropped (15 levels) and the footprint moves by up to 1.74 dB, silently.
Read sites: 'if (!(speed >= 0.0) ...' and 'std::hypot(east, north) <= 150.0' are the only finiteness-adjacent checks.
- **Scenario.** A radiosonde or reanalysis CSV exported with missing values ('nan' from numpy/R, or blank cells from a spreadsheet or pandas) is passed to --atmosphere. The run finishes with exit 0. The footprint is nan almost everywhere, or, for a humidity gap, tens of dB wrong with no nan to notice. A blank cell instead shortens the profile, which changes absorption and refraction with only a level count in the log to show for it.
- **Proposed change.** In read_atmosphere_profile, require std::isfinite on the height, temperature, humidity, pressure and wind cells of every kept row, and fail with the row number and column name (input_error). Have read_labeled_csv count the data rows it drops. Allow a drop only for the documented units line directly under the header; a dropped row after a parsed row should be an error in the profile reader, or at least a log warning naming the line. Add tests: nan in T/RH/p/direction, inf height, and a blank cell mid-file.
- **Expected impact.** Turns silent all-nan or tens-of-dB-wrong output into a clear input error. No effect on valid files.

### main-io-sel/A.r1.3: Observer files: the row shape is decided per row and unparseable rows are dropped silently, so a typo or a short row changes which observers are evaluated with exit 0

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort M, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:400`, `src/util/csv.hpp:36`, `src/util/csv.hpp:82`
- **Claim.** read_observer_file only errors when *every* row is unusable. Rows that fail parse_numeric_row are dropped by read_numeric_csv with no count or log line, and rows of 3, 4 or 5 fields are each accepted independently (`data.size() == 3` is x,y,z; 4 is id,x,y,z), so a 4-column file with one short row silently gains an observer whose 'id' is its x. Non-numeric IDs drop the row too. The output has no id column, so the result cannot be reconciled with the input afterwards. (Heights are guarded for all-or-none at lines 442-446; widths are not.)
- **Evidence given.** Observer file `id,x,y,z` / `1, 100, 200, 0` / `2, 300, 4oo, 0` / `3, 500, 600, 0` / `4, 700, 800` / `5, 900, 1000, 0`, run with `-b obs_bad.csv -L info`: exit 0, log says only 'Evaluating 4 observers'; output rows are `100,200,...` `500,600,...` `4,700,...` `899.99999999999989,1000,...`. Row 2 vanished; row 4 became an observer at x=4, y=700, z=800 ft (800 ft above the ground). Note also 900 comes back as 899.99999999999989: scattered output prints x/0.3048 of x*0.3048, so the coordinates written do not equal those read.
- **Scenario.** A 33-microphone array file with one malformed line runs to completion with 32 observers, or with a phantom airborne observer, and the footprint rows are compared with the wrong microphones.
- **Proposed change.** Fix the width from the first data row and require the rest to match (error naming the line); have read_numeric_csv return or log the number and line numbers of rows it dropped and make read_observer_file input_error if any were (the header is already consumed separately). Echo the observers' original coordinates rather than converted-and-back values.
- **Expected impact.** Malformed observer files fail loudly instead of changing the observer set; written coordinates round-trip.


## Option and atmosphere values not range-checked; late validation

### main-cli/A.r1.2: Atmosphere and ground option values are not range-checked; a unit slip gives NaN or a 4-10 dB shifted footprint with exit 0

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:2084`, `src/niceops.cpp:942`, `src/niceops.cpp:2111`, `src/atmosphere.cpp:34`, `src/util/sel.hpp:89`, `src/atmosphere_profile.cpp:359`, `src/trajectory.cpp:399`
- **Claim.** --temperature, --pressure and --humidity (and the vehicle JSON's Atmosphere values) overwrite the Atmosphere with no plausibility test, whereas the --atmosphere reader rejects the same slips (T outside 150-350 K, RH outside 0-100 or never above 1, pressure outside (0, 120] kPa). The only unit-sensitive quantities in the codebase (K, kPa, percent) are exactly the ones a user gets wrong. --receiver_height is also unchecked although the observer-file column of the same quantity is (niceops.cpp:430, '!(data[4] >= 0.0)'), and --turbulence/--flow_resistance ranges stated in the help are not enforced.
- **Evidence given.** B407 spectral database, coarse grid, baseline vs override (441 observers; mean / max abs change in dB):
--pressure 101325 (Pa typed for kPa): +4.02 / 6.19, exit 0
--temperature 15 (Celsius typed for K): +6.30 / 9.61, exit 0
--humidity 0.5 (fraction typed for percent): +4.77 / 6.77, exit 0
--humidity 500: +3.48 / 4.96, exit 0
--temperature -5: all 441 outputs are non-finite, exit 0
--pressure 0: all 441 non-finite, exit 0
--humidity -5: finite, -2.39 mean, exit 0
With ground effect: --receiver_height -1 (microphone below the ground) exit 0, +2.11 dB mean; --turbulence 1 (help says 0 to 16e-4) exit 0, +2.15 mean.
On the broadband demo, --temperature -5 makes Atmosphere::sound_speed() NaN and reception_factor's '!(c > 0.0)' (sel.hpp:89) silently turns reception-time weighting off (+0.11 dB mean, exit 0).
- **Scenario.** A user takes a weather report in Celsius or hPa and types it into --temperature/--pressure, or copies the vehicle JSON of another site. The run succeeds and writes a footprint that is several dB off (or all NaN) with nothing logged.
- **Proposed change.** One validate function (atmosphere.cpp) applying the same bounds as atmosphere_profile.cpp (150-350 K, 0 < p <= 120 kPa, 0 < RH <= 100 with the fraction guard when RH <= 1 and the value came from the command line) to the merged Atmosphere in process_trajectory after the overrides (line 948) and to the JSON values; call it from run() for the override options so it fails before the database loads. Add input_error range checks for --receiver_height >= 0, --turbulence in [0, 16e-4], --flow_resistance > 0, --roughness >= 0, --layer_depth > 0, --gamma_t >= 0.
- **Expected impact.** Turns unit slips into immediate errors; valid inputs are untouched.

### spline-util/B.r1.1: --temperature/--pressure/--humidity and the vehicle JSON skip the plausibility checks --atmosphere applies; T<=0 or p<=0 gives NaN footprints with exit 0

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:942`, `src/trajectory.cpp:399`, `src/atmosphere.hpp:28`, `src/atmosphere_profile.cpp:355`
- **Claim.** The three CLI overrides and the vehicle JSON's Atmosphere section are copied straight into Atmosphere and into ISO 9613-1 with no domain check. An --atmosphere CSV gets one (temperature within 150..350 K after unit inference, RH within 0..100 and not a fraction, pressure range), so the same quantities are validated on one entry path and not the other two. The spectral path is now the default for any database with spectra, so this is the ordinary route.
- **Evidence given.** B407_fixed_load_factor.nod (31 bands), --gridstep 1000, exit code and output scan per option:
  --temperature 0   -> exit 0, 'absorption spans nan dB/m at 10 Hz to nan dB/m at 10000 Hz', 10 of 14 output rows contain 'nan'
  --temperature -5  -> same (NaN, exit 0)
  --pressure 0      -> same (NaN, exit 0)
  --humidity -20    -> exit 0, absorption at 10 Hz = -1.34e-05 dB/m (negative, i.e. a gain), no message
  --humidity 250    -> exit 0, no message
  --temperature 15  (Celsius typed for kelvin) -> exit 0, 'at 15 K', absorption 4.3e-09..4.3e-03 dB/m, max level 87.17 vs 86.32 at 288.15 K, no message. atmosphere_profile.cpp:24-25 (kelvin_low 150, kelvin_high 350) would have refused it.
Code: niceops.cpp:942-948 `air.temperature = options.temperature_override; air.pressure = ...; air.relative_humidity = ...;` with nothing after.
- **Scenario.** A user passes --temperature 15 meaning degrees C: the run completes, the footprint is wrong by about 1 dB (more at range), and nothing says so except a log line. Or --pressure 0 / a typo'd JSON gives an all-NaN CSV with exit 0, which a batch script counts as success.
- **Proposed change.** Add one Atmosphere::validate(source) (or a free function next to atmosphere.hpp) that rejects non-finite values, T outside roughly 150..350 K, p <= 0, RH outside 0..100, with a message naming the option or file; call it once in run() after the overrides at niceops.cpp:948 (covers the JSON values too). Reuse the constants already in atmosphere_profile.cpp so the two entry paths agree. About 20 lines.
- **Expected impact.** Turns silent NaN or wrong-unit runs into a refused run with a message. No effect on valid runs (the shipped JSONs use 280.37 and 300.71 K).
- **Conflicts with docs.** None. README documents --temperature in K and --humidity in percent but nothing checks them.

### spline-util/A.r1.5: Atmosphere values are never range- or unit-checked, and a Celsius or Pa/fraction slip silently changes both absorption and the reception-time weighting

- Finder's severity low, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.6, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/atmosphere.hpp:28`, `src/niceops.cpp:942`, `src/trajectory.cpp:400`
- **Claim.** --temperature, --pressure, --humidity and the vehicle JSON's Atmosphere.temperature/pressure/relative humidity go straight into Atmosphere. The units are K, kPa and percent (the header calls the pressure unit 'easy to get wrong'), but nothing rejects 20 (Celsius), 101325 (Pa), 0.5 (fraction), or T <= 0. air.sound_speed() also feeds the broadband path's reception_factor (niceops.cpp:955), so the default run is affected, not only --spectral.
- **Evidence given.** Broadband demo (S-76D_M3.nod, no spectra): `niceops -S state/t.csv -o out.csv --temperature 20` vs 300.71 K: exit 0, no message, footprint shifts by median -0.23 dB, min -1.45 dB. `--temperature -5` and `--temperature 0` give identical outputs to each other (sound speed NaN and 0 respectively), differing from the 300.71 K run by up to +0.41 dB, exit 0. Absorption on the spectral path, direct call (dB/km at 293.15 K, 101.325 kPa, 50% RH -> mis-specified): 4 kHz: 29.67 -> 0.668 (T=20 as K), 5.91 (RH given as 0.5), 46.0 (pressure 101325), 65.5 (pressure 1013.25 hPa); 1 kHz: 4.66 -> 0.042 (T=20). p=0 or T<=0 return NaN coefficients.
- **Scenario.** A user who passes --temperature 20 or --humidity 0.5 (a natural slip) gets a footprint that is quietly off by 0.2-1.5 dB on the broadband path and by tens of percent to 100x in per-band absorption on the spectral path, with nothing in the log except the echoed value.
- **Proposed change.** Add Atmosphere::validate() (or check at the seam at niceops.cpp:942-949 and after the JSON read at trajectory.cpp:400-418) with ranges wide enough not to bite legitimate use, e.g. 200 K < T < 340 K, 30 < p < 130 kPa, 0 <= RH <= 100, and name the unit in the error ('--temperature is in kelvin; 20 looks like Celsius'). The ISO 9613-1 domain (roughly 253-323 K) can be used as a warning threshold.
- **Expected impact.** Catches unit slips at the seam; no effect on valid input. The ISO 9613-1 transcription itself is correct (constants, exponents and the humidity conversion checked term by term; agrees with ISO 9613-2 Table 2: 20 C/70% 1 kHz 4.98 vs 5.0 dB/km, 10 C/70% 8 kHz 118 vs 117).

### main-cli/B.r1.4: Ground and refraction options are silently inert or accepted out of range

- Finder's severity low, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.8, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:2111`, `src/niceops.cpp:2144`, `src/niceops.cpp:2400`
- **Claim.** run() rejects some conflicting combinations (2400-2447) but not the ground options that do nothing under the selected models, and it range-checks none of the numeric ground options. `--gamma_t` is read only by the harmonoise model, `--turbulence` only by chessell, `--porosity_rate` and `--layer_depth` only by their impedance models, and `--plate_mic_azimuth` only with a plate. `--flow_resistance`, `--receiver_height`, `--turbulence`, `--roughness` and `--exact_ground` are unused without --ground_effect. None of these produces a warning. The values are also unchecked: `--turbulence` help says 0 to 16e-4 and 1 is accepted; `--receiver_height -3` (a microphone under the ground) is accepted, although read_observer_file rejects a negative per-observer height at niceops.cpp:430; `--flow_resistance 0` is accepted, and `--flow_resistance -5` is caught only deep in the ground model and reported as a per-trajectory 'Skipping'.
- **Evidence given.** B407 database, coarse grid, cmp on the outputs. Without --ground_effect, the flags `--gamma_t 3e-5 --flow_resistance 3000 --receiver_height 10 --turbulence 8e-4 --porosity_rate 5 --layer_depth 1 --exact_ground --plate_mic_azimuth 45` give `cmp` IDENTICAL to the bare run, exit 0, and 0 'warn' lines in the log. With --ground_effect, adding `--gamma_t 3e-5 --porosity_rate 5 --layer_depth 1 --plate_mic_azimuth 45` is also byte-identical. `--receiver_height -3` changed the output (crude mean over all fields 43.22 to 45.20) and `--flow_resistance 0` changed it as well (43.76), both exit 0. `--flow_resistance -5` exited 1.
- **Scenario.** The README names ~3e-5 as the fitted --gamma_t. A user who passes `--gamma_t 3e-5` without `--turbulence_model harmonoise` gets exactly the run with no turbulence, and nothing says so. Ranges silently accepted produce plausible-looking numbers from a nonsensical setup.
- **Proposed change.** In the validation block, warn (or input_error) when an option was supplied (use the CLI11 count() pattern already used for the units seam) but its selector or --ground_effect is absent. Range-check receiver_height >= 0, flow_resistance > 0, turbulence >= 0, roughness >= 0 and layer_depth > 0 next to the existing --shadow_floor/--scattering checks. Prefer warn for the inert case so existing scripts that always pass them keep running.
- **Expected impact.** Catches misconfigured ground runs at the door. About +25 LOC.

### ground/A.r1.4: Ground-model preconditions h_s >= 0 and h_r >= 0 are not enforced; the exact model, the table and the gain bound disagree outside them

- Finder's severity low, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.7, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:2114`, `src/niceops.cpp:429`, `src/ground_effect.cpp:200`, `src/ground_effect.cpp:331`, `src/ground_effect.cpp:415`
- **Claim.** The observer-file path rejects a negative microphone height (niceops.cpp:429), but `--receiver_height` is taken as typed. The model and its bound assume 0 <= hs and 0 <= hr (rho = image/direct >= 1, path_delay > 0 for the sinc, cos_grazing >= 0), and GroundTable::power_ratio clamps hs to 0 (line 415) while the exact model does not, so `--exact_ground` and the default disagree whenever a state is below the z = 0 plane (a geodetic track descending below --ground_elevation).
- **Evidence given.** `niceops ... --ground_effect --receiver_height -3.937 --exact_ground --gridstep 492 -o integrated/neg.csv` -> exit 0, output file written, no diagnostic. Standalone model for grass, hr = 1.2, d = 200 m, seven bands 50-3150 Hz: at hs = -5 m the 200 Hz band is +9.54 dB, above ground_gain_bounds' 9.51 dB (marked as a violation), and at hs = -5 the 3150 Hz band is +6.32 dB exact while the table would return its hs = 0 value, -29.46 dB (a 36 dB disagreement in that band). Peak-search exactness relies on that bound (reflection_coefficient_bounds comment, lines 331-334, states rho >= 1).
- **Scenario.** A typo or sign error in --receiver_height, or a landing track whose altitude falls below --ground_elevation, gives plausible-looking output that differs between --exact_ground and the default and can defeat the windowed run's exactness.
- **Proposed change.** Reject a negative --receiver_height beside the existing observer-file check; clamp source_height to >= 0 inside excess_ground_attenuation (as the table already does) and log once when a state lies below the observer plane.
- **Expected impact.** Removes a silent-garbage path and makes exact and tabulated modes agree off-domain.

### ground/A.r1.2: Every non-passive band is refused, including bands outside --band_limits that the run never reads

- Finder's severity low, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/ground_effect.cpp:114`, `src/niceops.cpp:1091`, `src/plate_receiver.cpp:170`
- **Claim.** ground_impedance() throws on the first band with Re(1/Z) <= 0, and its callers pass every database band (niceops.cpp:1091 passes all `bands`; PlateReceiver::bound passes every sub-frequency, edge and center). The default --band_limits is 20-20000 Hz (weighting.hpp:30-31), so the 10, 12.5 and 16 Hz bands have zero gain and are never read (excess_ground_attenuation, GroundTable, the bounds and ShadowAttenuation are all restricted to [first_band, last_band)), yet a ground that is non-passive only there rejects the whole run.
- **Evidence given.** Scan of delany_bazley_layer grounds (Amedee-like sigma = 200): non-passive only below 20 Hz for sigma=200 with depth 0.035, 0.040, 0.045 m (also sigma=100/0.055, sigma=250/0.035, sigma=500/0.020-0.025). CLI: `niceops -d databases/B407_fixed_load_factor.nod -v databases/B407.json -t tracks/S-76D/197421AC.csv --ground_effect --impedance_model delany_bazley_layer --flow_resistance 200 --layer_depth 0.13123 --gridstep 492 -L warn -o integrated/layer.csv` (0.13123 ft = 0.040 m) -> exit=1, stdout and stderr empty, log: `Skipping tracks/S-76D/197421AC.csv: The ground impedance model is not passive at 10 Hz (Re(1/Z) = -0.000348099). A thin, low-resistivity Delany-Bazley layer does this at low frequency...`. --layer_depth 0.11483 (0.035 m) fails identically (Re(1/Z) = -0.000593155); 0.14764 ft (0.045 m) runs.
- **Scenario.** Fitting or sweeping layer thickness for a real ground: any thickness in the band where only the sub-20 Hz bands are non-passive is refused with a message about a band that the A-weighted, 20 Hz-limited metric cannot see, and the reason is visible only in log/niceops.log.
- **Proposed change.** Give ground_impedance() optional first_band/last_band (default whole vector) and test passivity only inside that range, computing the rest without checking; pass the run's range from niceops.cpp:1091 and the plate's from PlateReceiver::bound (bands and their edges/sub-frequencies within the run's range). Keep the whole-vector refusal as the default so existing callers and test_ground_effect.cpp:360-368 behave the same.
- **Expected impact.** Runs with limits that exclude the problematic low bands proceed; runs that use them are still refused.

### main-cli/B.r1.3: Cheap-to-check options are validated after the run, so bad invocations fail late, and figures skip the parent-directory rule

- Finder's severity low, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:2319`, `src/niceops.cpp:2400`, `src/niceops.cpp:2581`, `src/niceops.cpp:1828`, `src/niceops.cpp:55`, `src/niceops.cpp:1865`
- **Claim.** (1) The flag-combination checks at 2400-2447 need nothing from the database, but they run after `Database(...)` at 2319, which is 0.75-1.0 s of the run. (2) The 'no plotting support' refusal at 2582 runs after every trajectory has been computed and written. The comment there says 'Say so up front rather than running the whole case', and plotting_disabled.cpp:7-9 says 'main() refuses the plotting flags up front'. (3) Figure files never get ensure_parent_directory, unlike every other writer (CLAUDE.md: 'every writer must create its own parent directory'), so a missing directory fails only after the whole run. (4) iota_exclusive does `static_cast<std::size_t>(std::ceil((end - begin) / step))` with no check, so --cstep 0 or a negative step is a float-to-size_t conversion of inf or a negative, which is UB; --cmax <= --cmin ends in a matplotlib ValueError.
- **Evidence given.** Timings from the log: `--refraction linear` without --atmosphere logs `Database loaded in 0.986 seconds.` and only then the rejection. With build-gcc/niceops (plotting OFF), `niceops --gridstep 492 -f figures/b.png` writes integrated/197421_LAE.csv and only then logs `[error] This build has no plotting support`, exit 2, nothing on stderr. `niceops -f newfigs/x.png --gridstep 492` (plotting build) exits 2 after the run with `Stopping: FileNotFoundError: [Errno 2] No such file or directory: 'newfigs/x.png'`. `--cstep 0` and `--cstep -5` both end in `Stopping: vector`. `--cmin 100 --cmax 65` ends in `ValueError: Filled contours require at least 2 levels.` CI hides (3) with `mkdir -p ci-figures` (ci.yml:96).
- **Scenario.** A scripted run with a typo in a plotting option or a flag combination does the full computation, which is seconds to minutes with --ground_effect or --refraction, or 0.75 s of database load minimum, and only then exits 2 with nothing on the terminal (see the finding on stderr). On a fresh checkout `-f figures/x.png` fails after the run because figures/ is untracked.
- **Proposed change.** Move the validation block (2400-2447) above the Database load. Add up-front checks for `want_plots && !plt::available()`, `contour_step > 0` and `contour_max > contour_min`. Call ensure_parent_directory on figure_file, and on the state-figure base path, before or inside draw_figures. Then drop the CI `mkdir -p`. Only the `has_spectra()` checks inside process_trajectory need the database.
- **Expected impact.** Failures cost about 0 s instead of 1 s or a full run. It removes an UB conversion and makes the code match its own comments.

### main-cli/A.r1.7: Figure options fail or do nothing only after the whole run: no parent directory for savefig, late plotting-off refusal, tricontourf failure on line arrays, -p is a no-op

- Finder's severity low, category robustness, effort M, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/plotting.cpp:113`, `src/niceops.cpp:1863`, `src/niceops.cpp:1889`, `src/niceops.cpp:1830`, `src/niceops.cpp:1906`, `src/niceops.cpp:2581`, `src/plotting_disabled.cpp:7`
- **Claim.** (a) plt::savefig hands the path straight to matplotlib and no caller uses ensure_parent_directory, contrary to CLAUDE.md's 'every writer must create its own parent directory'; ci.yml:96 masks it with 'mkdir -p ci-figures'. (b) With NICEOPS_PLOTTING=OFF the refusal at niceops.cpp:2581-2588 comes after the database load, the whole acoustic loop and all outputs, although its comment says 'up front' and plotting_disabled.cpp:7-9 says main() refuses the flags 'up front'. (c) draw_figures has no error isolation: tricontourf raises for fewer than three or collinear scattered observers (a microphone line is a common layout), which also prevents the -F state figures. (d) -p alone builds the figures on the Agg backend and plt::show() cannot display them, so the flag has no visible effect.
- **Evidence given.** (a) $ niceops --gridstep 246 -f newfigs/sub/x.png -> exit 2, stderr empty, log 'Stopping: FileNotFoundError: [Errno 2] No such file or directory: newfigs/sub/x.png' (plus a Python traceback), after integrated/197421_LAE.csv was written.
(b) scratch build with -DNICEOPS_PLOTTING=OFF: $ niceops --gridstep 246 -f figures/x.png -o integrated/np.csv -> exit 2; log line 1346 'Acoustic calculations completed' precedes line 1349 'This build has no plotting support'; np.csv exists.
(c) 5 collinear observers: $ niceops -b line.csv -f figures/l.png -F figures/ls.png -> exit 2, 'Stopping: RuntimeError: Error in qhull Delaunay triangulation calculation: singular input data'; l.csv written, no figures at all.
(d) $ niceops --gridstep 246 -p -> exit 0, no window or file, stderr '<sys>:0: UserWarning: FigureCanvasAgg is non-interactive, and thus cannot be shown'. README.md:201 describes -p as 'Plot the footprint'.
- **Scenario.** A user asks for figures on a long spectral or refraction run (minutes) with a fresh output directory, a line array, or a build without plotting; the numbers are computed and saved but the invocation reports failure late, with the reason only in the log, and the state figures are lost with it. A user of -p alone gets nothing.
- **Proposed change.** Move the plt::available() test next to the other flag checks (before the database load); call ensure_parent_directory on figure_file and state_figure_file before drawing; wrap each figure block in try/catch(std::exception) so a footprint failure logs a warning, still draws the state figures and does not turn a finished run into 'Stopping' (fall back to plt::plot markers for < 3 or collinear observers); make -p without -f/-F an input_error or warning that Agg cannot show windows, or drop plt::show(). Make the CI figure step run without mkdir -p (or add a unit test for savefig into a new directory).
- **Expected impact.** Failures surface immediately or degrade gracefully; the documented ensure_parent_directory rule holds for figures.
- **Conflicts with docs.** CLAUDE.md ('every writer must create its own parent directory'), niceops.cpp:2584 comment, plotting_disabled.cpp:7-9, README.md:1078 and 201.

### main-setup/A.r1.6: A single -t track with no -o silently writes integrated/197421_LAE.csv, contradicting the help text

- Finder's severity low, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.97, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:1928`, `src/niceops.cpp:2023`, `src/niceops.cpp:2214`
- **Claim.** -o is documented as 'derived from the track name if omitted'. The default out_files = {"integrated/197421_LAE.csv"} is only replaced by track-derived names when path_count > 1 (the `out_files.size() == 1 && path_count > 1` test). With exactly one -t and no -o, the demo case's output name is used.
- **Evidence given.** `niceops -t tracks/S-76D/197401AC.csv --gridstep 984 -L warn` leaves integrated/197421_LAE.csv (the file regression_golden reads) and no 197401AC_LAE.csv. With two -t options the outputs are 197401AC_LAE.csv and 197402AC_LAE.csv.
- **Scenario.** Running any other single track from the repo root overwrites the demo output, or the user's own previous result, without notice.
- **Proposed change.** Track whether -o was given (count() == 0) and derive `integrated/<stem>_LAE.csv` for any -t. Keep the demo name only when -t is also defaulted. Update the README if it mentions the old behavior.
- **Expected impact.** Prevents accidental overwrites. The default demo run is unchanged.

### main-setup/B.r1.5: RunOptions defaults for ambient_level, db_cutoff and trim_start are 0.0, but NaN is their 'off' sentinel

- Finder's severity low, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: V1**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:542`, `src/niceops.cpp:591`, `src/niceops.cpp:2449`
- **Claim.** `double ambient_level = 0.0;` `double db_cutoff = 0.0;` `double trim_start = 0.0;` sit next to the comment 'NaN means the trim is off'. The consumers test `!std::isnan(...)` (add_ambient at 797, culling at 1266, the trim guard at 697). A default-constructed RunOptions therefore turns all three features on: a 0 dB ambient floor, culling at 0 dB, and a trim from x = 0. The 0 dB floor is exactly the D-default-NaN pitfall CLAUDE.md warns about. run() sets all three explicitly, so there is no live bug. But the comment at 2449-2452 says the initializer exists so that a forgotten field 'reads as its declared default', and for these three the declared default is 'on'.
- **Evidence given.** Source lines quoted above. Consumers: `const bool add_ambient = !std::isnan(options.ambient_level);` `const bool culling = !std::isnan(options.db_cutoff);` `if (!std::isnan(options.trim_start))`. RunOptions is file-local, so no other construction site exists today.
- **Scenario.** Someone adds a second entry point (a test harness, a batch driver) that builds RunOptions and forgets one of these fields. Every level gets a 0 dB ambient mix-in, or every run culls at -10 dB.
- **Proposed change.** Initialize the three fields to std::numeric_limits<double>::quiet_NaN(), matching humidity_override and the other overrides already in the struct.
- **Expected impact.** No behavior change today. Removes a trap that CLAUDE.md documents as already hit once.
- **Verifier (trace): confirmed, confidence 0.72.** Every factual claim checks out against the current files, and the change is zero-risk and bit-identical. The finding says itself that it is not a live bug, and I agree. Nothing constructs a default RunOptions today. It is a real but marginal, latent inconsistency. Re-read of the code:

- src/niceops.cpp:542 `double ambient_level = 0.0;`, :544 `double db_cutoff = 0.0;`, and :591-592 `// Meters, after the --units conversion; NaN means the trim is off.` followed by `double trim_start = 0.0;`. The quoted lines and the comment placement are accurate.
- The sentinels are NaN at every consumer. :797 `const bool add_ambient = !std::isnan(options.ambient_level);`, :1266 `const bool culling = !std::isnan(options.db_cutoff);`, :697 `if (!std::isnan(options.trim_start))`. A value-initialized RunOptions therefore has all three features switched on: a 0 dB ambient mix-in, culling at 0 dB, and a trim from x = 0.
- The CLI locals that feed them are correctly NaN: :1932 `trim_start = quiet_NaN()`, :1964 `ambient_level = quiet_NaN()` (with a comment recording the earlier 0 dB-floor bug), :1972 `db_cutoff = quiet_NaN()`. The pitfall was fixed at the CLI layer only. The struct's own defaults were left at 0.0. Sibling fields in the same struct (humidity/temperature/pressure_override at :566-568, frame_bearing) do use quiet_NaN.
- The comment at :2449-2453 says the brace initializer exists so that a forgotten field "reads as its declared default". For these three fields the declared default is the enabled state, so the safeguard works against its own purpose.

Guards and invariants that make it non-live today:
- RunOptions is in an anonymous namespace (:536-538) of src/niceops.cpp. The only construction site is `RunOptions options {};` at :2454, in run(). CMakeLists.txt:202 shows `add_executable(niceops src/niceops.cpp)`, so the file is not linked into any test. grep of tests/, tools/ and CMakeLists.txt finds no RunOptions or process_trajectory reference.
- run() assigns all three unconditionally: :2457 `options.ambient_level = ambient_level;`, :2459 `options.db_cutoff = db_cutoff;`, :2512 `options.trim_start = trim_start;`. They are at function scope, not inside an `if`. Nothing reads `options` between :2454 and those assignments.
- Changing the three initializers to quiet_NaN() therefore cannot alter any run. `<limits>` and `std::numeric_limits` are already used in the struct.

Why this is marginal:
- Triggering the scenario needs a second construction site inside niceops.cpp itself, or deleting one of three unconditional assignments. Both are speculative, and no test or tool can hit it today.
- It is worth doing only as a cheap hygiene fix, since it removes a comment-versus-code contradiction next to a documented past bug.
- I did not run an experiment. The behavior is a direct consequence of the initializers and the isnan tests, and there is no second entry point to exercise.
  - Refined claim: RunOptions (src/niceops.cpp:538) initializes ambient_level (:542), db_cutoff (:544) and trim_start (:592) to 0.0, while their consumers (:797, :1266, :697) use NaN as the "off" sentinel. A default-constructed RunOptions would enable a 0 dB ambient floor, 0 dB culling and a trim from x = 0. The comment at :2449-2453 claims the brace initializer makes forgotten fields read as their declared default, which for these three means "on". The sole construction site is run() at :2454. It sets all three unconditionally at :2457, :2459 and :2512, from CLI locals that are correctly NaN. There is no live bug. It is a latent trap, and it is reachable only through a new construction site in the same translation unit or a deleted assignment.
  - Verifier's fix: In src/niceops.cpp, initialize the three fields to `std::numeric_limits<double>::quiet_NaN()`: ambient_level at :542, db_cutoff at :544 and trim_start at :592. This matches humidity_override and the other overrides already in the struct. No other change is needed. The ctest suite and the golden comparison are unaffected.


## --trim past the end

### main-setup/A.r1.5: --trim with a start at or past the lowest/slowest point builds a reversed iterator range or a one-state trajectory

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.95, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: R C×4**
- Locations: `src/trajectory.cpp:778`, `src/niceops.cpp:697`
- **Claim.** watts_trim takes ixmax = the earlier of argmin(z) and argmin(mu) over the whole track, and ixmin = the last index with x <= start. It never checks ixmin <= ixmax. `std::vector<State> sub(states.begin() + ixmin, states.begin() + ixmax + 1)` with ixmin > ixmax is a reversed range (undefined behavior; in practice a length_error). With ixmin == ixmax the track silently becomes a single state and the SEL is one sample weighted by the 0.5 s default dt (nt < 2 skips the sample-interval logic). It also assumes x increases along the track, and it assumes the lowest/slowest point is after the start. A departure that starts in hover has ixmax = 0.
- **Evidence given.** Demo track, imin_z=5489, imin_mu=5131, x from -2499 m to 741 m. `niceops --trim 2500 --gridstep 984`: log 'Trimming trajectory from x = 741.364 to 100.861', then 'Skipping ...: vector', rc=1. `niceops --trim 331.0 --gridstep 984`: log 'Trimming trajectory from x = 100.861 to 100.861', rc=0, no 'Integrating with the trajectory's sample interval' line, and the footprint mean drops from 69.5 to 45.2 dB. There is no warning. No CTest case or unit test exercises --trim.
- **Scenario.** A user trims a departure or a partial track to an x beyond the minimum point. The result is a cryptic failure or a plausible-looking footprint from one sample.
- **Proposed change.** In watts_trim, if ixmin > ixmax raise input_error with both x values and indices. Warn or error if fewer than 2 states remain. Add a Catch2 test for both cases and one for the normal trim.
- **Expected impact.** Removes an undefined-behavior path and a silent one-sample footprint.

### main-setup/B.r1.2: --trim past the lowest/slowest point fails with the message 'vector' instead of a diagnosis

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: R V2 C×4**
- Locations: `src/trajectory.cpp:778`, `src/niceops.cpp:697`
- **Claim.** watts_trim takes ixmax = min(index of lowest z, index of lowest mu) and ixmin = last index with x <= xstart. When xstart lies past the x of the end point, ixmin > ixmax and `std::vector<State> sub(states.begin() + ixmin, states.begin() + ixmax + 1)` is built from a reversed range. libc++ throws std::length_error, whose what() is just 'vector'. The run logs 'Trimming trajectory from x = 103.352 to 100.861' (start beyond end) and then 'Skipping <track>: vector', exit 1. The result is a hard-to-diagnose failure for a plausible input. watts_trim has no test at all (grep finds none), and there is no --trim CTest either.
- **Evidence given.** Demo track, scratch root: `niceops --trim 331 ...` exit 0 ('Trimming trajectory from x = 100.861 to 100.861'). `niceops --trim 340 --gridstep 492 -o x.csv` exit 1; log: 'Trimming trajectory from x = 103.352 to 100.861' then '[error] Skipping tracks/S-76D/197421AC.csv: vector'. `--trim 400`: same. Code: `std::vector<State> sub(states.begin() + ixmin, states.begin() + ixmax + 1);`. Under libstdc++ (CI) the same reversed range gives a length_error or an oversized allocation attempt, but that was not tested.
- **Scenario.** A user trims an approach from a point past its lowest point, for example a 340 ft start on a track whose lowest point is at 331 ft, and gets 'Skipping ...: vector'. Nothing in the message mentions --trim.
- **Proposed change.** In watts_trim, if ixmin > ixmax, call input_error naming --trim, the start x, and the x of the end point in the user's unit (or clamp to a single state, if that is the intended reading). Add a small Catch2 case for watts_trim covering normal, boundary (start equals end x) and past-end starts.
- **Expected impact.** A legible error instead of an opaque one. Also puts the only trim logic under test.
- **Verifier (trace): confirmed, confidence 0.92.** I re-read the code and reproduced the failure with the shipped Release build from a scratch runenv directory (/Users/evg5332/.claude/jobs/8792c8f6/tmp/scratch/verif_trim_r12/run). I touched nothing under the repo, and `git status` still shows only the two pre-existing untracked entries.

Code, current file:
- src/trajectory.cpp:778-796: `ixmax = min(imin_z, imin_v)`, and `ixmin` is the last index with `x[i] <= xstart_meters`.
- Nothing checks `ixmin <= ixmax`. The next statement is `std::vector<State> sub(states.begin() + ixmin, states.begin() + ixmax + 1);`.
- src/niceops.cpp:697-700 calls `watts_trim` with no validation, and the `--trim` help text and README line 163 promise no error behavior.
- The reversed range is technically UB. In practice it throws.

Measured with the demo track and `--gridstep 492`:

| `--trim` (ft) | exit | log |
|---|---|---|
| 331 | 0 | `Trimming trajectory from x = 100.861 to 100.861` (one-state trajectory) |
| 335 | 1 | `Trimming ... from x = 101.985 to 100.861`, then `[error] Skipping tracks/S-76D/197421AC.csv: vector` |
| 340 | 1 | `Trimming ... from x = 103.352 to 100.861`, then `Skipping ...: vector`, then `1 of 1 trajectories could not be processed.` |
| 400 | 1 | `Trimming ... from x = 121.723 to 100.861`, same error |
| 332 | 134 | see below |
| -6000 | 0 | works, starts at x = -1828.94 |

- **Terminal output:** for `--trim 340`, stdout plus stderr is 0 bytes. The user sees only exit code 1 and must open log/niceops.log to find `vector`. That is worse than the finding says.
- **libstdc++ (CI's library):** I compiled a standalone reversed-range vector constructor with g++-16. It throws `cannot create std::vector larger than max_size()`. It is still caught by the `std::exception` handler and still gives no diagnosis. libc++ gives `vector`, as reported.
- **No tests:** `grep -rn trim tests/ CMakeLists.txt` finds nothing. `--trim` is unused by any CTest case and `watts_trim` has no unit test. The only hits are the option definition, the call site, README and header comments.

Scenario plausibility: it needs `xstart` past the x of the lowest/slowest point. That is a user error, but a plausible one:
- `--trim` is a single run-wide value applied to every trajectory in a multi-track run.
- Tracks end at different x.
- A start chosen against one track can be past another track's end.

It fails loudly with a non-zero exit, not with wrong numbers, which is why I rate it low-to-medium rather than medium-high.
  - Refined claim: In `Trajectory::watts_trim` (src/trajectory.cpp:778-796) nothing checks `ixmin <= ixmax`, and the vector is built from `[begin+ixmin, begin+ixmax+1)`. The failure has two modes.

- **`ixmin > ixmax + 1`** (for example `--trim 335/340/400` ft on the demo track): the vector constructor throws `length_error`. The run logs `Skipping <track>: vector` (libc++) or `cannot create std::vector larger than max_size()` (libstdc++, tested with g++-16) and exits 1. The terminal shows nothing.
- **`ixmin == ixmax + 1`** (for example `--trim 332` ft on the demo track, a window of about 0.28 m): the range is empty and no exception is thrown. `watts_trim` leaves an empty trajectory. The observer loop then hits `fatal("peak_range: empty level history; the trajectory produced no states.")` in worker threads. The process aborts with exit 134 and no terminal output. The reason is only in log/niceops.log. The finding did not mention this mode, and it is more severe because `input_error`'s per-track skip never runs.

Both modes come from the same missing guard.
  - Verifier's fix: In `watts_trim`, after computing `ixmin` and `ixmax`, add this guard:

```
if (ixmin > ixmax)
    input_error("--trim start x = ", x_start_in_user_units, " is past the trajectory's lowest/slowest point at x = ", x_end_in_user_units, "; nothing would be left to integrate.");
```

- Also handle `ixmin == ixmax` (a one-state trajectory) deliberately: warn, or refuse it.
- The trajectory stores meters, so `watts_trim` needs the `LengthUnit` for the message, or the caller in `run()` must format it.
- A single-state trajectory is currently accepted, so decide whether that is intended.
- Add a Catch2 case covering:
  - a normal trim;
  - a start equal to the end x (`ixmin == ixmax`);
  - a start one sample past the end (the abort case);
  - a start well past the end.
- If a clamp is preferred to an error, say so in the docs.
- Output effect: `changes-behavior`. It changes only inputs that already fail, and the demo and goldens are byte-identical.
- **Verifier (context): confirmed, confidence 0.9.** I reproduced the reversed-range failure on the shipped Release build and found a second, worse failure the reporter missed. `Trajectory::watts_trim` (src/trajectory.cpp:778-796) takes ixmax as the lowest-z or lowest-mu index and ixmin as the last index with x <= start. It never checks ixmin <= ixmax before `std::vector<State> sub(states.begin() + ixmin, states.begin() + ixmax + 1)` (line 794).

The demo track's end point is state 5131 at x = 100.861 m (330.9 ft), and states are about 0.28 m apart. Results, all with `--gridstep 492`:
- `--trim 331` and `--trim 331.5` give exit 0 with a single state.
- `--trim 333`, `335` and `340` give exit 1. The log reads "Trimming trajectory from x = 101.423 to 100.861" and then "[error] Skipping tracks/S-76D/197421AC.csv: vector".
- `--trim 332` and `332.5` give exit 134 (abort) with empty stdout and stderr. The window x[ixmax+1] <= start < x[ixmax+2] leaves ixmin == ixmax+1, so the vector is empty, not reversed. The only trace is `[fatal] peak_range: empty level history; the trajectory produced no states.` in log/niceops.log, logged once per worker thread. The window is one state wide, about 0.9 ft.

The problem also arises on real data, not only on a mistyped 340. The end point varies across the 84 shipped AC tracks, from -448 ft to 2226 ft. In `niceops -t tracks/S-76D/197419AC.csv -t tracks/S-76D/197421AC.csv --trim -200`, track 197419 (end at -257 ft) is skipped with "Trimming trajectory from x = -60.9929 to -78.34 / Skipping ...: vector" and the run ends "1 of 2 trajectories could not be processed" (exit 1). Track 197421 in the same run succeeds. `--trim` takes a single value applied to every track, so this can happen in any batch run.

The reporter's libc++ claim holds. I also checked libstdc++ with g++-16 on a reversed range: it throws length_error "cannot create std::vector larger than max_size()", so CI would show that message instead. Either way it is an exception thrown from undefined behavior (a reversed iterator range), not a diagnosis.

Nothing in the repo says this is deliberate.
- The D original (`c74bab3:trajectory.d` lines 239-258) does the same unchecked slice `states[ixmin .. ixmax + 1]`, so a reversed range failed there too. I did not run ldc2 to see how.
- The trim was added in 41ad8ed and the ported function is unchanged since 666046b, apart from the unit change in 07d30539.
- CLAUDE.md and README.md say nothing about a start past the end.
- `--trim` appears in no test, no CTest case and nothing under .github. `watts_trim` is not called from any test.

The repo already has the idiom for this. `input_error` (src/util/logger.hpp:265) logs and throws `runtime_error`. The per-track catch at src/niceops.cpp:2558-2561 turns that into a skip with a message. Commit 706742a ("fail loudly on an empty one") states the same policy: "Fail where the cause is legible."

The fix does not touch a rejected or load-bearing item. It runs once per trajectory in setup, outside the hot path, so nothing in CLAUDE.md's hot-path list applies.

It also changes no output that currently succeeds. The guard fires only when ixmin > ixmax, which today throws or aborts, so the goldens and byte-identity are untouched. The observable change is the error text, plus exit 134 becoming a skip with exit 1 for the empty-window case.

I rate the severity as low-to-medium, not medium. The failure is safe: the track is skipped and exit is nonzero. The console message is opaque, but the preceding log line shows start > end. The abort case is silent on the console.
  - Refined claim: watts_trim (src/trajectory.cpp:778-796) never checks that the trim start precedes the end point. Two failure modes result, and the reporter found only the first.
1. **Start beyond the end point by two or more states (ixmin >= ixmax+2).** The vector is built from a reversed iterator range, which is undefined behavior that in practice throws. The run logs "Skipping <track>: vector" on libc++ and "cannot create std::vector larger than max_size()" on libstdc++, then exits 1. Only the affected track is skipped.
2. **Start inside the one-state window just past the end point (ixmin == ixmax+1).** The sub-vector is empty and the run aborts (exit 134) in `peak_range`'s `fatal("...the trajectory produced no states.")`. The abort comes from the worker threads. There is no console output and nothing in the exception path, so a multi-track batch dies entirely.

This is reachable with the shipped data, not just a mistyped value: `--trim -200` skips tracks/S-76D/197419AC.csv, whose end point is at -257 ft, while 197421 in the same run is fine. The "Trimming trajectory from x = A to B" log line (line 793) prints meters with no unit label even though the user typed feet.
  - Verifier's fix: Add one guard in `Trajectory::watts_trim`, just before the sub-vector is built and after the info() line: `if (ixmin > static_cast<std::size_t>(ixmax)) input_error("--trim starts at x = ", x[ixmin], " m, past this trajectory's lowest/slowest point at x = ", x[ixmax], " m; nothing is left to integrate.");`

Use `>`, not `>=`. Since ixmin == ixmax is the working single-state case (`--trim 331`, exit 0), `>=` would change accepted behavior. `ixmin > ixmax` catches both the reversed-range and empty-range cases with one line.

Details:
- The message must label meters. `watts_trim` is documented to take meters, and the existing info line is already meters with no unit label. Converting to the user's unit would mean threading `LengthUnit` in or checking at the niceops.cpp:697 call site. That is optional. The label is enough, and a maintainer can do the conversion if wanted.
- Do NOT clamp to a single state as the reporter offered as an alternative. That would silently give a one-sample SEL.
- Do NOT use `fatal()`. It aborts the whole batch, and `input_error` is the documented per-input idiom.
- Add a Catch2 case in tests/test_trajectory.cpp on the demo track. It should cover a mid-flight start (first state x <= start, last state is the lowest/slowest point), a start at the end point's x (1 state), and starts one and two-plus states past it (`REQUIRE_THROWS`). This is the first coverage of `watts_trim`. A CTest `--trim` case is optional.

### trajectory-geodetic/B.r1.4: watts_trim() with a start x beyond the slowest/lowest point yields an inverted range: cryptic 'Skipping ...: vector' (exit 1) or an empty trajectory that aborts the process; no test covers --trim

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.95, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: R C×4**
- Locations: `src/trajectory.cpp:778`, `src/trajectory.cpp:629`, `src/niceops.cpp:697`
- **Claim.** watts_trim takes ixmax = min(argmin z, argmin mu) and ixmin = the last index with x <= xstart, then builds `std::vector<State> sub(states.begin() + ixmin, states.begin() + ixmax + 1)` with no check that ixmin <= ixmax. If --trim lies beyond the slowest/lowest point, the iterator range is inverted, which is undefined behavior; in practice libc++ and libstdc++ throw length_error. If ixmin == ixmax + 1 the result is an empty trajectory. The empty-states guard (trajectory.cpp:629-636) runs in the constructor, before watts_trim, so it does not protect this. No test in tests/ or CTest touches watts_trim or --trim, although the README uses --trim to reproduce the June 2020 reference figures.
- **Evidence given.** Demo track: slowest/lowest sample is index 5131 at x = 100.861 m (331 ft).
$ niceops --trim 3000 --gridstep 492  -> log `Trimming trajectory from x = 741.364 to 100.861` then `[error] Skipping tracks/S-76D/197421AC.csv: vector`, exit 1, nothing on the terminal (same for 9000 and 100000).
$ niceops --trim 332.291006 --gridstep 492 -> `Trimming trajectory from x = 101.142 to 100.861`, then `[fatal] peak_range: empty level history; the trajectory produced no states.`, exit 134 (abort), nothing on the terminal.
Direct calls: watts_trim(101.2823 m) -> 0 states; watts_trim(101.7041 m) -> throws `vector`; watts_trim(100.8611 m) -> 1 state.
- **Scenario.** A user trims an approach 'from 500 ft' on a track whose slowest point is at 331 ft. They get 'vector' in the log, or a silent abort, depending on which sample the cut lands on.
- **Proposed change.** After computing the indices, `if (ixmin > ixmax) input_error("--trim x = ", xstart_meters, " m lies past the slowest/lowest point at x = ", x[ixmax], " m; nothing would be left to integrate.")`. Add a Catch2 test: a trim before the point gives the expected sub-range, and a trim past it throws.
- **Expected impact.** A clear error naming the cause instead of a length_error or an abort. No change for valid trims (the demo `--trim -6000 --grid ...` recipe is untouched).

### trajectory-geodetic/A.r1.6: watts_trim throws an opaque 'vector' error when the crop start lies after the lowest or slowest point, and has no test

- Finder's severity low, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.85, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: R C×4**
- Locations: `src/trajectory.cpp:778`
- **Claim.** `ixmax` is the earlier of the min-z and min-mu indices, and `ixmin` is the last index with x <= start. Nothing checks `ixmin <= ixmax`. When the start is beyond the end point (a start past the lowest or slowest point, or a departure or hover-start track whose minimum is near index 0), `std::vector<State> sub(states.begin() + ixmin, states.begin() + ixmax + 1)` receives an inverted range. That is formally undefined behavior; in practice it surfaces as a length_error whose message is just `vector` on libc++ (an equally unhelpful string on libstdc++). The trajectory is then skipped with no hint about --trim.
- **Evidence given.** Demo track (lowest z at index 5489, x=686 ft): `niceops --trim 100000 -t tracks/S-76D/197421AC.csv ...` logs `Trimming trajectory from x = 741.364 to 100.861` (meters, unlabeled), then `[error] Skipping tracks/S-76D/197421AC.csv: vector`, exit 1. `--trim -6000` and `--trim -20000` work. `grep` finds no test that calls watts_trim and no CTest case that passes --trim.
- **Scenario.** `--trim 0` applied across a batch that includes a departure or hover-start track fails with `vector` and gives the user nothing to act on. Any regression in the trim rules would also pass CI.
- **Proposed change.** After computing the indices: `if (ixmax < ixmin) input_error("--trim start x=... lies after the lowest/slowest point at x=...; nothing to keep")`. Label the units in the info line, and add a unit test with a small constructed trajectory covering normal, start-before-track and inverted cases.
- **Expected impact.** Clear diagnosis instead of UB or an opaque exception. Pins the trim rule that CLAUDE.md says reproduces the 2020 reference figures.


## Grid count flips with feet-to-meter rounding

### main-io-sel/A.r1.0: iota_exclusive: a unit-converted (end-begin)/step that should be an integer rounds up, adding a column/row at the excluded end

- Finder's severity medium, category correctness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.95, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: R C×3**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:53`, `src/niceops.cpp:485`, `src/niceops.cpp:2370`
- **Claim.** With --units ft (the default) a user-supplied --grid/--gridstep are each multiplied by 0.3048 before iota_exclusive computes `ceil((end - begin) / step)`. The three roundings often make a quotient that is exactly integral in the user's units come out as N + 1ulp, so ceil returns N+1. The grid then contains a sample at `end`, violating the documented exclusive-end contract (the comment in gridded_observers and CLAUDE.md's 'iota is EXCLUSIVE' pitfall), and the same request gives a different grid under --units m. The comment 'Counted by index rather than accumulated, so the spacing cannot drift' is true of the values but not of the count.
- **Evidence given.** Ordinary request: `niceops -S state/x.csv --grid -3000 3000 -3000 3000 --gridstep 50 -L warn -o g.csv` writes 121 x values on line 2, last value `3000` (the excluded end); the exclusive answer is 120 values ending at 2950. Also 2949.9999999999995 as the penultimate value. Standalone: (3000*0.3048 - -3000*0.3048)/(50*0.3048) = 120.00000000000001. Minimal repro `--grid -53 1 -53 1 --gridstep 1`: 55 columns under ft, 54 under `--units m`. Scan of 402,000 (begin,end,step) integer-ft combinations whose span is divisible by the step: 56,498 (14%) gain a sample, none lose one (/tmp scratch program iota.cpp: `got=(long)ceil((b*f-a*f)/(s*f))` vs exact `((b-a)+s-1)/s`). Other affected common specs: `--grid -1500 1500 ... --gridstep 25` (121 vs 120). Unaffected: `-5000 5000 / 100`, `-2000 1100 / 25`. The default grid (meters, conversion skipped) and therefore the goldens are unaffected.
- **Scenario.** A user asks for a 6000 ft square at 50 ft spacing in the default unit. They get 121x121 observers, the last row/column on the edge they meant to exclude, and the output is not the same shape as the identical request in meters. Any script that reshapes the output by (end-begin)/step mis-sizes it.
- **Proposed change.** Decide the count in the user's own units, before conversion (pass LengthUnit into gridded_observers and build coordinates as (begin + step*i) * to_meters), or make iota_exclusive tolerant of the rounding: `ceil(q - 1e-9 * std::max(1.0, q))`. Either keeps 120 for the meter default. Add a unit test that sweeps integer feet specs and asserts count == ceil((b-a)/s).
- **Expected impact.** Removes a silently wrong observer grid on ~14% of round-number feet requests; also cleans the axis values printed back (they currently carry x/0.3048 noise such as 2949.9999999999995).

### main-io-sel/B.r1.4: iota_exclusive's element count flips by one with unit-conversion rounding (feet grids gain a spurious extra row and column)

- Finder's severity low, category correctness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.85, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: R V1 C×3**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:55`, `src/niceops.cpp:2370`
- **Claim.** Indexing the values by multiplication is right, but the count is `ceil((end - begin) / step)` on values that run() has each multiplied by 0.3048 separately. When the feet spec divides evenly, the quotient often lands a rounding error above the integer, so ceil gives n+1 and the grid is inclusive of its end point. Which specs are affected looks arbitrary, and the same numbers in meters are not affected.
- **Evidence given.** `niceops -S state/g.csv --units ft --grid -275 275 -275 275 --gridstep 5 -l log/u.log` -> log 'Evaluating 12321 observers' (111x111). The same command with `--units m` -> 'Evaluating 12100 observers' (110x110, the exclusive count the docs describe). Python model of the conversion: (550*0.3048/2*2)/(5*0.3048) = 110.00000000000001 -> ceil 111. Across 2216 even-dividing (span, step) pairs in feet, 452 (20%) get a wrong count, e.g. (550,5), (750,25), (850,10), (1100,20). The default grid (metric defaults, no conversion) is exact: 3000/25 = 120.
- **Scenario.** A user asks for a 550 ft square at 5 ft spacing and gets 111x111 points ending at +275 ft, where 550 ft at 10 ft gives 55 points and 600 ft at 5 ft gives 120. Files from runs that use different but equally 'round' extents have grids that are inconsistent with each other.
- **Proposed change.** Compute `q = (end - begin) / step`; if |q - round(q)| <= 1e-9 * max(1, round(q)) use round(q) as the count, else ceil(q). This is identical to D wherever the quotient is not within rounding of an integer, which includes the default grid and the default contour levels. Add a test that a feet spec and its meter equivalent give the same count.
- **Expected impact.** Grid extents match the documented exclusive rule for feet inputs. The default grid, the goldens and the shipped CTest cases (gridstep 492 ft on metric bounds gives 20.006, not near an integer) are unchanged.
- **Verifier (trace): confirmed, confidence 0.85.** The defect is real and I reproduced it with the shipped Release build. `iota_exclusive` (src/niceops.cpp:55-62) counts `ceil((end - begin) / step)`. `run()` (src/niceops.cpp:2370-2375) multiplies each user-supplied `--grid` bound and `--gridstep` by 0.3048 separately. When a feet spec divides evenly, the converted quotient often comes out a rounding error above the integer, so `ceil` gives n+1. The grid then includes its end point, contradicting the exclusive rule documented at src/niceops.cpp:487-489 and in CLAUDE.md.

The finding's own command gives 12321 observers (111x111) with `--units ft` and 12100 (110x110) with `--units m`. The last x in the feet output is 274.99999999999994 (the end bound); in the meters output it is 270. I also found a sharper case.
