The default grid spelled out in feet (`--grid -1500 1500 -1500 1500 --gridstep 25`) gives 14641 observers, against 14400 for the same numbers in meters.

Two details in the finding are wrong. Its control claim that 550 ft at 10 ft "gives 55 points" is false: that spec gives 3136 observers, which is 56x56 and also flipped. Its "20% of 2216 pairs" statistic did not reproduce. My sweep of 5177 symmetric integer specs found 603 (11.6%) wrong in feet and 0 wrong in meters. Neither error affects the mechanism.

Guards and tests: no guard upstream or downstream snaps the count. Input validation only checks `step > 0` and `xmin < xmax` (src/niceops.cpp:2236-2243). No test reaches `iota_exclusive`, which sits in an anonymous namespace. The only "iota" match in tests/ is an unrelated comment at tests/test_sphere_database.cpp:57. The shipped CTest cases and the default grid are unaffected, and the goldens do not move.

Severity is low. The only effect is one extra row and column of observers at the far edge, which is a spurious inconsistency rather than wrong physics. The niceops-validation harness's own feet grids (-6800 1300 and -1900 1900 at 50 ft) evaluate to exact quotients 162 and 76, so they are not hit. The inconsistency is still real and cheap to remove, and it undermines `--units` as a pure relabeling seam.
  - Refined claim: iota_exclusive's ceil((end-begin)/step) is ill-conditioned exactly where the exclusive-end rule matters, when the span is an integer multiple of the step. The per-value 0.3048 conversion in run() leaves a one-ULP-scale positive error on many such feet specs, so ceil returns n+1 and the far edge is included. Whether a spec is affected depends on rounding, not on the spec. Verified: 550 ft at 5 ft and 550 ft at 10 ft flip (111 and 56 points per axis), and the default grid spelled out in feet (-1500..1500 at 25) gives 121 per axis. 600 ft at 5 ft, 2000 ft at 10 ft and 1000 ft at 20 ft do not flip. The finding's claim that 550 ft at 10 ft gives 55 points is incorrect, and its 20% rate did not reproduce (11.6% on my symmetric sweep). The defect is confined to counts on evenly-dividing feet specs. Meter specs with integer values are unaffected, though fractional meter steps such as 0.1 could flip in principle.
  - Verifier's fix: Snap the count in iota_exclusive: q = (end - begin) / step; r = std::round(q); count = (|q - r| <= 1e-9 * max(1, r)) ? r : ceil(q). This is identical to the current code except within rounding of an integer, which leaves the default grid, the contour levels, the goldens and the 492 and 984 ft CTest steps unchanged. Add a unit or CTest case that a feet spec such as --grid -275 275 -275 275 --gridstep 5 (and the default grid spelled in feet) gives the same count as the same numbers under --units m. iota_exclusive is in an anonymous namespace in niceops.cpp, so such a test needs the helper exposed or a CLI-level check of the "Evaluating N observers" log line.

### main-cli/A.r1.6: Grid point counts are decided by 1-ulp noise that the ft-to-meter seam introduces (--grid -1500 1500 --gridstep 100 gives 31 points, -1000 1000 gives 20); unchecked size_t casts on inf/0 steps

- Finder's severity low, category accuracy, effort S, finder's confidence 0.85, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: R C×3**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:55`, `src/niceops.cpp:492`, `src/niceops.cpp:1828`, `src/niceops.cpp:2370`
- **Claim.** iota_exclusive takes ceil((end - begin) / step) of doubles that the seam has already multiplied by 0.3048 separately (bounds and step). A ratio that is an exact integer in the user's unit becomes n(1+eps) in meters about one time in five for round-number ft grids, so the 'exclusive of end' contract flips to inclusive at random. The cast static_cast<size_t>(ceil(x)) is also undefined for inf/NaN/huge x.
- **Evidence given.** Python replay of the port's arithmetic: ft symmetric round grids 30 of 160 wrong (e.g. -4500..4500 step 10, -9000..9000 step 20); meters with integer steps 0 of 21890 wrong, so the noise comes from the seam. Actual binary (--units ft):
--grid -1500 1500 -1500 1500 --gridstep 100 -> 31 points, last x = 1500
--grid -1000 1000 -1000 1000 --gridstep 100 -> 20 points, last x = 899.99999999999989
LDC probe (scratch, outside repo): D's iota on the same doubles returns the same lengths (31, 20, 121 for -3000..3000/50), so the port reproduces D exactly; the noise arises because D was never fed pre-multiplied feet. Also: --gridstep inf passes the '> 0' check (niceops.cpp:2236), yields 0 observers, writes a lone header and exits 0 (the failure CLAUDE.md fixed for observer files); --cstep 0 casts inf to size_t and dies after the whole run with 'Stopping: vector'.
- **Scenario.** A user runs the same footprint twice with different round-number bounds and gets differently shaped output files (one with an extra edge row and column), which breaks comparisons and downstream reshaping that assume (hi-lo)/step points.
- **Proposed change.** Snap the ratio before ceil (if |r - round(r)| <= 1e-9 max(1, |r|) use round(r)), or better, compute the count in the user's units before conversion; reject non-finite steps/bounds and counts above a sanity limit before the cast (also covers --gridstep inf and --cstep <= 0). The default grid (ratio exactly 120) and contour ratio (exactly 8) are unaffected.
- **Expected impact.** Grid shape follows the documented exclusive-end rule for round-number requests in either unit; no goldens move.
- **Conflicts with docs.** niceops.cpp:489-491 documents exclusive-end iota semantics; the port matches D bit-for-bit for the same doubles, so this is a departure only in the sense that the seam, not D, produces the noisy inputs. Port-fidelity rule: the measured case is above.


## Geodetic units: origin lookup and bracketed header units

### trajectory-geodetic/A.r1.0: Default local origin is looked up in feet whatever --units says, so --frame_bearing is silently dropped for metric geodetic tracks

- Finder's severity high, category correctness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.97, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: Rc**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:2287`, `src/niceops.cpp:2364`, `src/trajectory.hpp:132`, `src/trajectory.cpp:99`
- **Claim.** run() settles the frame at niceops.cpp:2291 with `first_geodetic_position(path)`, which takes the default `LengthUnit unit = feet` (trajectory.hpp:133). `const LengthUnit unit = unit_name == "m" ? ...` is not declared until line 2364, so the unit cannot reach the call. find_geodetic_columns then looks for the feet-only altitude spellings. A track headed `alt_m` (the spelling that only exists under --units m) is 'not positioned', the function returns nullopt, `reference` stays empty and `frame` is never built. Each trajectory then centers its own frame on its first position, east/north, and --frame_bearing is ignored. With several tracks they no longer share a frame either.
- **Evidence given.** Demo flight rewritten as a metric WGS84 file `time,lat,lon,alt_m` (every 10th row). Run: `niceops -t geo_altm.csv -v databases/S-76D.json -d databases/S-76D_M3.nod --units m --frame_bearing 210 --ground_elevation 16.5 --gridstep 492 --no-spectral -S state/a.csv`. Log: `[info] Projecting geo_altm.csv about its first position, 39.8291, -74.41, with x east and y north.` There is no 'Taking the local origin' line and the exit code is 0. The identical run with the origin given explicitly (`--origin 39.82914323 -74.40996141`) puts the last state at (3219.6, -158.2) m; without it the last state is at (-1738.3, -2710.3) m. On a 7x7 grid the two footprints differ by a median of 10.9 dB and a maximum of 31.4 dB (peak 85.1 vs 82.6 dB). With the header `alt_ft` under --units m, the same call finds the origin, then the load fails loudly. Only the metric spelling is affected.
- **Scenario.** A metric user follows the README and writes `alt_m` with `--units m`, relying on 'without --origin, the first position of the first geodetic track is used' plus `--frame_bearing`. The footprint is computed in a rotated frame the observers were not laid out for, and the run succeeds. A multi-track run additionally mixes frames.
- **Proposed change.** Move the `unit` declaration above the frame-settling block, since it depends only on `unit_name`, and pass it: `first_geodetic_position(path, unit)`. Better still, drop the defaulted parameter so the omission cannot recur. Add a test that `first_geodetic_position` returns a position for an `alt_m` file when given `meters` (the existing 'altitude column may only claim the unit in force' test never calls it).
- **Expected impact.** Removes an entire class of silently wrong footprints for --units m runs with unit-named altitude headers. No cost.
- **Conflicts with docs.** README.md:146 and 992-993 promise the first-position default. README.md:989 and CLAUDE.md ('alt_m is deliberately not a recognized altitude spelling') contradict the code, which accepts alt_m under --units m.

### trajectory-geodetic/B.r1.0: first_geodetic_position() is called without --units, so a meter-unit geodetic track loses its run frame and --frame_bearing (15 dB footprint error)

- Finder's severity high, category correctness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.97, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: Rc**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:2288`, `src/niceops.cpp:2364`, `src/trajectory.hpp:132`, `src/trajectory.cpp:99`, `src/trajectory.cpp:181`
- **Claim.** run() settles the frame at niceops.cpp:2291 with `first_geodetic_position(path)`, which takes `LengthUnit unit = feet` (trajectory.hpp:133). The real unit is only built later, at niceops.cpp:2364 (`const LengthUnit unit = unit_name == "m" ? meters : feet;`). find_geodetic_columns accepts `alt_m`-style headers only when unit.to_meters == 1.0 (trajectory.cpp:102-104). So under `--units m`, with an `alt_m` altitude header (the spelling the README tells meter users to use) and no --origin, first_geodetic_position looks for `alt ft`, finds no altitude column and returns nullopt. run() then has no reference and no frame. Each Trajectory falls into the 'lone trajectory centers its own frame' fallback (trajectory.cpp:181-190): x east, y north about its own first point. --frame_bearing is ignored without a word, and several tracks in one run no longer share a frame although they share the observers. The only trace is an info log line.
- **Evidence given.** Setup (scratch copy of the demo track's lat/lon/alt columns, every 50th row, altitude converted to meters): geo_alt_m.csv has header `time,lat,lon,alt_m`; geo_alt.csv has the identical data with header `time,lat,lon,alt`.
$ niceops -t geo_alt_m.csv --units m --frame_bearing 210 --gridstep 150 --grid -600 600 -500 500 -o integrated/geo_alt_m.csv
log: `Projecting geo_alt_m.csv about its first position, 39.8291, -74.41, with x east and y north.`
$ niceops -t geo_alt.csv (same flags)
log: `Taking the local origin from the first position of geo_alt.csv: 39.8291, -74.41.`
The two 56-cell footprints differ by max 15.18 dB, mean 6.48 dB, exit 0 both times.
The unit test 'an altitude column may only claim the unit in force' (tests/test_trajectory.cpp:497) only exercises the Trajectory constructor, never first_geodetic_position with meters. With --latlon the run does fail (`--latlon has no frame to invert`), so only the plain path is silent.
- **Scenario.** A user with a meter-based planner export (`time,lat,lon,alt_m`) runs `niceops -t track.csv --units m --frame_bearing 210` with the observer grid defined in the track-aligned frame. The track is projected east/north instead, so it sits in the wrong place relative to the observers. Exit 0, and the footprint is off by several dB on average.
- **Proposed change.** Build `unit` before the frame block (or compute `unit_name == "m" ? meters : feet` there) and pass it: `first_geodetic_position(path, unit)`. Remove the default argument `= feet` from the declaration so no caller can forget it again. Add a test: first_geodetic_position on an `alt_m` file returns a value under `meters` and nullopt under `feet`. Optionally make first_geodetic_position unit-agnostic, since it only needs lat/lon.
- **Expected impact.** Removes a silent wrong-frame path for the documented `--units m` + `alt_m` combination; demo, goldens and feet runs are unaffected.

### trajectory-geodetic/A.r1.1: Header normalization discards parenthesized units, so the --units guard is bypassed and time and velocity units are never checked

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort M, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/util/csv.hpp:132`, `src/trajectory.cpp:90`, `src/trajectory.cpp:199`, `tests/test_csv.cpp:117`
- **Claim.** LabeledCsv::normalize stops at the first '(' or '[' (`if (c == '(' || c == '[') break;`), so `Alt (ft)`, `Altitude (m)` and `Altitude [ft]` all become the bare spelling `alt`/`altitude`. find_geodetic_columns treats bare spellings as claiming no unit ('always accepted'), so the check that stops a foot column being read as meters only fires for underscore spellings (`alt_ft`). The time column (`Time (ms)`, `Time (min)`) and the velocity columns (`Climb Rate (fpm)`, `vE (kt)`) have no unit check at all: they are always read as seconds and as `--units` per second. Two columns that differ only in a parenthetical (`Altitude (MSL)`, `Altitude (AGL)`) collapse to one name and the leftmost silently wins.
- **Evidence given.** Trajectory(file, S-76D.json, frame, unit) with a 300-altitude row: `alt_ft` under meters is rejected, as documented. `Alt (ft)` under meters is accepted (z0=300 m; a foot value read as meters puts the aircraft 3.28x too high). `Altitude (m)` under feet is accepted (z0=91.44 m, 3.28x too low). `Altitude [ft]` under meters is accepted. `Time (ms)` with 20 ms spacing gives 7961 resampled states, `mu=7.8e-5` against a true 0.136 (30 m/s over 220 m/s). `Climb Rate (fpm)` = 600 gives vz = 182.88 m/s (3.05 m/s expected). `Altitude (MSL)=1000, Altitude (AGL)=100` gives z = 1000 ft. tests/test_csv.cpp:117 pins `normalize("  Alt (ft) ") == "alt"`, so the discarding is by design and untested against the guard.
- **Scenario.** A spreadsheet export headed `Altitude (m)` is run with the default feet, or `Alt (ft)` with `--units m`. This is exactly the header decoration normalize was written to accept. The run succeeds with a 3.28x wrong height (or 1000x wrong time base), and the README says a disagreement 'fails the file rather than being silently believed'.
- **Proposed change.** Have normalize (or a companion `unit_of(header)`) also return the parenthesized or bracketed token. Map known tokens (ft, m, s, ms, min, kt, mps, fps, fpm, deg) and, in find_geodetic_columns, reject a column whose declared unit disagrees with the unit the code will apply (altitude vs --units, time vs seconds, velocity vs --units per second). Treat duplicate normalized names among the matched columns as an error.
- **Expected impact.** Closes the documented guard against 3.28x altitude and 1000x time misreads for the common decorated-header case.
- **Conflicts with docs.** README.md:108 ('Where a file names its own unit in a column header ... the name is checked against --units'). README.md:989 says punctuation and a parenthesized unit are ignored, which is the cause.

### trajectory-geodetic/B.r1.3: A bracketed unit in the altitude header ('Altitude (m)') bypasses the unit check that README promises

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/util/csv.hpp:135`, `src/trajectory.cpp:94`, `tests/test_csv.cpp:115`
- **Claim.** README.md:108-110 says that where a file names its own unit in a column header, the name is checked against --units and a disagreement fails the file. That only holds for the underscore/space spellings (`alt_m`, `alt ft`). LabeledCsv::normalize stops at the first '(' or '[' (csv.hpp:140-141), so `Altitude (m)` normalizes to the bare alias `altitude`, which claims no unit and is always accepted (trajectory.cpp:99). The bracketed spelling is the usual spreadsheet/planner one, and test_csv.cpp:117 pins the stripping as intended for `lat (deg)`. The velocity columns (`vE`, `vN`, `vUp`, aliases `climb rate`, `climb`) have no unit check at all and are always read as ft/s (or m/s), so a `climb rate (ft/min)` or `vE (kt)` header is taken as ft/s.
- **Evidence given.** Direct Trajectory construction with `input.frame` set, 3-row file, altitude 300:
`time,lat,lon,alt_m` under feet -> refused (as designed)
`time,lat,lon,Altitude (m)` under feet -> LOADED, first z = 91.44 m (300 read as feet)
`time,lat,lon,Altitude [m]` under feet -> LOADED, first z = 91.44 m
`time,lat,lon,alt (ft)` under meters -> LOADED, first z = 300.00 m (feet read as meters)
`time,lat,lon,alt_ft` under meters -> refused.
- **Scenario.** A user exports a track with 'Altitude (m)' and forgets `--units m`. The aircraft flies 3.28x too low, exit 0. The guard the docs advertise is exactly the one that would have caught it.
- **Proposed change.** Keep the bracketed text when reading headers (e.g. `LabeledCsv::unit_tag(i)`) and, for the altitude column found via a bare alias, refuse a tag that names the other length unit ('m', 'meters', 'meters' versus 'ft', 'feet'), with the same error message as today. Optionally extend to the velocity columns (m/s, ft/s, kt, ft/min). Extend 'an altitude column may only claim the unit in force' with the bracketed spellings.
- **Expected impact.** Closes the gap between the documented and actual unit safety net; no effect on files that use bare names or the underscore spellings.


## DNL on a clockless geodetic track

### main-setup/A.r1.2: DNL for a WGS84 track with no utcsec column and no --start_utc is silently classified as night, giving +10 dB

- Finder's severity medium, category accuracy, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: R C×3**
- Locations: `src/trajectory.cpp:312`, `src/niceops.cpp:2004`, `src/niceops.cpp:750`
- **Claim.** read_geodetic_states sets utc_at_start = input.start_utc when the file has no utcsec column, and --start_utc defaults to 0.0, i.e. UTC midnight. is_night(0 + utc_offset*3600) is true for any offset that puts local time in [22:00, 07:00), for example utc_offset 0 or +1 to +7. So -D on such a track puts every sample in the night window without any indication.
- **Evidence given.** geo.csv (time, lat, lon, alt_ft columns, taken from the demo AC track) run with `--origin 39.8104276 -74.4263086 --frame_bearing 210 --ground_elevation 54.2651 --gridstep 984 -D dnl.csv -o sel.csv`. Without --start_utc, max(DNL - SEL) over the grid is 10.000000000000043 dB, from one operation. With --start_utc 43200 it is 1.4e-14. The log has no warning (grep of src for 'start_utc' shows no diagnostic). The utc_offset sign is right: on the AC demo track, --utc_offset=9 gives +10.000 and -5 gives 0.000.
- **Scenario.** A European user (utc_offset +1/+2) computes DNL from a lat/lon/alt/time track and forgets --start_utc. Every operation is penalized by 10 dB and the DNL contours are wrong. The README says only 'default puts it at UTC midnight'.
- **Proposed change.** Make start_utc default to NaN. If -D is requested and a state's utcsec is NaN (geodetic track without utcsec and no --start_utc), raise input_error naming --start_utc. Non-DNL runs are unaffected, since utcsec is read only by the night classifier.
- **Expected impact.** Turns a silent 10 dB error into a legible refusal.

### main-cli/A.r1.5: -D on a WGS84 track with no clock silently classifies the whole flight as night (DNL = SEL + 10.000 dB)

- Finder's severity medium, category accuracy, effort S, finder's confidence 0.8, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: R C×3**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:2004`, `src/niceops.cpp:2134`, `src/niceops.cpp:750`, `src/trajectory.cpp:312`, `src/util/sel.hpp:127`
- **Claim.** --start_utc defaults to 0.0 (UTC midnight). A geodetic trajectory without a utcsec column takes utcsec = start_utc + t, and is_night() treats 00:00-07:00 as night, so with the default --utc_offset 0 every state gets the +10 dB penalty and the DNL is exactly SEL + 10. Nothing is logged. The trajectory.cpp comment shows the midnight default is deliberate for the time axis, but nothing makes the user aware it also decides the DNL penalty.
- **Evidence given.** Geodetic track built from the demo track's time/lat/lon/alt columns (every 25th row), then
$ niceops -t geo.csv --frame_bearing 210 --ground_elevation 54.2651 --gridstep 492 -o sel.csv -D dnl.csv
$ niceops ... --start_utc 43200 -o sel2.csv -D dnl2.csv
mean(dnl - sel) = 10.000 dB with the default; 0.000 dB with --start_utc 43200. No warning in the log for either run (grep -i 'night|start_utc|midnight' finds nothing).
- **Scenario.** Planning DNL for a set of proposed routes given as waypoints: the user supplies -D and forgets --start_utc; every route is reported 10 dB louder in DNL than the same route at midday, and the sum over routes is biased by the same amount.
- **Proposed change.** When want_dnl and a trajectory has no utcsec column and --start_utc was not given (start_utc_option->count() == 0), fail with input_error naming --start_utc (or at minimum warn and log the assumed local time). AC-format tracks always carry utcsec and are unaffected.
- **Expected impact.** Prevents a systematic +10 dB DNL bias on clock-less tracks; DNL for tracks with a clock, and all goldens, unchanged.
- **Conflicts with docs.** trajectory.cpp:312-314 states the midnight default as intent; README.md:150 does not say the default lands in the night window.

### main-loop/A.r1.4: -D with a WGS84 track that has no utcsec column and no --start_utc silently classifies the whole flight as night (+10 dB); DNL values, the penalty and multi-trajectory summation are not tested

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: R C×3**
- Locations: `src/trajectory.cpp:311`, `src/niceops.cpp:2004`, `src/niceops.cpp:1704`, `src/niceops.cpp:2569`
- **Claim.** start_utc defaults to 0, so states get utcsec = t, is_night(0..131 s) is true, and every sample takes the 10 dB penalty. Nothing is logged. The AC 27-column path is unaffected because it carries utcsec. Also, every CTest that passes -D uses it only to force the marched path (CMakeLists lines 269, 276, 332, 373, 375, 378) and never reads the DNL file, so a mutation that drops the penalty, inverts `night`, or turns `dnl_energy[index] +=` into `=` passes the whole suite.
- **Evidence given.** geo.csv (time,lat,lon,alt sampled from the demo track), `niceops -t geo.csv --frame_bearing 210 --gridstep 984 -D integrated/geo_dnl.csv -o integrated/geo_lae.csv`: LAE cell 69.608059 vs DNL cell 79.608059 (exactly +10). With `--start_utc 43200` the two files are equal. `grep -i 'utc|night' log/niceops.log` shows only the "Writing day-night level" line. Also note (documented, faithful to D): the result is sum of 10^(SEL/10) without the 1/86400 s normalization, so it is 49.4 dB above a conventional DNL and README says a single day operation equals its SEL; the D original also carried an `ambient_dnl` term (c74bab3 lineage, 1c9ea8d niceops.d:818-834) that the port dropped.
- **Scenario.** A planner gives waypoints from a flight-planning tool (lat, lon, alt, time) and asks for DNL; without --start_utc the day flight is reported 10 dB louder than its SEL.
- **Proposed change.** When -D is given and a trajectory has no utcsec column and --start_utc was not supplied, either input_error asking for --start_utc or log a warning stating t=0 is taken as 00:00 UTC. Add a CTest that runs the same track twice with -D (expect SEL+3.01 dB by day, +10 dB shift with --utc_offset placing it at night) and compares against the LAE file. Decide separately whether the 1/86400 normalization is intended.
- **Expected impact.** Removes a silent 10 dB error in the day-night output and pins the DNL path.
- **Conflicts with docs.** README.md:150 documents --start_utc but not that omitting it means night.


## DirectionBounds not an upper bound at the nose pole

### db-load/A.r1.1: DirectionBounds is not an upper bound near the nose pole: home_bin drops the azimuth of the theta=0 row

- Finder's severity medium, category correctness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.92, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: R C×3**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:1824`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1868`, `src/sphere_database.hpp:256`, `tests/test_sphere_database.cpp:163`
- **Claim.** home_bin() places a grid cell by calling DirectionBounds::bin(cos t, sin t cos p, sin t sin p, 1). At theta = 0, sin(0) is exactly 0, so across = +/-0.0 and up = 0 for every phi, and bin() puts all 36 pole cells in azimuth bin 4 whatever their phi. The table then pads each cell by only +/-2 azimuth bins, so bins 0, 1 and 7 never receive the theta=0 row. But a query direction within 0-5 degrees of the nose has a bilinear stencil that includes that row with weight up to 1, and its own azimuth (a function of the small offset) can be any of the 8 sectors. Where the pole row is louder than the neighboring cells, the true level exceeds the tabulated bound. The tail pole is unaffected only because std::sin(pi) = 1.2e-16 is not zero, so the theta=180 cells keep their phi. The code documents this bound as 'provable' and the windowed peak search as exact; the property does not hold.
- **Evidence given.** Sweep (scratch r1_db/boundpole2.cpp): S-76D_M3.nod, 918 blend states (mu 0..0.34, gamma -24..24, CT 3 values), every direction phi -90..90 step 0.5, theta 0..180 step 0.25, level = db.eval(R, phi, theta, blend) vs 10*log10(bounds.numerator(state, bin)/R^2): 238,937,958 points, 707 violations, all in theta-bin 15 / azimuth-bin 7, 6 states, worst 0.731 dB at phi=90 theta=0.25 (mu=0.12 gamma=12 CT=0.0045). B407_M3.nod: 873 violations, all azimuth-bin 0, worst 1.611 dB (mu=0.14 gamma=21). AW139_M1, B407_ambient_gated, AW139_fixed_load_factor, B407_fixed_load_factor: 0.
Why: for the S-76D blend at (0.12, 12, 0.0045) the theta=0 row is 92.08 dB for every phi, versus 84-89 dB at theta=5,10,15 for phi 60..100 (r1_db/polecells.cpp), and only the latter reach bin (15,7).
Real track (scratch r1_db/trackviol.cpp, default 25 m grid, every state x every observer, level_from_geometry vs bounds.numerator): tracks/S-76D/197421AC.csv (the demo): 2 of 94,331,498 pairs violate, worst 0.102 dB at observer (1000 m, 25 m), state 6348 (t=126.96 s, mu 0.148, fpa_e 12.7); 197419AC: 1 pair, 0.025 dB; the other 28 S-76D tracks: 0.
No shipped output changes: niceops default vs -i (full march) on the demo track for observers including (1000,25) m give byte-identical CSVs (cmp IDENTICAL).
Fix verified in a scratch copy (git archive of HEAD, only home_bin edited): `const double theta = std::max(thetas[i_theta], 1e-6) * pi / 180.0;` gives 0 violations in the same sweep (S-76D_M3) and 0 pairs on 197421AC, the demo output is byte-identical to golden/197421_LAE.csv, and `niceops_unit_tests [sphere_database]` passes (81017 assertions in 25 cases).
The existing test samples observers on a 137 m x 149 m lattice over 4 km for 48 states, never within a few degrees of a pole in an affected sector, so it cannot see this.
- **Scenario.** An observer 0-5 degrees off the nose of a climbing/descending state (straight-ahead centerline observers on a departure or approach) in the S-76D or B407 databases: the peak search or window-end scan can skip a state whose true level is up to 0.7 dB (S-76D) or 1.6 dB (B407_M3) above its bound. The effect on SEL is bounded by that excess landing exactly on the peak or a window edge, so it is very small in practice, but the 'exact, byte-identical' guarantee is not actually a guarantee, and other databases with a louder pole row could do worse.
- **Proposed change.** Make the nose-pole row spread over the azimuth it stands for. Smallest change: in Database::home_bin clamp theta to a tiny positive value (as in the verified edit above) so cells at theta=0 get the bin of their own phi like the theta=180 row does; both direction_bounds() and spectral_direction_bounds() use home_bin, so both are fixed. Alternatively pad pole cells over all azimuth bins. Add a test that sweeps (phi, theta) directly, including theta < 5 and > 175, through DirectionBounds::bin and eval() for a few blends, requiring numerator >= 10^(L/10) R^2 everywhere.
- **Expected impact.** Restores the invariant the windowed SEL exactness rests on; costs at most a marginally looser bound in azimuth bins near the pole (no measurable change in evaluated fraction expected).
- **Conflicts with docs.** Contradicts CLAUDE.md's claim that the windowed path is 'exact' and that tests/test_sphere_database.cpp checks the bound 'is never exceeded'; does not re-propose any rejected item.

### db-eval/A.r1.1: DirectionBounds tables leave the theta=0 row out of azimuth bins 0, 1 and 7, so the bound is not an upper bound near the nose axis

- Finder's severity medium, category correctness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: R C×3**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:1824`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1875`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1949`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:2016`, `src/sphere_database.hpp:256`
- **Claim.** home_bin() files a grid cell by the azimuth of its center. At theta = 0 the azimuth is 0/0: `std::sin(0.0)` is exactly 0, so up and across are both +/-0, `magnitude > reference * tan` is false, sector 0 and `across >= 0.0` give azimuth bin 4 for every phi. The padding then spreads that row only over bins 2..6 (azimuth_pad = 2). A direction one hair off the pole can be in any azimuth bin (bin() sees the real across/up ratio), and it reads the theta=0 cells at weight up to 1 through the bilinear stencil. In bins 0, 1 and 7 the polar row is therefore never included, and the table can sit below the level actually produced. The spectral tables use the same home_bin() and the same padding, so they share the gap. The theta=180 row works only because sin(pi) = 1.2e-16 is not exactly 0.
- **Evidence given.** (a) Deterministic repro, 10-degree grid, 60 dBA everywhere except the theta=0 row at 100 dBA, EAA=0, r=200 m (pole6.cpp, hot_pole.nod): theta=0.5, phi=-80 -> bin (15,0) level 81.66 dBA > bound 43.66 dBA; phi=-60 -> (15,1) 81.66 > 43.66; phi=+80 -> (15,7) 81.66 > 43.66; phi=0/60 -> (15,4)/(15,6) 81.66 <= 83.66. (b) Shipped databases, 4000 random conditions x 8 azimuth bins, polar level (interpolate_sphere) vs table: bound short of the polar level in S-76D_M3 19 pairs (worst 1.71 dB), B205_M1 585 (1.40 dB), AW139_M1 344 (0.59 dB), B407_M3 18 (1.59 dB), S-76D_M4 111 (14.4 dB, at mu=0.28 gamma=-24.5 CT=0.031). Real level vs bound at r=31 m on that S-76D_M4 state: 138.96 dBA vs 125.32 dBA at theta=0.25 deg. Inside a nominal S-76D_M3 envelope (mu<=0.25, gamma -12..8, CT<=0.009): 0 of 6000. (c) End-to-end on the hot-pole database with a 10 m-altitude level pass starting 12 km out (level3.csv), default windowed run vs `-i` full march: SEL differs, -0.0082 dB at y=+/-187 ft and -0.0120 dB at y=60 ft; the windowed result is lower. So the documented 'byte-identical' claim fails by construction. The error stays tiny only because the coarse seed sweep (nt/128) evaluates some polar states unconditionally. (d) 112 shipped track x database runs (S-76D x M3/M4, B205_M1, AW139_M1, 88-observer sets along and beside each track) are byte-identical, so the shipped case data do not expose it. (e) The existing test 'DirectionBounds really bounds the level' does put 624 of 38880 samples within 5 deg of a pole, but S-76D_M3 at those conditions happens to have neighbors louder than the polar value. Both the test and the code pass with the gap present.
- **Scenario.** Observer within a few degrees of the nose axis at ranges where the (dropped) excess-attenuation term is still small, roughly r under 200 m: low passes and final-approach or pad observers. Any database whose polar value exceeds the loudest theta 5-20 deg cell in azimuth bins 0, 1 or 7 (B205, S-76D_M4 and AW139 already do at some conditions). The windowed peak search or the window-edge scan then skips a state that is above the threshold, so SEL is lost from the tail. The peak error is bounded by the seed spacing, but the exactness contract is broken and nothing tests it.
- **Proposed change.** In direction_bounds() and in the home[] fill of spectral_direction_bounds(), treat a cell whose sin(theta) is (near) zero as spanning every azimuth bin: `const bool pole = std::fabs(std::sin(thetas[n]*pi/180.0)) < 1e-9; const int az_reach = pole ? DirectionBounds::azimuth_bins : azimuth_pad;` and loop `da` over +/-az_reach (a home azimuth clamps to 0..7 already). I applied this to a scratch copy: the four shipped databases go from 19/111/585/344 violating (state, bin) pairs to 0 of 32000 each, and hot_pole.nod's ten queries all sit at or under 83.66 dBA. Add a test that puts a hot theta=0 row in a synthetic database and asserts numerator(bin(15,a)) covers it for all 8 a, on both the broadband and spectral tables.
- **Expected impact.** Restores the exactness of the peak-expanding path (windowed == marched to the bit) near the nose axis at a negligible cost: a few more bins carry the polar value, so slightly more states are evaluated.

### db-eval/B.r1.0: DirectionBounds tables do not cover the theta=0 pole: the bound is exceeded by up to 0.78 dB within ~2 degrees of the nose axis

- Finder's severity medium, category correctness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: R C×3**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:1824`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1875`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1949`, `src/sphere_database.hpp:256`, `tests/test_sphere_database.cpp:163`
- **Claim.** home_bin() files a grid cell by the bin of its center direction, but at theta = 0 the center direction has up = across = 0, so bin() returns azimuth sector 0 / bin 4 for every phi. The pole row is then padded by only +-azimuth_pad (2) bins, i.e. recorded in azimuth bins 2..6. A query at theta of 0.5 to 2 degrees and |phi| > ~45 degrees has azimuth bin 0, 1 or 7, and its bilinear stencil weights the pole cells at up to 0.9, but no pole value was ever written into those bins. Bound < level results whenever a pole value exceeds the other cells in that bin. theta = 180 escapes only by accident: sin(pi) = 1.2e-16 is not exactly 0, so that row keeps its azimuth. The 'never exceeded' property (CLAUDE.md: 'the whole thing rests on that one property') is therefore false in a 2-degree cone about the nose.
- **Evidence given.** home_bin: `return DirectionBounds::bin(std::cos(theta), std::sin(theta) * std::cos(phi), std::sin(theta) * std::sin(phi), 1.0);` -> for theta=0: along=1, up=0, across=0 -> `while (sector < half_azimuth-1 && magnitude > reference*tan)` is false at 0 > 0 -> az bin 4. The pole row of S-76D_M3 is constant in phi (87.9 dB) while the theta=5 row at phi=20 is 90.3 and theta=10 is 92.9, so the pole cell is often the local maximum of its bin.
Experiment (repo Release lib, standalone programs in scratch/dbbounds): states = 4375 real blends from source_state() over the whole (mu,gamma,CT) envelope, frame nose=+x normal=+z, R=100 m, level via level_from_geometry, bin via DirectionBounds::bin, compare numerator >= 10^(L/10) r^2 (1-1e-12):
  S-76D_M3: 305 violations of 19,906,250 directions, worst 0.778 dB (theta 0.5, phi 90); all 305 at theta<90, 0 near theta=180
  AW139_M1: 13,688 violations, worst 0.220 dB (theta 0.5, phi -70)
  B205_M1: 56,238 violations, worst 0.370 dB (theta 0.5, phi 90)
  B407_M3: 0.  Single-condition blends on S-76D_M3: 8,160 of 23.3M, worst 1.77 dB.
  Spectral path (spectral_direction_bounds, A-weighted level, no absorption): AS350_fixed_load_factor 36 of 4,372,550, worst 0.14 dB (theta 0.5, phi -90); EC130/B407 fixed_load_factor 0.
The shipped unit tests cannot see it: replicating their geometry (test_sphere_database.cpp:174-210) gives theta range [0.59,170.6], only 8 of 1620 observer positions per state within 2 degrees of a pole; the spectral test reaches 2 of 1104.
Fix tried in a scratch copy (pole cells, |sin theta|<1e-9, written into all 8 azimuth bins of their theta bin, in direction_bounds and in spectral_direction_bounds' home loop): the same sweeps, plus a whole-sphere sweep (theta 0..180 step 0.7, phi -90..90 step 1.3, 4375 blends, 156.9M directions per database on S-76D_M3, AW139_M1, B205_M1, B407_M3) give 0 violations; AS350 spectral 0 violations.
- **Scenario.** An observer almost dead ahead of (or behind) the aircraft along the TPP nose axis, e.g. a low approach or a taxi/hover-taxi pass with the observers on the extended track, at a state whose true level is within ~0.8 dB of the window edge or of the current best. DirectionBounds::numerator understates the level, so `numerator < best_energy * squared_range` (niceops.cpp:341-344) or `certainly_below` (niceops.cpp:368-375) skips a state that is not provably below; the peak or a window end is then wrong and the SEL differs from a full march. On the demo and on 840 observers along its track, and on 1782 observers under a 30-85 ft flyby with B205_M1, AW139_M1 and AS350 databases, the windowed and marched (-i) outputs were byte-identical, so no output error was produced; the defect is latent because level near the nose is usually far below the peak.
- **Proposed change.** In direction_bounds() and in the home[]/band_max loop of spectral_direction_bounds(), treat a cell with |sin(theta)| below a small epsilon as having no azimuth: write its value into every azimuth bin of its theta bin (and of the +-theta_pad neighbors), instead of `home_bin +- azimuth_pad`. Do not rely on sin(pi) being nonzero for the theta=180 row. Add a sweep over theta in [0,5] and [175,180] for all phi to 'DirectionBounds really bounds the level' and to the spectral bounds test.
- **Expected impact.** Restores the exactness contract of the windowed path in the polar cones; the table grows only in the pole bins (evaluated fraction 93.9326% -> 93.9365% on the demo, 3.7k more of 94M); no hot-path change.


## Constant, zero-span or duplicate operating-condition axis

### db-write/A.r1.0: A constant operating-condition axis normalizes to NaN and every query silently returns one arbitrary sphere (the repo's own test fixture hits it)

- Finder's severity high, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.93, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: Rc C×8**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:1172`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1216`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1247`, `tests/test_sphere_database.cpp:990`
- **Claim.** The constructor and blend_at normalize each axis as (x - min) / (max - min). If every sphere shares one mu, one gamma, or one CT (and fixed_load_factor is not set), that is 0/0 = NaN. Delaunay is then built from NaN coordinates without error. Every later blend_at returns weights [1,0,0,0] on whatever sphere the degenerate walk ended in, whatever the query. Only the CT axis is guarded, and only via the fixed_load_factor flag with its own consistency check. Nothing checks mu or gamma. The load logs a normal 'Triangulated N operating conditions' line and the run exits 0.
- **Evidence given.** Synthetic 6-sphere database, gamma = 0 for all, mu in {0,0.1,0.2}, CT in {0.004,0.008}, dBA = 90+100*mu+1000*CT, so the correct level at (mu=0.05, CT=0.005) is 100.
shipped code: blend_at(0.05,0,0.005) -> w=[1 0 0 0] idx=[4 0 0 0] level=114.000, and the SAME 114.000 for (0.05,0.007), (0.15,0.005) and (0.15,0.007) (truth 100/102/110/112). Log: 'Triangulated 6 operating conditions in 3 dimensions (12 vertices after extending the domain, 25 simplices)'.
The same happens with mu constant (level 100 everywhere, idx=[4]) and with CT constant and no flag (level 110 everywhere, idx=[7]).
The repo's own fixture is affected: tests/test_sphere_database.cpp:990 `uniform_conditions` = {0,0,.005},{1,0,.005},{0,1,.005} has constant CT and no flag. blend_at(0.3,0.3,0.005) gives weights=[1 0 0 0] indices=[1 0 0 0], although the test comment says 'every condition weighted'. blend_at at condition 0's own coordinates (0,0,.005) also returns sphere 1. So the test 'a direction empty in one condition takes the others' level' (lines 999-1015) never blends the gated sphere 0 and is vacuous. Mutation check in a scratch copy: with is_empty() forced to return false, `niceops_unit_tests [empty]` fails only 1 of 4 cases (line 1043). With the span guard below plus the same mutation it fails 2 (lines 1012 and 1043), so the guard makes that test real.
Proposed guard tried in the scratch copy: `auto unit_span=[](double x,double lo,double hi){return hi>lo?(x-lo)/(hi-lo):0.0;};` used in the constructor's three normalization loops and in blend_at. The cases above then give 100/102/110/112 (correct), and 95/97 and 95/105 for the other two cases. The full unit suite passes (183 cases, 25,883,898 assertions) and the demo output is cmp-identical to golden/197421_LAE.csv.
- **Scenario.** A user builds a level-flight-only database (single gamma = 0, several speeds and load factors), or a hover-only CT sweep, or an older database with constant CT but no fixed_load_factor flag. The database loads cleanly, and every trajectory state is then evaluated against one sphere regardless of speed or thrust. The footprint is wrong by the spread of the database across that axis (14 dB in the synthetic case), with nothing on stderr.
- **Proposed change.** Guard the three normalizations so a zero-span axis maps to 0.0, as in the `unit_span` lambda above. Real points on the constant axis then sit at 0 and get twins at -0.1 from build()'s existing domain extension, which keeps the 3D triangulation non-degenerate, and the clamped query lands on the real layer. Alternatively, or in addition, input_error when an axis is constant and count > 1 and the flag is not set. Optionally refuse non-finite operating conditions in blend_at: blend_at(NaN,...), blend_at(...,NaN) and blend_at(...,CT=NaN) currently return sphere 1308 of S-76D_M3 with a finite 85.7 dB. Then add a unit test with a constant-gamma database whose spheres differ in level, and use non-degenerate conditions in the [empty] fixtures.
- **Expected impact.** Removes silent wrong output for an input class the project's own tests already construct. The [empty] test gains teeth. Zero cost on the hot path, since this is load time and once per state.

### delaunay-exact/A.r1.0: A zero-range or non-finite condition axis is not rejected; the triangulation is built on NaN and every query then gets one arbitrary sphere

- Finder's severity high, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: Rc C×8**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:1171`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1216`, `src/interpolator.cpp:34`, `src/interpolator.cpp:95`, `src/delaunay.cpp:15`, `src/predicates.cpp:229`, `src/predicates.cpp:105`
- **Claim.** Database::Database normalizes each axis by (max - min) with no guard. Only CT is special-cased, and only when fixed_load_factor is set. A database whose spheres share one mu, gamma or CT therefore gets NaN for that whole axis. Nothing downstream validates finiteness: ConditionInterpolator::build checks only dim==0/count==0, and the Delaunay constructor accepts any doubles. A NaN reaches the predicates as follows. filtered_sign returns 2 because fabs(NaN) > bound is false. sign_of then returns 0 because fabs(NaN) > fabs(largest) is false. So orientation() and in_sphere() answer 0 (degenerate) for every NaN-bearing simplex. The mesh is garbage, locate() never moves off its hint, and barycentric() reports 'inside' with weights (1,0,0,0) on the hint simplex's first vertex. Every trajectory state then receives the same sphere, and the run exits 0 with only an info line. A single NaN among 30 random points is enough to wreck all interpolation, not just near that point.
- **Evidence given.** (1) Zero-range axis, using the Database's exact normalization:
$ cd /Users/evg5332/.claude/jobs/8792c8f6/tmp/scratch/r1_predel && ./nanaxis
  normalized gamma of sphere 0 = nan
  [info] Triangulated 40 operating conditions in 2 dimensions (42 vertices after extending the domain, 79 simplices).
  query mu_n=0.05 -> (sphere 38, w 1.000) ...
  query mu_n=0.30 -> (sphere 38, w 1.000)
  query mu_n=0.55 -> (sphere 38, w 1.000)
  query mu_n=0.80 -> (sphere 38, w 1.000)
Every query returns sphere 38, whatever mu is.

(2) S-76D_fixed_load_factor conditions built 3-D, as when the fixed_load_factor flag is absent and CT is constant:
$ ./nanflag cond_S-76D_fixed_load_factor.txt golden/state_197421.csv
  CT range 0.00586344..0.00586344 -> normalized CT of sphere 0 = nan
  6551 trajectory states: distinct blends with CT axis NaN: 1 ; with the axis dropped (fixed_load_factor path): 15

(3) Full pipeline on a scratch copy of S-76D_fixed_load_factor.nod with every flight_path_angle set to 0 (constA.nod):
$ build/niceops -d constA.nod -S state/constA.csv -o integrated/constA.csv --gridstep 492
  exit=0; log: 'gamma = [0 , 0]' then 'Triangulated 164 operating conditions in 2 dimensions (166 vertices after extending the domain, 327 simplices).' No warning.

(4) One NaN coordinate among 30 random 3-D points:
$ ./qcheck in_nan1.txt 2000
  queries 2000: located 2000, ... bad reconstruction 2000, w=(1,0,0..) 2000

(5) Affinely dependent but finite conditions: 40 coplanar points give 'real simplices 0' from ./validate2 in_coplanar3.txt, so every query silently takes the nearest-vertex fallback.

All 20 shipped databases have non-zero mu and gamma range, and CT range or the fixed flag. Their triangulations validate, so nothing shipped hits this.
- **Scenario.** A user runs a hover-only database (mu=0 and gamma=0 at 8 thrust coefficients), a level-flight-only database (single gamma), or a single-CT database written without the fixed_load_factor byte. Load succeeds. Every state on every trajectory is rendered with the same sphere, independent of the flight condition, and the footprint is silently wrong.
- **Proposed change.** Add three guards, none of which touches the hot path.
1. In ConditionInterpolator::build, input_error() on any non-finite coordinate.
2. In Database::Database, for each used axis with max == min either drop the axis (generalizing the fixed_load_factor precedent at sphere_database.cpp:1178-1203) or input_error() naming the axis. Apply the same rule in blend_at() so the query side agrees.
3. After building the mesh, if mesh_->simplex_count() == 0 for count >= dim+1, warn or error that the conditions are affinely dependent.
Optionally count the nearest-condition fallbacks in ConditionInterpolator::barycentric (mutable atomic) and log the total once.
- **Expected impact.** Turns a silent whole-run wrong answer into a clear load-time error, or a correct lower-dimensional interpolation. No effect on the shipped databases.

### db-write/B.r1.0: A database whose spheres share one mu/gamma/CT value normalizes that axis 0/0 = NaN, and every blend then silently returns one arbitrary sphere

- Finder's severity medium, category correctness, effort M, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: Rc C×8**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:1145`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1172`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1216`, `src/interpolator.cpp:145`
- **Claim.** Only CT is special-cased (via the fixed_load_factor flag, line 1181). If all spheres share one advance ratio or one flight path angle (a level-flight-only or hover-only database), (x - min) / (max - min) is 0/0 = NaN for that axis, both in the loader (1173-1177) and in blend_at (1216-1218; clamp01 of NaN is NaN). The NaN coordinates go into the Delaunay build, which 'succeeds' with a garbage triangulation, and every query falls to the nearest-vertex scan, where 'distance < best_distance' is false for NaN, so a fixed index wins. Nothing is logged or thrown, and even queries at a stored sphere's own condition read back the wrong sphere. Separately, a query outside the hull (a database with a partial envelope) also takes the nearest sphere with no log line, so the footprint steps at the hull edge unannounced.
- **Evidence given.** Built 12-sphere databases with /Users/evg5332/.claude/jobs/8792c8f6/tmp/scratch/sphere_r1/mkdb.cpp and queried them with blend_probe.cpp (Database::blend_at + eval). 'const_gamma' (mu in {0,.1,.2,.3} x CT in {.005,.006,.007}, gamma == 0, sphere level = 100+10mu+100CT): log says 'Triangulated 12 operating conditions in 3 dimensions (22 vertices after extending the domain, 55 simplices)', then EVERY query prints 'w=[1 0 0 0] idx=[10 0 0 0] level=103.6', including q=(0,0,0.005) whose own sphere holds 100.5. 'const_mu' (hover only): every query 'idx=[7 0 0 0] level=106.6'. Exit code 0, no warning. Partial envelope ('hole', mu=0.3 corners missing), hole_probe.cpp along gamma=5.5: mu=0.20 -> 'w=[0.5 0.0833 0.417 0] level=108.05' but mu=0.24..0.30 -> 'w=[1 0 0 0] idx=[16 0 0 0] level=108.50', with no log line. 1-D triangulation works (dim1.cpp: ConditionInterpolator::build(1,4,...) gave correct weights e.g. q=0.1 -> [0.6 on x=0, 0.4 on x=0.25]), so dropping a constant axis is feasible. Shipped data is unaffected: hull_cover.cpp probed 200,000 random conditions on each of 6 shipped databases, 0 outside the hull; fallback_count.cpp on the demo's 6551 states: 0 single-sphere blends, 52 distinct blends (matches CLAUDE.md).
- **Scenario.** A user loads a panam database built for level flight only (gamma = 0) or hover only, or one with two spheres differing only in CT. The run completes with exit 0 and a footprint computed from one sphere regardless of speed and thrust. Only --self_test (not run by default) would notice.
- **Proposed change.** Generalize active_condition_dims_ / the fixed_load_factor_ special case into a per-axis mask: any axis with zero span is left out of the triangulation (packed in the loader at 1195-1203, in blend_at at 1228/1252), with Blend padded as the fixed_load_factor branch already does (weights[k]=0, indices[k]=indices[0]). With no varying axis it is the existing count < dim+1 nearest path. Add a test with a gamma-constant database that checks blend_at at each stored condition returns that sphere. Also count the queries that take the nearest fallback (mutable atomic in ConditionInterpolator) and log the total once after the trajectory loop. Cheapest stopgap: input_error when a database has >= 2 spheres and a non-CT axis has zero span.
- **Expected impact.** Removes silent wrong answers for a class of legitimate databases; no cost for shipped ones.

### db-load/B.r1.1: A constant operating-condition axis (max == min) without fixed_load_factor normalizes to NaN and silently collapses every blend to one sphere; five blend tests run on exactly such a fixture and are vacuous

- Finder's severity medium, category test-gap, effort M, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: Rc C×8**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:1162`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1215`, `tests/test_sphere_database.cpp:557`, `tests/test_sphere_database.cpp:990`
- **Claim.** The constructor divides by (max - min) for mu, gamma and (unless fixed_load_factor) CT (lines 1172-1184) and blend_at does the same (1216-1218, 1247-1248). The only guard is the converse case (flag set but CT varies, line 1162). When any axis is constant across more than one sphere and the flag is absent, the normalized coordinate is 0/0 = NaN, the Delaunay is built over NaN coordinates ('Triangulated N operating conditions in 3 dimensions' is logged as if fine), and every query falls to the nearest-vertex fallback with weight 1 on one arbitrary sphere. The test fixture write_shared_grid_test_database never writes fixed_load_factor and all its condition sets have a constant CT (0.005), so it hits exactly this; the tests that claim to blend across 'every condition' only ever read sphere 1.
- **Evidence given.** (1) 16 spheres on a mu x gamma grid, CT constant, flag unset, dBA = 90 + 20 mu + 0.5 gamma (degen2.cpp): log says 'Triangulated 16 operating conditions in 3 dimensions (28 vertices ... 73 simplices)'; blend_at(0,-6), (0.075,-4), (0.10,0), (0.12,3) all return weights [1 0 0 0] idx [14 0 0 0]; eval = 94.000 each vs expected 87.0, 89.5, 92.0, 93.9. Six spheres with only mu varying: every query returns idx 3.
(2) The tests' own fixture (`uniform_conditions`, line 990: {0,0,.005},{1,0,.005},{0,1,.005}, flag false): blend_at(0.3,0.3), (0.0,0.0), (1.0,0.0) and (0.5,0.5) ALL give idx [1 0 0 0] weights [1 0 0 0] -- it cannot even select sphere 0 at its own coordinates. Test 'a direction empty in one condition takes the others' level, not silence' (gated channel is in sphere 0) therefore never touches the gated sphere.
(3) Mutation: in a scratch copy make `is_empty` always return false (disables the condition- and corner-stage empty handling) and run `niceops_unit_tests '[empty]'`: 3 of 4 pass; only 'an empty grid corner drops out of the bilinear step' fails. The 'empty in one condition' and 'nothing empty' tests pass under the mutant.
(4) Adding `fixed_load_factor = 1` to the fixture writer (write_shared_grid_test_database: one nc_def_var + one nc_put_var_short, 4 lines) makes blending real: all 25 [empty]/[sphere_database] cases still pass (81035 assertions), and the same mutant is now caught by two tests ('empty in one condition' and the corner test).
(5) Scan of all 21 databases/*.nod via the load log: every one either has three non-degenerate axes or is flagged fixed_load_factor with constant CT, so shipped data never hits this.
- **Scenario.** Hand-built or partial databases (level-flight-only: gamma constant; hover-only: mu constant; a single-CT build from an older panam that predates the flag) load without complaint and produce a footprint from one arbitrary sphere. Separately, a regression in blend_present's condition-stage empty handling (the fix for the 10-25 dB holes documented at sphere_database.cpp:376-388) would not be caught by the suite.
- **Proposed change.** Loader: compute the set of axes with max > min; build `conditions` from those only (this generalizes the existing fixed_load_factor special case, which can then shrink to just the level-correction bookkeeping), or at minimum input_error when n > 1 and an axis is constant without the flag. Keep n == 1 working (write_sphere round trip relies on the nearest-vertex path). Tests: write fixed_load_factor=1 (or vary CT) in write_shared_grid_test_database, and add one assertion per fixture that blend_at at a stored condition returns that sphere (indices[0] == its index, weight 1).
- **Expected impact.** Turns a silent wrong-answer path into a correct blend or a loud error, and makes ~6 existing tests (the [empty] group plus the shared-grid equivalence test, which also only ever compared sphere 1) test what their comments say. Loader change is roughly LOC-neutral (removes the duplicated CT branch in both the constructor and blend_at).

### db-load/A.r1.4: A zero-range condition axis normalizes to NaN and every query silently takes one arbitrary sphere; a NaN gamma from an unclamped asin does the same

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort M, finder's confidence 0.85, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: Rc C×8**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:1170`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1205`, `src/interpolator.cpp:112`, `src/trajectory.cpp:434`
- **Claim.** The constructor normalizes each condition axis as (x - min)/(max - min) and blend_at does the same for the query. Only thrust_coefficient has a zero-range guard (fixed_load_factor); if mu or gamma (or CT without the flag) is constant across the file the divisor is 0 and every normalized coordinate is NaN. std::clamp(NaN, 0, 1) returns NaN, the Delaunay build/locate gets NaN, barycentric() fails and falls back to 'nearest', which with NaN distances is an arbitrary fixed sphere. The load succeeds. The same silent fallback catches any NaN query, and one source is real: trajectory.cpp computes `fpa_e = 180/pi * std::asin(vn_hat)` with no clamp, and vn_hat = (u.n)/|u| exceeds 1 by round-off when the velocity is parallel to the normalized TPP normal (acosc exists in this file for exactly this reason).
- **Evidence given.** r1_db/constaxis.cpp (writer: 6 spheres, dBA 100 - 2*i, mu = 0.05*i):
- gamma constant (fixed_load_factor, 2-D): mu = 0, 0.05, ... 0.25 all return 'weights 1.000 0.000 0.000 0.000 idx 4 0 0 4  level(R)=92.000' while the stored levels run 100 ... 90. Same with a 3-D file (idx 10, 90.000). mu constant: idx 3, 94.000. Exit 0, no message beyond the 'gamma = [0 , 0]' info line.
r1_db/nanref.cpp on S-76D_M3.nod at mu=0.15, CT=0.006, eval(10R, 10, 60): gamma=-4 -> 67.751 dB, gamma=12 -> 65.440 dB, gamma=NaN -> 69.833 dB (sphere 143 with weight 1), i.e. a 2-4 dB error with no diagnostic; NaN mu and NaN CT likewise return a fixed sphere.
r1_db/asinnan.cpp: derive_flight_parameters on 200,000 states with the load-factor vector parallel to the velocity gives fpa_e = NaN for 33,227 of them (e.g. v=(-0.3956343641345928, 1.0915179133283306, 14.819403738101313), n=(-0.026615495570149524, 0.073429643176915582, 0.99694518550342892)).
No shipped track leaves the S-76D_M3 envelope (r1_db/envelope.cpp over all 30 S-76D tracks: zero states outside mu/gamma/CT ranges), so nothing on the demo path is affected.
- **Scenario.** A level-flight-only or single-speed database (gamma or mu constant, a natural request), or a track with a vertical segment whose thrust vector is parallel to the velocity: the run completes with a plausible-looking but wrong sphere selection.
- **Proposed change.** At load, if an axis has max == min, either input_error naming the axis or drop that axis from the triangulation the way fixed_load_factor drops CT (and treat the query coordinate as 0 for it). In blend_at, reject a non-finite condition with input_error rather than letting clamp/nearest absorb it. In derive_path_angles clamp vn_hat to [-1, 1] before asin, mirroring acosc.
- **Expected impact.** Prevents silent wrong-sphere selection on degenerate databases and on NaN flight conditions; no change for the shipped databases or tracks.
- **Conflicts with docs.** None; extends the existing fixed_load_factor treatment of a zero-range CT axis to the other two axes.

### db-eval/A.r1.2: A database whose flight-path-angle, advance-ratio or CT axis is constant silently returns the wrong sphere for every query

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: Rc C×8**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:1171`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1216`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1247`, `src/interpolator.cpp:114`
- **Claim.** The constructor and blend_at() normalize each operating-condition axis as `(x - min) / (max - min)`. Only CT on a fixed_load_factor database is protected against max == min. If mu, gamma, or CT without the flag is the same on every sphere, every stored coordinate and every query coordinate is 0/0 = NaN (std::clamp passes NaN through). The triangulation is built over NaN points, barycentric() then fails, and the nearest-vertex fallback picks an arbitrary sphere with weight 1. No error, no warning, exit code 0.
- **Evidence given.** Synthetic 4x4-grid databases written with netCDF (mkdb.cpp); `Database::blend_at(mu, 0, 0.006)` then `eval(30.48, 0, 90, blend)`. Two spheres, mu=0 (90 dB) and mu=0.2 (96 dB), same gamma and CT: mu=0.0, 0.05, 0.1, 0.15, 0.2, 0.3 all give w=(1,0,0,0) idx=0, level 90.000 (the mu=0.2 query should return 96). Five spheres, gamma constant (a level-flight-only database), mu 0..0.3 at 90..96 dB: every query, including mu=0 and mu=0.15, returns idx 3 (the mu=0.3, 96 dB sphere) at 96.000 dB. Same for a constant-mu database (idx 3 always). The log only says 'Triangulated 5 operating conditions in 3 dimensions'. Existing handling is limited to `if (fixed_load_factor_ && !approx_equal(thrust_coefficient_min, thrust_coefficient_max)) input_error(...)` (l.1162).
- **Scenario.** A hand-built or reduced database: a level-flight-only set (gamma const), a hover-plus-cruise pair at one gamma and CT, or a non-flagged database whose spheres share one CT. A single exported sphere works only by accident: all three axes are NaN, mesh_ is null and nearest() with NaN distances returns index 0.
- **Proposed change.** Detect (max - min) below a tolerance per axis in the constructor. Either drop that axis from the triangulation, generalizing the existing active_condition_dims_ handling from a trailing CT to any set of axes (Blend already pads with weight 0), or fail with input_error naming the axis. Normalize the query the same way, mapping a constant axis to 0. Add a test with a two-sphere, mu-only database asserting the mu=0.2 query returns the second sphere.
- **Expected impact.** Removes a silent multi-dB wrong-sphere failure for reduced databases. No shipped database has a constant axis.

### delaunay-exact/B.r1.1: ConditionInterpolator::build validates nothing: duplicate conditions abort with a bare fatal, and a constant axis yields NaN points that silently select one arbitrary sphere

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.7, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: Rc C×8**
- Locations: `src/interpolator.cpp:31`, `src/delaunay.cpp:160`, `src/delaunay.cpp:110`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1172`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1216`
- **Claim.** Two failure modes on malformed or unusual condition sets. (1) Two spheres with identical (mu, gamma, CT): the second insertion creates zero-volume simplices, a later insertion's visibility walk cannot terminate, and `fatal("Delaunay: could not locate a simplex for point N")` aborts, leaving the reason only in log/niceops.log. Every other malformed-database path in Database uses input_error (throws, reported per trajectory). (2) A database whose spheres share one value on an axis (e.g. level-flight-only, constant gamma): sphere_database.cpp:1173 divides 0/0 giving NaN for every sphere's coordinate; the triangulation is then built over NaN points and every query returns the same sphere with weight 1, ignoring the axes that do vary.
- **Evidence given.** (1) Real S-76D_M3 conditions with a copy of row 500 inserted next to it (cond_dup2.txt, 1313 rows): `[fatal] Delaunay: could not locate a simplex for point 811.`, process aborts (exit 134). Random 200-point sets with point k+1 == point k: 13 of 13 runs (k = 3, 10, 30, 50, 120, 190, seeds 2-9) abort the same way. The same duplicate appended as the LAST row does not abort but silently leaves degenerate simplices (10052 vs 10049). (2) `ConditionInterpolator::build(3, 15, pts)` with the gamma column NaN: queries (0.3,0.5,0.5), (0.1,0.1,0.1), (0.9,0.5,0.2), (0,1,1) all give `weights 1 0 0 0 idx 13 0 0 0`. Cause chain: NaN orientations are 0 (filtered_sign returns 2 on NaN, sign_of returns 0), so locate stops at the hint; delaunay.cpp:403-412 then puts all weight on the first vertex, and interpolator.cpp:130 (`raw[v] >= 1.0`) takes that sphere whole. Command: build the repo's interpolator.cpp/delaunay.cpp/predicates.cpp into a test program with clang++ -O3 -DNDEBUG (scratch dir exp/small.cpp, nan.cpp, dup2.cpp under /Users/evg5332/.claude/jobs/8792c8f6/tmp/scratch/delaunay). All 21 shipped databases have distinct conditions and varying axes, so neither path is hit today.
- **Scenario.** A database built by panam with a repeated condition (merged runs, a re-exported sphere), or a small custom database with one flight-path angle or one advance ratio. The first kills niceops with nothing on the console; the second produces a footprint from a single sphere with no error or warning.
- **Proposed change.** In ConditionInterpolator::build, before triangulating: reject any non-finite coordinate with input_error naming the axis, and detect exact duplicate rows (sort an index vector lexicographically, O(N log N)) and raise input_error naming the two sphere indices. Independently, Database should treat max==min on an axis as a constant axis (drop it from the triangulation the way fixed_load_factor drops CT) or refuse it, instead of dividing 0/0. Add two Catch2 cases (duplicate row, NaN column) using REQUIRE_THROWS.
- **Expected impact.** Turns a silent abort and a silent wrong-sphere result into diagnosable input errors; no cost on valid input (one sort of ~2000 rows, well under 1 ms).

### delaunay-exact/A.r1.1: A duplicated operating condition leaves permanent zero-volume simplices: load aborts with a cryptic message, or every query fails through a 95k-step walk

- Finder's severity medium, category correctness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: C×8**
- Locations: `src/delaunay.cpp:160`, `src/delaunay.cpp:259`, `src/delaunay.cpp:115`, `src/delaunay.cpp:335`, `src/predicates.cpp:335`, `src/interpolator.cpp:31`
- **Claim.** Neither ConditionInterpolator::build nor Delaunay::insert handles a point that coincides with an existing vertex. locate() returns a simplex that has the duplicate as a vertex. The cavity is only that simplex, because neighbors that contain the duplicate give in_sphere == 0. Each boundary facet that contains the duplicate then becomes a new simplex with two identical vertices, i.e. a flat one. Its orientation is 0, so it is neither swapped nor rejected (delaunay.cpp:266). in_sphere() returns 0 for any simplex with orientation 0 (predicates.cpp:336-337), so a flat simplex can never join a later cavity and is permanent. Later visibility walks get trapped in these flat simplices. Depending on insertion order the outcome is either (a) fatal 'could not locate a simplex for point N' during construction, or (b) locate() burning its full 8*S+64 step limit on every query. That -1 is indistinguishable from 'outside the hull', so barycentric() returns false and the caller silently uses the nearest sphere. hint_ is only updated on success (delaunay.cpp:153), so every later query repeats the failing walk.
- **Evidence given.** Real S-76D_M3 conditions with one condition duplicated (dupwalk = Delaunay on the normalized, extended points):
  dup of cond 100 inserted last : 6 flat simplices; 100/100 queries locate()==-1, barycentric==false; up to 64 ms per query
  dup of cond 1200 inserted last: 6 flat; 100/100 fail, 46 ms per query
  dup of cond 5 inserted last   : 3 flat; 3/100 fail
  dup of cond 100 at position 50/600/1000, cond 700 at 300/1100, cond 1200 at 200, cond 5 at 10: all exit 134, 'Delaunay: could not locate a simplex for point 161' etc.
End to end, on scratch copies of S-76D_M3.nod with one group's three condition scalars overwritten by another's (rewrite2):
  dupMid.nod (group 50 := group 100): niceops exits 134; the reason is only in log/dm.log ('[fatal] Delaunay: could not locate a simplex for point 160.'), nothing on stdout/stderr.
  dupLast.nod (group 1311 := group 100): exit 0, 34.7 s CPU against 0.97 s for the unmodified database, and the footprint differs from baseline by up to 2.20 dB (mean 0.68 dB, 405 of 441 cells > 0.1 dB). The log says only 'Triangulated 1312 operating conditions ... 10050 simplices' (baseline 10049).
Small cases: 30 random 3-D points plus one duplicate lose 339 of 7969 in-hull queries (7630 located against 7969) and 3 of 31 vertex queries. Prism input loses 165 in-hull queries.
Fix check: a scratch delaunay.cpp that returns from insert() when the located simplex has a vertex equal to the query gives 0 flat simplices and 0 failed queries for all six real-database cases above. The small random and prism cases return to their no-duplicate counts (7969 and 13379 located).
- **Scenario.** A .nod file containing two spheres with identical (mu, gamma, CT), for example from a re-run or a merged database. The user sees either an abort with no console output, or a run that is about 36x slower and quietly interpolates by nearest sphere.
- **Proposed change.** 1. In ConditionInterpolator::build, detect exact duplicate normalized points (sort or hash) and input_error() naming the two sphere indices. Two spheres at one condition are ambiguous unless identical.
2. Defence in depth in Delaunay::insert: after locate(), if the query equals a vertex of the located simplex, skip the point. This is the four-line patch verified above.
3. Make locate() report 'step limit exceeded' distinctly from 'outside the hull'. Log it, and reset hint_ to a valid real simplex on failure so one bad walk does not repeat 6551 times.
- **Expected impact.** Removes an abort with no console message and a 36x slowdown on malformed input. Unchanged bits on databases without duplicates.


## Grid fill: unreached cells read as 0 dBA, dropped channels silent

### db-load/A.r1.2: Grid fill has no coverage or plausibility check: unreached cells are silently 0 dBA, unmatched channels are dropped, and the 1% approx_equal window smears fine grids

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.85, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: C×4**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:225`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:946`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1086`
- **Claim.** Axes come from sphere 0 only (line 950) and the grid is zero-initialized (line 956: Sample {0.0, 0.0}). Every channel of another sphere that is not approx_equal (1% relative or 1e-5 absolute) to a sphere-0 knot is dropped with `continue`, and any cell no channel reaches keeps dBA = 0, EAA = 0, which is a legitimate-looking level to the blends (the 'empty' handling only recognizes <= -999). Nothing counts or logs either case. Independently, approx_equal_range accepts every knot within 1% of the channel angle, so on an axis whose spacing is under about 1% of the angle the accepted range spans several knots, and the 'later channel wins' rule shifts data by a grid step. There is also no sanity check on the data: the loader accepts exact (dBA, EAA) = (0, 0) pairs and levels of hundreds of dB.
- **Evidence given.** All in /Users/evg5332/.claude/jobs/8792c8f6/tmp/scratch/r1_db (mkdb.hpp writer modeled on the test fixtures).
(a) offgrid.cpp: four spheres, sphere 1's phi labels shifted +2 deg. Database() returns normally with no message; sphere 1: '16 of 16 cells left at 0 dBA / 0 EAA'.
(b) finegrid.cpp: theta axis 0..180 in 1-degree steps, dBA = 60 + theta/3 on every phi. '81 of 181 cells differ from the file's channel at that angle, worst 0.333 dB' (theta=99: stored 93.3333, file 93.0000): from theta 99 up every cell holds its upper neighbor's value, because 1% of the angle exceeds the 1-degree spacing. Shipped grids (phi step 10, theta step 5) are safe by a factor of 2.8 at the worst angle, so nothing warns when a finer database is built.
(c) Data already in the tree: databases/B407_M3.nod (untracked, version 1) has 934 channels with dBA == 0 and EAA == 0 exactly, in 12 of 1296 conditions (gamma 16.9-23.7 deg, mu 0.113-0.156), e.g. condition 496 at phi 80/90/100, theta 55-130. eval() on the blend at that condition gives, at range == R, theta=60: 89.98 / 89.48 / 44.74 / 0.00 / 0.00 / 0.00 dB for phi 60/70/75/80/85/90 (r1_db/zeros.cpp): a 90 dB hole to the side of a steep descent. (gridscan.cpp over all 22 shipped .nod files finds 0 unfilled cells elsewhere.)
(d) databases/S-76D_M4.nod and databases/S-76D_fixed_load_factor.nod (both tracked) carry 14 cells per condition (condition 62 in the latter; 736-743 in M4) at phi=-120, theta 150-175 with dBA 252.8, 357.2, 493.9, ... 1463.5 and EAA up to 283.8 (r1_db/hot.cpp). These are in the folded upper half (phi is looked up as atan(across/|up|), so |phi| <= 90 and they are unreachable by eval), but they set max_level_ (used for find_range's search bracket at sphere_database.cpp:2157, giving ~1e71 m) and sphere_max_ (cull_range and the direction bounds), and would be written out by --export_sphere.
- **Scenario.** Any database built with a different phi/theta layout on some spheres, a finer grid, a builder that writes 0 for unmeasured microphones (B407_M3 today), or a corrupt fit (S-76D_M4): the tool loads without a word and produces footprints with 0 dB holes, one-step-shifted directivity, or unusable bound/cull ranges.
- **Proposed change.** (1) Initialize grid cells to {no_energy_floor_db, 0.0} instead of {0, 0}, so a never-written cell is 'not measured' and drops out of blends through the existing empty logic (bit-identical for every shipped file, none has an unfilled cell). (2) After the fill, count unfilled cells per sphere and log a warning (or input_error above a threshold). (3) When building each axis, input_error if two adjacent knots are approx_equal to each other (the acceptance test is then unambiguous). (4) Log a warning with counts for channels with dBA == 0 && EAA == 0 and for dBA above about 200 dB. Do not change approx_equal itself (port contract).
- **Expected impact.** Turns three silent wrong-answer modes into diagnostics or into the existing 'not measured' semantics; identifies B407_M3.nod and S-76D_M4.nod as needing a panam rebuild.
- **Conflicts with docs.** CLAUDE.md says approx_equal mirrors D's approxEqual and that tightening it drops channels; this proposal leaves it alone and only validates around it. Per the memory note the zeros in B407_M3 are panam's data, so item (4) is a diagnostic, not a reinterpretation.

### db-eval/A.r1.3: Grid cells no channel writes to stay at {dBA 0, EAA 0} and are interpolated as a real 0 dB measurement

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.85, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: C×4**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:956`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1086`
- **Claim.** The level grid is initialized to `Sample {0.0, 0.0}` and filled by matching channels to cells with approx_equal_range. Nothing checks that every (phi, theta) cell of every sphere received a channel. An unfilled cell is 0 dB, which is above the empty floor (-999), so blend_conditions()/eval() treat it as a genuine measured level instead of dropping it the way a gated -inf direction is dropped. The same applies to spectra_ (assigned 0.0 at l.977/990). The failure cases are a channel set that is not a full phi x theta product, a later sphere whose channel angles do not match sphere 0's axes (channels are silently `continue`d, l.1089-1093), or a sphere with hole rows. The loader logs nothing.
- **Evidence given.** Synthetic single-sphere database, uniform 90 dBA, 4x4 grid (phi -90,-30,30,90; theta 0,60,120,180) with the (phi=30, theta=60) channel omitted (mkdb.cpp 'one_missing.nod', 15 channels). `eval(30.48, phi, theta, blend)` (r = R so spreading and EAA are 0) at phi = 0/30/45/60 and theta 30/60/90: 67.5, 45.0, 67.5 / 45.0, 0.000, 45.0 / 56.25, 22.5, 56.25 / 67.5, 45.0, 67.5 (the truth is 90 everywhere; the complete database returns 90.000 at all of them). Log lines from the load are only `[info] First sphere contains 15 channels.` etc.; no warning.
- **Scenario.** Any incomplete or hand-edited .nod, or a database with heterogeneous per-sphere angle layouts, which the loader is otherwise careful to read per sphere (l.914-924). The footprint acquires 30-90 dB holes with exit code 0. The CLAUDE.md history lists this same symptom ('most grid cells zero') as the signature of an earlier porting bug.
- **Proposed change.** Count, per sphere, the cells written by the fill loop. After each sphere, either input_error if any cell is unfilled (with the count and first missing angle pair), or initialize the grid to `{no_energy_floor_db, 0.0}` so that blend_present() drops the holes, and log a warning with the count. Initialize spectra_ to no_energy_floor_db the same way.
- **Expected impact.** Turns silent garbage from incomplete databases into either a clear error or correct 'not measured' handling. No effect on complete databases.

### db-write/A.r1.2: Channels that match no grid cell are dropped silently and unfilled grid slots read as 0 dBA 'measurements' (self_test only sees it if run)

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.85, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: C×4**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:956`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1086`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:977`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:990`
- **Claim.** The grid axes come from sphere 0 only, and grid is initialized to `Sample {0.0, 0.0}`. In the fill loop a channel whose phi or theta matches no axis value hits `continue` with no log. Any slot never assigned therefore stays at dBA = 0.0, which is_empty() does not recognize (it only treats <= -999 as empty). It is blended as a real 0 dB level. The spectra_ buffer is likewise zero-initialized (lines 977, 990). The code comments explicitly anticipate heterogeneous layouts ('mixed microphone layouts'), and a sphere whose labels differ from sphere 0's loses its whole slice without a message.
- **Evidence given.** (a) 8 spheres on a 4x4 grid at a uniform 100 dBA, but sphere 3's phi labels are shifted by +7 degrees: `sphere 3 ... eval at radius, phi=-30 theta=60 -> 0.000 dBA` (the file says 100), while spheres 0-2 and 4-7 give 100.000. The log has no warning, and the neighbors of sphere 3 are pulled toward 0. `self_test` does flag it ('Self test failed on 16 of 128 samples') but only when --self_test is passed.
(b) One channel absent from all groups (non-product layout): levels 50.0, 75.0 and 66.7 dBA next to the hole where the truth is 100, and 0.000 at the hole. self_test PASSES, because it only queries channels that exist.
(c) Instrumented scratch build over all 21 local databases: 0 unfilled grid slots and 0 channels matching no cell in every one. Validation is therefore free on the current data.
(d) Real-data precedent: databases/B407_M3.nod (untracked) has 934 cells of exactly 0.0 dBA in 12 spheres, in the gamma 17-24 degree steep-climb spheres at theta 50-135. These are 0.0 written by the builder as a fill, and the loader blends them as 0 dB.
- **Scenario.** A database whose spheres do not share one channel layout (the case shared_grid_and_frequency exists to distinguish), or with a missing channel. It loads without comment and produces footprints with the affected conditions and directions tens of dB low.
- **Proposed change.** Count unmatched channels and unfilled slots during the load. input_error if any (or at least a loud warn with counts). Optionally initialize unfilled slots to {no_energy_floor_db, 0.0} so the existing empty logic drops them instead of treating them as 0 dB. Optionally warn on exact-0.0 dBA cells, which are a builder fill, not a level.
- **Expected impact.** Turns silent data loss on load into a clear error. Bit-identical on all 21 local databases.

### db-load/B.r1.2: Grid cells no channel reaches are initialized to a measured 0 dBA, and channels/cells that miss are dropped without a word; the sentinel-zero data in B407_M3.nod loads the same way

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.8, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: C×4**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:956`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1086`
- **Claim.** `grid.assign(..., Sample {0.0, 0.0})` (line 956) is the initial value of every cell, and the fill loop `continue`s silently for any channel whose phi or theta matches no axis entry (1089-1093). A cell that no channel of a sphere lands in therefore stays at dBA = 0, EAA = 0, which is above empty_level_db, so blend_conditions/eval treat it as a real, very quiet measurement and interpolate toward it -- the opposite of the 'not measured drops out' rule the same file goes to lengths to implement for gated (-inf) cells (lines 366-419). No count of unreached cells or dropped channels is logged.
