The CLAUDE.md comment about later channels winning a shared cell and dba_max covering matched channels only documents the fill, but not this consequence.
- **Evidence given.** Synthetic (hole.cpp): 3 spheres, 4x4 grid, dBA = 100 everywhere, channel 5 (phi -30, theta 60) omitted from every group. eval at range = radius (no spreading): (phi -30, theta 60) -> 0.00 dB; (-45, 40) -> 50.00 dB; (-60, 90) -> 75.00 dB; (60,150) -> 100.00 dB. interpolate_sphere shows cell(1,1) = 0.00 dBA. Log contains no warning.
Real data: after load, counted grid cells equal to exactly {0,0} in every databases/*.nod: 0 for all 20 tracked files; databases/B407_M3.nod (untracked, dated Sep 17) has 934 such cells in 12 of 1296 spheres. Raw file check (rawhole.cpp): sphere 840 has 1332 distinct (phi,theta) channels, none missing, but 170 of them are stored as dBA == 0 and EAA == 0 (all other channels 79.7-107.5 dB), e.g. phi -90, theta 50..65; sphere 480: 86 such channels. So that file carries sentinel zeros (unmeasured directions written as 0) which the loader passes through as data. The newer B407_ambient_gated.nod has none.
- **Scenario.** A database with a missing channel (a builder that drops a failed direction), a channel whose angle differs from sphere 0's axis by more than approx_equal tolerance (axes come from sphere 0 only, line 950), or sentinel zeros like B407_M3 produces footprint dips of tens of dB in the affected directions, with nothing in the log to explain them -- the same symptom class that motivated blend_present ('narrow-strip footprints and 10-25 dB holes').
- **Proposed change.** Initialize the grid to {no_energy_floor_db, 0.0} so an unreached cell is 'not measured' and drops out through the existing blend_present machinery; count unreached cells and unmatched channels per load and `warn` once with the totals (and the first offending sphere); optionally warn on channels with dBA == 0 && EAA == 0 as a probable sentinel. Tracked databases are unaffected (0 unreached cells in all 20).
- **Expected impact.** Correctness on malformed inputs; no cost on the hot path (initial value only). Bitwise identical for all tracked .nod files.


## netCDF reads take the storage type on faith

### db-load/B.r1.0: netCDF scalar/array readers take the file's storage type on faith: a float32 .nod loads as garbage and the CLI exits 0 with an all-NaN footprint

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: C×2**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:83`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:172`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:148`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:862`
- **Claim.** get_scalar<T> calls `nc_get_var(ncid, varid, value)` (line 94) and get_array<T> calls `nc_get_vara(ncid, varid, &startp, &countp, values.data())` (line 185). Those read the variable in its EXTERNAL type with no conversion, and nc_get_var reads the WHOLE variable, so (a) a phi/theta/dBA/EAA/radius/advance_ratio stored as NC_FLOAT is copied byte-for-byte into a double buffer, and (b) a 'scalar' whose dimension is longer than 1 (radii, condition) overruns the single stack double it is read into. Every instantiation in the tree uses T = double, so the template buys nothing. The file already knows the hazard: get_int_scalar (line 148) exists because same_grid is short in old files and byte in new ones, and the `amplitude` read (line 1012) uses nc_get_var_double. Nothing validates the result, so a wrong-typed file loads 'successfully'.
- **Evidence given.** Wrote a 3-sphere fixed_load_factor .nod with the netCDF C API twice, identical except NC_DOUBLE vs NC_FLOAT variables, and loaded each with Database:
  NC_DOUBLE: 'Sphere radius set to 30.48', 4 unique phi, eval(range=radius,phi=0,theta=90)=100.000000
  NC_FLOAT : '5 unique phi values', 'Max level found in database is: 2.81475e+14', 'Sphere radius set to 0', eval=nan
End to end: `niceops -d /tmp/r1_f32.nod -t tracks/S-76D/197421AC.csv -v databases/S-76D.json --gridstep 492 -o integrated/f32.csv` -> exit=0, every level in integrated/f32.csv is `nan` (21 of 21 columns of row 5); the f64 twin gives 99.31,99.57,99.86,... The log's only hint is 'Sphere radius set to 0' at info level.
Overrun check (wide.cpp): variable 'radius' with a length-3 dimension {30.48,111,222}: `nc_get_var(f,v,&s.radius)` returns status 0 and overwrites s.neighbor1=111 and s.neighbor2=222; `nc_get_var1_double(f,v,&idx0,&one)` returns 30.48 and touches nothing else.
Cost of the fix is nil: fresh-process timing of 5 scalar reads x 1312 groups of S-76D_M3.nod (CPU s, two runs each): nc_get_var 0.121/0.121, nc_get_var_double 0.125/0.133, nc_get_vara_double 0.114/0.126, nc_get_var1_double 0.126/0.123; arrays: nc_get_vara 0.117/0.121 vs nc_get_vara_double 0.111/0.109. The time is the lazy first-touch H5Dopen (~18-21 us per variable), not the call flavour.
- **Scenario.** Any builder change that stores angles or levels as float32 to shrink the 30-65 MB files (netCDF4-python 'f4' is the obvious spelling), or a database whose radius/rotor_scale sit on a dimension longer than 1. The tool runs to completion and writes NaN or wildly wrong levels; the assert(isfinite) guards that would notice are compiled out under the shipped NDEBUG Release.
- **Proposed change.** Make the four helpers non-template and convert on read: scalars via `nc_get_var1_double(ncid, varid, &index0, value)` (index 0; it also ignores the index for a true 0-D variable), arrays via `nc_get_vara_double`. Optionally also `nc_inq_varlen`/`nc_inq_vartype` once for group 0 and input_error on a scalar variable with length != 1. Reads of the shipped double-typed files are unchanged, so the change is a pure widening of what loads correctly.
- **Expected impact.** Removes a silent-corruption path at zero measured cost; -6 LOC (templates collapse). Bitwise identical for all 21 shipped .nod files; changes results only for non-double or non-scalar files, from garbage to correct.

### db-load/A.r1.3: netCDF scalar and array reads are untyped and unchecked: float/int variables load as garbage, a longer 'scalar' smashes the stack, an oversized amplitude overflows the heap

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: C×2**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:88`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:172`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1009`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:862`
- **Claim.** get_scalar<T> calls nc_get_var(ncid, varid, value) and get_array<T> calls nc_get_vara(..., values.data()). Both are the untyped void* calls, which write the variable's on-disk type and size into the caller's memory with no conversion and no shape check (get_int_scalar's own comment says as much for ints, but the double reads were not given the same treatment). So a non-double variable fills only part of the double, and a variable with more than one element overruns the local. The amplitude read is sized from the channel-derived axes (`phis.size() * thetas.size() * n_bands`), not from the variable's dimensions, so a larger amplitude overruns the heap.
- **Evidence given.** Scratch files written with r1_db/mkdb.hpp (netCDF C API), loaded through Database(); r1_db/types.cpp:
- doubles (control): CT range [0.005, 0.006], levels sane.
- mu/gamma/CT/radius/rotor_scale stored as NC_FLOAT: exit 0, 'mu = [2.122e-314, 2.6384e-314]', R = 0, every eval NaN. NC_INT: same.
- phi/theta/dBA/EAA stored as NC_FLOAT: exit 0, phi axis '-2.19902e+13 -4.29497e+09 0 4.29497e+09 2.19902e+13', theta axis '0 1.09951e+12 5.6295e+15', every level 0.000.
- radius(radii) with radii = 2: exit 0, the second double lands in a neighboring stack local and the CT range prints as 'CT = [30.48, 30.48]', so the levels no longer vary with mu (91.95 for mu 0, 0.1, 0.2 against 99.95/95.95/91.95 in the control).
- r1_db/ampshape.cpp (spectral file whose amplitude has 2 extra PHI rows) compiled with sphere_database.cpp under -fsanitize=address: 'heap-buffer-overflow ... WRITE of size 384 ... 0 bytes after 256-byte region ... #13 NC4_get_vars ... #15 niceops::Database::Database sphere_database.cpp:1013'.
nc_get_var_double/nc_get_vara_double convert on read, so the existing get_int_scalar approach is available for doubles at no cost.
- **Scenario.** A builder change that stores float32 (a natural way to halve a 60 MB .nod), a database with several radii, or an amplitude array laid out with the pre-2026-09-25 38-row completion order against a 36-value axis: the tool either produces nonsense levels with exit status 0, or corrupts memory before any diagnostic. panam is a separate repo that writes these files.
- **Proposed change.** Replace nc_get_var/nc_get_vara with nc_get_var_double/nc_get_vara_double (or nc_get_var1_double for scalars), drop the template, and before each read check the variable's element count via nc_inq_vardimid + nc_inq_dimlen against what the caller sized (1 for scalars, `countp` for arrays, n_phi*n_theta*n_bands for amplitude), calling input_error on mismatch. For NC_DOUBLE files the values read are bit-identical.
- **Expected impact.** Files that would silently load garbage or corrupt memory are read correctly (float) or refused with a message (shape); no change for the shipped double files.
- **Conflicts with docs.** None.


## Sphere nose at zero or vertical velocity (partly fixed by 9fc714a)

### main-setup/A.r1.0: sphere_frame returns a zero nose for zero velocity or vertical flight, so every direction looks up the same sphere point

- Finder's severity high, category correctness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.95, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: partly fixed C×4**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:678`, `src/niceops.cpp:756`, `tests/test_trajectory.cpp:41`
- **Claim.** sphere_frame() builds the nose by projecting the normalized velocity onto the rotor plane. When the velocity is exactly zero, or exactly parallel to the thrust normal (pure vertical climb or descent with nx=ny=0), the projection is the zero vector. Vec3::normalized() leaves a zero vector alone, so nose and starboard both come out (0,0,0). lookup_angles then returns phi=0, theta=90 for every propagation direction, and every observer at every azimuth and elevation gets the directivity of one sphere point. Nothing detects or logs this.
- **Evidence given.** Standalone program linked against build/libniceops_core.a: sphere_frame(Vec3(0,0,0), Vec3(0,0,1)) -> nose=(0,0,0) starboard=(0,0,0). lookup_angles then gives phi 0.000, theta 90.000 for all 12 directions tried (el -80/-45/-10 deg x az 0/90/180/270). With v=(1e-6,0,0) the same directions give phi from -80 to 80 and theta from 10 to 170. sphere_frame(Vec3(0,0,-2.5), Vec3(0,0,1)) also gives nose=(0,0,0). End to end, I took the demo track and set the vx, vy, vz columns to 0 (hov0.csv) or to 1e-6 ft/s (hov_x.csv, hov_y.csv, hov_z.csv), then ran `niceops -t <track> --gridstep 984 -L warn`. The v=0 footprint differs from the v=1e-6 one by up to 2.47 dB (mean -0.81 dB). The database's hover spheres are not azimuthally symmetric: nose +x versus nose +y differ by up to 3.23 dB, so the nose reference matters and a degenerate one cannot be right. The existing test 'Hover states do not produce NaN flight path angles' says hover is 'meant to be reachable' but only checks fpa, mu and CT, never the sphere frame. None of the 84 shipped tracks has a zero-velocity row (checked by script), so the goldens never see it.
- **Scenario.** A user builds a hover or vertical take-off/landing AC track by writing zero velocities (the only way to get exact hover), or uses --speed_reference air where the aircraft holds station in the wind. Every hover state gets a direction-independent directivity, and the footprint is wrong by a few dB. There is no error or warning, and the output is finite.
- **Proposed change.** In sphere_frame, when the projected nose has negligible length (|v - (v.n)n| below a small threshold), fall back to a defined nose. Options are the previous state's nose (carry forward in the per-state loop), the horizontal heading, or a fixed axis projected onto the TPP. Log once with the count of degenerate states. Add a unit test that sphere_frame with zero velocity, and with velocity along the normal, returns an orthonormal frame and direction-dependent lookup_angles.
- **Expected impact.** Removes a silent error of about 2-3 dB for hover and vertical-flight states. No effect on any shipped track.

### db-load/A.r1.0: sphere_frame gives a zero nose for exact hover or velocity parallel to the TPP normal; every observer is then looked up at (phi=0, theta=90)

- Finder's severity medium, category correctness, effort M, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: partly fixed C×4**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:678`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:708`, `src/vec3.hpp:87`, `tests/test_trajectory.cpp:41`
- **Claim.** sphere_frame() builds the nose as normalize(v_hat - (v_hat.n) n). When the air velocity is exactly zero, or exactly parallel to the TPP normal, that projection is the zero vector; Vec3::normalized() returns a zero vector unchanged, so nose and starboard are both (0,0,0). lookup_angles/level_from_geometry then see along = 0 and across = 0 for every observer, so theta = acos(0) = 90 deg and phi = atan(0/|up|) = 0 deg regardless of where the observer is. The sphere lookup stops depending on direction at all (it is the straight-down cell), while the same state with a 1e-9 m/s velocity is looked up correctly.
- **Evidence given.** Scratch programs (linked against build/libniceops_core.a): /Users/evg5332/.claude/jobs/8792c8f6/tmp/scratch/r1_db/hover.cpp and vertical.cpp.
Code: `frame.nose = v - v.dot(frame.normal) * frame.normal; frame.nose.normalize();` (sphere_database.cpp:684-685), and phi_degrees: `const double phi = std::atan(across / std::fabs(up));` (713).
hover.cpp: S-76D_M3.nod, source at 150 m, n=(0,0,1), mu=0, gamma=0, CT=0.006, observers on the ground along +x. Level in dB for v=0 | v=(1e-9,0,0) | v=(0,1e-9,0):
  x=50    71.332 | 72.592 | 72.530   (sphere lookup (0,90) vs (0,71.6))
  x=150   68.569 | 72.059 | 72.003
  x=300   64.111 | 70.372 | 67.955   (26.6 deg elevation: 6.3 dB low)
  x=1200  49.576 | 49.091 | 52.701
  x=2400  38.961 | 40.065 | 41.272
vertical.cpp: v=(0,0,-2) with n=(0,0,1) prints nose=(0,0,0) starboard=(0,0,0) and lookup_angles returns phi=0.00 theta=90.00 for directions (0.71,0,-0.71), (0,0.71,-0.71) and (-0.98,0,-0.20); with v=(1e-6,0,-2) the same directions give theta 45, 90 and 168.7. So the answer is discontinuous at zero, and the last row above shows the hover sphere depends on nose azimuth by 2.4-3.6 dB, so a nose taken from velocity noise near zero is arbitrary too.
tests/test_trajectory.cpp:41-48 says the hover regime 'is meant to be reachable' but pins only that mu/fpa/CT are finite; no test builds a frame from zero or normal-parallel velocity (grep of tests/ for sphere_frame/lookup_angles: only non-degenerate cases).
No shipped track exercises it: an awk pass over all 84 tracks/*/*.csv finds no row with |v| < 1 m/s, which is why the goldens cannot see it.
- **Scenario.** A trajectory that starts or ends at rest (v == 0 exactly in the vx/vy/vz columns), a simulated hover, a vertical landing/climb whose load-factor vector is exactly along the velocity, or --speed_reference air where the profile wind equals the ground velocity. Each such state is evaluated as if every observer were directly below it, up to 6 dB wrong at 27 degrees elevation and more at low elevation.
- **Proposed change.** In sphere_frame(), if the projected nose has (squared) length below a small tolerance, substitute a deterministic unit vector orthogonal to the normal instead of the zero vector: preferably the previous state's nose or the heading (the AC file's heading column is parsed but never stored in State), else normalize(x_hat - (x_hat.n) n), falling back to y_hat if that also degenerates. Add unit tests with v = 0 and v = c*n asserting that lookup_angles varies with the observer direction and that (phi, theta) are finite.
- **Expected impact.** Removes a direction-independent (straight-down) lookup on hover and vertical-flight states, an error of about 6 dB at 27 degrees elevation in the S-76D example. No effect on shipped tracks.
- **Conflicts with docs.** None. CLAUDE.md documents the 0/0 pole guard in phi_degrees but nothing about a zero nose; the trajectory side of hover (fpa = 0, mu = 0) is tested, the sphere side is not.

### db-eval/A.r1.0: Sphere nose is undefined at zero or negligible velocity: hover SEL is set by noise, or by nothing

- Finder's severity medium, category accuracy, effort M, finder's confidence 0.85, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: partly fixed C×4**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:678`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:727`, `src/niceops.cpp:757`
- **Claim.** sphere_frame() builds the sphere's azimuth reference (the 'nose') from the direction of the velocity projected into the TPP. When the velocity is exactly zero, or parallel to the TPP normal, nose and starboard are the zero vector. lookup_angles() then returns (phi=0, theta=90 deg) for every observer, so a stationary source radiates one direction-independent value. When the velocity is tiny but nonzero, the nose is set by the direction of that negligible vector and the level moves by several dB. There is no fallback to the aircraft heading (AC column 8 is never read) and no carry-over of the last well-defined nose. flight-path angles get an explicit hover branch in derive_path_angles; the nose does not.
- **Evidence given.** Code: `frame.nose = v - v.dot(frame.normal) * frame.normal; frame.nose.normalize();` (l.684-685) with Vec3::normalized() leaving a zero vector alone, then `frame.starboard = frame.nose.cross(frame.normal)` (l.687). Library probe (source 60 m up, mu=0, CT=0.006, S-76D_M3), level in dBA on the ground at 120 m: v=0 -> 72.85, v=1e-9 east -> 78.93, v=1e-9 north -> 76.78 (at 30 m: 79.13 / 80.44 / 82.51). End-to-end (synthetic AC hover at 100 ft for 50 s, 8-point rings, `niceops -t hover_v0.csv -b obs_ring.csv`): SEL at r=100 ft is 99.87 dB at all 8 azimuths for v=0, versus 101.2-103.4 dB with v=(1e-3 ft/s, 0); rotating that 1e-3 ft/s vector from east to north changes SEL by up to 5.2 dB at 300 ft and 800 ft. With realistic hover noise (vx, vy ~ N(0.3, 0.3) ft/s per row, seed 3) against a steady 0.3 ft/s nose, SEL over the same 24 observers differs by rms 1.35 dB, max 2.7 dB. Even at mu=0.02 a 15 deg error in nose direction moves the 100 m ring by rms 1.2 dB, max 2.1 dB (30 deg: rms 2.0, max 3.8; mu=0.10: 30 deg gives max 6.2 dB). No shipped track goes below 3 ft/s (awk scan of all tracks/*/*.csv), so the goldens never see this. Scripts: /Users/evg5332/.claude/jobs/8792c8f6/tmp/scratch/r1db_eval/{hover.cpp,nose.cpp} and run/hover_*.csv.
- **Scenario.** Any takeoff from rest, hover, hover-taxi or the last seconds of an approach, whether a WGS84 waypoint track (velocity is a spline derivative, exactly 0 on a constant-position segment) or an AC track with sub-ft/s velocity jitter. The hover sphere (mu=0) is azimuth-dependent, so its footprint is oriented by whatever the velocity noise happens to point at. This is the low-and-slow regime the project memory (forward-line-error-source) flags as +2 to +3 dB high at short range; it is a candidate contributor to check, though nothing here shows it is the cause.
- **Proposed change.** Give the frame a reference azimuth that does not depend on a vanishing vector. Parse the heading column into State for AC tracks and use the last well-defined ground track for geodetic tracks. Blend or switch to it when the TPP-projected air speed falls below a small threshold (say mu < 0.005), and carry the previous nose forward when the projection is exactly degenerate. As a minimum, stop returning a zero nose: hold the previous state's nose in process_trajectory. Add a test that levels are continuous as |v| goes 1e-3 -> 0 -> 1e-3.
- **Expected impact.** Removes multi-dB, orientation-random hover and low-speed footprint error. Nothing changes on any shipped track, whose minimum speed is above 3 ft/s.
- **Conflicts with docs.** Departure from D (Eigen normalized() also leaves a zero vector alone). The port-fidelity bar is met by measurement: the result flips by 3-5 dB for a 1e-3 ft/s change of input. Goldens are unaffected.

### db-load/B.r1.5: sphere_frame degenerates at zero velocity (or velocity parallel to the TPP normal): every observer is looked up at (phi 0, theta 90), discontinuous with an arbitrary nose at 1e-3 m/s

- Finder's severity low, category correctness, effort M, finder's confidence 0.55, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: partly fixed C×4**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:678`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:711`, `src/trajectory.cpp:441`
- **Claim.** sphere_frame builds the nose as the velocity projected into the TPP and normalized; Vec3::normalized() leaves a zero vector alone, so with |v| = 0 (or v parallel to the normal, or |v| below ~1e-162 where the squared norm underflows) nose and starboard are the zero vector, along = 0 and across = 0 for every observer, and lookup_angles returns theta = 90, phi = 0 whatever the direction: the straight-down level for everyone, a footprint that depends on range only. Trajectory explicitly supports true hover (derive_path_angles, trajectory.cpp:441-455, 'Fixed issue giving NaN flight path angles in hover'), so the hover state reaches this frame. The result is also discontinuous: any nonzero velocity, even 1e-3 m/s, picks a full directional lookup along an arbitrary nose.
- **Evidence given.** lookup_angles(src=(0,0,150), v=(vx,0,0), n=(0,0,1)) for observers at (200,0), (0,200), (-300,0), (0,500): vx = 1e-3 -> (phi,theta) = (0,36.87), (-53.13,90), (-0.00,153.43), (-73.30,90); vx = 1e-9 the same; vx = 1e-300 or 0 -> (0,90) for all four. On the shipped S-76D mu=0 sphere (Database::level_at_observer, CT 0.0065, source 150 m up) the exact-zero-velocity level vs the 1e-3 m/s level differ by -1.3..-4.0 dB at 50-1000 m ground range and vary with azimuth only in the second (e.g. at 400 m: 0.33, -2.58, +1.09, -3.99 dB at bearings 0/90/180/270), while the zero-velocity value is identical at all four bearings. No shipped AC track has a row with |v| < 0.5 m/s (awk over all tracks/*/*.csv), so the goldens do not see it.
- **Scenario.** A geodetic waypoint track with a hover dwell (the spline of constant positions has exactly zero velocity) or a synthetic hover test case: azimuth directivity of the mu = 0 sphere is lost, by up to a few dB on the S-76D sphere. A track with tiny noisy velocity instead gets a random nose per state.
- **Proposed change.** Give sphere_frame a defined fallback when the projected nose is degenerate: carry the previous state's nose forward (per-trajectory loop in process_trajectory), or use the track's heading if plumbed into State; at minimum count degenerate states and warn once. Needs a maintainer decision on the intended hover azimuth reference; the port-fidelity rule does not cover it because the D original's normalize of a zero vector was undefined (NaN).
- **Expected impact.** Removes a step change in hover-dwell footprints (measured up to ~4 dB on one sphere); affects only degenerate states.


## -a ambient added as pressure amplitude

### main-loop/A.r1.6: -a ambient is added as coherent pressure amplitude instead of incoherent power, overstating levels by up to 3 dB and mis-shaping the SEL window

- Finder's severity low, category accuracy, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: Rc C×4**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:1586`, `src/niceops.cpp:1698`
- **Claim.** `level = 20*log10(10^(level/20) + ambient_pressure)` is a sum of pressure amplitudes, which is right only for perfectly coherent in-phase signals. Aircraft noise and ambient noise are uncorrelated, so mean-square pressures add: 10*log10(10^(L/10) + 10^(A/10)). The amplitude sum is inherited from the D original (c74bab3 niceops.d:172-173), so it is a port-fidelity question, but the physics case is unambiguous and the effect is large near the ambient level.
- **Evidence given.** Difference between the two, by margin above ambient: L-A = 0 dB: +6.02 vs +3.01 dB; 3 dB: +4.65 vs +1.76; 6 dB: +3.53 vs +0.97; 10 dB: +2.39 vs +0.41; 15 dB: +1.42 vs +0.14; 20 dB: +0.83 vs +0.04 (python check). On the demo (scattered observer at (-4900,-4900) ft): SEL 64.68 dB without -a, 71.41 dB with `-a 45` (+6.7 dB). README.md:159 says only "mixed into every sample". Culled samples under --db_cutoff also drop the ambient term (they become -inf, not ambient), so -a and --db_cutoff disagree.
- **Scenario.** A user models community noise against a 40-50 dB ambient: footprint edge cells, which sit within 10 dB of ambient, are raised by 2-6 dB more than an energy sum gives, and the 10 dB-down window widens accordingly.
- **Proposed change.** If the maintainers accept physics as the measured case required by CLAUDE.md's port-fidelity rule, switch both sites to `10*log10(10^(L/10) + 10^(A/10))` and precompute the ambient power once; otherwise document in README that -a adds pressure amplitudes coherently. The goldens are unaffected (no -a).
- **Expected impact.** Correct incoherent mixing for -a runs; removes up to 3 dB of bias at the ambient floor.
- **Conflicts with docs.** CLAUDE.md port-fidelity rule: departing from D needs a measured case; the D original has the same amplitude sum.

### main-setup/A.r1.7: -a ambient is mixed in by amplitude (coherent sum) rather than by energy: +6.02 dB at equal levels where uncorrelated noise gives +3.01

- Finder's severity low, category accuracy, effort S, finder's confidence 0.85, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: Rc C×4**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:1585`, `src/niceops.cpp:1695`, `src/niceops.cpp:797`
- **Claim.** With -a, each sample is level = 20*log10(10^(L/20) + 10^(A/20)). That adds pressure amplitudes, which is right only for perfectly in-phase (coherent) signals. Aircraft noise and ambient noise are uncorrelated, so their mean-square pressures add: 10*log10(10^(L/10) + 10^(A/10)). The amplitude form over-counts ambient by up to 3.01 dB (at L = A), and the error is positive at every level. The mixed level then enters the SEL window and the peak search.
- **Evidence given.** `niceops --gridstep 492 -i timehistory/raw` and `-a 50 -i timehistory/amb`, observer 100. At a sample with raw L = 50.001 dB the code gives 56.021, the amplitude formula 56.021, and the energy sum 53.011. Ported verbatim from the D original (git show c74bab3:niceops.d, line 173: `10.0 ^^ (time_history[it] / 20.) + 10.0 ^^ (ambient_level / 20.)`).
- **Scenario.** Any run with -a: quiet observers near the ambient level are 1-3 dB too loud. The bound and windowing paths are unaffected, since -a already disables them.
- **Proposed change.** Use the energy sum: `10*log10(pow(10, 0.1*L) + ambient_energy)` with the energy hoisted like ambient_pressure. This departs from D, so per the port-fidelity rule it needs the maintainer's decision and no golden changes (the goldens do not use -a). Alternatively document that -a is a coherent sum.
- **Expected impact.** Corrects a 0-3 dB overestimate for -a runs only.
- **Conflicts with docs.** CLAUDE.md port-fidelity rule: departing from D needs a measured case. The analytic case above (L = A gives +6.02 versus +3.01) is the argument, and the D source line is the evidence of the inheritance.

### main-setup/B.r1.4: -a/--ambient adds ambient noise as pressure amplitudes (coherent), overstating the mix by up to 3 dB; the option is untested

- Finder's severity low, category accuracy, effort S, finder's confidence 0.7, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: Rc V1 C×4**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:797`, `src/niceops.cpp:1585`, `src/niceops.cpp:1695`
- **Claim.** `level = 20*log10(10^(level/20) + ambient_pressure)` sums the two pressure amplitudes linearly. Aircraft noise and ambient noise are uncorrelated, so the levels must add in energy, 10*log10(10^(L/10)+10^(A/10)). Two equal levels give +6.02 dB here instead of +3.01, and a level 10 dB below the ambient still raises it by 2.4 dB instead of 0.4. This is faithful to the D original (`niceops.d` at c74bab3: `20.0 * log10(10.0 ^^ (t/20.) + 10.0 ^^ (ambient/20.))`). No ctest or unit test exercises -a. Under the port-fidelity rule this needs the measured case below and the maintainer's decision.
- **Evidence given.** Single observer (-1000,300,0 ft), demo track, `-i` history. First sample level with no ambient: 42.9944 dB. With `-a 60` the same sample is 61.1470 dB; energy addition gives 10*log10(10^4.29944 + 10^6.0) = 60.0862 dB, so the code is 1.06 dB high. `-a 80`: 80.1218 against an energy sum of 80.0004. SEL for that observer: 86.81 (none), 89.14 (-a 60), 102.25 (-a 80). `grep -rn ambient tests/ CMakeLists.txt` finds only unrelated database 'ambient gating' tests.
- **Scenario.** A user models a 55 dBA background with -a 55 to find where the helicopter emerges from it. Cells near the background level read up to 3 dB higher than an energy sum, so the emergence contour lands in the wrong place.
- **Proposed change.** Decide deliberately. Either change the two sites (and the ambient_pressure hoist) to an energy sum, `10*log10(10^(L/10) + ambient_energy)`, with ambient_energy = 10^(A/10) hoisted, or document that -a is a coherent amplitude sum. Add a CTest or unit case pinning whichever. Departing from D needs the maintainer's sign-off. The arithmetic above is the measured case.
- **Expected impact.** Correct physics for -a. It only runs when -a is given, so the default path and the goldens are untouched. Windowing already refuses -a, so no bound is affected.
- **Verifier (trace): confirmed, confidence 0.85.** I re-read the code and ran the shipped build from scratch directories. The claim holds as stated: -a sums linear pressure amplitudes (coherently) at every place it applies the ambient. Nothing upstream or downstream masks it, and no test or CTest case exercises -a. The change is small, opt-in and needs maintainer sign-off, so the finding is real but low value.

Code, current HEAD 84462d7:
- src/niceops.cpp:797-798: `const bool add_ambient = !std::isnan(options.ambient_level); const double ambient_pressure = add_ambient ? std::pow(10.0, options.ambient_level / 20.) : 0.0;`
- src/niceops.cpp:1585-1586, in the `refracted_level` lambda: `level = 20.0 * std::log10(std::pow(10.0, level / 20.) + ambient_pressure);`
- src/niceops.cpp:1695-1698, in the straight-ray marched loop for broadband and spectral: the same expression.
- These are the only three sites. `grep -n -i ambient src/niceops.cpp` shows nothing else that mixes ambient in.

Upstream and downstream guards do not change this:
- `level_is_transformed = add_ambient` (line 1267) makes `bounded` false (line 1270). That leaves `expand_from_peak` false, so windowing refuses -a as the finding says.
- The refraction site at line 1585 is still reachable, because `refracted_level` is also used by the marched refraction loop. I ran it with `--refraction linear`.
- No other code path rescales or corrects the level afterwards.

Attribution to the D original: `git show c74bab3:niceops.d` lines 169-174 contain `20.0 * log10(10.0 ^^ (time_history[it] / 20.) + 10.0 ^^ (ambient_level / 20.))`. The C++ is a faithful port, not a porting slip. It is a physics error inherited from D, not a quirk.

Tests and docs: `grep -rn "ambient_level\|add_ambient" tests tools` finds nothing. No CTest command line in CMakeLists.txt passes -a or --ambient, and there is no .github workflow. The only ambient hits in tests/ are unrelated "ambient gating" cases. README.md:159 says only "Ambient level in dB, mixed into every sample." README.md:85 says -a goes into `level_dBA` only. Nothing documents a coherent sum.

Physics: aircraft noise and background noise are uncorrelated and broadband, so they add in energy. (a+b)^2 versus a^2+b^2 gives an overstatement of 10*log10((a+b)^2/(a^2+b^2)), which peaks at 3.01 dB when the two levels are equal. The finding's arithmetic checks out: two equal levels give 6.02 versus 3.01 dB, and a level 10 dB below the ambient gives +2.39 versus +0.41 dB. panam, the sibling tool that builds the databases, handles ambient in the power domain (PSD gate and subtraction in flight_acoustics.py).

The finding's numbers are slightly off. It says the energy sum at the first sample is 60.0862 dB and 80.0004 for -a 80. I compute 60.0857 and 80.0009. The 1.06 dB gap is unchanged.
  - Refined claim: With -a, the ambient is mixed by summing linear pressure amplitudes, `20*log10(10^(L/20) + 10^(A/20))`, at three sites: src/niceops.cpp:1585-1586 (marched refraction), 1695-1698 (marched straight ray, broadband and spectral), and the hoist at 797-798. Uncorrelated noise should add in energy, `10*log10(10^(L/10) + 10^(A/10))`. The code therefore reads up to 3.01 dB high, at L equal to A, and 2.4 dB instead of 0.4 dB when L is 10 dB below A. The behavior is identical in the D original, is undocumented, and has no test. It does not touch the default path, the goldens, or the windowed peak search, which -a already disables.
  - Verifier's fix: The maintainer decides between two options.
1. Switch to an energy sum. Hoist `ambient_energy = std::pow(10.0, A / 10.)` at line 798 in place of `ambient_pressure`. At the three sites use `level = 10.0 * std::log10(std::pow(10.0, level / 10.) + ambient_energy)`. Add a small test that a level equal to the ambient gives +3.01 dB, then mention the change alongside the other deliberate D departures in CLAUDE.md.
2. Keep the coherent sum. Document it in README.md:159 as an amplitude (in-phase, upper-bound) sum, and add a test that pins the current behavior.

Either way add a test, since nothing exercises -a now.

One separate observation from the runs, not part of this finding: with -a 80 the SEL is 102.25 against 86.81 with no ambient. An ambient-dominated record keeps the 10-dB-down window open across the whole track, so the SEL integrates the ambient over the full 131 s. That is a semantics question for the same option, not a defect in the arithmetic above.

### main-io-sel/A.r1.7: -a/--ambient adds the ambient to the signal as pressure amplitudes (coherent), overstating uncorrelated-noise mixing by up to 3 dB; the path has no test

- Finder's severity low, category accuracy, effort S, finder's confidence 0.55, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: Rc C×4**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:794`, `src/niceops.cpp:1586`, `src/niceops.cpp:1698`, `src/niceops.cpp:542`
- **Claim.** `level = 20*log10(10^(L/20) + 10^(La/20))` sums the two pressure amplitudes linearly, i.e. assumes the aircraft and the ambient noise are perfectly correlated. Uncorrelated noise adds in power, `10*log10(10^(L/10) + 10^(La/10))`. The bias relative to the power sum is 3.01 dB at L = La, 1.97 dB at 10 dB signal-to-ambient, 0.79 dB at 20 dB. It is inherited from the D original (c74bab3 niceops.d:172-173), so the port-fidelity rule protects it, and the rule requires a measured case before departing; this is a decision to make, not a mistranslation. Also latent: RunOptions::ambient_level defaults to 0.0 (line 542) while the sentinel that means 'off' is NaN; run() overwrites it (line 2457), but any other construction of RunOptions turns on a 0 dB ambient (the pitfall CLAUDE.md warns about).
- **Evidence given.** Arithmetic: coherent 20log10(1+10^(-x/20)) vs incoherent 10log10(1+10^(-x/10)) above L: x=0 -> 6.02 vs 3.01; x=10 -> 2.39 vs 0.41; x=20 -> 0.83 vs 0.04. `grep -rn ambient tests/*.cpp CMakeLists.txt` shows no test of -a (only the unrelated ambient-gated database cases in test_sphere_database.cpp). Not measured on a footprint (would need a rebuild).
- **Scenario.** A user emulating a measurement with the ambient floor included (`-a 55`) gets levels up to 3 dB above the energy sum, concentrated in the tails, so the window also moves.
- **Proposed change.** Either document -a as a coherent (correlated) mix, or switch to the power sum under the fidelity rule's exception with a measurement to justify it; give RunOptions::ambient_level a NaN default; add a ctest for -a.
- **Expected impact.** Correct or explicitly documented ambient mixing; removes the 0 dB trap.
- **Conflicts with docs.** CLAUDE.md 'Port fidelity outranks correctness, with one deliberate exception' and the D-default-NaN pitfall.


## Smoothing spline natural end conditions

### spline-util/A.r1.2: Natural end conditions force zero acceleration at the first and last sample, which biases the derived TPP normal near the end of an approach and moves the footprint by up to 1.8 dB

- Finder's severity medium, category accuracy, effort M, finder's confidence 0.8, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/smoothing_spline.cpp:213`, `src/trajectory.cpp:290`, `src/trajectory.cpp:340`
- **Claim.** SmoothingSpline is natural by construction (curvature.assign(n_, 0.0), interior gamma only), and read_geodetic_states takes nx, ny, nz (load-factor vector, and with it the TPP normal that orients the sphere frame) from fit_*.derivative(t, 2). A smoothing spline's boundary bias is O(1) within about a smoothing length of each end: at the default 3.05 m RMS the solved lambda is 1.8e4-1.3e5 s^3 (bandwidth ~10 s), so the last several seconds of a track have their acceleration pulled toward zero. For an approach that ends in a flare or deceleration this is exactly the part of the track nearest the landing-side observers.
- **Evidence given.** Demo track (golden/state_197421.csv positions, 6551 samples), within_tolerance with 3.048 m RMS per axis, angle between spline-derived normal (x'',y'',z''/g + 1) and the file's own n: interior t in [20,100) 1.67 deg rms; t in [126,130) 7.5 deg rms (max 10.8); final second 11.0 deg rms (max 12.6). At t=131 the file has n=(0.081, 0.198, 0.977); the spline gives (-6e-23, 4e-24, 1). Footprint effect, isolated with AC-format proxy tracks (tracks/S-76D/197421AC.csv with columns ax, ay, az replaced by spline-derived values, everything else including velocity from the file; ran build/niceops on each): A = spline acceleration everywhere vs the file's own: median 0.117 dB, p99 0.87, max 1.83, 87 cells >1 dB. C = file acceleration kept only for t>121 s: 23 cells >1 dB, so A vs C (the end-of-track effect) is 64 of the 87 cells, max 1.83 dB at x=2461 ft, y=410 ft (under the landing end; the track ends at x=741 m). D = file acceleration kept only for t<10 s: A vs D max 0.109 dB, i.e. the start is harmless. Candidate remedy tried outside the repo: pad each end with a local quadratic (fit to 3 s, 10 s of virtual points at 0.02 s, same RMS budget) before within_tolerance: normal error drops to 2.0 deg rms over the last 5 s and 5.0 deg in the final second, and the proxy footprint goes to median 0.076 dB, p99 0.60, max 1.40, 34 cells >1 dB (87 -> 34).
- **Scenario.** Any WGS84 approach track that ends while still decelerating or turning gets its last ~10 s flown with a TPP normal off by up to ~12 degrees and a load factor pulled toward 1 g, so the levels under the landing zone are shifted by ~1-2 dB. README's geodetic table (145 cells over 1 dB at the default) likely includes this and attributes it to 'differentiating twice'.
- **Proposed change.** Remove the end bias: before within_tolerance in fit_path, extend each end by ~3 smoothing lengths of local-quadratic extrapolation (window ~3 s, extension ~10 s, or scaled from the solved lambda: (lambda*h)^(1/4)), fit, and evaluate only inside the original range. Alternatively add an end-condition mode to SmoothingSpline that leaves the end curvature free. Keep tolerance = rms^2 * original_n so the RMS semantics do not change. Add a test that fits a parabola (constant acceleration) and requires the end second derivative within a few percent of 2a, which today is exactly 0. Re-measure README's geodetic table.
- **Expected impact.** Removes roughly three quarters of the >1 dB cells caused by the smoothing on the demo track (87 -> 34 in the proxy) and the 7-11 degree normal error over the last seconds; adds two extra virtual segments to the O(n) fit, negligible cost.
- **Conflicts with docs.** None: the AC path, golden/197421_LAE.csv and golden/state_197421.csv do not use SmoothingSpline (Trajectory::adapt is commented out in main). README's 'do not re-derive' note concerns the path_smoothing table; this finding decomposes it, and the proxy (AC file with spline accelerations, file velocities) is not the geodetic pipeline itself, so the README table would need re-measuring after any fix.

### trajectory-geodetic/A.r1.4: Natural end conditions on the smoothing spline bias speed, acceleration and fpa_e over the last ~6 s of a geodetic track

- Finder's severity medium, category accuracy, effort M, finder's confidence 0.75, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/trajectory.cpp:290`, `src/trajectory.cpp:337`, `src/smoothing_spline.hpp:54`, `tests/test_trajectory.cpp:388`
- **Claim.** The fitted path is a natural cubic spline (f''=0 at both ends). Velocity and load factor are its first and second derivatives, so the acceleration at the track ends is pinned to 0 g regardless of the flight. At the 10 ft default budget the smoother is stiff, so the boundary layer is seconds long, not a few samples. A track that ends in a stop, such as an approach to a landing point where an observer is loudest, has its final ~6 s given too little deceleration, too high an end speed and too small a TPP tilt. The tests know about the end cells (`Skip the two end cells`) but no test or doc quantifies this for the smoothed geodetic path.
- **Evidence given.** Synthetic 30 m/s to 0 deceleration at 1.5 m/s^2, 50 Hz, 0.5 mm noise, `within_tolerance(rms=3.048 m)`. Fitted decel as a share of true: 0% at the end, 6% at 1 s, 21% at 2 s, 57% at 4 s, 102% at 8 s. Through the real reader (noise-free lat/lon, default 10 ft): the last state has mu=0.0246 (5.4 m/s) where the true speed is 0; fpa_e is -0.57 deg at 1 s and -4.98 deg at 4 s before the end against -8.70 deg true. The same fit on the same flight with 20 s of hover appended gives -5.50 and -7.90 deg at those points, and mu 0.0078 at the stop. CT moves only 0.3-0.6%, so the effect is in mu and fpa_e, which select the sphere. Footprint of this track, default vs `--path_smoothing 0`: median 0.16 dB, p95 0.62, max 3.4 dB over 3721 cells. That figure mixes end effect with interior smoothing bias, so it is an upper bound on the end effect alone.
- **Scenario.** An approach ends at the pad and is exported as WGS84. The last few seconds are evaluated as a slow, nearly untilted flight when the aircraft is actually flaring hard. The hover-transition spheres are the loudest for near observers.
- **Proposed change.** Remove the boundary artifact. Pad each end with a quadratic or linear extrapolation of the last ~1 s (or reflected data) for a few multiples of the smoothing length, fit, and discard the padding. Alternatively use clamped end slopes estimated from the end samples. Keep it geodetic-only. Goldens are unaffected because the AC path does no smoothing.
- **Expected impact.** Removes multi-degree fpa_e and mu errors at the loudest part of terminating tracks. Interior smoothing bias remains and is documented.
- **Conflicts with docs.** CLAUDE.md quotes 0.085 dB median for the 10 ft default, measured on the mid-track-dense demo case, not on a track that ends in a deceleration.

### trajectory-geodetic/A.r1.3: Velocity columns are interpolated exactly and differentiated into the load factor, with no smoothing option or warning

- Finder's severity medium, category accuracy, effort M, finder's confidence 0.6, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/trajectory.cpp:296`, `src/trajectory.cpp:326`, `src/trajectory.cpp:258`
- **Claim.** When vE/vN/vUp are present, `velocity_tolerance = 0.0` makes the three splines interpolate every sample, and the load factor (nx, ny, nz, hence LF, CT and the TPP normal behind fpa_e) is their first derivative. Any quantization in the velocity columns is amplified by 1/dt, which is the same mechanism the position path documents at 11.6 dB. Only the position path has a budget (`--path_smoothing`) and a warning (lines 258-273 are guarded by `!velocity_given`). The velocity path has neither, and `--path_smoothing` explicitly does not reach it.
- **Evidence given.** Standalone program: demo track written as WGS84 plus vE/vN/vUp (rotated to ENU, 17 digits), velocity rounded to a quantum q, read with the demo frame, compared state by state with the AC reference (50 Hz). q=0: CT error median 0.26%, fpa_e error median 0.12 deg. q=0.01 ft/s: CT median 0.47%, fpa_e p95 0.7 deg. q=0.05 ft/s: CT median 1.9%, p95 5.2%, max 10%; fpa_e p95 2.9 deg, max 4.9 deg. q=0.17 ft/s (0.1 kt): CT median 3.1%, p95 14.7%, max 26%; fpa_e median 1.8 deg, p95 8.5 deg, max 15.7 deg. q=0.5 ft/s: CT p95 44%, max 76%; fpa_e max 38.6 deg. At 5 Hz the same quantum costs far less (0.17 ft/s: CT p95 4.2%).
- **Scenario.** A dense (10-50 Hz) file whose velocity columns are rounded to 0.1 kt, or to 0.05 m/s, is supplied. The velocity spline passes through every step, the derivative is spiky, and the run selects spheres at the wrong CT and fpa_e with no warning. The user cannot mitigate it, because velocity columns are exempt from the smoothing option.
- **Proposed change.** Give velocity a budget analogous to path_smoothing (an RMS in speed units applied through within_tolerance instead of 0). At minimum emit the position path's style of warning when `velocity_given && mean_step < 0.5`, and estimate the quantum (smallest non-zero |dv|) to say so. Alternatively derive the load factor from the smoothed position spline when velocity columns look coarse.
- **Expected impact.** Bounds CT and fpa_e error for coarse-resolution velocity input (tens of percent CT at 0.1 kt/50 Hz) with a knob comparable to the existing one.
- **Conflicts with docs.** The comment at trajectory.cpp:298-306 defends zero tolerance ('what a file that bothers to carry velocity columns means by them') without a measurement. The README/CLAUDE.md analysis of derivative noise covers positions only.


## Sphere export and self_test

### db-write/B.r1.3: interpolate_sphere adds the load-factor correction to empty (floor) cells, lifting them above the empty threshold, so a re-imported sphere reads -117 to -809 dB next to a gated direction

- Finder's severity medium, category correctness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.95, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:1697`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:369`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1730`
- **Claim.** For a fixed_load_factor database queried above its reference CT, interpolate_sphere does 'sphere.cells[...].dBA += blend.level_correction_db' unconditionally (line 1705). A cell that blend_cell returned as empty (-1000, no_energy_floor_db) becomes e.g. -993.98, above empty_level_db (-999), so after write_sphere and reload it is no longer 'empty': the bilinear step then blends toward -994 dB, which is exactly the 10-25 dB-hole failure blend_present exists to prevent. The spectral branch of the same function guards this (lines 1732-1735: 'if (value <= empty_level_db) { ... = no_energy_floor_db; continue; }'), and eval() is unaffected because it adds the correction after blending. Only the export path is wrong.
- **Evidence given.** Synthetic 3-sphere fixed_load_factor database with channel 0 (phi=-90, theta=0) = -inf on all spheres (mkdb.cpp 'fixed_gated'), gated_export.cpp exports at CT = 2x reference (+6.02 dB) and compares the source database with the re-imported sphere at phi=-80: exported cell[0] = -993.98; theta 0/20/30/45 -> source 113.52 dB every time, re-imported -809.40 / -501.76 / -347.94 / -117.21 dB. Control at CT = reference: cell[0] = -1000.00, source and re-imported both 107.50. With the one-line guard in the scratch copy ('if (sphere.cells[...].dBA > empty_level_db) ... += ...') the re-imported values are 113.52 dB at all four points. Not reachable with shipped data: empty_export.cpp on all 9 *_fixed_load_factor.nod found 0 cells below -500 dB (corrections 9.6-20.5 dB at CT=0.02), and the *_ambient_gated.nod files are not fixed_load_factor.
- **Scenario.** panam builds a database with both load_factors=None and ambient gating, and the user exports a sphere at a thrust above the reference to reuse it. The exported sphere gives absurd levels in a wide cone around every gated direction.
- **Proposed change.** Skip the correction for empty cells, as the spectral branch does: 'if (cell.dBA > empty_level_db) cell.dBA += blend.level_correction_db;'. Add a test using write_shared_grid_test_database with channel_dba returning -inf plus the fixed_load_factor flag, exporting at 2x CT and requiring eval() on the reloaded sphere to equal eval() on the source.
- **Expected impact.** Exported spheres match the source database in every direction; no change to any current output.

### db-write/A.r1.1: interpolate_sphere adds the load-factor correction to empty (floor) cells, so exported spheres lose their 'not measured' marker and reload with up to -1000 dB holes

- Finder's severity medium, category correctness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.95, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:1699`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1728`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:369`
- **Claim.** For every cell, interpolate_sphere does `sphere.cells[...].dBA += blend.level_correction_db`. A cell empty in every blended condition comes out of blend_cell as the floor (-1000). Adding a positive correction lifts it above empty_level_db (-999), so the marker is lost. The spectral branch a few lines below handles this correctly (`if (value <= empty_level_db) { ... = no_energy_floor_db; continue; }`). The dBA branch does not. The correction is positive on fixed_load_factor databases queried above their reference CT, and those are the ambient-gated databases that actually have empty directions.
- **Evidence given.** B407_fixed_load_factor.nod (fixed_load_factor=1, 31 bands, 3399 non-finite dBA cells). Group 94 (mu=0.103814, gamma=23.9905) has 826 empty cells. Exported at its own condition with CT x2.0 (exp2 harness):
shipped: `dBA cells exactly at floor=0, recognized empty(<=-999)=0, ESCAPED (-999,-900)=826 [-993.98..-993.98]; amplitudes at floor=27750`. Reloaded database vs original in-memory eval over 45,428 directions: `5653 differ by >1e-6 dB, worst 1085.943 dB at phi=120.0 theta=96.0 (orig 91.96, reloaded -993.98)`.
At CT x1.0 (no correction) the same export is clean: 826 recognized empty, 0 differing directions.
With `if (cell.dBA > empty_level_db) cell.dBA += level_correction_db;` in a scratch copy: `recognized empty=826, ESCAPED=0` and `0 differ by >1e-6 dB` on the same 45,428 directions.
No test covers it. Mutation: deleting BOTH correction lines (cells and amplitudes) from interpolate_sphere passes all 183 unit tests.
- **Scenario.** `niceops -d databases/B407_fixed_load_factor.nod --export_sphere s.nod --sphere_condition <mu> <gamma> <2x reference CT>` on a gated sphere. The written .nod holds -993.98 dB cells that Database treats as real measurements. Any evaluation whose bilinear stencil touches one is dragged down by weight x ~1000 dB, up to a 1086 dB error at the edge of an emptied region, instead of the corner dropping out as designed. The AAM export is unaffected, because its bands keep the floor, so the two exports of the same sphere disagree.
- **Proposed change.** Skip the correction when the blended cell is empty: `if (cell.dBA > empty_level_db) cell.dBA += blend.level_correction_db;`. Add a test that exports a gated fixed_load_factor sphere at CT > reference, reloads it, and requires empties to stay <= empty_level_db and evals to match.
- **Expected impact.** Exported spheres round-trip exactly for load factors above 1. Export-only path. No effect on trajectory runs, where eval() applies the correction after the empty check.
- **Conflicts with docs.** The interpolate_sphere comment says a sphere exported at a held operating condition 'comes back bit-for-bit as that sphere's own data'. That fails for empty cells under a load-factor correction.

### db-eval/A.r1.4: interpolate_sphere lifts an empty (-1000 dB floor) cell above the empty threshold when a load-factor correction is applied, corrupting exports

- Finder's severity low, category correctness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:1701`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1730`
- **Claim.** For the broadband cells, `sphere.cells[...].dBA += blend.level_correction_db;` is unconditional. A cell that blend_conditions() returned as empty (dBA = no_energy_floor_db = -1000) becomes -1000 + correction. Once the correction exceeds 1 dB (CT above 1.12x the reference, on a fixed_load_factor database) that is above empty_level_db (-999), so a reloaded export treats it as a real measurement. The spectral branch right below it handles this correctly, since it writes no_energy_floor_db and `continue`s for empty bands; the two branches disagree.
- **Evidence given.** Synthetic fixed_load_factor database with one channel at -inf in every sphere (mkdb.cpp 'flf_gated.nod'). `interpolate_sphere(0.3, 0.3, ct)`: at CT=0.005 the empty cell is -1000.000; at CT=0.010 it is -993.979. Export with write_sphere and reload: `eval(30.48, 45, 45, blend)` is 115.021 dB on the original database and -508.792 dB on the re-loaded export (the empty corner is no longer dropped, so it drags its neighbors). No shipped fixed_load_factor database has empty cells (checked all nine: no empty and no floor+correction cells), so no shipped export is affected.
- **Scenario.** `--export_sphere` from a database that combines ambient-gated (-inf) directions with fixed_load_factor, at a query CT above the reference.
- **Proposed change.** Apply the correction only where `cell.dBA > empty_level_db`, as the amplitude branch does.
- **Expected impact.** Exported spheres round-trip. Two-line change.

### db-write/A.r1.6: write_sphere drops the root metadata the loader interprets: speed_reference and tip speed do not survive an export

- Finder's severity low, category correctness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:2209`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:106`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:823`, `tests/test_sphere_database.cpp:331`
- **Claim.** write_sphere writes only same_grid and database_version at the root. Database reads a root speed_reference attribute (default ground) and main_rotor_tip_speed_meters_per_sec. An exported sphere from a database that records speed_reference = air reloads as ground-referenced with NaN tip speed. The flight conditions on the exported sphere are air-referenced, and the 'database decides the reference by default' rule then applies the wrong one. The existing test (lines 331-339) adds the attribute by hand with nc_put_att_text after write_sphere, which is a sign the writer does not carry it.
- **Evidence given.** Copy of B205_fixed_load_factor.nod with the attribute speed_reference='air' added (nc_put_att_text). Database load: `source database speed_reference = air, tip speed = 248.100 m/s`. interpolate_sphere + write_sphere + reload: `exported-and-reloaded speed_reference = ground, tip speed = nan`. No shipped database has the attribute yet ('panam's side is not built yet' per CLAUDE.md), so this is latent until panam writes it.
- **Scenario.** Once panam emits air-referenced databases, an exported sphere silently flips reference. Runs against it index mu and gamma with the ground velocity, and nothing warns.
- **Proposed change.** Carry speed_reference and tip speed through Database::Sphere (two fields) and write them at the root in write_sphere. Extend the round-trip test in (test-gap 5) to assert them.
- **Expected impact.** An exported sphere carries the same semantic root metadata as its source.

### db-write/B.r1.6: write_sphere drops the source's speed_reference and tip speed, so a sphere exported from an air-referenced database reloads as ground-referenced

- Finder's severity low, category correctness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.85, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:2205`, `src/sphere_database.hpp:475`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:821`
- **Claim.** Database::Sphere carries neither the root speed_reference attribute nor main_rotor_tip_speed_meters_per_sec, and write_sphere writes only same_grid and database_version at the root. The exported .nod is documented as loadable 'straight back', but it comes back with speed_reference() == ground and tip_speed() == NaN. refer_to_database_speed (niceops.cpp:632-640) then treats the sphere as ground-referenced, silently skipping both the wind requirement and the air-referenced nose, and the tip-speed disagreement check (niceops.cpp:686-688) is skipped. The existing speed_reference test hand-patches the attribute after write_sphere (test_sphere_database.cpp:335-345), which shows the export cannot carry it.
- **Evidence given.** Copied databases/S-76D_fixed_load_factor.nod, added ':speed_reference = "air"' (patch_att.cpp), ran 'niceops -d degen/air_src.nod --export_sphere degen/air_sphere.nod --sphere_condition 0.15 -4 0.006 -S state/x.csv': source 'ncdump -h' shows ':speed_reference = "air"' and the main_rotor_tip_speed variable; the exported file's root has only 'short same_grid(scalar); int database_version(scalar)'. The tip speed also feeds write_aam_sphere's SPEED via database.tip_speed() (niceops.cpp:2340), which is fine for the direct path but lost on any re-export chain.
- **Scenario.** Once panam writes air-referenced databases (CLAUDE.md says its side is not built yet), a user exports one sphere and runs it in a windy --atmosphere case. The run neither refuses nor refers to the air.
- **Proposed change.** Add speed_reference and tip_speed to Database::Sphere, fill them in interpolate_sphere, and have write_sphere write the text attribute (NC_CHAR, as read_speed_reference expects) and main_rotor_tip_speed_meters_per_sec. Turn the hand-patching test into a plain round trip.
- **Expected impact.** Round-trips the two properties the loader and run() consult; no effect on today's databases (ground, tip speed present in shipped ones would now be kept).

### db-write/B.r1.2: write_sphere and write_aam_sphere ignore nc_close's status, so a failed flush reports success and leaves a truncated, unreadable .nod

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.95, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:28`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:2203`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:2321`
- **Claim.** Every nc_put_* is checked by nc_check, but with HDF5 buffering a write failure (disk full, quota, EIO) surfaces only in nc_close, whose return value NcGuard's destructor discards ('nc_close(id);' line 34). Both writers log 'Wrote ... sphere' before the guard closes the file, so --export_sphere / --export_aam exit 0 with a corrupt file.
- **Evidence given.** Simulated a write failure with a file size limit. Original binary: bash -c 'ulimit -f 20; trap "" XFSZ; /Users/evg5332/Code/D/NICEOPS/build/niceops -d databases/S-76D_M3.nod --export_sphere out/full.nod --sphere_condition 0.15 -4 0.006 -S state/x.csv; echo exit=$?' -> 'exit=0'; out/full.nod is 20480 bytes and 'ncdump -h' says 'NetCDF: HDF error' (sphere_r1_nc/run). A stand-alone program with the same calls (close_status.cpp) prints 'put phi=0 ... put EAA=0' then 'close=-101 (NetCDF: HDF error)'. Prototype in the scratch copy (NcGuard::close() plus 'nc_check(guard.close(), "closing " + path);' before each 'Wrote' log): the same command exits 2.
- **Scenario.** Exporting spheres in a batch to a nearly full volume or a quota-limited share: the batch script sees exit 0, and the corrupt .nod fails much later (or, if only the tail is lost, is read as valid data).
- **Proposed change.** Give NcGuard an explicit close() that returns the status and disarms the destructor; call 'nc_check(guard.close(), "closing " + path)' just before the final log line in both writers. Optionally remove the partial file when a write throws.
- **Expected impact.** Turns silent output corruption into a non-zero exit. No effect on the success path.

### db-write/A.r1.5: Export CLI: --export_sphere/--export_aam can overwrite the database being read, and out-of-envelope conditions are silently clamped but labeled as requested

- Finder's severity low, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:2325`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:2202`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:2320`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1679`
- **Claim.** (1) nc_create(..., NC_NETCDF4 | NC_CLOBBER) is used unconditionally. Nothing compares the export path with the database path, so `-d X --export_sphere X` replaces a multi-tens-of-MB derived database (README: 'far too large' to track, rebuilt in panam) with a one-sphere file. (2) blend_at clamps mu/gamma (and CT on non-fixed databases) into the envelope with clamp01, but interpolate_sphere stores the requested condition in the Sphere and write_sphere/write_aam_sphere write it into the file. AAM SPEED = requested mu x tip speed.
- **Evidence given.** (1) Copy of B205_fixed_load_factor.nod (5,687,881 bytes): `niceops -d /tmp/x.nod --export_sphere /tmp/x.nod --sphere_condition 0.1 0 0.0031` -> exit=0, file now 56,984 bytes.
(2) S-76D_M3 has mu in [0, 0.301775]. `--sphere_condition 5.0 0.0 0.006` and `9.0 0.0 0.006` both exit 0 with 0 bytes on stderr and an identical dBA payload (same md5), i.e. the mu-max sphere. The 9.0 export's advance_ratio variable reads 9. With --export_aam that becomes SPEED = 9 x tip speed x 1.94384 kt.
- **Scenario.** A scripted export that reuses the database path as output, or a typo such as 3 for 0.3. Both give a plausible-looking, wrongly labeled result with exit code 0.
- **Proposed change.** In the export block: input_error if the output path is equivalent to any -d database (std::filesystem::equivalent); log a warn (or input_error) with the envelope when the requested condition lies outside [min, max] of any axis that blend_at clamps; optionally have interpolate_sphere record the clamped condition.
- **Expected impact.** Prevents data loss and mislabeled exports.

### db-write/A.r1.4: Sphere export payload is essentially untested: swapped dBA/EAA and a dropped load-factor correction pass all 183 unit tests, and write_aam_sphere has no test at all

- Finder's severity medium, category test-gap, effort M, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `tests/test_sphere_database.cpp:293`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:2273`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:2306`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1679`
- **Claim.** The 'Shaft power round-trips' test says 'And the levels still survive the round trip' but asserts only reloaded.sphere_radius() == sphere.radius (lines 316-317). The spectra test checks amplitudes only, on a synthetic ramp. No test compares the reloaded broadband dBA/EAA cells to the exported ones. No test loads a --export_aam file (grep for write_aam_sphere in tests/ and CMakeLists.txt finds nothing), so its axis order, units and conversions are unpinned. interpolate_sphere's level_correction_db branch is unpinned for both cells and amplitudes.
- **Evidence given.** Scratch copy of HEAD, one mutation at a time, then `./build/niceops_unit_tests` (whole suite):
(1) write_sphere: `dba[c] = sphere.cells[c].EAA; eaa[c] = sphere.cells[c].dBA;` -> 'All tests passed (25883898 assertions in 183 test cases)'.
(2) interpolate_sphere: both `+ blend.level_correction_db` terms removed -> 'All tests passed (25883898 assertions in 183 test cases)'.
The write_sphere round trip is in fact exact on the demo database (S-76D_M3 at 0.15,-4,0.006: worst |orig - reloaded| = 0.000e+00 dB over 51,600 directions), so a strict test would cost nothing.
AAM file inspected by hand from B407_fixed_load_factor (mu 0.15): RADIUS=100 (ft), SPEED=67.26659 (kt), and PHI = -180..170 in 36 values, i.e. the completed full circle, although the writer's comment says it emits 'the AAM/RNM hemisphere layout, as the source spheres themselves carry it' (source spheres span phi in [-90,90] per CLAUDE.md). Whether a consumer accepts a full-circle PHI is unverifiable here (no AAM reference file in the repo), which is exactly what a pinned test or fixture would settle.
- **Scenario.** A refactor of write_sphere's flatten loop, of the AAM dimension order, or of the correction branch ships green. Exported spheres then carry swapped levels, a transposed AMPLITUDE array, or lose the load-factor scaling.
- **Proposed change.** Add (a) a round-trip test that requires reloaded.interpolate_sphere cells and amplitudes to equal the exported ones exactly (dBA, EAA, phis, thetas), including an empty cell and a fixed_load_factor database queried above reference CT; (b) a write_aam_sphere test that reads back PHI/THETA/FREQUENCY/AMPLITUDE with nc_get_var and checks the AMPLITUDE element order, the unit attributes and RADIUS/SPEED against hand-computed values; (c) a decision recorded on hemisphere vs full-circle PHI.
- **Expected impact.** Pins the only route by which computed spheres leave NICEOPS.

### db-write/B.r1.4: self_test compares at 1% relative tolerance although eval reads every stored value back bit-exactly, and checks neither EAA nor spectra

- Finder's severity medium, category test-gap, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:2495`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:742`
- **Claim.** self_test asks 'approx_equal(dBA, level)' with the D defaults (1e-2 relative, 1e-5 absolute), about 0.8 dB at 80 dBA. At a sphere's own condition and grid node the blend is [1,0,0,0] and the bilinear weights are [1,0,0,0], so eval returns the stored double exactly. The comparison is therefore 6-9 orders looser than the data allows, and a channel filed one grid cell off (the class of bug the test exists for: misfiled hover directivity, per-sphere layouts) passes on most samples. It also never looks at EAA (the loader fills it into the same grid) or the per-band spectra.
- **Evidence given.** selftest_tol.cpp re-reads every group of all 21 shipped databases like self_test does and calls Database::eval at each channel: 'max |eval - stored| = 0' on every one (about 22 million samples), e.g. S-76D_M3 'channels 1844672, exact 1844672'. Replacing the channel's value by its one-step theta neighbor would still satisfy approx_equal on 72.7-89.5% of samples (S-76D_M3 73.8%), and a phi neighbor on 62.6-89.8% (S-76D_M3 62.6%), so a defect touching a handful of channels escapes with high probability (RO-44_ambient_gated: 0.895^10 = 31% for ten bad channels). EAA can be checked through the public eval: selftest_eaa.cpp recovers it as (eval(R) - eval(R+1000)) - 20 log10((R+1000)/R) and matches stored EAA to 9e-15 (S-76D_M3), 1.5e-14 (AS350_ambient_gated), 5.7e-14 (S-76D_fixed_load_factor). Existing test_case 'self_test does not flag a direction ambient gating legitimately emptied' only checks the floor case.
- **Scenario.** A future loader change (grid fill, shared-grid path, repair) misplaces a few channels of a few spheres. --self_test, the only validator of a new database, stays green.
- **Proposed change.** Compare with an absolute 1e-9 dB (evaluate at sphere_radius(), not the group's radius*scale, since the loader tolerates 1% radius differences and the spreading term would otherwise add up to 0.09 dB), add the EAA check via eval(R+1000) as above, and for spectral databases compare eval_spectrum (zero absorption, range R) with the stored bands. Do not touch approx_equal itself, which the grid fill depends on.
- **Expected impact.** self_test becomes able to catch single-channel and sub-dB defects; still passes on all 21 shipped databases.
- **Conflicts with docs.** CLAUDE.md warns that tightening approx_equal silently drops channels; that concerns the channel matching in the grid fill. This proposal leaves approx_equal and the fill untouched and only changes what self_test compares.

### db-write/A.r1.3: self_test tolerates errors of ~1% of the level (0.7 dB), never compares EAA or spectra, and every shipped database reproduces its own levels exactly

- Finder's severity medium, category test-gap, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:2486`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:2501`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:742`
- **Claim.** self_test compares stored dBA against eval() with approx_equal, whose defaults are 1e-2 relative / 1e-5 absolute. That is 0.70 dB at the quietest S-76D_M3 level (69.9 dBA) and ~1 dB at 100 dB. The value is a port choice for angle matching, not a sensible level tolerance. EAA is read into EAA_data (lines 2486-2487) and never used. Even if it were compared, eval at range == radius multiplies EAA by (range - radius) = 0, so EAA cannot show through this call. Spectra are not touched. The true discrepancy on every shipped database is exactly 0.
- **Evidence given.** Reference-vs-eval max |dBA - eval| over all channels, using the same blend_at/eval calls self_test uses: exactly 0.000e+00 dB on all 21 databases in databases/ (S-76D_M3 1,844,672 samples; B407_M3 1,726,272; B407_ambient_gated 1,907,424; ...), including the gated and fixed_load_factor ones.
Mutation in a scratch build: the grid fill changed to `grid[...] = {dBA + 0.5, 0.0}` (every level +0.5 dB, every EAA zeroed). `niceops --self_test` then logs 'Self test passed on all 1844672 samples.' and exits 0. Only the golden regression would notice.
Sensitivity: mixing 5% of the nearest other sphere into the query blend already fails a few samples, but at 1% mixing (0.15 dB max shift) all 1,844,672 pass.
- **Scenario.** --self_test exists to vet a freshly built database's load path. A loader bug that mis-files EAA (wrong channel index, transposed layout) or shifts levels by under ~0.7 dB, or a spectra layout mistake, passes it. panam has already produced one such layout bug (the amplitude row order fixed 2026-09-25), which the self test could not have seen.
- **Proposed change.** Compare with an absolute tolerance like `approx_equal(dBA, level, 0.0, 1e-9)`. All 21 databases pass at exactly 0. Also verify EAA (query at range = radius + 1000 m and compare dBA - EAA, or expose the blended Sample), and spot-check a few spectra against the stored amplitude(phi,theta,frequency). Keep the 1% form only for matching channel angles.
- **Expected impact.** Makes --self_test able to catch level shifts, EAA mis-filing and spectra mis-ordering on new databases, and turns the CTest self_test case into a real regression check of the loader.
- **Conflicts with docs.** README ('--self_test: Validate every sphere in the database against its own stored levels') and CLAUDE.md (self_test 'validating every sphere ... against its own stored levels') overstate it. Only dBA within 1%, at 1,344 or so directions per sphere, is checked.


## golden_compare blind to NaN/inf

### main-loop/A.r1.0: golden_compare treats NaN and +/-inf as agreeing with any value, so every golden and "windowed is exact" test is blind to non-finite output

- Finder's severity high, category test-gap, effort S, finder's confidence 0.98, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: R**
- Locations: `tests/golden_compare.cpp:96`, `CMakeLists.txt:260`
- **Claim.** The comparison `if (std::abs(candidate[i] - golden[i]) > tolerance * scale)` is false whenever either operand is NaN (NaN compares false) or infinite (scale becomes inf, so the tolerance is inf and `inf > inf` is false). A candidate file full of nan, inf or -inf therefore passes against any golden. Every CTest that uses golden_compare (regression_golden, state_golden, all *_windowed_is_exact, *_is_straight, *_is_homogeneous, band_history_*, mixed_*) inherits this.
- **Evidence given.** Experiment in scratch dir /Users/evg5332/.claude/jobs/8792c8f6/tmp/scratch/r1_a/run with the shipped build/golden_compare:
  bad.csv = good.csv with row 3 all `nan`, row 4 all `inf`, row 5 all `-inf`
  $ golden_compare bad.csv good.csv  -> "golden_compare: 483 values agree within 1e-09 relative tolerance." exit=0
  $ golden_compare good.csv bad.csv  -> same, exit=0
  Real golden: rows 3..20 of golden/197421_LAE.csv replaced by `nan`:
  $ golden_compare g_bad.csv golden/197421_LAE.csv -> "14640 values agree within 1e-09 relative tolerance." exit=0
  Control: one finite wrong row (all 50) -> "mismatch at value 168: candidate 50 vs golden 72.8651" exit=1.
I also ran all 30 niceops CTest command lines from CMakeLists.txt in scratch and grepped the outputs: no nan/inf is present today, so nothing is currently masked; the harness just cannot see it if it appears. Non-finite output is the natural failure signature here (0/0 in the refraction solves, log10(0), culled -inf).
- **Scenario.** A change makes the windowed peak search, a refracted ray solve, or the ground table return NaN for some observers. regression_golden, refraction_point_windowed_is_exact, stratified_windowed_is_exact etc. all still report PASS, and CI stays green while the footprint is corrupt.
- **Proposed change.** In golden_compare compute `const double c = candidate[i], g = golden[i];` and fail unless `(std::isnan(c) && std::isnan(g)) || (std::isinf(c) && c == g) || std::abs(c - g) <= tolerance * scale` (i.e. write the test as an accept condition so NaN falls through to failure). Add two WILL_FAIL CTests feeding a NaN file and an inf-vs-finite file so the harness itself is pinned. Consider `cmake -E compare_files` for the *_windowed_is_exact pairs, since CLAUDE.md and README claim byte-identical output but CI only enforces 1e-9.
- **Expected impact.** CI can detect non-finite regressions in every existing golden/equivalence test at zero runtime cost. May expose latent masked failures (none found in the shipped configurations).


## --db_cutoff README claim

### main-loop/A.r1.2: --db_cutoff "exact for every cell at or above L" is false for the SEL cells the README talks about; -m metrics are meaningless with its -inf cells; no test runs --db_cutoff at all

- Finder's severity medium, category accuracy, effort M, finder's confidence 0.93, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: C×2**
- Locations: `README.md:231`, `src/niceops.cpp:1322`, `src/niceops.cpp:236`, `src/niceops.cpp:890`
- **Claim.** cull_below = db_cutoff - 10 is compared with the per-sample level, but the output cell is a SEL, which sits about 10*log10(window seconds) above the observer's peak sample (about +13.8 dB for the 24 s windows on the demo). An observer with SEL >= L can therefore have a peak below L and lose window samples between peak-10 and L-10. The literal guarantee (observer whose peak sample reaches L) does hold, but README's demo sentence "every cell at or above 85 dB is unchanged to the last bit" does not. Separately, append_metrics_file averages every cell including -inf (and inf), and its "area" is the fraction of cells >= 65 dB, so -m with --db_cutoff writes garbage. CMakeLists has no --db_cutoff test (only comments at line 263).
- **Evidence given.** README's own example (default 120x120 grid, `niceops -j 6 --db_cutoff 85`): log "Culled 90626136 of 94334400 evaluations (96.069%) as provably below 75 dB" (matches README's 96%), yet of 592 cells >= 85 dB in the full run, 157 differ from the full run, max drop 0.431 dB (0 became -inf).
On a 40x40 grid: cutoff 85: 20 of 63 cells >= 85 changed (max 0.26 dB); cutoff 70: 86 of 1061 (0.08 dB); cutoff 65: 3 of 1622 (0.05 dB).
Per-observer split, 150 scattered observers with SEL 66-92 (`-i` histories, cutoff 70): observers whose peak sample >= 70: 19 of 19 unchanged (the literal claim holds); peak < 70: 42 of 131 changed (max 0.46 dB), including 10 of 87 with SEL >= 70. Example observer: Lmax 75.5 dB, window 65.5..75.5 dB, 215 window samples at 65.3..68.5 dB culled, 6.1% of window energy, -0.27 dB.
Long dwell: a 400 s hover, observer at (-2000,-0) ft: SEL 83.670 dB in the full run, `--db_cutoff 80` -> -inf (peak 57.6 dB is below the 70 dB cull level).
Metrics on the demo, gridstep 492: `-m` without cutoff: `197421AC,72.84,96.4487,0.9546`; with `--db_cutoff 85`: `197421AC,-inf,96.4487,0.1995`.
- **Scenario.** A user wants the 65 dB SEL contour exact and passes --db_cutoff 65 (or 85 on the README case), trusting the README: cells at or above the cutoff shift by tenths of a dB, and for a long-dwell operation a cell above the cutoff comes out -inf. Feeding the same run to -m yields an average of -inf and an area that depends on the cutoff, not on the noise.
- **Proposed change.** (1) Correct README 231-234 to state the sample-level semantics (exact for observers whose peak sample reaches L; SEL cells at or above L are only approximately preserved). (2) Optionally make the promise true for SEL: cull_below = db_cutoff - sel_window_db - 10*log10(D) with D the trajectory's reception duration, since SEL <= Lmax + 10*log10(D); measure the loss of culling efficiency (about 21 dB lower for the 131 s demo track) before adopting. (3) In append_metrics_file skip non-finite cells (or refuse/warn on -m with --db_cutoff), and say the 65 dB threshold and that area is a cell fraction (README says "counts"). (4) Add a CTest that runs --db_cutoff against a full run and checks the exactness claim on observers whose peak reaches the cutoff.
- **Expected impact.** Removes a false exactness promise on a flag whose whole point is trading accuracy for speed; makes -m usable with culling; closes an untested option.
- **Conflicts with docs.** README.md:231-234 states cells at or above 85 dB are unchanged to the last bit; measured otherwise on the same case. niceops.cpp:890-915 comment (exact for observers whose peak reaches the cutoff) is consistent with the measurement.

### main-setup/B.r1.0: --db_cutoff exactness claim is false on the demo: 157 of 592 cells at or above the cutoff change (up to 0.43 dB), and no test covers the flag

- Finder's severity medium, category docs-drift, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: V2 C×2**
- Locations: `README.md:231`, `src/niceops.cpp:890`, `src/niceops.cpp:1319`, `CMakeLists.txt:263`
- **Claim.** The README says the 10 dB margin makes --db_cutoff 'exact wherever it matters' and that 'every cell at or above 85 dB is unchanged to the last bit'. The code comment says the only exception is a dip inside the window, that 11 of 404 observers had one on an EC130 grid, and 'none changed'. The cull is applied to the instantaneous level (cull_below = cutoff - 10), but the output cells are SELs, which exceed the peak level by 10log10(window seconds), about 10-14 dB here. A cell can therefore be at or above the cutoff in SEL while its whole level history sits below the cull threshold, and culled samples inside its 10 dB window are dropped. The README claim dates from 2026-09-18, the day before the 4x EAA removal lifted the demo footprint by 9.93 dB mean, which moved many more cells into that band. No CTest or unit test runs --db_cutoff end to end; the only 'cull' hits in tests/ are cull_range and comments.
- **Evidence given.** Ran the shipped Release build in a scratch copy of the repo root: `niceops --db_cutoff 85 -o cut85.csv`. Log: 'Culled 90626136 of 94334400 evaluations (96.069%) as provably below 75 dB.' Compared with golden/197421_LAE.csv over the 14400 level cells, skipping the 3 header rows.
