Cells with golden >= 85: 592, of which 157 differ, worst 0.4313 dB (85.0824 -> 84.6511). By threshold: >=86: 62 of 457 differ (worst 0.227 dB); >=87: 16 of 353 (0.070 dB); >=88: 0 of 264; >=90 and >=92: 0. Mechanism, from the worst cell's history (-b single observer, -i, with and without the cutoff): its peak level is 74.43 dB, below the 75 dB cull line, while its SEL is 85.08 dB. The window is 1409 samples, and 371 in-window samples came back -inf, all with true levels of at most 68.6 dB. Code: `const double cull_below = options.db_cutoff - sel_window_db;` and `levels[it] = culled_level;` (-infinity). `grep -n 'db_cutoff' CMakeLists.txt tests/*.cpp` finds no test.
- **Scenario.** A user runs the demo with --db_cutoff 85 to get an exact 85 dB contour, as the README promises. Cells that are really 85.0-87 dB SEL come out up to 0.43 dB low, or -inf, and the 85 dB contour moves. The comment at niceops.cpp:898 also tells maintainers this cannot happen.
- **Proposed change.** 1) Rewrite the README paragraph and the niceops.cpp:890-909 comment. The cutoff bounds the instantaneous dBA level, not the SEL. A cell is exact only if no sample in its window falls below cutoff-10, and SEL is typically 10-14 dB above the peak level. Quote the demo measurement: unchanged from about cutoff + 3 dB. 2) Add a ctest that runs the demo with --db_cutoff and checks that cells at or above cutoff + a stated margin equal the golden, since the flag is currently untested on the broadband, spectral and refraction cull paths. 3) Optional, changes behavior: to make the promise true, cull whole observers by bound (skip an observer only when its bounded peak is below the cutoff) and use the exact windowed path for the rest, instead of per-sample culling.
- **Expected impact.** Removes a false exactness guarantee. The test pins the flag's behavior, which nothing does today.
- **Conflicts with docs.** README.md:231-235 and src/niceops.cpp:898-909 assert exactness for cells at or above the cutoff; the measurement contradicts them.
- **Verifier (trace): confirmed, confidence 0.85.** Reproduced exactly with the shipped Release build from a scratch root. The README's demo sentence ("every cell at or above 85 dB is unchanged to the last bit", README.md:233-234) is false. The cells are SEL, while --db_cutoff culls instantaneous level. The rest of the finding needs two corrections. (a) The niceops.cpp:898-909 comment is NOT contradicted. It promises exactness only for observers whose PEAK instantaneous level reaches the cutoff, and I checked that this holds. So the fix belongs in the README, not in that comment. (b) The finding's "SEL exceeds peak by about 10-14 dB" is overstated. Measured on the 592 cells, SEL minus peak has min 1.3, mean 7.8 and max 12.2 dB. The untested-flag claim is also true. The doc/test drift is real, but it is opt-in and low-severity, so I rate it low-to-medium rather than medium.
  - Refined claim: The README's demo sentence (README.md:233-234) is false: with `--db_cutoff 85` on the demo, 157 of the 592 cells with SEL >= 85 dB change (worst 0.43 dB, none -inf). Cells are unchanged only from about cutoff + 3 dB (88 dB) upward. The exactness the code promises is per observer PEAK instantaneous level (peak >= cutoff), and that holds: all 157 changed observers have peak < 85. The README conflates that with SEL cells. Its "-inf" sentence also fails: 2730 cells below 85 SEL come out finite, and so do the 500 of the 592 cells that never reach 85 instantaneously. No CTest exercises --db_cutoff end to end.
- **Verifier (context): confirmed, confidence 0.9.** I reproduced the numbers and found the mechanism. It is real, but the reporter attributed it to the wrong places in two respects and understated it in a third.

1. README.md:233-234 ("every cell at or above 85 dB is unchanged to the last bit") is false on the current demo. With the shipped Release build, `--db_cutoff 85` changes 157 of 592 cells at or above 85, worst 0.4313 dB, exactly as reported. The 3-row header offset matters: skipping only 2 header rows gives 650 cells.

2. The underlying rule is correct, but it applies to the wrong quantity in the README. `cull_below = db_cutoff - sel_window_db` (niceops.cpp:1322) is applied to the instantaneous level, while the cells are SELs. The README's first sentence (231-233, "any observer that reaches L") and the code comment at niceops.cpp:890-909 are correct about an observer's peak instantaneous level. I gave the 592 cells at 85 dB SEL their per-observer peaks by running `-b` with `-i` and no cutoff. Result: 0 of 92 observers with peak >= 85 changed, and 0 of 202 with peak in [80,85). Changes occur only for peaks below 80: 91 of 108 at [75,77) and 23 of 147 at [77,80). At `--db_cutoff 75`, 0 of 1524 observers with peak >= 70 changed, but 1332 of 1853 observers whose SEL is >= 75 while their peak is < 65 changed. So the error is the README demo sentence, plus the same conflation in commit 03d5198's message. It is not the code comment. The reporter misattributes the "cannot happen" claim to niceops.cpp:898. That comment says "observer whose peak reaches the cutoff" and names the dip exception, which is accurate.

3. History supports the reporter's chronology. The README sentence has not changed since 69c159b (2026-09-18). The 4x EAA multiplier was removed in 031015e (09-19). The SEL window became first-to-last within 10 dB in 9d6e986 (09-25). The 03d5198 measurement (2443 cells >= 75, max delta 0) was taken before both. Today the same test has 4871 cells >= 75, of which 1369 change. I did not build 03d5198, so "the footprint change is what exposed it" is plausible but unverified.

4. The test gap is confirmed. `grep -rn 'db_cutoff\|cull' tests/ CMakeLists.txt` finds only cull_range unit tests (tests/test_sphere_database.cpp:819-835) and three comments (CMakeLists.txt:263, test_ground_effect.cpp:208, test_spectral_propagation.cpp:255). Nothing runs the flag end to end, so the wiring is untested: `is_culled`, the -inf fill, and the window on a culled history.

5. Overstated and additional points:
- The scenario's "or -inf" is wrong for cells at or above the cutoff. Their -inf count is 0 at every cutoff tested (65, 75, 80, 85, 90).
- README:236 ("Observers that never reach the cutoff come out as -inf") is also wrong. At 85, 2766 changed cells are finite versus 11078 -inf. Their median drop is 1.46 dB and the worst is 35.2 dB (78.35 -> 43.15). 1767 cells with true SEL < 85 are more than 1 dB low. These look like valid data and will plot.
- The "unchanged from about cutoff + 3 dB" margin does not generalize. The highest changed cell sits +0.84 dB above the cutoff at L=65, +7.02 at 75, +4.19 at 80, +2.66 at 85 and +0.97 at 90. At L=75, 28% of cells at or above the cutoff change.
- niceops.cpp:1372-1373 already says "--db_cutoff ... its culling is a deliberate metric change", which contradicts the README's "exact" framing. The repo sends two messages.

6. Not covered by a rejected item or by the golden path. `--db_cutoff` is off by default. `regression_default` and the goldens do not use it, and `cut0` (`--db_cutoff 0`, nothing culled) is bit-identical to golden.
  - Refined claim: `--db_cutoff L` culls on the instantaneous level, at L-10. It is exact for any observer whose peak instantaneous level reaches roughly L (0 changes in 92 observers at L=85 and in 1524 at L=75; only deep-dip cases could differ). README.md:233-234, "every cell at or above 85 dB is unchanged to the last bit", treats the cutoff as an SEL threshold, and that is false. SEL exceeds peak by 1-12 dB across the cells above 85 in the demo, and by about 10-14 dB for the cells that matter at L=75, so some cells at or above L come from observers whose peak sits below the cull line. Their culled in-window samples are dropped. On the demo, 157 of 592 cells at or above 85 change (worst 0.43 dB), and 1369 of 4871 at L=75. No affected cell is -inf. The code comment at niceops.cpp:890-909 is correct as written about peaks. It lacks the peak-versus-SEL distinction. README:236 ("come out as -inf") is also inaccurate: 2766 of the cells that change at L=85 are finite, and up to 35 dB low. Nothing tests the flag end to end.
  - Verifier's fix: Do the reporter's items 1 and 2, corrected. Skip its item 3 as written.

1. Rewrite README.md:225-238, the `--db_cutoff` row at README:191, and the help text (niceops.cpp:2074) to say:
   - L is a threshold on the instantaneous A-weighted level. Evaluations that provably fall below L-10 are skipped.
   - The result is exact for an observer whose peak level reaches about L. Cite the measured figures: 0 changes in observers with peak >= L-5 at both L=85 and L=75.
   - SEL exceeds peak by roughly 10-14 dB, so a cell with SEL at or above L is not guaranteed exact. It changes when its observer's peak is below the cull line.
   - Delete the demo sentence "every cell at or above 85 dB is unchanged to the last bit" and do not replace it with a fixed margin. The observed margin varies from +0.8 to +7 dB by cutoff.
   - Non-reaching observers come out as -inf or as finite, silently low values (up to 35 dB low at L=85), not only -inf.
   - To get an exact SEL contour at S, set L about 15 dB or more below S (SEL minus peak is about 12-14 dB, but not proven bounded).

2. In the niceops.cpp:890-909 comment, keep the text and add one line: "cells are SELs, which sit 10-14 dB above the observer's peak, so a cell at or above the cutoff is exact only if its peak is". Reconcile it with niceops.cpp:1373, which already calls the culling "a deliberate metric change".

3. Add a ctest that pins the property that actually holds, not an SEL margin. An SEL margin is footprint-dependent, and the EAA removal moved it once already. Options:
   - Run the demo at a coarse gridstep with a low `--db_cutoff L`, then compare with `golden_compare` extended with a floor option (for example `--min_reference S`). Require bit-equality where the reference is at least S = L + 15, with 15 dB set by the 12-14 dB SEL-minus-peak gap seen here.
   - Or run `-b` plus `-i` on a few observers whose peak is at or above L and require they equal the unculled run.
   `golden_compare` has no floor mode today (tests/golden_compare.cpp), so this needs a small extension.

4. Do not adopt the reporter's item 3 (skip an observer only when its bounded peak is below the cutoff, then run the exact windowed path) as the fix for this finding. It keeps the peak-versus-SEL gap, so cells with SEL >= L and peak < L would still be dropped. It also layers the windowed path onto culling, which the niceops.cpp:1372-1373 comment says to keep separate. If the goal is a true SEL cutoff, redefine the flag, for example skip an observer only when 10*log10(sum of bound energy * dt * reception factor) < L, which bounds its SEL from above. That is a separate design decision and probably several times slower than today's 96% cull, though I did not measure it.


## Docs describe the retired SEL window and windowed path

### main-io-sel/B.r1.3: CLAUDE.md and README still describe the retired first-crossing walk-out-from-the-peak window

- Finder's severity medium, category docs-drift, effort S, finder's confidence 0.95, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: V2 C×8**
- Locations: `CLAUDE.md:289`, `CLAUDE.md:318`, `CLAUDE.md:354`, `CLAUDE.md:167`, `CLAUDE.md:835`, `README.md:938`, `src/niceops.cpp:264`
- **Claim.** sel.hpp's window is ten_db_down_interval (first to last sample >= peak-10, dips included; EPNL/14 CFR 36 A36.4.5), adopted deliberately in 9d6e986. The docs still say `peak_range` 'walks out from the peak and stops at the first sample below peak - 10', that the faithful D first-crossing rule is 'left alone under the port-fidelity rule', that `expand_window()` exists, that 70% of states are evaluated and the loop is 1.2x faster, and that the path is skipped for --spectral and not yet extended to --ground_effect. The code and tests contradict all of this.
- **Evidence given.** grep for `expand_window` across src/ and tests/ finds no matches; it appears only in CLAUDE.md:322. tests/test_peak_range.cpp:33-39 pins the new rule ('The D original's walk out from the peak stopped at the dips and gave [2, 4]'). Shipped log for the demo: `Evaluated 88610720 of 94334400 (93.9326%)`, not 70.0%. The windowed path is on for --spectral: niceops.cpp:1272-1310 builds spectral_direction_bounds, and `--ground_effect` is already included through `bound_gains[band] *= ground_gains[band]` (L1307-1308), yet CLAUDE.md:365-378 says the bound 'does not bound the band-limited weighted level' and that extending to --ground_effect 'is not done'. CLAUDE.md:835-838 says `--spectral` is opt-in and 'rejected together with --db_cutoff', but the option help says 'default on' and niceops.cpp:1330 (`cull_by_direction = culling && spectral`) supports the combination. CLAUDE.md:167 says 'CTest defines five tests'; `grep -c add_test CMakeLists.txt` = 53. README:938-952 repeats the 70.0% example and the list of options that skip the path, which wrongly includes --spectral.
- **Scenario.** A maintainer or agent reads CLAUDE.md as the authoritative design record. They will reason from a first-crossing window and a 1.2x speedup, try to restore `expand_window`, or 'extend' the path to ground effect where it already exists.
- **Proposed change.** Rewrite the 'oversampled' and 'Walking out from the peak' sections around the outermost-crossing rule. Keep the stride table but label it as measured under first-crossing. Say the bound now serves only the peak certificate plus tail scans and evaluates about 90-95% on wide grids. Correct the --spectral and --db_cutoff statements and the test count. Update README's 'How much gets evaluated' example. Correct the niceops.cpp:264-267 and sphere_database.hpp:178-183 comments. If finding 1 is acted on, most of these paragraphs go away instead.
- **Expected impact.** Removes claims a future change would be made on; no runtime effect.
- **Conflicts with docs.** This finding is the conflict: the docs against sel.hpp:22-33, tests/test_peak_range.cpp and the log line above.
- **Verifier (trace): confirmed, confidence 0.95.** I re-read every cited location in the current files and ran the shipped build from a scratch directory. Every stale claim the finding lists is wrong, and all of them are checkable facts, not wording. The one imprecision is that it under-counts the drift.

1. Window rule. src/util/sel.hpp:22-33 and 39-59 define ten_db_down_interval as the first to the last sample >= peak-10, dips included. peak_range (sel.hpp:72) calls it. Commit 9d6e986 ("Spectral by default, standard SEL...") adopted this deliberately and regenerated golden/197421_LAE.csv. tests/test_peak_range.cpp:33-35 pins it: "The D original's walk out from the peak stopped at the dips and gave [2, 4]." CLAUDE.md:289 and :332 still say peak_range "walks out from the peak and stops at the **first** sample below peak - 10". CLAUDE.md:312-314 still says the first-crossing rule is "left alone under the port-fidelity rule". The departure is never recorded as an exception to the port-fidelity rule.

2. expand_window(). `grep -rn expand_window src tests tools CMakeLists.txt README.md CLAUDE.md` returns only CLAUDE.md:322. The real helpers are ten_db_down_interval and sel_over_window (sel.hpp:39 and 140), called from niceops.cpp:376-377.

3. The 70% and 1.2x figures. The shipped demo log says `Evaluated 88610720 of 94334400 (93.9326%); the rest is provably outside every observer's 10 dB window.` CLAUDE.md:356 ("70% of states evaluated"), CLAUDE.md:319 ("1.2x faster") and README.md:946 (the example log line, 70.0%) are all stale. At 93.9% evaluated, at most 6.1% of model evaluations are skipped, so a 1.2x speedup cannot hold. I did not time it; the machine is busy and the argument is from counts.

4. --spectral and --ground_effect. niceops.cpp:1377 reads `expand_from_peak = direction_bounds.usable() && !culling && !needs_full_history`, with no spectral exclusion. niceops.cpp:1277-1310 builds spectral_direction_bounds for the spectral path, and `bound_gains[band] *= ground_gains[band]` at :1307-1308 folds in the ground gain. Runs on B407_fixed_load_factor.nod exercised both:
   - `--spectral --gridstep 492`: `Evaluated 2706240 of 2888991 (93.6742%)`.
   - `--ground_effect --gridstep 492`: `Evaluated 2817314 of 2888991 (97.519%)`.
   
   Both took the windowed path. CLAUDE.md:365-378 says --spectral skips it because the bound is broadband, and that ground_effect support "is not done". README.md:950-951 lists --spectral among the options that skip it, while README.md:934-936 itself documents the spectral and ground bounds.

5. --spectral and --db_cutoff. CLAUDE.md:835-838 says "--spectral is opt-in" and is "rejected together with --db_cutoff". The CLI help at niceops.cpp:2076-2079 says "default on". `niceops ... --spectral --db_cutoff 60 --gridstep 492` exits 0. niceops.cpp:1330 (`cull_by_direction = culling && spectral`) implements the combination.

6. Test count. CLAUDE.md:167 says "CTest defines five tests". `grep -c add_test CMakeLists.txt` gives 53.

7. Code comments. niceops.cpp:264-267 says the scans "step over most of the track unevaluated" and that the bounds cost "little beyond the window itself". The demo skips 6.1%. sphere_database.hpp:178-183 still argues the median slack is "under the window, and the peak search collapses inside it". With the tail scans, a state is certified below peak-10 only when its level is roughly slack dB under that threshold. The evaluated set therefore extends well past the 10 dB points, which fits the 94%.

A maintainer would act on this. CLAUDE.md is the design record, it names functions that do not exist, and it says a combination is rejected when it runs.
  - Refined claim: CLAUDE.md, README.md and two code comments describe the window and the windowed path as they were before commit 9d6e986. They call the window the D original's first-crossing walk out from the peak, name a nonexistent expand_window(), give 70% evaluated and a 1.2x speedup (the demo now evaluates 93.9%), and say the path is skipped for --spectral and not extended to --ground_effect (it is on for both). CLAUDE.md:835-838 says --spectral is opt-in and rejected with --db_cutoff, but it is default-on and the combination runs. CLAUDE.md:167 says five CTest tests (there are 53).
  - Verifier's fix: Update the docs and comments as the finding proposes, and add the extra stale items.
- CLAUDE.md "oversampled" and "Walking out from the peak" sections: describe ten_db_down_interval (first to last sample >= peak-10). Keep the stride table but label it as measured under first-crossing. Record that 9d6e986 deliberately departed from D here and regenerated the golden. Replace the 70%, 1.2x and 64%/56% figures with the measured ~94% (demo 93.9%, B407 spectral 93.7%, ground_effect 97.5%).
- State that the bound now serves the peak certificate plus the two tail scans.
- Remove the "--spectral is skipped" and "ground_effect not done" text, and the "--spectral is opt-in / rejected with --db_cutoff" text.
- Correct the CTest count (53), the unit-test count (183) and the golden-provenance paragraph.
- README.md:938-951: new example log line, remove --spectral from the skip list, and reword "finds the peak and walks out".
- niceops.cpp:264-267 and sphere_database.hpp:178-183: drop "step over most of the track" and "peak search collapses inside [the window]".
- niceops.cpp:2405 and 2411: fix the "(--no-spectral or --db_cutoff)" wording.
- **Verifier (context): confirmed, confidence 0.94.** Every specific factual claim I checked holds, and I checked them against the current files and by running the shipped build in a scratch directory.

The rule change is deliberate and documented in code, not in docs. `src/util/sel.hpp:22-33` defines `ten_db_down_interval` as the first to the last sample at or above peak-10, dips included. It says it "replaces the D original's walk out from the peak ... up to 0.7 dB at a single microphone". `git show 9d6e986` shows `expand_window` deleted from `sel.hpp` and `golden/197421_LAE.csv` regenerated (240 lines). That commit did not touch CLAUDE.md, which last changed in 84462d7. `tests/test_peak_range.cpp:33-39` pins the new rule. So this meets the port-fidelity bar and is not a regression. The docs simply were not updated.

CLAUDE.md is stale in each place the reporter cited:
- Line 289 says `peak_range` "walks out from the peak and stops at the first sample below peak - 10".
- Line 322 names `expand_window()`, which no longer exists.
- Lines 318-320, 332 and 354 still describe the walk-out design, the "1.2x" speedup and "70% of states evaluated". Lines 318-333 also still say the `== threshold` tie rule decides ~0.7% of observers.
- Line 301 calls the outermost-crossing window "itself a metric change", but that change is the shipped rule.
- Lines 365-372 say the path is skipped for `--spectral`. In fact `niceops.cpp:1377` is `expand_from_peak = direction_bounds.usable() && !culling && !needs_full_history`, and `niceops.cpp:1272-1310` builds `spectral_direction_bounds` when spectral is on. The skip conditions that remain true are `-a`, `-i`, `--band_history`, `-D` and `--db_cutoff`.
- Lines 374-378 say extending the path to `--ground_effect` "is not done". `niceops.cpp:1279-1310` already raises the band gains by `ground_gain_bounds`, and the CTest cases `ground_effect_windowed_is_exact` and `plate_windowed_is_exact` exist.
- Lines 832-838 say `--spectral` is opt-in and is "rejected together with --db_cutoff". The option help says "default on", and `niceops.cpp:1330` is `cull_by_direction = culling && spectral`. I ran `--ground_effect --db_cutoff 60` on the B407 database. It was accepted (rc=0) and logged "Culled ... as provably below 50 dB".
- Line 166 says "CTest defines five tests"; `grep -c add_test CMakeLists.txt` gives 53.

README.md lines 938-952 show the "Evaluated 66041828 of 94334400 (70.0%)" example and list `--spectral` among the options that skip the path. Lines 934-936 of the same README say `--db_cutoff` works on the spectral path, so the README contradicts itself.

The demo run shows the real figure. `niceops -S state/ci_state.csv` logged "Evaluated 88610720 of 94334400 (93.9326%)". `grep -rn expand_window src tests` finds nothing.

The reporter's proposed fix is mostly right, with the corrections listed under fix_recommendation.
  - Refined claim: Confirmed as docs drift, with a wider footprint than reported. The SEL window changed from the D first-crossing walk to the outermost-crossing 10 dB-down interval in 9d6e986. That commit regenerated the demo golden and updated `sel.hpp` and the tests, but not CLAUDE.md or the README's "How much gets evaluated" section. As a result CLAUDE.md and README describe the following that no longer exist:

- The `expand_window` walk from the peak, which is gone.
- A 70% evaluated fraction and a 1.2x speedup.
- The tie rule and the ~0.7% fragility, which belong to the old first-crossing rule.
- The claim that the path is skipped for `--spectral`, that `--spectral` is opt-in and that it is rejected with `--db_cutoff`.
- The claim that extending the path to `--ground_effect` is not done.
- "Five" CTest tests.

Beyond the reporter's list, these are also stale:
- CLAUDE.md:171 says "All 7 cases pass (~26k assertions)"; `niceops_unit_tests --list-tests` now reports 183 test cases.
- The CLAUDE.md "Golden reference files" section says `golden/197421_LAE.csv` was last regenerated when the 4x EAA multiplier was removed. It was regenerated again in 9d6e986 for the SEL rule and reception time, so it is even less D-derived than the docs say.
- README.md:168 and :176 still document `--spectral` as opt-in ("Needs a database carrying spectra", "Needs `--spectral`"), although it is the default.
- The user-facing errors at `niceops.cpp:2404-2405` and `:2410-2411` call `--db_cutoff` a "broadband path", which is false.

One reporter claim needs narrowing. The `niceops.cpp:264-267` comment's "a little over half the track" is still about right. I measured the current-rule window at a mean of 60% of the track (57% under first-crossing) on the 441-observer demo grid. What is wrong there is "costs little beyond the window itself" (94% is evaluated, against a 60% window). What is wrong at `sphere_database.hpp:178-183` is "the peak search collapses inside it" and "nothing to save".
  - Verifier's fix: Do a docs-and-comments-only edit. It is safe with no build or golden impact: output_effect is none-bitwise, and there is no risk to the goldens or byte-identity guarantees.

**Sequencing.** Decide finding 1 first. If the windowed path is retired or reworked, delete the "Walking out from the peak" section rather than rewriting it. If it stays, rewrite it as below.

**Corrections to the reporter's proposal.**
1. Do not relabel the whole stride table (CLAUDE.md:294-298) as "measured under first-crossing". It has two columns, and the outermost-crossing column is the shipped rule.
   - Keep the section's conclusion: decimation is still unsafe, since the current rule still moves cells by 1.1-1.5 dB at strides 2 to 64.
   - Reword line 301 ("is itself a metric change") as a change that was made in 9d6e986.
   - Drop or qualify the first-crossing "~0.7% of observers ... port-fidelity rule" paragraph and the `== threshold` tie-rule remarks. Both ends are now `>= threshold`.
2. Replace "70%" / "1.2x" with the measured figures: about 94% evaluated on the demo, about 97.5% on the B407 ground-effect case, and no CPU difference against a march. Say that the bound now serves the peak certificate plus the tail scans, and that it skips only about 6% of states.
3. Fix the skip list to `-a`, `-i`, `--band_history`, `-D`, `--db_cutoff` and a database with a negative EAA. Remove `--spectral`. State that the ground gain is bounded through `ground_gain_bounds` and that the ctest `*_windowed_is_exact` cases cover it. Delete the "not done" paragraph.
4. Fix CLAUDE.md:832-838: `--spectral` is the default, with a broadband fallback when the database has no spectra, and it composes with `--db_cutoff` via `cull_by_direction`.
5. Update CLAUDE.md:166 (53 CTest cases), :171 (183 unit-test cases) and the golden provenance note. Record the SEL-window departure, with its measured 0.7 dB case, next to the 4x-EAA precedent as a deliberate exception to port fidelity.
6. In README, correct the "How much gets evaluated" example and skip list (938-952). Also correct the `--spectral` and `--ground_effect` rows (168, 176), which still say opt-in and "Needs `--spectral`".
7. In code comments, fix only the parts that are wrong.
   - `niceops.cpp:264-267`: "costs little beyond the window itself" (keep "a little over half the track").
   - `sphere_database.hpp:178-183`: "collapses inside it / nothing to save".
   - The error strings at `niceops.cpp:2404-2405` and `:2410-2411` that call `--db_cutoff` a broadband path.

### main-loop/A.r1.7: CLAUDE.md and README describe a first-crossing walk-out window, a non-existent expand_window(), and say the windowed path skips --spectral and does not cover --ground_effect; the code does the opposite

- Finder's severity medium, category docs-drift, effort S, finder's confidence 0.95, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: C×8**
- Locations: `CLAUDE.md:289`, `CLAUDE.md:311`, `CLAUDE.md:322`, `CLAUDE.md:332`, `CLAUDE.md:365`, `CLAUDE.md:838`, `README.md:942`, `src/util/sel.hpp:22`
- **Claim.** Since commit 9d6e986 the window is ten_db_down_interval(): first to last sample at or above peak-10, dips included (sel.hpp:22-60), found by scanning in from both ends with the bound as certainly_below (niceops.cpp:361-376). CLAUDE.md still says peak_range "walks out from the peak and stops at the first sample below peak - 10", that this first-crossing rule is a faithful D port left alone, that moving to the outermost crossing "is itself a metric change" (it already happened), that expand_window() exists in sel.hpp, that the walk means "nothing beyond it needs a bound at all", and that the path is skipped for --spectral ("--db_cutoff is refused there") and that extending it to --ground_effect "is not done". All of that is implemented: spectral_direction_bounds with ground_gain_bounds (niceops.cpp:1272-1315), spectral --db_cutoff (1330-1335), CTests ground_effect_windowed/plate_windowed/stratified_windowed_is_exact.
- **Evidence given.** `grep -rn expand_window src tests README.md CLAUDE.md` -> only CLAUDE.md:322. Running `niceops -d databases/B407_fixed_load_factor.nod -v databases/B407.json --ground_effect --gridstep 492` logs "Direction bounds built in 0.0188 s" and "Evaluated 2817314 of 2888991 (97.519%)", and adding `--db_cutoff 80` logs "Culled 1778243 of 2888991 evaluations (61.5524%)": both spectral+ground paths use the bounds. Windowed and marched outputs were byte-identical (cmp) for 29 shipped S-76D/AW139/B205 tracks (broadband), and for spectral, ground-effect, plate, linear and stratified refraction (including a duct-like profile and scattering), 150 scattered observers within 3-300 m of the track, and thread counts 1/3/8.
- **Scenario.** An agent or maintainer following CLAUDE.md proposes "extending the peak search to --ground_effect" (done), reinstates the first-crossing walk to match "the D original", or reasons about decimation error using the stale first-crossing table, and breaks the standard-SEL window that golden/197421_LAE.csv was regenerated for.
- **Proposed change.** Rewrite the affected CLAUDE.md sections (oversampling, walking out, the skip list, the propagation-paths bullet) and README.md:950 to describe ten_db_down_interval, the two-ended scan with certainly_below, spectral and ground-effect coverage, and spectral --db_cutoff; drop expand_window(); reword the port-fidelity note on the SEL window.
- **Expected impact.** Prevents misdirected work and regressions of the window semantics; no code change.
- **Conflicts with docs.** CLAUDE.md lines cited versus src/util/sel.hpp and src/niceops.cpp as above.

### db-eval/B.r1.1: CLAUDE.md and README describe the windowed peak search as evaluating ~70% of samples and running 1.2x faster; since the outermost-crossing SEL window it evaluates 94% and gains ~2.5%

- Finder's severity medium, category docs-drift, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: C×8**
- Locations: `CLAUDE.md:289`, `README.md:940`, `src/util/sel.hpp:26`, `src/niceops.cpp:258`
- **Claim.** Commit 9d6e986 replaced the D walk-out-from-the-peak window (stop at the first sample below peak-10) by 'first to last sample >= threshold' (ten_db_down_interval), and the windowed path now has to prove every state outside that window is below the threshold with the bound instead of never looking at it. CLAUDE.md still says the loop is '1.2x faster than evaluating everything', that 70% of states are evaluated against a 56% floor, that `peak_range` walks out and stops at the first crossing, that `expand_window()` exists (it does not; grep finds it only in CLAUDE.md), and that ~0.7% of observers depend on a first-crossing tie rule. README shows a log line 'Evaluated 66041828 of 94334400 (70.0%)'. On the current tree the demo evaluates 93.9% and the window itself is 59.0%, so 33M evaluations (35% of all samples) are made outside the window, and the whole windowing machinery buys about 2.5% of the acoustic loop on the demo.
- **Evidence given.** Repo Release binary, scratch run dir, `niceops -S state/x.csv`: log 'Evaluated 88610720 of 94334400 (93.9326%)'. Same for B407/AS350/EC130_fixed_load_factor (spectral, S-76D track): 92.0267%, 92.263%, 94.4877%.
Instrumented scratch copy (per-observer counters, output unchanged): window total 55,665,726 samples = 59.0089%; evaluated outside the window 32,944,994 = seed 765,064 + main pass 10,162,244 + end-scan 22,017,686. Range/slack of those outside evaluations (mean bound-minus-level slack): 1-2 km 12.7M samples at 12.6 dB, 2-4 km 15.2M at 16.2 dB, 0.5-1 km 3.9M at 10.3 dB. (CLAUDE.md quotes a 8.6 dB median for the slack.) Seeding the peak perfectly (oracle best) leaves the total evaluated at exactly 88,610,720, so the seed sweep is not the cause; the total is the number of states whose bound is >= the final threshold.
CPU loop time, -j 1, six alternating runs (busy machine, min of 6): windowed 3.01 s (3.01-3.31), forced march via -D 3.09 s (3.09-3.15): 2.6% gain.
- **Scenario.** A maintainer reading CLAUDE.md to decide whether the bound machinery earns its complexity, or tuning bin counts against the '70% vs 56% floor' numbers, is working from measurements of an algorithm that is no longer in the tree.
- **Proposed change.** Update CLAUDE.md ('The SEL integral is oversampled...' and 'Walking out from the peak...') and the README sample log to the current rule and numbers: window = first..last sample >= peak-10; evaluated 93.9% vs 59.0% window floor on the demo (92-94% spectral); the end scan, not the peak search, is the excess; `expand_window` -> `ten_db_down_interval`; the 0.7% first-crossing sensitivity no longer applies to the default. Say that the speed-up over a march is now a few percent on broadband and that its value is the expensive paths (spectral ~150 ns/eval, ground ~1 us/eval). Then decide deliberately (see the tightening finding) whether to keep the path as is.
- **Expected impact.** Removes a wrong cost model from the docs; no runtime change.
- **Conflicts with docs.** CLAUDE.md:319 ('1.2x faster'), :356 ('70% ... 56% floor'), :322 (`expand_window()`), :289/:332 ('walks out from the peak'), README.md:942-946.

- **Scenario.** A maintainer or agent reading CLAUDE.md's 'obvious next move' item plans to build the ground-effect bound that already exists, or believes the default path is the EAA one and reasons about goldens and timings for the wrong configuration. The demo (S-76D_M3.nod, no spectra) still takes the broadband fallback, which is why the goldens are unaffected and the drift went unnoticed.
- **Proposed change.** Rewrite the five spots: default is spectral with a broadband fallback for databases without spectra; the peak search and --db_cutoff both use Database::spectral_direction_bounds (with ground/plate gain bounds); the skip list is -a, -i, --band_history, -D, --db_cutoff, unusable bounds; databases/B407_fixed_load_factor.nod carries 31 bands; drop or replace the 'not done' paragraph. Fix README 168/176/283-290/950 to match the CLI help.
- **Expected impact.** Removes a misleading design note that invites redoing finished work, and makes README's flag table match --help.
- **Conflicts with docs.** This finding is the conflict: the statements above against src/niceops.cpp and the shipped CTest cases.

### main-setup/B.r1.1: CLAUDE.md and README still describe first-crossing, 70%-evaluated, 1.2x windowing; the code is outermost-crossing, evaluates 93.9% on the demo, and shows no speedup there

- Finder's severity medium, category docs-drift, effort S, finder's confidence 0.85, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: V1 C×8**
- Locations: `CLAUDE.md:286`, `CLAUDE.md:317`, `CLAUDE.md:835`, `README.md:938`, `src/util/sel.hpp:17`, `src/niceops.cpp:1279`
- **Claim.** Commit 9d6e986 (2026-09-25) replaced the walk-out-from-peak window with the outermost crossing: ten_db_down_interval takes the first to the last sample at or above peak-10, dips included, and sel.hpp:17-31 says so. It also made spectral the default and extended the bounds to --spectral, --ground_effect, plates and refraction. The docs were not updated. CLAUDE.md still says: peak_range stops at the first sample below peak-10 (lines 289, 332); expand_window() exists in sel.hpp (322; it does not); the windowed path makes the loop 1.2x faster (319) and evaluates 70% of states against a 56% floor (356); --spectral skips the path (365-366); extending it to --ground_effect is 'not done' (373); --spectral is opt-in and rejected with --db_cutoff (835-838). README.md:946 shows 'Evaluated 66041828 of 94334400 (70.0%)' and README.md:950 says the path is skipped with --spectral. In the code, expand_from_peak is true for spectral and ground runs, and --db_cutoff works on the spectral path. The window is now roughly the whole track for most of the default grid, so the machinery no longer pays on the demo case.
- **Evidence given.** Default demo log (`niceops -S state/ci_state.csv`, scratch root): 'Evaluated 88610720 of 94334400 (93.9326%)' (docs: 70.0%). B407 spectral --ground_effect coarse run: 'Evaluated 2817314 of 2888991 (97.519%)', so the spectral path does use the window. Grid half-extent 4921/2000/1000/300 ft gives 93.9/81.0/62.7/32.4% evaluated. A/B at -j 1, acoustic-loop wall time in seconds, 8 alternating runs on a loaded machine, byte-identical output (cmp): windowed default 2.525 2.566 2.8 2.926 2.94 2.96 2.962 2.987; full march via `--db_cutoff=-1000` 2.479 2.496 2.643 2.74 2.856 2.869 2.899 2.946. An earlier 5-pair set gave windowed 2.91-3.03 against march 2.70-2.98. Hypothesis tested and rejected: caching the two per-sample `(observer - pos).norm()` calls in the windowed reception weight (scratch-tree switch, byte-identical) gave medians of about 2.85 s for base, cached and march alike, so it is not the missing cost. The `-j 1` timings are noisy because the machine is shared.
- **Scenario.** A maintainer or agent reads CLAUDE.md, believes the windowed path is worth 1.2x and exact-by-first-crossing, and makes a decision on that basis: keeps building on DirectionBounds, avoids the --spectral path, or re-derives the tie-rule fragility that the code no longer has.
- **Proposed change.** Update CLAUDE.md sections 'The SEL integral is oversampled...', 'Walking out from the peak...' and the --spectral bullet at 835-838, plus README 'How much gets evaluated', to the current window rule, the current 93.9% figure and the current applicability list. Re-measure whether the windowed path still pays on the default grid. It stays clearly useful only for tight grids (32-63% evaluated) and, in principle, for the 1160 ns/eval ground path. If it does not pay, the maintainers can decide to gate it on expected window size. Do not remove it on the basis of this single A/B.
- **Expected impact.** The design docs match the code. The re-measurement decides whether the peak-search machinery (DirectionBounds table build, visited stamps, second bound pass) is worth its complexity for the default case.
- **Conflicts with docs.** CLAUDE.md:319, 322, 332, 356, 365-373, 835-838; README.md:940-950.
- **Verifier (context): confirmed, confidence 0.9.** The docs drift is real and I reproduced every checkable number. Commit 9d6e986 (2026-09-25) replaced the walk-out rule with ten_db_down_interval. It edited README.md only (57 lines: the spectral --db_cutoff line and the ground-model options) and did not touch CLAUDE.md or the README "How much gets evaluated" section. `git log -S expand_window` shows the function added in 6577bbe and removed in 9d6e986. `grep expand_window src` is now empty, yet CLAUDE.md:322 still names it. Stale text confirmed at these lines:
- CLAUDE.md:289 and 332 (first-crossing walk, "stops at the first sample below peak-10").
- CLAUDE.md:294-303 (table treats the outermost crossing as a hypothetical metric change).
- CLAUDE.md:311-313 (a "faithful port of D" fragility), 319 (1.2x), 326 (`==` tie rule), 356 (70% vs 56% floor).
- CLAUDE.md:365-366 (path skipped with --spectral) and 373 ("not done" for --ground_effect).
- CLAUDE.md:835-838 (--spectral opt-in, rejected with --db_cutoff).
- README.md:942-951.

Run checks:
- The demo logs 'Evaluated 88610720 of 94334400 (93.9326%)'.
- The output is byte-identical to golden/197421_LAE.csv.
- B407 spectral logs 93.7%, and 97.5% with --ground_effect.
- `--spectral --ground_effect --db_cutoff 60` exits 0.
- The ground_effect_windowed and ground_effect_marched ctests exist.
- niceops.cpp:1377 sets expand_from_peak = direction_bounds.usable() && !culling && !needs_full_history, with no spectral or ground exclusion.

The reporter's causal explanation is wrong, and it matters for the fix. "The window is now roughly the whole track" is refuted. From `-i` histories of the same 441 observers (--gridstep 492) I computed the outermost window (first to last sample >= peak-10). It is 60.0% of the track on average, median 59.4%, against 57% for the old first-crossing rule and the documented 56% floor. On that grid the run evaluated 94.5%. So about 40% of states lie outside the window and about 86% of those are still evaluated. That is not the window widening. The outermost rule makes the code prove each tail state below peak-10 with the direction bound to skip it, which is the "bound tighter than 10 dB" requirement CLAUDE.md's "Culling states the SEL window will discard" item and the "Walking out" section say the design deliberately avoids. The walk-out rule needed the bound only to beat `best`. The docs should say that.

The "no speedup" claim holds and is stronger than the reporter's noisy wall-clock evidence. I compared the windowed default against `--db_cutoff=-1000` (full march, byte-identical output) at -j 1 using `/usr/bin/time -l`. Instructions retired are load-independent. They cover the whole process, including the common database load.

| Grid (evaluated) | Windowed instructions | Full march instructions | Difference in instructions | Difference in cycles |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Default (93.9%) | 76.08G (3 runs, stable) | 74.63G | +1.9% | about +1% |
| Half-extent 1000 ft (61.9%) | 31.96G | 37.57G | -15% | -11% |
| Half-extent 300 ft (30.9%) | 41.9G | 70.7G | -41% | -35% |

CPU time over 6 alternating pairs was 3.96 s (windowed median) against 3.89 s (march), which is noise. So the machinery is break-even to about 2% worse on the default grid and still clearly pays on tight grids. I do not support gating on expected window size, and I do not support removing the machinery.

Two reporter details are slightly off:
- Refraction bounds came from 1ab9e60, not 9d6e986, and CLAUDE.md already documents them.
- The README was partly updated in 9d6e986 (line 934 says --db_cutoff works on the spectral path), so "the docs were not updated" applies fully only to CLAUDE.md and the "How much gets evaluated" section.
  - Refined claim: CLAUDE.md and README describe a superseded design. The code (sel.hpp:22-60, niceops.cpp:349-380) uses the outermost-crossing 10 dB-down interval, in reception time. It applies to the spectral and --ground_effect paths as well as broadband, and --db_cutoff works with --spectral. The docs still say first-crossing walk-out, `expand_window`, 1.2x, 70% evaluated against a 56% floor, path skipped with --spectral, --ground_effect "not done", and --spectral rejected with --db_cutoff.

The demo now evaluates 93.9%, not 70%. That is not because the window is wide: the true window is about 60% of the track. It is because states in the two tails are only skipped if the direction bound proves them below peak-10, and about 86% of the outside states cannot be proven, so they are evaluated. On the default grid the windowed path retires about 2% more instructions than a full march.

The drift also covers two adjacent items the reporter did not list:
- CLAUDE.md:138 says the golden was regenerated "at the commit that removed the multiplier", but it was regenerated again at 9d6e986 (`git log -- golden/197421_LAE.csv`).
- CLAUDE.md:~969-972 says the 4x EAA multiplier is the one deliberate departure from D, but the SEL window rule and reception-time weighting are a second, deliberate, golden-changing one.

The user-facing error strings at niceops.cpp:2405 and 2411 still say the broadband path is "--no-spectral or --db_cutoff", which is no longer true.
  - Verifier's fix: Do the documentation fix, and correct the mechanism while doing it.

1. Rewrite CLAUDE.md "The SEL integral is oversampled..." (282-314) and "Walking out from the peak..." (316-375).
   - State the current rule: outermost crossing, dips inside, in reception time. Drop the `expand_window`, tie-rule and 1.2x text.
   - Move the first-crossing versus outermost table into history, or relabel it as the pre-9d6e986 measurement.
   - Keep the conclusion that decimation is unsafe. The table's outermost column (1.13 dB at stride 2) still supports it.
   - Record the measured behavior: about 60% window, 93.9% evaluated on the demo, and 62% and 31% on 2000 ft and 600 ft grids.
   - Explain why: the outermost rule reintroduced per-state proofs against the threshold, so only about 14% of the outside states are skipped.
   - Record the instruction counts (+2% on the default grid, -15% and -41% on tight grids) so nobody re-derives them or gates or removes the path on one noisy A/B.
   - Update the applicability list (365-373) to: skipped only for -a, -i, --band_history, -D, --db_cutoff, and databases with a negative EAA. Spectral, --ground_effect, plates and refraction are covered.
2. Fix CLAUDE.md:831-838 (spectral is the default, with broadband fallback for databases without spectra, and --db_cutoff works with it). Fix CLAUDE.md:138 (golden regenerated again at 9d6e986). Fix the port-fidelity exception at ~969 (add the SEL window rule and reception-time weighting).
3. Update README "How much gets evaluated" (938-951): a current log line, a current applicability list, and the wording "finds the peak and walks out".
4. Fix the two error strings at niceops.cpp:2405 and 2411 so they no longer name --db_cutoff as a broadband path. This changes message text only.
5. Do not gate or remove the peak-search machinery. If anyone wants the default-grid case to pay, the lever is the tail proofs (a tighter bound on the far-range states). That is a new hypothesis I did not test. Check it against CLAUDE.md's rejected "Culling states the SEL window will discard" item before starting.

### db-eval/B.r1.4: Docs and a test header say no shipped database carries spectra and --spectral is opt-in; both are false, and the documented cost model is not the default path

- Finder's severity low, category docs-drift, effort S, finder's confidence 0.85, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: C×8**
- Locations: `CLAUDE.md:803`, `CLAUDE.md:831`, `README.md:247`, `tests/test_spectral_propagation.cpp:3`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1458`
- **Claim.** Commit 9d6e986 made spectral propagation the default and the *_fixed_load_factor databases (AS350, EC130, B407, AW139, B205, Be206L3, RO-44, RO-66; S-76D_fixed_load_factor has none) carry 31-band spectra, yet CLAUDE.md says 'No database in databases/ carries spectra' and '--spectral is opt-in: the goldens are the broadband path', README says 'None of the databases in databases/ carries spectra', and test_spectral_propagation.cpp opens with the same claim while its last case (line 259) loads AS350/EC130_fixed_load_factor.nod and REQUIREs has_spectra(). The cost model in CLAUDE.md (19 ns/eval, 1.55 CPU-s loop) describes the broadband demo only; the default path for most shipped databases is about 8x more expensive per evaluation. A comment in eval_spectrum also claims the compiler vectorizes the band loop across bands, which it does not on Apple clang/arm64.
- **Evidence given.** src/niceops.cpp:2076-2080: `--spectral,!--no-spectral` 'default on; falls back to broadband when the database carries no spectra'; niceops.cpp:1973 `bool spectral = true`. `niceops -d databases/AS350_fixed_load_factor.nod -v databases/S-76D.json`: real 1.94 s, user 19.93 s (14 cores, busy machine), 'Acoustic calculations completed in 1.69 seconds', 'Evaluated 86812833 of 94334400 (92.0267%)'.
Single-thread cost of one spectral evaluation on AS350 (31 bands, 28 active), CPU time, observers walking a ground grid, packed spectra: full spectral_level_at_observer 149-161 ns = straight_ray+lookup_angles 8.5 + eval_spectrum 75 (of which 14.5 fixed overhead, ~55 the band loop) + weighted_level 54 (28 libm exp + log10) + ~18 other. (Random observers instead of a walking grid: 202 ns, the 4 KB of packed values per call stop hitting L1.)
The fast band loop (`LBB44_62` in the -O3 arm64 assembly) is scalar: `ldr d`/`fmadd d`, 17 loads and 19 FMAs per band. A standalone copy of the loop measured 34 ns for 31 bands; an explicit 2-wide vector_size version 43 ns and a restrict-parameter version 33 ns, so vectorizing it does not help.
- **Scenario.** A contributor trusts 'spectral is opt-in', profiles the broadband path to speed up the default run, and optimises the 10% share that most databases no longer use; or spends a day trying to make the band loop vectorize.
- **Proposed change.** Fix the three statements and the test-file header (say which shipped databases carry spectra), add the spectral per-evaluation breakdown above to the cost model, reword the eval_spectrum comment ('one branch-free loop over contiguous bands; measured scalar on clang/arm64 and explicit 2-wide lanes were slower'), and note the 'Evaluated' fraction is ~92% there too.
- **Expected impact.** Correct cost model; prevents repeating the vectorization experiment.
- **Conflicts with docs.** CLAUDE.md:803, 831-836; README.md:247; tests/test_spectral_propagation.cpp:3; the eval_spectrum comment at sphere_database.cpp:1461-1464.


## Other docs drift

### trajectory-geodetic/B.r1.6: Docs say 'transverse Mercator' but the projection is the American polyconic; the README/CLAUDE.md also contradict themselves on whether alt_m is recognized

- Finder's severity low, category docs-drift, effort S, finder's confidence 0.95, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: Rc**
- Locations: `src/geodetic.hpp:8`, `src/geodetic.cpp:60`, `README.md:1050`, `CLAUDE.md:840`, `README.md:985`, `CLAUDE.md:865`, `README.md:108`, `CLAUDE.md:213`
- **Claim.** (a) geodetic.cpp:79-84 computes `x = ms*sin(lon*sin(lat))` and `y = (M(lat) - M(lat0)) + ms*(1 - cos(lon*sin(lat)))`, with ms = N cos(lat)/sin(lat). That is PROJ's `poly` (American polyconic) forward, not a transverse Mercator. geodetic.hpp:8, geodetic.cpp:60, README.md:1050 and CLAUDE.md:840 all say 'transverse Mercator'. (b) README.md:989-990 says `alt_m` 'is deliberately *not*' recognized and CLAUDE.md:865-867 repeats it, but since --units landed the code recognizes `alt m` / `altitude m` / `alt msl m` under --units m (trajectory.cpp:99-105), and README.md:108-110 / CLAUDE.md:213-215 say `alt_m` is checked against --units. The README column table (lines 985-986) still says `alt` is 'Feet above mean sea level' and vE/vN/vUp are 'ft/s' without mentioning --units.
- **Evidence given.** PROJ cs2cs with ellps=WGS84, lon_0=-74.40996141, lat_0=39.82914323, points from 3 km to 35 km away:
`+proj=poly` matches LocalFrame::to_local to 1e-6 m at all four points: e.g. (39.85, -74.38) -> 2564.109808, 2316.189117 in both; (40.10, -74.0) -> 34956.941022, 30154.852888 in both.
`+proj=tmerc` differs: x 2564.109877 (0.07 mm), 9403.713154 vs 9403.709743 (3.4 mm at ~10 km), 34957.116233 vs 34956.941022 (0.18 m at 35 km).
The existing tests only check the projection against the shipped tracks' own x/y (sub-meter), so nothing pins which projection it is.
- **Scenario.** Someone cross-checking with PROJ/pyproj, or replacing the transform, picks `tmerc` on the strength of the comments and gets a mismatch that grows with distance. A meter-track user reads README:989 and concludes `alt_m` is unsupported, or reads README:108 and concludes it is.
- **Proposed change.** Rename to 'American polyconic (PROJ `poly`, as in chrberger/WGS84toCartesian)' in the four places. Optionally pin three PROJ `poly` values in tests/test_geodetic.cpp. Delete the two 'alt_m is deliberately not recognized' passages and make the README column table say 'in --units'.
- **Expected impact.** Docs match code; no behavior change.

### main-setup/B.r1.6: --help text for --grid, --gridstep, --receiver_height and --path_smoothing still says feet; the unit follows --units

- Finder's severity low, category docs-drift, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: Rc V1**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:2118`, `src/niceops.cpp:2200`, `src/niceops.cpp:2229`
- **Claim.** These four options are the ones the --units change moved. README.md:164, 178 and 97-98 correctly say 'in --units'. Their CLI11 descriptions were not updated: --grid 'Observer grid bounds in feet', --gridstep 'in feet (default 82.021 ...)', --receiver_height 'ft (default 3.937 ...)', --path_smoothing 'How far, in feet RMS ... Default 10'. --trim, --ground_elevation, --layer_depth and --roughness do say 'in --units'.
- **Evidence given.** `niceops --help` prints: '--grid FLOAT x 4  Observer grid bounds in feet: xmin xmax ymin ymax. Default +/-4921.26 (= 1500 m).'; '--gridstep FLOAT  Observer grid spacing in feet (default 82.021, = 25 m).'; '--receiver_height FLOAT  Receiver height above ground for --ground_effect, ft (default 3.937, = 1.2 m).'; '--path_smoothing FLOAT  How far, in feet RMS ... Default 10.' With `--units m` all four take meters. The code converts them only when count() > 0 (niceops.cpp:2309-2320), so the defaults stay physical quantities.
- **Scenario.** A user running with --units m reads --help, sees 'feet', and either converts by hand (the grid comes out 3.28 times too small) or stops trusting --units.
- **Proposed change.** Reword the four descriptions to 'in --units (default 1500 m / 4921.26 ft, ...)' as README.md:164 does. Also fix the --path_smoothing 'Default 10' to say the default is 10 ft (3.048 m) whatever --units says.
- **Expected impact.** Help matches README and behavior.
- **Verifier (context): confirmed, confidence 0.93.** The claim is true. It is a real but minor documentation defect, and correcting it is safe and cheap. I checked five things.

1. The shipped `build/niceops --help` (binary built Sep 29, HEAD 84462d7, clean tree) prints "feet" or "ft" for `--grid` (line 32), `--gridstep` (line 33), `--receiver_height` (line 55) and `--path_smoothing` (line 62).
2. In the same output, `--trim`, `--ground_elevation`, `--layer_depth` and `--roughness` say "in --units". So the four stale options are the ones whose semantics moved.
3. Under `--units m` the values really are meters. I ran `niceops -S state/ci_state.csv --gridstep 150 --grid -1500 1500 -1500 1500 --units ft` and the same with `--units m`. The two runs use identical numbers on the command line. Under `m` the output header reads "x(m)" and the axis values are exactly -1500, -1350, ... 1350 in meters. The help text "in feet" is therefore wrong whenever `--units m` is given.
4. The cause is clear from history. Commit e27cd4e ("Put every command-line length in feet") wrote these strings. Commit 07d3053 ("Add --units") converted the same four options in code, with `to_meters(gridstep_option, ...)` and friends at niceops.cpp:2370-2376, but left the four descriptions alone. The README was updated: line 164 says "in `--units`", and lines 93-98 and 178 agree. So the drift is unintentional, not a deliberate choice. CLAUDE.md never says these strings must stay in feet. It only says defaults are physical quantities, which the suggested rewording preserves.
5. Nothing greps or depends on the strings. No test, CMake file or workflow references them, and a repo-wide grep excluding build directories finds only the four source lines.

Corrections to the report:
- The cited line numbers are wrong. The current lines are niceops.cpp:2055-2060 (`--grid` and `--gridstep`), 2114-2116 (`--receiver_height`) and 2137-2140 (`--path_smoothing`). The cited 2118-2123, 2200-2202 and 2229-2233 hold unrelated options and input-handling code.
- The reporter missed README.md:999, a narrative paragraph that says "`--path_smoothing` says how far, in feet RMS **per axis**" while README.md:149 correctly says "in `--units`".
  - Refined claim: The CLI11 descriptions for `--grid` (src/niceops.cpp:2055-2057), `--gridstep` (2059-2060), `--receiver_height` (2114-2116) and `--path_smoothing` (2137-2140) still say "feet" or "ft". Since commit 07d3053 the values are in `--units`, and are converted to meters only when the option was given (`count() > 0`, niceops.cpp:2370-2376). `niceops --help` is wrong for anyone running `--units m`. `--path_smoothing` "Default 10" is also ambiguous, because the default is 10 ft (3.048 m) whatever `--units` says. README.md:999 has the same stale wording ("in feet RMS per axis").
  - Verifier's fix: Edit the four strings in src/niceops.cpp.
- `--grid`: "Observer grid bounds in --units: xmin xmax ymin ymax. Default +/-1500 m (4921.26 ft), whichever --units is in force."
- `--gridstep`: "Observer grid spacing in --units (default 25 m = 82.021 ft, whichever --units is in force)."
- `--receiver_height`: "..., in --units (default 1.2 m = 3.937 ft)."
- `--path_smoothing`: "How far, RMS per axis in --units, ... Default 10 ft (3.048 m) whatever --units says."

This matches the wording of the already-correct `--trim`, `--ground_elevation`, `--layer_depth` and `--roughness`. The help text cannot be made dynamic, because `--units` is parsed after the descriptions are built. Also change README.md:999 "in feet RMS per axis" to "in `--units` RMS per axis". Do not touch the conversion seam or the defaults, which are load-bearing per CLAUDE.md. No golden or test changes are needed.

### refraction-profile/B.r1.4: Header comments contradict the code and CLAUDE.md (stratified model, rigid-ground rate, wind use, refracted_ray)

Do not "fix" a soft ground coming out quieter than rigid'; grep for refracted_ray( finds only tests/test_refraction.cpp:328,355 and the declaration/definition.

### delaunay-exact/B.r1.3: The choice among cospherical tessellations moves every state's blend by up to 0.5, but the docs call it free and only the 100-s end-to-end golden pins it

- Finder's severity low, category docs-drift, effort S, finder's confidence 0.7, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/delaunay.cpp:167`, `src/delaunay.cpp:197`, `src/delaunay.hpp:11`, `tests/test_delaunay.cpp:100`
- **Claim.** delaunay.hpp says 'Any consistent choice among them is a correct answer' and insert() says leaving on-sphere simplices standing 'is what keeps the choice consistent'. For the shipped prism input this is not what the rule does: flipping it to `>= 0` still yields a valid Delaunay mesh (0 empty-sphere violations, all neighbor links symmetric, same 11,833 alive simplices) but a different tessellation, and every Blend of the demo trajectory changes. So the strict test (together with insertion order) is load-bearing for the goldens, not for mesh validity, and no unit test notices.
- **Evidence given.** Mutation: delaunay.cpp:197 `if (in_sphere(...) > 0)` -> `>= 0`. tests/test_predicates.cpp + test_delaunay.cpp: 'All tests passed (3740 assertions in 14 test cases)'. Validity checker over all 10,049 real simplices x 2,132 vertices: 'empty-sphere violations 0, bad neighbor links 0, non-positive orientation 0'. Blend of all 6551 states in golden/state_197421.csv: 6551 of 6551 differ from the original, max per-sphere weight difference 0.4986. Similarly a filter bound weakened 16x (bound/16) passes the whole predicate/Delaunay suite and the real mesh; only /64 is caught. The Delaunay tests assert only that barycentric weights sum to 1 and reconstruct the query (check_barycentric), which any covering tessellation satisfies. I did not run regression_golden under the mutation, so the claim that it is the only guard is by inspection of tests/ (no test pins Blend weights on the prism; test_sphere_database.cpp blend_at cases use one-sphere or 2D fixtures).
- **Scenario.** Someone speeds up the build by changing insertion order (e.g. spatial/BRIO ordering, an obvious idea), the enclosing-simplex scale, or the tie rule, believing 'any consistent choice is correct'. All unit tests stay green; every footprint changes and only regression_golden fails, with no pointer to the cause.
- **Proposed change.** Reword delaunay.hpp and the CLAUDE.md delaunay entry: valid but non-unique, and the golden-pinned choice is strict `> 0` plus lexicographic insertion order plus the hint walk. Add a fast unit test on a small deterministic prism (the existing 20x5 fixture) that (a) checks the empty-sphere and neighbor-symmetry invariants with in_sphere and (b) pins a hash or a handful of Blend weights at 1e-12. About 40 LOC, milliseconds.
- **Expected impact.** Turns the most likely refactoring accident in this area into a local unit failure, and documents the constraint that any speedup to the build (including the performance findings above) must preserve.

### spline-util/B.r1.7: atmosphere.hpp says the ISO 9613-1 code derives from BSD-3 python-acoustics, but THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md carries no such notice

- Finder's severity low, category docs-drift, effort S, finder's confidence 0.4, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: Rc**
- Locations: `src/atmosphere.hpp:5`, `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md:1`
- **Claim.** The header states the module is ported term for term from panam's iso_9613_1_1993.py 'which is in turn derived from python-acoustics (BSD-3-Clause)'. THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md lists netCDF, CLI11, nlohmann/json, pybind11 and Catch2 only, and CLAUDE.md says it is regenerated from fetched dependencies, so a code-level derivation is not covered. BSD-3-Clause requires source redistributions to retain the copyright notice and disclaimer.
- **Evidence given.** `grep -n -i 'acoustics\|panam' THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md LICENSE` returns nothing. I confirmed every constant and exponent of the port against ISO 9613-1:1993 from memory (T0 293.15, T01 273.16, pr 101.325 kPa, C = -6.8346 (T01/T)^1.261 + 4.6151, fO and fN formulas, 1.84e-11, 0.01275, 0.1068, 2239.1, 3352, 8.686, c = 343.2 sqrt(T/T0)) and spot-checked outputs at 20 C 70 percent RH: 500 Hz 2.79, 1 kHz 4.98, 2 kHz 9.04, 4 kHz 23.1 dB/km against the standard's tabulated 2.8, 5.0, 9.0, 22.9, so the physics is right; the gap is attribution only. The panam source is not in the repo, so I could not see whether its file carries a notice.
- **Scenario.** The repo is MIT-licensed with a notices file that reads as complete. If it is published, the BSD-3 clause the header itself invokes is unmet for this module.
- **Proposed change.** Either add python-acoustics' BSD-3-Clause notice (copyright line taken from its LICENSE) to THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md under a 'ported code' heading, or, if the C++ was written from the standard's formulas alone, drop the derivation sentence from atmosphere.hpp. Owner decision; no code change.
- **Expected impact.** License hygiene before any public release.


## Windowed peak search and bounds: performance

### main-io-sel/B.r1.0: Peak-expansion (windowed) SEL path no longer pays on the shipped grids since the window became the outermost crossing

- Finder's severity medium, category performance, effort M, finder's confidence 0.85, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: V2**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:291`, `src/niceops.cpp:1377`, `src/niceops.cpp:1589`, `src/util/sel.hpp:38`
- **Claim.** The window is now ten_db_down_interval: first to last sample >= peak-10, tails included. The peak-expansion machinery was built for the old first-crossing walk. Its bound cannot prove tail states below the threshold, so the scans in windowed_exposure evaluate most of what the main pass skipped. On the demo case it evaluates 93.9% of (observer,state) pairs, and it forms geometry, bin and numerator about 142M times to save 5.7M evaluations. It retires about 4.6% more instructions and about 5% more user CPU than marching every state. Output is byte-identical either way.
- **Evidence given.** Method: scratch copy of HEAD (/Users/evg5332/.claude/jobs/8792c8f6/tmp/scratch/sel_review/tree) with only an env switch NICEOPS_NOWINDOW that forces expand_from_peak=false. Demo `niceops -S state/ci_state.csv -L warn`, 3 repeats, `/usr/bin/time -l`.
- windowed: 76,087,773,016 / 76,108,642,462 / 76,087,837,269 instructions; user 4.37 / 4.33 / 4.44 s; cycles 14.77G / 14.65G / 14.92G.
- marched: 72,628,921,570 / 72,571,994,176 / 72,550,403,407 instructions; user 4.16 / 4.09 / 4.21 s; cycles 14.38G / 14.10G / 14.41G.
- database-load-only baseline (--gridstep 9000): 10.16G instructions, so the acoustic phase is 65.9G windowed vs 62.4G marched (+5.6%).
- `cmp` of the two CSVs: identical bytes, and identical to golden/197421_LAE.csv.
- Shipped log line: `Evaluated 88610720 of 94334400 (93.9326%)`.
Counters from an instrumented copy on the demo (pairs 94,334,400): main_tests=92,476,800; main_skips=29,608,852; main_evals=62,867,948; scan_geoms=27,747,970 (geometry re-formed inside certainly_below); scan_evals=22,024,290; window_states=55,665,726 (59% of pairs). Of the 29.6M states the main pass skipped, about 23.9M (81%) are evaluated afterwards by the two scans or by sel_over_window. Only 5.7M pairs (6.1%) are never evaluated. Each state that survives certainly_below also runs geometry_of a second time inside `evaluate` (22M states).
Sweep of 30 S-76D tracks (1 run, -t x30, --gridstep 328): windowed 138.22G vs marched 133.52G instructions (+3.5%). 30 of 30 output files byte-identical. Evaluated fraction 89.1-95.2%, mean 92.0%.
AW139_M1 / 192201AC (--gridstep 246): evaluated 90.7%; acoustic phase +2.8% instructions.
Where it still wins (small, near-track sets): coyle_approach.csv (32 mics, B407 spectral) evaluates 80.4% and the acoustic phase is 0.64G windowed vs 0.80G marched. A 13x13 near-track S-76D grid evaluates 68% and its acoustic phase is 0.45G vs 0.56G. B407 --ground_effect --gridstep 492 evaluates 97.5% and is neutral (17.28G vs 17.36G total).
sel.hpp:22-33 states the outermost-crossing rule was adopted deliberately in 9d6e986. CLAUDE.md and the comment at niceops.cpp:264-267 still assume a walk from the peak.
- **Scenario.** Default demo, or any wide footprint grid with a cheap broadband evaluation (about 660 instructions per pair): every observer pays a bound test on all 6551 states, plus a second geometry pass in the tails, for a 6% skip. The near-track win exists only on small arrays, which cost well under a second in total.
- **Proposed change.** Pick one. (a) Adaptive gate: in the parallel_chunks body keep a per-chunk evaluated/pairs tally. If a chunk evaluated more than about 85% of its pairs, march the next few chunks (the marched path is already byte-identical and already exists for -i/-D/-a), and re-probe periodically. About 25 LOC. (b) Delete the peak-expansion path: windowed_exposure, windowed_sound_exposure_level, the visited/travel_stamp arrays and generation counter, the expand_from_peak dispatch, RefractedGeometry, the `certainly_below` parameter of ten_db_down_interval, and the peak-only bounds build. Keep DirectionBounds for --db_cutoff. Also collapse the *_windowed_is_exact CTest pairs (27 CMake lines mention windowed/marched) to a single marched run. Net about -220 lines of src and -40 lines of CMake. In either case, if the path is kept, cache `ranges[it]` when a state is evaluated: reception_factor calls range_at twice per window sample, about 111M extra norm() calls, against 94M in the marched path.
- **Expected impact.** Demo: about -4.6% total instructions and about -5% user CPU (about 0.2 CPU-s of 4.4; wall change within the noise of a shared machine). Wide-grid runs with cheap evaluations: same. Option (a) keeps the roughly 20% acoustic-phase saving on near-track arrays. Option (b) removes about 260 lines and one exactness invariant, and gives that saving up. Runs with expensive per-pair evaluation would gain or lose at most about 2%, because the skip is 2.5% on the ground case.
- **Conflicts with docs.** CLAUDE.md L318-320 ('the reason the loop is 1.2x faster than evaluating everything'), L356 ('70% of states evaluated') and the 'is now the default path' remark about culling. Also niceops.cpp L266-267 ('costs little beyond the window itself'). None of these hold since the outermost-crossing rule; new evidence: 93.9% evaluated, marched 4.6% cheaper. This does not re-propose the rejected per-state culling, because it questions whether the current default still wins.
- **Verifier (trace): confirmed, confidence 0.87.** The shipped binary logs `Evaluated 88610720 of 94334400 (93.9326%)` on the demo. In my own scratch build, the peak-expansion path costs more CPU than marching every state on every wide-grid case I ran, and the output is byte-identical in each. The saving is small in absolute terms, so this is a low-to-medium item. Its clearest payoff is the stale documentation.

I built a fresh scratch copy of HEAD (`/Users/evg5332/.claude/jobs/8792c8f6/tmp/scratch/ver_sel_b1/tree`) with two changes. An env switch `NICEOPS_NOWINDOW` forces `expand_from_peak=false`, and stage counters plus a diagnostic pass are added. The repo was not touched and `git status` is unchanged.

Code trace at HEAD:
- Main pass (`niceops.cpp` ~L330-350): skips a state only if its bound is below `best`, meaning it is provably not the peak. It says nothing about the 10 dB window.
- Scans (`ten_db_down_interval`, `sel.hpp` L38-60): a tail state is skipped only if `certainly_below` proves it is under `peak-10`. Every non-provable tail state is evaluated, and `evaluate()` re-forms the geometry for it.
- `reception_factor` (`sel.hpp` L~100) calls `range_at` twice per window sample, and nothing caches it.

Measured on the demo (3 repeats each; hooked windowed vs marched):

| Metric | Windowed | Marched | Difference |
|---|---|---|---|
| Instructions retired | 76.16-76.17G | 72.34-72.36G | +5.3% total |
| Cycles | 14.60-14.74G | 13.93-14.08G | about +4.9% total |
| User CPU | 4.53-4.59 s | 4.27-4.32 s | +5.6% |

- The database-load-only run costs 10.08G instructions and 3.46G cycles. That puts the acoustic phase at 66.1G vs 62.3G instructions (+6.1%).
- `cmp` shows the two CSVs are identical to each other and to `golden/197421_LAE.csv`.

Counters over 94,334,400 pairs, which reproduce the finder's numbers:
- `main_geoms` 92,476,800; `main_skips` 29,608,852; `main_evals` 62,867,948.
- `scan_geoms` 27,747,970; `scan_evals` 22,024,290; `window_states` 55,665,726 (59%).
- `window_evals` 1,860,882, plus 129 seeds per observer (1,857,600 in all).
- The evaluation counts sum exactly to the logged 88,610,720.
- Of the 29.6M pairs the main pass skipped, 23.9M (81%) are evaluated later. Only 5.72M pairs (6.06%) are never evaluated.
- Geometry is formed 92.48M + 27.75M + 22.02M ≈ 142M times, plus 1.86M in the window. Only about 120M of those also run `bin` and `numerator`, so the finder's "142M times to form geometry, bin and numerator" slightly overstates the bin and numerator count.
- `range_at` is called 2 × 55.67M = 111.3M times, against 94.3M `norm()` calls in the marched path.

I also checked the mechanism, which the finder had not isolated. A diagnostic pass re-tests every state against the final peak and threshold:
- `ideal_main` = 62,684,051, against an actual `main_evals` of 62,867,948. Pass ordering, meaning the running `best` rising over time, is therefore not the cause.
- `prov_below` = 5,838,233. The bound proves only 5.8M states below the threshold.
- 38.67M states lie outside the window, and 32.83M of them cannot be proven below the threshold.
- Evaluated = window (55.67M) + non-provable outside states (32.83M) = 88.5M. The 93.9% is therefore the structural floor for this `DirectionBounds`, not a scheduling artifact.

Other configurations, each windowed vs marched, all with byte-identical output:

| Case | Evaluated | Instructions | Cycles |
|---|---|---|---|
| S-76D 197403, default grid | 88.6% | +1.97% | +1.57% |
| S-76D 197423, default grid | 91.4% | +3.68% | +3.84% |
| AW139 192201 | 90.4% | +2.46% | +3.43% |
| B205 (first track) | 91.3% | +2.41% | +3.23% |
| Demo track, `--trim -6000` June-2020 reference grid | 89.3% | +5.2% | within noise, user +10% |
| B407 `--ground_effect` `--gridstep 492` | 97.5% | -0.58% | +0.25% (neutral) |
| `coyle_approach.csv` (B407, 32 mics) | 80.4% | -4.8% | -2.8% (still wins) |
| S-76D 600x600 near-track grid | 31.2% | -2.6% | -1.7% (still wins) |

Documentation and comments that no longer hold:
- CLAUDE.md L319 ("1.2x faster") and L356 ("70% ... 56% floor").
- CLAUDE.md L280 ("is now the default path").
- `niceops.cpp` L267 ("costs little beyond the window itself").
- README.md L946 still shows `Evaluated 66041828 of 94334400 (70.0%)`, which the finder did not list.
- The whole CLAUDE.md section was written for the first-crossing walk. The outermost-crossing rule arrived in 9d6e986. No test pins the evaluated fraction.

Weaknesses of the finding:
- The default demo's wall-clock effect is roughly 1-2%. That is about 0.02 s at 14 threads and about 0.25 s at `-j 1`.
- The "about 5%" figure is the best case, on the demo track. Other tracks give 2-4%.
- The saving is roughly zero on expensive-eval paths (ground effect, refraction).
- The path still wins on small and near-track sets, so deleting it (option b) trades that away.
  - Refined claim: The peak-expansion path evaluates 89-94% of pairs on wide footprint grids with cheap broadband evaluation. It does this because the bound proves only about 6% of pairs, 5.8M of the 38.7M outside the window, below the outermost-crossing threshold. The rest is window (59%) plus non-provable tails. It costs 2-5% more total CPU than marching every state (demo: +5.3% instructions, +4.9% cycles, +5.6% user CPU) and gives byte-identical output. It stays neutral on ground-effect runs (97.5% evaluated). It still wins by about 3-5% on small and near-track sets. The "1.2x faster" and "70% evaluated" statements in CLAUDE.md and the README are stale since the outermost-crossing rule in 9d6e986.
  - Verifier's fix: Whichever code option is chosen, fix the stale statements in the same change: CLAUDE.md L280, L319 and L356, README.md L946 and the "How much gets evaluated" text, and the `niceops.cpp` L264-267 comment. Cite the measured 89-94% on wide grids.

Then choose between keeping the path and deleting it:
- **Deleting it (option b):** this is simple and loses only a 3-5% instruction win on small and near-track sets that cost well under a second in total. It also drops the ~260 lines and the extra exactness invariant. The windowed/marched CTest pairs could then collapse to single runs.
- **Gating it (option a):** this is safer, but make the gate a deterministic function of the input rather than of scheduling. A per-chunk tally read from `parallel_chunks`, where chunks are dealt out on demand, would make path choice vary between runs. Byte-identical output across `-j` counts would then depend on the two paths being bit-equal, and CTest checks windowed against marched only at 1e-9 through `golden_compare`. A fixed probe is enough, for example the evaluated fraction over a set of observers chosen by index, or the expected window fraction from the grid extent and track.

Caching `ranges[it]` for evaluated states is a real but minor extra. It saves about 17M `norm()` calls net, and only if the write does not cost more.

Do not try to tighten `DirectionBounds` to rescue the path. The diagnostic shows only 5.8M of 38.7M outside-window states are provable below the threshold. CLAUDE.md already records that bin-count sweeps barely move the result.
- **Verifier (context): confirmed, confidence 0.8.** The core measurement is true and I reproduced it independently, but the reported severity and the proposed fixes are both off.

1. Reproduced on the demo (`niceops -S state/ci_state.csv -L warn`). I used the reporter's hook binary, which is HEAD plus a `NICEOPS_NOWINDOW` env switch, from my own run directory under scratch/ver_sel_b2/run, 2 repeats each.
   - Windowed: 76.04G and 76.02G instructions retired, 4.56 and 4.53 s user, 14.6G cycles.
   - Marched: 72.49G and 72.48G instructions, 4.35 and 4.33 s user, 14.1G cycles.
   - That is +4.9% instructions, +4.6% user CPU and +3.4% cycles.
   - `cmp` shows the two CSVs are byte-identical to each other and to `golden/197421_LAE.csv`.
   - The shipped `build/niceops` logs `Evaluated 88610720 of 94334400 (93.9326%)`. That confirms the reporter's 93.9% and their 5.7M never-evaluated pairs.
2. The cause is confirmed by reading the code.
   - Commit 9d6e986 (2026-09-25) switched `ten_db_down_interval` in `src/util/sel.hpp` to first-to-last sample at or above peak-10, deliberately, for EPNL/panam parity.
   - The same commit rewrote the peak-expansion scans around `certainly_below`, which can only prove states more than about 8.6 dB below the threshold.
   - No performance number was recorded for that change, so the regression was an unmeasured side effect.
   - `certainly_below` then `evaluate` forms the same geometry twice for every tail state that survives the bound. For refraction that geometry is a `form_ray` solve.
3. The stale docs are the most solid part, and they are wrong in ways that mislead.
   - `CLAUDE.md` line 319 says "1.2x faster". Line 356 says "70% of states evaluated". Lines 332-333 say `peak_range` "stops at the first sample" and the window is "contiguous by construction". Line 322 names `expand_window()`, which no longer exists.
   - `CLAUDE.md` lines 373-375 call extending the path to `--ground_effect` "the obvious next move" and say it would save "close to the full 30%". Measured skip on the ground cases is 2.5-3%, so that guidance would send a future session toward little gain.
   - `README.md` line 946 shows a 70.0% log line. `src/niceops.cpp` lines 262-267 claim the scans "step over most of the track" and cost "little beyond the window itself".
4. The reporter overstated the case in three places.
   - Severity: absolute impact is tiny. The demo loop is about 0.16 s wall on 14 threads, so 4.6% is roughly 8 ms of a roughly 1 s run, and the shared machine's wall clock cannot resolve it.
   - Ground-effect runs are not neutral; they are a small win for windowed. Instructions, windowed vs marched:
     - plate B407 grid: 36.45G vs 36.92G (-1.3%)
     - `--ground_effect --turbulence`: 17.21G vs 17.32G (-0.6%)
     - mixed receivers: 4.12G vs 4.33G (-4.8%)
     - coyle_approach, straight rays: 2.83G vs 2.96G (-4.7%)
   - Deleting the path would forfeit those wins and remove the only tests of the bound's exactness, the `*_windowed_is_exact` ctest pairs.
5. There is a loss the reporter did not list. With `--refraction stratified --ground_effect` on a B407 `--gridstep 984` grid, windowed retires 121.57G against 120.19G marched. The 4-observer `--gridstep 9000` baseline is 94.83G, so the acoustic phase is 26.7G against 25.4G, which is +5.5%. It evaluates 95.4% of pairs. I did not measure a stratified run without `--ground_effect`.
   - `--refraction linear` on a smaller grid is neutral (127.76G vs 127.74G).
   - coyle_approach with stratified refraction is +0.4% total (100.22G vs 99.79G), so the near-track win does not extend to refraction.
6. The reporter's range-cache suggestion does not work. I tried a stamp-based lazy cache of `range_at`. It gave no gain (V_RANGE 78.81G vs 78.74G without it). `reception_factor` is not a meaningful cost center once a stamp check and a store are added.

Determinism check for the adaptive-gate option: windowed and marched output was byte-identical in all 8 configurations I ran (demo, linear, stratified, plate, ground-effect, mixed receivers, coyle, coyle with stratified). The ctest `*_is_exact` cases only compare at 1e-9, though, so bitwise equality is not guaranteed by the suite. A gate driven by runtime chunk order would make output depend on scheduling if the two paths ever differed by an ULP, which would break the README guarantee that `-j 1` and `-j 32` are byte-identical.
  - Refined claim: Since 9d6e986 changed the SEL window to the outermost 10 dB-down crossing, the peak-expansion path evaluates 93.9% of pairs on the demo. That is 88.6M of 94.3M, against the documented 70% and "1.2x faster".
