Its output is byte-identical to marching, but it retires about 4.9% more instructions and uses about 4.6% more user CPU on cheap broadband wide grids (about 3.4% more cycles). On stratified refraction the acoustic phase is 5.5% dearer, partly from forming each surviving tail state's ray twice. On ground-effect, plate, mixed-receiver and near-track straight-ray runs it is a small win of 0.6-5% total, so the path is roughly break-even overall, not a broad regression. The strongest confirmed defect is that `CLAUDE.md`, `README.md` and comments still describe a first-crossing walk with 70% evaluated, which points future work at a premise that no longer holds.
  - Verifier's fix: Do not delete the path, and do not add a runtime-adaptive gate. In order of value:

1. Fix the documentation and comments now.
   - Update `CLAUDE.md` lines 319, 322, 332-333, 356 and 373-375, the `README.md` log example and prose at lines 942-946, and the comment at `src/niceops.cpp` lines 262-267.
   - Replace the "1.2x" claim with the measured numbers. The window is 59% of pairs, 93.9% are evaluated on the demo, and the path is within about +5% to -5% of marching. It costs about 5% on cheap broadband wide grids and on stratified refraction, and wins 0.6-5% on ground-effect, plate and near-track runs.
   - Retract the "obvious next move: extend to `--ground_effect`" advice, because the skip there is 2.5-3%.
   - Drop the stale first-crossing "fragility" paragraphs, since the outermost-crossing rule replaced that behavior in 9d6e986.
   - Record the measurements so nobody re-derives them.
2. Optionally, take the one bit-identical code fix that measured positive: hand the geometry that `certainly_below` formed straight to `evaluate` through a one-entry cache. It saves the duplicate `form_ray` in the refraction tails, worth about 2.7% of the stratified acoustic phase (-0.72G of 26.7G), and is byte-identical. It is worth about 0.5% on the demo, so it will not flip the sign there.
3. Skip the `range_at` cache. It measured no gain.
4. If a gate for cheap broadband wide grids is still wanted (worth at most about 5% of a loop of roughly 0.16 s wall):
   - Decide it once, deterministically, before the observer loop, from a fixed probe of a few observers, or from the run configuration (broadband, straight ray, more than N observers).
   - Do not decide it from per-chunk tallies in scheduling order. The windowed/marched equivalence is only pinned at 1e-9 in ctest, so a scheduling-dependent choice could make output vary with `-j`.
5. Keep `DirectionBounds`, the bound-exactness tests and the `*_windowed_is_exact` pairs regardless.

### main-loop/B.r1.1: The windowed peak search no longer pays: 89-98% of pairs are still evaluated, it costs 2-6% more CPU than marching on the demo, and certainly_below recomputes geometry (a full ray solve under refraction) for every skipped state

- Finder's severity medium, category performance, effort S, finder's confidence 0.8, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:292`, `src/niceops.cpp:1377`, `src/niceops.cpp:1589`, `src/util/sel.hpp:38`
- **Claim.** Commit 9d6e986 changed the SEL window from 'walk out from the peak, stop at the first dip' to 'first to last sample at or above peak-10' (ten_db_down_interval). The end scans now have to prove every state outside the window is below the threshold, which is the 'bound tighter than 10 dB' question CLAUDE.md calls hopeless, and the bound is not that tight. On the demo the main pass stops at 68.6% evaluated, matching the documented 70%, but the two end scans then evaluate another ~22M states, giving 93.9%. The overhead (geometry plus bin lookup for every pair) is not repaid at that fraction. On top of that, certainly_below (:368-375) calls geometry_of(it) a second time for every state the main pass skipped, instead of reusing the numerator and squared range it already computed. Under refraction geometry_of is an eigenray solve.
- **Evidence given.** Instrumented scratch copy (tree at .../scratch/r1perf/tree; counters printed to stderr) on the demo `niceops -S state/ci_state.csv`: log says 'Evaluated 88610720 of 94334400 (93.9326%)' (README.md:946 claims 66041828, 70.0%). Split by phase: seed 1.86M, main pass 62.87M, end scans 22.02M, remainder in sel_over_window. Main-pass geometry calls 92.48M; certainly_below calls 38.7M, of which 27.7M recompute geometry (30% extra bound work). Evaluated fraction elsewhere (--gridstep 246): S-76D_M3 tracks 197401/197410/197421 = 92.7/90.0/94.2%; AW139 192201/194302 = 90.5/88.9%; B205 190337 91.4%; B407 spectral 93.6%; +--ground_effect 97.8%; refraction linear 92.3%; stratified+ground 96.1%. CPU (user+sys, 5-7 interleaved repeats, medians, machine at load ~25; 'M' is a scratch env switch NICEOPS_MARCH that forces expand_from_peak=false, 'C' is a scratch cache of the skipped states' numerator and r2): demo W 4.81-4.88, C 4.78-4.80, M 4.59. B407 spectral: W 6.50, C 6.5, M 6.74. B407 +ground gridstep 246: W 3.62, M 3.70. Refraction linear (example_windy, --frame_bearing 210): W 4.04, C 3.87, M 3.89. Stratified+ground gridstep 492: W 14.25, C 13.80, M 13.72. W, C and M outputs are byte-identical (cmp) in all five cases. W and M are also byte-identical on 17 database x track combinations, including the broadband fixed_load_factor databases, so exactness is not in question. Bound slack, dumped per state for observers 7260 and 2500: median bound-minus-level 11.8 and 11.5 dB (min 2.3, max 20-22) over the whole track, against CLAUDE.md's 'median 8.6 dB' and its own requirement of under 10 dB. Rebuilding with DirectionBounds at 32x16 bins gave 91.4% evaluated: main pass 62.9M -> 56.8M, but the end-scan evaluations went 22.0M -> 23.2M, so the bin count is not the lever.
- **Scenario.** Any default broadband run (the demo, all S-76D/AW139/B205 broadband databases) pays ~5% more CPU than simply marching. Every refraction run pays 3-4% for the repeated ray solves.
- **Proposed change.** (1) Cache: in windowed_exposure, when the main pass skips a state, store its `num` and `g.squared_range` in two nt-long scratch vectors allocated beside `visited`. For an unvisited state, certainly_below then tests `skip_num[it] < threshold_energy * skip_r2[it]` instead of calling geometry_of again. Every unvisited state after the main pass was skipped with those values stored, so this is the same expression and bit-identical. It also removes one eigenray solve per skipped state under --refraction. (2) Require `spectral` in the expand_from_peak condition (:1377). Broadband evaluation (~19 ns) is cheaper than the bound test and the wall is lost at ~94% evaluated. Spectral windowing still gains ~2-4%. (3) Alternatively retire the windowed path (windowed_exposure, windowed_sound_exposure_level, the visited/travel/generation state, 6 windowed/marched ctest pairs, ~100 doc lines): the best measured gain left after (1) is ~3% on spectral runs. DirectionBounds stays for --db_cutoff culling. Pick (1)+(2) if keeping it.
- **Expected impact.** (1) -2% CPU on the demo (within noise there), -3-4% on refraction runs. (2) -4.6% CPU on broadband runs. Together the windowed path goes from a net loss to parity or a small win.
- **Conflicts with docs.** CLAUDE.md:319 ('loop is 1.2x faster'), :356 ('70% of states evaluated', 'window itself is the 56% floor') and :340-343 ('median 8.6 dB ... the search collapses inside the window') are contradicted by the measurements above: 89-98% evaluated, median slack 11.5-11.8 dB. The design argument at :332-336 (walk out from the peak, no bound tighter than 10 dB needed) describes the old first-crossing rule. This is new evidence, not a re-proposal of a rejected item.

### main-setup/B.r1.7: Small dead-work and misleading-message cleanups in the cull/bound setup

- Finder's severity low, category simplification, effort S, finder's confidence 0.85, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: V1**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:1266`, `src/niceops.cpp:1329`, `src/niceops.cpp:1395`, `src/niceops.cpp:317`
- **Claim.** Four small items, none affecting results. (a) `DirectionBounds` is built whenever `bounded && (culling || ...)`, but on the broadband path with --db_cutoff nothing reads it: is_culled uses cull_range_squared, and expand_from_peak is forced false by `!culling`. Measured cost is small (3 ms), so this is dead work, not a speed problem. (b) The packed-spectra setup finds each state's blend in the first loop (`found`), then repeats a linear std::find per state in a second loop (1408-1412) to recover the same index. Recording `found` per state removes the second search and about 8 lines. (c) `--db_cutoff` with `-a` on the spectral path fails with '--db_cutoff: no bound on this run's spectral level (no band inside --band_limits?)', naming the wrong cause. The bound is unusable because `bounded = !level_is_transformed` is false under -a. The check runs per trajectory, after the database and track are loaded, rather than in run() with the other incompatible-flag checks. (d) `std::size_t peak` in windowed_exposure is assigned at 324 and 350 and never read (clang -Wunused-but-set-variable).
- **Evidence given.** (a) Broadband `--db_cutoff 60` run: log 'Direction bounds built in 0.0031 s' followed by 'Culled 0 of 11012231 ...'; grep shows direction_bounds is read only under cull_by_direction (spectral), expand_from_peak, and the spectral-only refraction branch. (c) B407 spectral run: `--db_cutoff 60 -a 40` gives '[error] Skipping ...: --db_cutoff: no bound on this run's spectral level (no band inside --band_limits?), so nothing could be culled safely.' (d) `clang++ -Wall -Wextra -Wunused` on HEAD niceops.cpp reports only: niceops.cpp:317:17 variable 'peak' set but not used.
- **Scenario.** A user combines --db_cutoff and -a on a spectral database and is sent looking at --band_limits.
- **Proposed change.** (a) Build the table only when `cull_by_direction || (!culling && !needs_full_history)`, which means moving the cull_by_direction definition up. (b) Store found per state in the first loop and drop the second find. (c) Reject `--db_cutoff` together with `-a` in run() on the spectral path with a message that names -a. (d) Delete `peak`. Net about -15 LOC.
- **Expected impact.** Slightly less code and one clearer error. Negligible speed change (3-18 ms per trajectory).
- **Verifier (context): confirmed, confidence 0.8.** All four observations check out against HEAD 84462d7, and none is deliberate or documented. Two of the reporter's numbers and one proposed fix are wrong, so I have refined the claim.

(a) True, and an accident. Before commit 9d6e986 the condition was `!level_is_transformed && !needs_full_history && !culling`, so broadband `--db_cutoff` never built the table. 9d6e986 changed it to `bounded && (culling || !needs_full_history)` to serve spectral culling and started building it for broadband culling too. On a broadband culling run nothing reads `direction_bounds`:
- `cull_by_direction = culling && spectral` is false, so `is_culled` takes the `cull_range_squared` branch.
- `expand_from_peak` needs `!culling`, so it is false.
- The refraction branch that reads it (niceops.cpp:1661) needs `effective_sound_speed`. `--refraction` requires `--atmosphere`, which requires the spectral path (input_error at lines 2425 and 2431).
I reproduced this: S-76D_M3 with `--gridstep 492 --db_cutoff 60` logs "Direction bounds built in 0.00306629 s" and then "Culled 0 of 2888991". The build has no side effects beyond the log line. Cost is about 3 ms.

(b) True, but negligible. The second loop at 1408-1413 does repeat the `std::find` that the first loop already did. On the real B407 case there are 33 blends across 6551 states, and a micro-benchmark of that second loop measured 0.053 ms. Even at 300 blends it is 0.5 ms, and `pack_spectra` costs about 0.5 ms per blend (41.6 MiB for 33), which would dwarf it. The "3-18 ms" impact figure does not apply to this item. Net lines saved are about 2, not 8, because a per-state index vector has to be added.

(c) True, and slightly worse than reported. The message blames `--band_limits`, but that cause is unreachable: line 1003 already fails with "--band_limits ... excludes every one of the database's bands" (confirmed with `--band_limits 1 2`). `spectral_direction_bounds` returns unusable only for an empty trajectory, zero bands, a gain-size mismatch or no active bands, so the only reachable cause is `-a` making `bounded` false. Reproduced: B407 with `--db_cutoff 60 -a 40` exits 1 with the wrong message. No test or doc references the message text.

The reporter's fix for (c) is wrong. `spectral` is only settled per trajectory after the database loads, because a database without spectra falls back to broadband. In `run()` it is just the CLI default (true). A check there would reject `--db_cutoff -a` for no-spectra databases, which works today: S-76D_M3 with `--db_cutoff 60 -a 40` exits 0 through the broadband path, and so does B407 with `--no-spectral`.

(d) True. `peak` is assigned at lines 324 and 350 and never read, and it is a leftover from before `ten_db_down_interval` took over. The build enables no warning flags, which is why nobody has seen it.

No conflict with CLAUDE.md. The load-bearing hot-path items are untouched, and the rejected-optimization list does not cover any of this. Nothing in the numeric path changes, so the goldens are safe.

Overall value is low. Only (c) is a user-facing improvement. (a) and (d) are opportunistic tidy-ups, and (b) is not worth doing.
  - Refined claim: Three real, low-value items and one negligible one, all output-neutral:
(a) Broadband `--db_cutoff` builds a `DirectionBounds` table that nothing reads (about 3 ms). It is a regression from 9d6e986, and the previous code excluded culling.
(c) The `--db_cutoff` failure under `-a` on the spectral path says "no band inside --band_limits?", a cause that is unreachable because line 1003 rejects it first. The real and only reachable cause is `-a`, which makes `bounded` false so no table is built. This is the only user-visible item.
(d) `std::size_t peak` in `windowed_exposure` is set and never read.
(b) The repeated `std::find` for packed spectra is real but costs about 0.05 ms at the actual 33 blends, and removing it saves about 2 lines.
The reporter's fix for (c), rejecting in run(), is wrong and would break a working combination.
  - Verifier's fix: (a) Change the condition at niceops.cpp:1272 to `if (bounded && (culling ? spectral : !needs_full_history))`. `spectral` is already known there, so `cull_by_direction` does not need to move. This is equivalent to the reporter's condition and a one-line change. Only the "Direction bounds built" log line disappears from broadband culling runs.
(c) Do not move the check into run(). Keep it at line 1331, where `spectral` is resolved, and branch the message: when `level_is_transformed`, say that `--db_cutoff` on the spectral path cannot be combined with -a/--ambient because the ambient is added after the level is evaluated, so the bound does not apply (suggest `--no-spectral` or dropping -a). Keep the current text only for the residual empty-trajectory and no-bands cases.
(d) Delete `peak` and its two assignments.
(b) Skip it. If it is done anyway, record `found` in a per-state index vector, but the saving is about 2 lines and 0.05 ms.
Bundle (a), (c) and (d) as one tiny commit. Existing tests need no changes, and output is byte-identical apart from that log line and the error text.

### main-loop/B.r1.4: The bound test is spelled out four times, and windowed_exposure carries a dead `peak` variable

- Finder's severity low, category simplification, effort S, finder's confidence 0.7, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:317`, `src/niceops.cpp:341`, `src/niceops.cpp:372`, `src/niceops.cpp:1344`, `src/niceops.cpp:1659`
- **Claim.** `g.squared_range > sphere_radius_squared && bounds.numerator(it, DirectionBounds::bin(along, up, across, squared_range)) < E * squared_range` appears at :341, :372, :1350 and :1660 with slightly different argument shapes. The 'inside the sphere, evaluate rather than trust it' guard is what makes each one a valid bound, so one spelling is safer than four. `std::size_t peak` (:317, assigned at :324 and :350) is never read. `RefractedGeometry` (:1465-1469) copies the four StateGeometry fields by hand in its lambda (:1607-1610) instead of deriving from StateGeometry.
- **Evidence given.** `clang++ -std=c++17 -fsyntax-only -Wall -Wextra -Wshadow src/niceops.cpp` reports exactly one project warning: `niceops.cpp:317:17: warning: variable 'peak' set but not used [-Wunused-but-set-variable]`. CMakeLists.txt enables no warning flags at all, which is why it went unseen. Reading the four sites confirms the same test.
- **Scenario.** A future change to the bound (a new guard, a tolerance factor like the spectral (1+1e-9)) has to be made in four places, and the culling and windowing copies can drift apart.
- **Proposed change.** Add `bool DirectionBounds::excludes(std::size_t state, const StateGeometry& g, double energy, double sphere_radius_squared) const` (guard, bin and comparison in one place; StateGeometry would have to move into the header) and call it from the four sites. Delete `peak`. Make RefractedGeometry derive from StateGeometry. If the bound-caching change from the windowing finding is made, apply it at the same time since it touches the same lines. Optionally add -Wall -Wextra to the niceops targets, since this file is otherwise warning-clean.
- **Expected impact.** About -18 net lines; one place to keep the sphere-interior guard correct. Risk is low because it is mechanical. The helper must stay inline and keep the `bin` arguments unchanged for the hot path, and the demo golden plus the 6 windowed/marched ctest pairs cover it.

### db-eval/B.r1.2: Tighten DirectionBounds with a range-dependent envelope (level minus attenuation): 94% -> 81.8% evaluated, byte-identical, but the table leaves L1 and the spectral build needs parallelising

- Finder's severity low, category performance, effort L, finder's confidence 0.45, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:1832`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1843`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1905`, `src/niceops.cpp:334`
- **Claim.** The 33M outside-window evaluations (previous finding) are there because the bound drops the whole excess attenuation (`numerator` is level minus spreading only). At 1-4 km, where 28M of them lie, the missing term is 10-25 dB. Bounding jointly, env(d) = max over the cells/conditions a bin can read of (dBA - EAA*d/1000), sampled at 64 range knots of 80 m and applied as a step function (a decreasing function, so the knot below d is a valid upper bound), removes about 12 percentage points of evaluations. On the spectral path the same idea, with the per-spectrum convex bound evaluated at the absorbed spectrum (log-sum-exp is convex, so max over stored spectra of L_i(d) bounds every convex blend of them), removes 11 points. Also loosening the +1 pad slack and the pole fix add 3-4 more. The per-bin minimum EAA (cheaper) only buys 7 points and a per-band absorbed bound only 1.4 points, because they pair the loud cell with a different cell's attenuation.
- **Evidence given.** Scratch copy with env-toggled variants; all outputs cmp-identical to the unmodified run.
Demo broadband S-76D_M3 (window floor 59.0%): baseline 93.93% (88.61M); min-EAA per bin 86.66%; joint envelope 81.77% (77.13M); joint + pad slack 0 + pole fix 78.42% (73.98M). Pad slack 0 alone: 92.20%. Outside-window evaluations 32.9M -> 21.5M -> 18.3M.
Spectral AS350_fixed_load_factor (floor 62.2%): baseline 92.03%; per-band absorbed bound 90.67%; absorbed-spectrum envelope 81.32%; with slack 0 + pole fix 77.20%. B407_fixed_load_factor: 92.26% -> 81.41% -> 77.34%. Direction bounds build 0.025 s -> 0.33 s (single-threaded, before the parallel loop).
CPU time, -j 1, 5 alternating runs, min: broadband base 3.24 s, joint 3.10 s, joint+slack0+pole 3.01 s (noise about +-5%).
Pad slack is safe by construction once the poles are fixed: bin_padding() is ceil(5 deg / 7.2 deg)=1 and ceil(10 deg / 22.5 deg)=1 plus the `+ 1`; whole-sphere sweeps with slack 0 and the pole fix gave 0 violations on four databases (156.9M directions each).
- **Scenario.** Default spectral runs on the eight shipped fixed_load_factor databases with spectra cost about 150 ns per evaluation (see cost-model finding), 28M outside-window evaluations at that price is ~4 CPU-s of the ~20 CPU-s (1.7-1.9 s wall on 14 threads) of a demo run.
- **Proposed change.** Optional. If pursued: (1) add the joint envelope as a coarse second stage tested only on states that survive the cheap table test, with one sqrt and a knot index per survivor; build the spectral one per distinct condition in parallel and with 16-24 knots rather than 64 so it stays well under 0.1 s; (2) after fixing the poles, drop the `+ 1` in bin_padding() (or keep it and accept 1.7 points). Do not do it for broadband alone: the sqrt and lookup on survivors cost about what the 10M saved evaluations gain (net -4 to -7% CPU, inside the noise).
- **Expected impact.** Spectral: roughly 8-10% fewer CPU-seconds (10-14M fewer 150 ns evaluations) minus the bound build; broadband demo: -4 to -7% CPU; ground_effect (about 1 us/eval): proportionally larger but needs its own bound. Table grows from ~53 KB to ~3.4 MB (64 knots x 128 bins x 52 blends x 8 B), i.e. L2-resident, which conflicts with the 'tens of kilobytes' design note.
- **Conflicts with docs.** CLAUDE.md: 'The table has to stay in cache. ... tens of kilobytes' and 'Bin counts were swept ... the choice among them hardly matters' (measured under the old first-crossing rule, where the outside-window cost did not exist). New evidence is the 33M outside-window evaluations under the current rule.


## History writers: performance

### main-loop/B.r1.0: Band-history and time-history writers format every value through std::ofstream: 3-6x slower than std::to_chars, and --band_history stops scaling past 4 threads

- Finder's severity medium, category performance, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:66`, `src/niceops.cpp:166`, `src/niceops.cpp:195`, `src/niceops.cpp:1714`
- **Claim.** write_time_history_file and write_band_history_file stream each double with `file << value` on an ofstream set to setprecision(17) (open_output, :71) or setprecision(10) (:210). That costs ~320 ns (p17) / ~173 ns (p10) per value against ~48 ns for std::to_chars(general, precision), which prints byte-identical text. Both writers run inside the worker threads (call sites :1718, :1728). With many threads the band-history run also spends most of its CPU in the kernel, presumably from many small buffer-sized writes to one directory. One write() per file removes that.
- **Evidence given.** Microbenchmark (/tmp/r1perf_mb/fmt_bench3.cpp, built with cxxlink.sh): precision 10, ostringstream 173 ns/value vs to_chars(general,10) 47 ns; precision 17, 320 vs 48 ns; the text is byte-identical in both cases. I then rewrote the two writers in a scratch copy (tree at /Users/evg5332/.claude/jobs/8792c8f6/tmp/scratch/r1perf/tree). A helper `append_number(std::string&, double, int)` calls `std::to_chars(buf, buf+48, v, std::chars_format::general, precision)`. Each file is built in one std::string and written with a single file.write; headers stay on the ostream. Case: `niceops -d databases/B407_fixed_load_factor.nod -v databases/B407.json -t tracks/S-76D/197421AC.csv -b arrays/coyle_approach.csv --ground_effect --band_limits 5 20000 --band_history DIR/x` (32 microphones, 98 MB). Shipped build vs rewrite: -j 1 CPU (user+sys) 1.90 -> 0.81 s (baseline run without band history: 0.35 s). -j 4 wall 1.16 -> 0.58 s. -j 14 wall 1.60 -> 0.40 s, user+sys 4.22+7.36 -> 0.73+0.16 s. The shipped build's -j 1/2/4/8/14 user+sys is 1.77/2.15/3.32/8.14/12.67 s for the same 32 files. All 32 band-history files are byte-identical (cmp). With `--db_cutoff 90`, which gives 4498 '-inf' rows per file, all 32 are still identical. Time history, `-S state/ci_state.csv --gridstep 492 -i DIR/th`: 441 files byte-identical (also with --db_cutoff 75, which gives all-'-inf' files). -j 1 CPU 2.1 -> 1.3 s against 1.1 s with no -i, so writer cost 1.0 -> 0.2 s. At --gridstep 246 -i -j 14, CPU fell 11.5-13.4 -> 2.3 s but wall stayed ~3-3.5 s in both, so the time-history wall is limited by something else, probably file creation. The machine was at load average ~25, so absolute sys times are inflated; the ratios are what matter.
- **Scenario.** A validation run of a 96-microphone array with --band_history writes 96 x 6551 rows x ~37 values = ~23M formatted doubles. At ~173 ns that is ~4 CPU-s of formatting, against a sub-second acoustic loop (the same 32-microphone case computes in 0.35 s total when band history is off). --band_history is the newest option (HEAD commit) and is meant for exactly this kind of array run.
- **Proposed change.** Add a small `append_number(std::string&, double, int precision)` helper (to_chars, chars_format::general) near open_output. Use it in write_band_history_file (precision 10, one std::string per file sized rows*(7+nb)*13, one file.write) and write_time_history_file (precision 17). Keep the header lines on the ostream. Verified byte-identical, including inf and -inf. Portability caveat: floating-point to_chars needs GCC >= 11 and, on macOS, a libc++ recent enough to provide it. It built and ran here with Apple clang; the CI GCC was not checked.
- **Expected impact.** Band-history runs on arrays: writer share of CPU drops ~3-4x (whole 32-mic run 1.9 -> 0.8 CPU-s), and wall at default threads drops ~4x by removing kernel contention. Time-history (-i) writer CPU drops ~5x. No effect on runs that write no histories.


## Database load: performance

### db-write/B.r1.1: Database load hides nothing behind nc_close: the ~78 ms Delaunay build runs serially before a ~100 ms nc_close that needs nothing from it

- Finder's severity medium, category performance, effort S, finder's confidence 0.85, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:758`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1203`, `src/interpolator.cpp:31`, `CMakeLists.txt:189`
- **Claim.** Database::Database finishes reading the file, then runs interpolator_.build (line 1203) on the calling thread, then returns, at which point NcGuard (function scope, line 759) calls nc_close, netCDF's ~100 ms teardown of the 1312 groups' metadata. The triangulation reads only the packed condition array; nc_close touches only netCDF state. Running the build with std::async while the main thread closes the file hides min(78, ~100) = ~78 ms, about 8% of the ~0.96 s default run (about 10% of the load). This does not touch the netCDF costs CLAUDE.md lists as unreachable; it overlaps the 14% Delaunay share with the 12% nc_close share.
- **Evidence given.** Log timeline of three default runs (sphere_r1_load/run): 'Opening netCDF file' -> 'There are 1312 groups' 0.37 s (nc_open); -> 'Max level found' 0.30 s (group loop); -> 'Triangulated' 0.08 s; -> 'Database loaded' 0.105 s, and nothing but nc_close runs between the last two. 'sample' on 8 back-to-back loads (load_loop.cpp): of 3304 main-thread samples, NC_open 1311 (39.7%), nc_close 403 (12.2%), ConditionInterpolator::build 311 (9.4%). Standalone CPU time of build on the 1312 real conditions (interp_time.cpp): best-of-5 77.7 ms. Prototype in the scratch copy (sphere_r1_tree/tree/src/sphere_database.cpp): 'auto triangulation = std::async(std::launch::async, [&]{ interpolator_.build(active_condition_dims_, advance_ratios.size(), conditions.data()); }); nc_close(file_ncid); guard.id = -1; triangulation.get();'. Demo output cmp-identical to golden/197421_LAE.csv. Interleaved 8+8 runs, 'Database loaded in' (s): without: 0.83 0.855 0.877 0.885 0.904 0.917 0.968 0.97; with: 0.745 0.75 0.763 0.765 0.809 0.809 0.878 0.885 (wall clock on a shared machine, so only the direction is meaningful; the size comes from the CPU numbers above).
- **Scenario.** Every invocation, including short runs where the load is most of the 1 s. The 3.2 s original and 1.0 s current run times in CLAUDE.md suggest this size of gain matters to the maintainers.
- **Proposed change.** Add NcGuard::close() (returns the status, sets id = -1, destructor skips a closed id) and in the constructor launch the build with std::async after the conditions are packed, close the file on the calling thread, then get(). Link Threads::Threads PUBLIC on niceops_core: pe.cpp:431 and refraction.cpp:604 already use std::thread inside the library, but only niceops and niceops_benchmark link Threads (CMakeLists.txt:204, 245), so niceops_unit_tests and the pe tools rely on glibc >= 2.34 folding pthread into libc.
- **Expected impact.** About -75 ms wall on the ~0.96 s default run and on every unit test that loads the database; CPU time unchanged.
- **Conflicts with docs.** CLAUDE.md 'Where the database load time goes' presents the load as netCDF-bound (78%). That still holds; this only overlaps the Delaunay share with nc_close. Not a rejected item.

### delaunay-exact/B.r1.0: Overlap the ~85-105 ms serial Delaunay build with the netCDF group reads

- Finder's severity medium, category performance, effort S, finder's confidence 0.65, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:859`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1195`, `src/interpolator.cpp:95`, `src/delaunay.cpp:26`
- **Claim.** The 3D triangulation of the operating conditions runs single-threaded, strictly after the group loop, although it needs nothing from netCDF except three scalars per group. It costs 8-10% of a default run and is not on the rejected list in CLAUDE.md, which counts it as part of the ~0.75 s that is 'ours' (14% of load).
- **Evidence given.** Standalone build of the real S-76D_M3 conditions with the shipped Release flags, min CPU over 15 builds: 79-89 ms. Other databases: Be206L3_ambient_gated 102, B407_ambient_gated 108, RO-66 91, S-76D_M4 94 ms. Log of a demo run (loaded machine, timeline only): 'Database format version' 11:12:44.503, group loop done 44.857 (354 ms), 'Triangulated 1312 operating conditions ... (2132 vertices ... 10049 simplices)' 44.944 (87 ms, serial), 'Database loaded in 1.141 seconds' 45.060. Code: the loop reads `require_scalar(group_id, "advance_ratio", ...)`, flight_path_angle and thrust_coefficient at sphere_database.cpp:862-864, and the only consumer is the last statement of the ctor, `interpolator_.build(active_condition_dims_, advance_ratios.size(), conditions.data());` (1203). The build touches no netCDF handle, Logger::write is mutex-guarded (logger.hpp:127,161), and main reads `Database` only after construction. `input_error` thrown from the build would propagate through std::async's future.
- **Scenario.** Every run that loads a full 3D database (all 14 non-fixed_load_factor .nod files in databases/, 800-1464 spheres) pays the build serially after ~350 ms of netCDF reads that leave every other core idle. The default demo is ~1.0-1.1 s, of which ~0.09 s is this.
- **Proposed change.** Hoist the three condition-scalar reads (lines 862-864) into a first pass over group_ids that fills advance_ratios/flight_path_angles/thrust_coefficients (the main loop then uses the stored values instead of re-reading, so there are no extra nc lookups), compute min/max and the normalized `conditions` array immediately, and launch `interpolator_.build(...)` with std::async(std::launch::async). Join as the last statement of the ctor (no future member, so Database's move semantics are unaffected). The mesh is constructed by exactly the same deterministic code, so results are unchanged. Optionally skip the async launch when the run is -j 1.
- **Expected impact.** Hides the full ~85-105 ms build behind ~250-350 ms of remaining loop time: roughly -8% to -10% wall on the default demo, and the same on every batch job that switches databases. Not measurable by CPU time; the argument is structural.
- **Conflicts with docs.** CLAUDE.md 'Where the database load time goes' tables the Delaunay build as 14% of load and says the netCDF share is unreachable. The build itself is not listed as rejected. Note the `-j 1` harness use (a dozen concurrent processes) if the async is unconditional.

### delaunay-exact/B.r1.2: Delaunay build CPU can be cut about 35% bit-identically: subset-DP filtered determinant plus inline-storage expansions (exact path is far from 'allocation-free')

- Finder's severity low, category performance, effort M, finder's confidence 0.8, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/predicates.cpp:185`, `src/predicates.cpp:22`, `src/predicates.cpp:135`, `src/delaunay.cpp:208`
- **Claim.** In the 94 ms build, 61% is in_sphere: the recursive double `laplace` (copying a minor per column per level, 23% of samples) and the exact path (11,545 of 75,231 in_sphere calls, 15%; ~41 ms) whose Expansion is std::vector<double>, so malloc/free is ~25% of the whole build. CLAUDE.md's statement that the exact path is 'worth keeping allocation-free' does not describe it: a single build performs 1,709,101 heap allocations.
- **Evidence given.** Instrumented copies (exp/pred_cnt.cpp etc.): orientation 188,393 calls, 8,549 exact, all true zeros; in_sphere 75,231 calls, 11,545 exact, 11,541 true zeros and 4 nonzero, so the filter constant (4*size+8)*eps is not costing extra exact trips and tuning it cannot help. Per exact in_sphere: ~35 multiply() calls, ~179 add_component calls, ~437 two_sum iterations; largest expansion seen anywhere is 15 components. `sample` profile: laplace 506/2193, malloc/free family ~550/2193. operator-new counter: 1,709,101 per build. Prototypes: (a) `laplace` as a subset DP over column masks (det[mask] = expansion along row size-popcount(mask), ascending columns, same `d += (j%2==0)?value:-value` order) is operation-for-operation the same arithmetic with each minor formed once; (b) Expansion as a fixed-capacity inline array (cap 40, prototype aborts on overflow) with an in-place remove_zeros; (c) exact_determinant as the same subset DP. Min CPU ms over 15 builds, alternating runs, -O3 -DNDEBUG: original 79.2-88.9; (a) only 62.8-73.8; (b) only 65.4-67.0; (a)+(b)+(c) 52.6-58.1. operator-new calls drop to 813,390 (the rest is insert()'s per-simplex/Boundary/std::map<vector> bookkeeping, still unoptimised). Identity check: hash of every alive simplex's vertex/neighbor arrays and the (weights, indices) of Blend for all 6551 golden states is identical to the original (structure 79b70210c442c2ab, barycentric fe99ed17a2a793d5); tests/test_predicates.cpp + test_delaunay.cpp (14 cases, 3740 assertions) pass; 23,200 random/near-degenerate/prism cases of orientation and in_sphere in dims 2-5 (incl. ulp nudges and coordinates shifted by 8000 and 1e5) match exact Fraction arithmetic in Python with 0 mismatches.
- **Scenario.** Runs once per database load (and ~45 times across ctest, each unit or golden case that loads a database). Matters mainly for -j 1 batches and CI; if the overlap finding lands, the wall effect on a normal run is hidden.
- **Proposed change.** Replace laplace() and exact_determinant_recurse()/the pointer-array minors with one subset-DP over column masks each (n*2^(n-1) products instead of a factorial recursion with minor copies, no pointer arrays). Give Expansion inline capacity (about 32 doubles) with a heap fallback, since only the observed max of 15 is known, not a proof; the fallback must exist. Optionally replace Simplex/Boundary vectors and the std::map<std::vector> facet key in Delaunay::insert with fixed arrays (dim+1 <= 8); this remaining ~0.8M allocations was not prototyped. Net LOC about +30. Verify with the barycentric/simplex hash above, since the tessellation is golden-pinned (see the tie-break finding).
- **Expected impact.** -27 to -31 ms CPU per database load (-35% of the build); unmeasured additional few ms from the Delaunay bookkeeping.
- **Conflicts with docs.** CLAUDE.md predicates entry: 'The exact path is taken often here ... so it is worth keeping allocation-free.' Only the minor recursion is; the Expansion vectors are the dominant allocation source (1.7M per build). Not on the rejected-optimizations list.

### db-load/B.r1.4: Grid fill re-derives the same channel->cell matches for all 1312 spheres (and again for the spectral row map); plus 1312 identical 'Could not find variable ShaftPower' log lines

- Finder's severity low, category performance, effort S, finder's confidence 0.85, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:1086`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1025`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:83`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:885`
- **Claim.** For each sphere the fill loop calls approx_equal_range twice per channel (1406 channels x 2 x 1312 spheres = 3.7 M calls) although phis_scattered/thetas_scattered are identical for all but the 24 repaired hover spheres (and are literally the shared root vectors when shared_grid_and_frequency is set). The spectral path repeats a second per-sphere channel->cell pass (the `cell_of` construction, with its own `mapped`/`taken` allocations). Separately get_scalar logs at info level when a variable is absent, and ShaftPower is absent from every shipped database, so the log gets 1312 identical lines (1312 of 1349 lines of a default run), each an ostringstream + gmtime_r + mutex + file write + flush.
- **Evidence given.** Instrumented scratch copy of HEAD (thread-CPU timers around the fill and around the ShaftPower get_scalar, S-76D_M3.nod, 5 runs each): fill 0.0429/0.0423/0.0456/0.0440/0.0419 s; with the mapping cached across spheres whose label vectors compare equal (prev == current, one memcmp-sized compare per sphere): 0.0080/0.0082/0.0084/0.0074/0.0082 s, i.e. ~35 ms saved of a ~1.0 s default run. Output with the cache is byte-identical to the uncached build and to golden/197421_LAE.csv (cmp on the full 14400-observer run). ShaftPower probe + log: 6.7-8.6 ms per run, of which the failing nc_inq_varid is 0.6 ms (standalone: 1312 probes = 0.0006 s), so ~6-7 ms is logging. `wc -l log/niceops.log` = 1349, `grep -c 'Could not find variable'` = 1312.
For calibration of what is NOT reachable (CLAUDE.md's claim holds): standalone netCDF timing on the same file, CPU s: nc_open 0.36-0.38 in plain, NC_DISKLESS and nc_open_memio modes alike, nc_close 0.11, first-touch reads ~0.23 (11.8 K H5Dopen at ~18-21 us). No open mode or read-call variant moves any of them. HDF5 here reports Threadsafety: OFF, so deferring nc_close to a background thread is not safe either.
- **Scenario.** Every run pays ~3.5% of wall time (35 ms of ~1.0 s) in re-matching identical labels, plus log noise that buries real warnings (a genuine 'Repaired legacy hover ...' line is 1 of 1349).
- **Proposed change.** Build a `std::vector<ChannelCells>` ({phi_lo, phi_hi, theta_lo, theta_hi}) when a sphere's label vectors differ from the previous sphere's, and reuse it for the fill and for the spectral `cell_of` check; move the optional-variable probe for ShaftPower to a quiet nc_inq_varid (log only if it appears on some spheres and not others, which is already an error). Extracting the fill and the spectra scatter into two small functions also shortens a 450-line constructor.
- **Expected impact.** ~35 ms of ~1.0 s (3.5%; ~5% of the ~0.75 s database load) plus ~6 ms of log time; spectral databases save the second per-sphere pass. Net LOC about -10 to -20 after sharing the table.

### db-write/B.r1.5: The grid fill re-runs two binary searches per channel for every sphere although all 1312 spheres share one channel layout

- Finder's severity low, category performance, effort S, finder's confidence 0.85, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:1086`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:225`
- **Claim.** For each sphere the loop calls approx_equal_range(phis...) and approx_equal_range(thetas...) for each of the 1406 channels, 1312 x 1406 x 2 = 3.7M searches, to find the same (phi range, theta range) per channel every time; only the 24 repaired hover spheres differ. Caching the per-channel ranges and reusing them while phis_scattered/thetas_scattered compare equal to the previous sphere's keeps the semantics CLAUDE.md marks load-bearing (channels still visited in file order so a later channel wins a shared cell; dba_max/sphere_max_/eaa_min still over matched channels only).
- **Evidence given.** In-process steady_clock around the loop in a scratch copy (sphere_r1_tree, baseline): place_ms = 47.3, 47.2, 44.7, 49.7, 51.8 per load, about 5% of the ~0.9 s load; 'sample' agrees (Database::Database self time 204 of 3304 samples). With a cache keyed on 'phis_scattered == placed_phis && thetas_scattered == placed_thetas': 10.6, 10.3, 11.8, 11.8, 9.7 ms (what remains is the 28 MB strided grid write). Demo output cmp-identical to golden/197421_LAE.csv, and identical to the unmodified binary's integrated output for B407_M3, AW139_M1, S-76D_fixed_load_factor and EC130_ambient_gated on a 246 ft grid.
- **Scenario.** Every run: about 35 ms of a ~0.96 s default invocation.
- **Proposed change.** Keep 'std::vector<std::pair<std::array<size_t,2>, std::array<size_t,2>>> placement' plus the two arrays it was computed for; recompute only when the current sphere's phi/theta arrays differ. About +12 lines.
- **Expected impact.** About -36 ms per load (roughly 4% of the default run).
- **Conflicts with docs.** Extends, does not undo, the CLAUDE.md note that the fill walks channels and places them; both listed semantics are preserved.

### spline-util/B.r1.6: Database load logs an identical 'Could not find variable ShaftPower' line for each of the 1312 groups, 97 percent of a demo run's log

- Finder's severity low, category performance, effort S, finder's confidence 0.85, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:84`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:886`
- **Claim.** ShaftPower is an optional variable (the comment at 880-883 says no shipped database has it), but the probe goes through get_scalar(), which logs `log("Could not find variable ", ...)` on every miss. Each line is formatted, timestamped, concatenated and flushed to disk under the logger mutex.
- **Evidence given.** Demo run (`niceops -S state/ci_state.csv -l log/demo.log`): log/demo.log has 1349 lines, of which `grep -c 'Could not find variable'` = 1312, all '[info] Could not find variable ShaftPower in ncid N.'. Timing 1312 identical log() calls with the project's logger to a file: 3.1 to 4.4 ms CPU (3.1 to 6.6 ms wall) per repeat. That is 0.3 to 0.6 percent of the 1.0 s run, inside the 0.75 s database load.
- **Scenario.** Every run of every shipped database pays it, and it buries the 37 informative lines under 1312 copies, which also makes the log harder to read when a real problem needs finding.
- **Proposed change.** Probe with a bare nc_inq_varid for optional variables (or add a `quiet` flag to get_scalar) and log once, after the loop, whether ShaftPower was present. About 5 lines.
- **Expected impact.** About 3 to 6 ms of CPU per run and a 97 percent shorter log. No effect on results.
- **Conflicts with docs.** CLAUDE.md's load-time breakdown attributes the load to netCDF (78 percent) and the Delaunay build; this 0.3 to 0.6 percent is inside 'our variable reads' and small, so the value is mostly the log.


## Spectral weighting: performance

### spline-util/B.r1.4: weighted_level spends about 60 ns per call on 28 scalar libm exp calls; a 4-wide exp over the contiguous band run is about 38 percent faster

- Finder's severity low, category performance, effort M, finder's confidence 0.6, output effect: sub-1e-9. **Evidence: C×2**
- Locations: `src/weighting.cpp:45`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1634`
- **Claim.** On the default spectral path (any database with spectra) each observer-state pair ends in weighted_level, which does `total += gains[band] * std::exp(band_levels[band] * ln10_over_10)` for every band with a non-zero gain: 28 independent scalar exp calls on a contiguous array (31 bands, 3 below 20 Hz). It is 25 to 27 percent of the roughly 230 ns a spectral evaluation costs, and unlike the rejected scalar acos/atan/log10 items it is a batch of independent inputs a vector routine can run 4 at a time. The two overloads (with and without `ratios`) are otherwise copies of one another.
- **Evidence given.** Per-evaluation cost: B407_fixed_load_factor.nod, -j 1, -L info, user CPU minus load: (2.57 s at gridstep 246 with 10,303,049 evaluations) - (0.81 s at gridstep 492 with 2,706,240) = 1.76 s over 7.6 M evaluations = about 232 ns each. Microbenchmark (CPU time via clock(), 31 bands, 28 live, L1-resident random levels 20..80 dB, linked against build/libniceops_core.a): weighted_level 59.7 ns, with ratios 62.6 ns, 28 bare exp calls 51-53 ns. Prototype with clang vector_size(32) double lanes: magic-number rounding, ln2 split by two FMAs, degree-12 Taylor on |r| <= 0.347, 2^k built by shifting the integer bits into the exponent; started at the first live band so 7 groups of 4 cover 28 bands: 51.4-54.8 ns (libm) vs 32.0 ns (vector); with degree 9 about 37 ns on the unaligned variant. Accuracy: max relative error of exp4 vs libm 3.1e-16 over [-20, 30]; max weighted-level difference 2.8e-14 dB over 4096 random spectra. -fno-math-errno plus -fveclib=Accelerate does not vectorize on arm64 (still calls _exp; 76 ns vs 77 ns), so it has to be explicit. All timings are CPU time on a shared machine; the ratio was stable across repeats but treat absolute numbers as indicative.
- **Scenario.** Spectral broadband-ground runs (no --ground_effect) do 94M weighted_level calls on the demo-size grid; at about 20 ns saved per call that is roughly 9 percent of the acoustic loop's CPU. With --ground_effect the loop is about 1100 ns per evaluation, so the saving falls to about 2 percent.
- **Proposed change.** Have weighted_level take [first_band, last_band) (already computed in niceops.cpp:996-1000), evaluate exp with a portable `vector_size` helper (the project already uses that spelling for Pair in sphere_database.cpp), and implement both overloads as one template on a ratio pointer that may be null. Keep the horizontal sum order fixed so results are independent of thread count. The windowing bound's 1e-9 relative slack (sphere_database.cpp:2054-2056) covers a 3e-16 exp difference. Skip if the maintainers prefer bit-for-bit libm results on the spectral path.
- **Expected impact.** About 20 ns of about 232 ns per spectral evaluation (roughly 9 percent of the no-ground spectral loop); about 12 fewer lines from merging the overloads.
- **Conflicts with docs.** Adjacent to CLAUDE.md's rejected 'hand-rolled acos, atan and log10' (libm ~8 cycles, 3 calls per evaluation that already overlap). The difference is batchability: 28 independent exps in one contiguous loop, measured here at 51 ns scalar vs 32 ns vector. It touches only the spectral path, not the goldens' broadband loop.

### db-eval/B.r1.6: weighted_level spends ~35% of a spectral evaluation in 28 scalar libm exp calls; a 2-lane polynomial exp measured 27% faster

- Finder's severity low, category performance, effort M, finder's confidence 0.5, output effect: sub-1e-9. **Evidence: C×2**
- Locations: `src/weighting.cpp:45`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1634`
- **Claim.** Every spectral evaluation ends in `total += gains[band] * std::exp(band_levels[band] * ln10_over_10)` over ~28 active bands plus one log10. On this platform that is 1.8-1.9 ns per exp (55-58 ns of ~150 ns per evaluation, ~66M evaluations on the demo). The blend is in dB so the exponentials cannot be precomputed per cell, but they can be computed two at a time with the GCC/Clang vector_size type already used for `Pair` in this file.
- **Evidence given.** Scratch microbenchmark (scratch/dbbounds/vexp.cpp, clang -O2, CPU time, min of 7): 32 bands, scalar libm exp loop + log10: 62.0 ns; two-lane range-reduced polynomial (ln2 split, degree-13 Taylor, 2^n by exponent-bit construction) + log10: 45.1 ns. Max relative error against libm exp over x in [-230, 30]: 2.2e-16. specbench2: weighted_level alone 53-58 ns of a 155-160 ns spectral evaluation, bare 31 x std::exp 56-59 ns.
- **Scenario.** Spectral default runs (about 66M weighted_level calls on the demo, 20 CPU-s); saves roughly 14-17 ns per call.
- **Proposed change.** Optional: an `exp_pair()` (or a 4-lane loop where AVX2/NEON allows) used by weighted_level and the bounds' log-sum-exp, keeping the current scalar loop as fallback for non-GNU compilers. Accumulate in fixed band order to keep thread-count independence.
- **Expected impact.** About 9-11% of a spectral evaluation; less on the broadband and ground paths (ground_effect is dominated elsewhere).
- **Conflicts with docs.** CLAUDE.md rejects hand-rolled acos, atan and log10 because libm does each in ~8 cycles; exp is not on the rejected list, and the gain here comes from two lanes, not from a cheaper scalar routine. The spectral path is not pinned by the byte-level golden, only by 1e-9 dB tests (test_spectral_propagation, ground_effect_* windowed-vs-marched).


## Refraction and shadow tables: performance

### refraction-profile/B.r1.0: hankel1_0's asymptotic branch divides complex numbers through __divdc3 once per series term; it is 56% of the ShadowTransition table build

- Finder's severity high, category performance, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: sub-1e-9. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/exact_solutions.cpp:413`, `src/refraction.cpp:646`
- **Claim.** ShadowTransition's constructor spends 56% of its samples in hankel1_0 (via upward_pressure -> modal_sum), and 58% of that is libgcc/compiler-rt's complex divide __divdc3 with its NaN/Inf recovery, from `term *= I * (-odd * odd) / (8.0 * k * z)` executed up to 60 times per call. The same trap CLAUDE.md records for std::complex in faddeeva (real arithmetic instead of std::complex) applies here and was not applied.
- **Evidence given.** exact_solutions.cpp:422 `term *= I * (-odd * odd) / (8.0 * k * z);` and :430 `return std::sqrt(2.0 / (pi * z)) * std::exp(I * (z - pi / 4.0)) * sum;` (a second complex divide, a complex sqrt and a complex exp per call). `sample` of the shipped build during the table build (windy point-receiver run): ShadowTransition::ShadowTransition 2505 samples, upward_pressure 2401, hankel1_0 1407, of which __divdc3 816 and hypot (std::abs per term) ~160. Micro-benchmark, 200k args |z| 15..3000, Im z >= 0, CPU time, 3 repeats: library hankel1_0 150 ns/call; reciprocal-once variant 100 ns (bit-identical in 99,999 of 100,000 args, max rel diff 4.8e-17); fully real-arithmetic variant (1/z once, per-term factor -odd^2/(8k) real, squared-magnitude stopping test, exp(-Im z)(cos,sin)) 54 ns/call, max rel diff 5e-16. Whole-program, scratch tree with only this change plus the unrelated apply() edits (the run makes 26k apply calls): `niceops -d databases/B407_fixed_load_factor.nod -v databases/B407.json -t tracks/S-76D/197421AC.csv --atmosphere atmospheres/example_windy.csv --refraction linear --frame_bearing 210 --ground_effect --gridstep 9000 -j 1` (table-dominated): user 6.03 s -> 3.23 s, instructions retired 94.26G -> 50.39G (/usr/bin/time -l). Prototype: /Users/evg5332/.claude/jobs/8792c8f6/tmp/scratch/refr_perf_lens/tree/src/exact_solutions.cpp. `niceops_unit_tests "H0 matches Mathematica on both sides of its switch"` and [shadow] pass on the prototype.
- **Scenario.** Any run with --refraction {linear,stratified} and --ground_effect builds one ShadowTransition per distinct microphone height plus one per plate. Measured for 31 bands at -j 1: 6.6 CPU-s per table (12 lowest bands 0.35 s each, from a per-band build). On a 32-microphone scattered run (arrays/coyle_approach.csv, -j 1) the table is ~5.6 s of 6.7 s user. CLAUDE.md says the validation harness runs a dozen -j 1 processes at once. The demo golden path never builds this table.
- **Proposed change.** Rewrite the asymptotic loop of hankel1_0 in real arithmetic: compute 1/z = (zr, -zi)/|z|^2 once, step the term with the real factor f_k = -odd^2/(8k) as term *= i f_k / z, stop on the squared magnitude (`n2 > last2`, `last2 < 1e-34`) instead of std::abs, and form the prefactor as sqrt(complex(2/pi*ir, 2/pi*ii)) * complex(e*cos(zr - pi/4), e*sin(zr - pi/4)), e = exp(-zi). Leave the power-series branch alone (its complex/double divides do not go through __divdc3). The 1e-9 H0 test against Mathematica values already covers both sides of the switch.
- **Expected impact.** hankel1_0 2.8x faster; table build about 46% fewer instructions (94.3G -> 50.4G on the table-dominated run), i.e. ~3 CPU-s saved per distinct microphone height per run. No effect on the broadband demo path.

### refraction-profile/B.r1.1: ShadowTransition::apply and ShadowAttenuation::rate recompute per-ray quantities (cbrt, log, log10) in every band, and the lit-far early-out fires on only 2.4% of calls

- Finder's severity medium, category performance, effort M, finder's confidence 0.85, output effect: sub-1e-9. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/refraction.cpp:808`, `src/refraction.cpp:549`, `src/refraction.cpp:736`
- **Claim.** For --refraction linear (one boundary) `a` is the same for every band, yet the band loop evaluates std::cbrt(pi*f/a) (line 858), ShadowAttenuation::rate's std::log(a) and std::exp(2*log_s) (lines 555, 557, on shadowed bands), and std::log10(s/s_min) twice (once in level(), line 740, once again for the V(0) lookup, line 868). All of these are (per-band constant) x (per-ray constant) and can be a multiply. apply() is 23% of the acoustic loop in a windy point-receiver run, and its early return (lines 833-838) is sized by the lowest band's diffraction length, so it almost never triggers.
- **Evidence given.** Instrumented scratch build, same run at --gridstep 492 -j 1: apply calls 2,808,960; no boundary 815,291 (29%); lit-far early-out 67,729 (2.4%); full band loop 1,925,940 (68.6%); band iterations 53.9M (28 per call), of which 11.9M shadowed (log+exp in rate) and 27.4M reach the table (two log10). `sample` of the shipped build over the loop: process_trajectory 5297 samples; ShadowTransition::apply 1243 (23%), of which cbrt 345, log10 96, ShadowAttenuation::rate 141; reflected_eigenray 936; direct_ray 296. Prototype (per-band cbrt(pi f) and log10(cbrt(pi f)) stored at construction; per ray cbrt(a), log10, lazy log(a); s = cbrt_pi_f/cbrt_a; xi = (r - r_b) cbrt(a)^2 / c0 * cbrt(pi f) since pi f = cbrt(pi f)^3; one log10 shared by level_at() and the V(0) interpolation; plate_offset() computed once): loop cycles 6.94G -> 6.23G (-10%) with the first version, 5.89G (-15%) together with finding 4, obtained as (full run) - (gridstep-9000 run, same table) of `/usr/bin/time -l` cycles elapsed. Max abs difference of the output CSV vs the shipped binary: 3.6e-14 dB. Unit tests [shadow],[refraction] and ctest -R 'refraction|stratified|atmosphere|band_history|golden' (36 tests) pass on the prototype.
- **Scenario.** Every (observer, state) evaluation of a --refraction linear --ground_effect run with an upwind component (68.6% of evaluations in the shipped example_windy profile) takes the full ~28-band loop; 14.7% of evaluations are in a shadow. On the demo grid this is ~94M evaluations. The stratified/band_boundary variants (between/fallback) still need one cbrt per band, but band_boundary()'s cbrt can use the same stored cbrt(pi f) since x = c0 / (gap cbrt(a_lin)^2 cbrt(pi f)).
- **Proposed change.** Store cbrt(pi f_b) and log10 of it per band in the ShadowTransition constructor. In apply(), for the single-boundary case compute cbrt(a_one), its log10 and log(a_one) once per ray (log lazily, only when shadow_depth > 0), pass log(a) to a new ShadowAttenuation::rate(band, a, log_a, c0) overload that rate(band, profile) delegates to, derive s and xi by multiplies, and share the single log10(s/s_min) between the table lookup and the V(0) lookup (split level() into a private level_at(band, xi, j, t)). Optionally, because xi_b is monotone in f, fill the inert suffix of bands with (weight 1, loss 0) after the first band with xi <= xi_ray (estimated at most ~2% more; not measured).
- **Expected impact.** About 10-12% of the acoustic loop for windy point-receiver runs (cycles), growing linearly with grid size; none on runs without --refraction.
- **Conflicts with docs.** CLAUDE.md rejects hand-rolled replacements for acos/atan/log10; this removes redundant calls to the unmodified libm functions and replaces none of them. It also does not touch the ShadowTransition fitted constants or the 'fitting cannot improve the law' notes.

### refraction-profile/B.r1.2: airy_asymptotic uses two complex pow calls, a complex exp and a complex divide; after the Hankel fix it is half of the remaining table build

- Finder's severity medium, category performance, effort S, finder's confidence 0.85, output effect: sub-1e-9. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/airy.cpp:45`
- **Claim.** With hankel1_0 fixed, the Newton/zero-following work in upward_pressure/first_impedance_airy_zero is dominated by airy() -> airy_asymptotic (531 of 1046 ShadowTransition-constructor samples). It computes zeta = (2/3) std::pow(z, 1.5), quarter = std::pow(z, 0.25) (complex pow = log + exp + sincos each), std::exp(-zeta), `e / quarter` (__divdc3), and std::abs(term_u) (hypot) every term. z^1.5 = z sqrt(z) and z^0.25 = sqrt(sqrt(z)) on the principal branch, so the powers, the divide and the hypots can be real arithmetic.
- **Evidence given.** airy.cpp:47 `const complex zeta = (2.0 / 3.0) * std::pow(z, 1.5);`, :69 `const complex quarter = std::pow(z, 0.25);`, :70 `ai = e / quarter * sum_u;`. `sample` after the Hankel change (table-only run, -j 2): ShadowTransition ctor 1090, upward_pressure 1046, airy 531, airy_asymptotic 498, newton 393, ordinary_zero 394. Micro-benchmark, 200k args |z| 5..60, arg in +-2pi/3: library 185 ns/call, real-arithmetic version 85 ns/call; max rel diff between them 4.1e-13. Against a long-double evaluation of the same series at |z| 20..60 the library's error is 4.2e-13 and the new version's 1.2e-13 (the exp of a large zeta amplifies rounding of pow; the old form is not the more accurate one). Whole-program on the table-dominated run: user 3.23 s -> 2.30 s, instructions 50.4G -> 37.9G on top of finding 1 (baseline 6.03 s / 94.3G). Prototype: scratch tree src/airy.cpp; unit tests [shadow],[refraction],[atmosphere_profile],[benchmark],[pe_shadow] all pass (4193 assertions, including 'the complex Airy function matches tabulated values and itself').
- **Scenario.** Same as finding 1: every ShadowTransition build (and the ShadowAttenuation table, which is only 4 ms). airy() is also called from tools and tests but only the transition table build is heavy.
- **Proposed change.** In airy_asymptotic form r = hypot(x, y), sqrt(z) and sqrt(sqrt(z)) with the quadrant-safe real formulas (t = sqrt((r +- x)/2) chosen by sign of x), zeta = (2/3) z sqrt(z), -1/zeta by conj/|zeta|^2, run the series in real (re, im) pairs stopping on u_k^2 |power|^2, take exp(-zeta) as exp(-Re zeta)(cos, -sin)(Im zeta), and use 1/quarter = conj(quarter)/sqrt|z|. airy() and the connection formula near the negative axis call it unchanged.
- **Expected impact.** airy_asymptotic 2.2x faster; a further ~0.9 CPU-s (-25% instructions) off each table build after finding 1. Combined findings 1+2: 6.03 s -> 2.30 s on the table-dominated run and 6.69 s -> 2.73 s user on the 32-microphone coyle_approach run (-j 1).

### refraction-profile/B.r1.3: reflected_eigenray recomputes the converged legs() and evaluates tan() next to sin/cos in every miss() call

- Finder's severity low, category performance, effort S, finder's confidence 0.8, output effect: sub-1e-9. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/refraction.cpp:141`, `src/refraction.cpp:190`, `src/refraction.cpp:271`
- **Claim.** Each miss(theta) builds a Legs (cos, sin, sin again, tan, three sqrt) and discards it; after the secant converges, line 192 `const auto l = legs(theta1);` (and line 271 `const auto l = legs(theta);` in the Illinois fallback) computes the same Legs again. tan(theta) at line 151 is sin/cos of values already in hand. A solve makes 3.4 miss() calls on average, so the redundant call is about one fifth of the work; reflected_eigenray is 17.7% of the acoustic loop in the profiled point-receiver windy run.
- **Evidence given.** refraction.cpp:143 `const double sine2 = std::sin(theta) * std::sin(theta);`, :151 `const double d_s = h_s * std::fabs(std::tan(theta));`, :192 `const auto l = legs(theta1);`. Micro-benchmark, 100k random solves (z_s 30-300 m, r 100-2500 m, gradient -0.012..0.03 /s, 3.41 miss() calls per solve counted): library 160 ns/solve vs 135 ns/solve with miss() keeping its last Legs, sin computed once and tan = sin/cos (max |dlaunch| 2.8e-16 rad, max rel dpath 8.8e-16 where the secant path is taken; my copy omitted the Illinois fallback, which the library takes for 0.3% of solves). Together with finding 3 the prototype loop is -15% cycles; this part is about 3% of the point-receiver refraction loop (sample: reflected_eigenray 936/5297 with __sincos_stret 241, atan2 198, tan 93 under it). clang and GCC-16 (checked with -S) already fuse the repeated sin, so only the reuse and the tan matter.
- **Scenario.** finish_ground() runs once per surviving (observer, state) pair for a point receiver (GroundGeometry::point): ~2.8M times in the --gridstep 492 windy run, ~94M on the demo grid.
- **Proposed change.** Let miss() store its Legs (or return them) and use the stored value for the converged theta in both the secant and Illinois branches; compute `const double sine = std::sin(theta)` once and use sine*sine and sine/cosine for d_s. The reuse is bit-identical; replacing tan by sin/cos moves d_s by an ULP.
- **Expected impact.** About 16% of a reflected_eigenray call, ~3% of a windy point-receiver loop; none for plate/bare receivers.


## Smoothing-spline solves: performance and error message

### spline-util/B.r1.5: within_tolerance solves the system 40 times per axis normally and 200 times up to lambda = 1e200 when the tolerance exceeds the line's residual

- Finder's severity low, category performance, effort M, finder's confidence 0.6, output effect: sub-1e-9. **Evidence: C×3**
- Locations: `src/smoothing_spline.cpp:340`, `src/smoothing_spline.cpp:373`
- **Claim.** The bracket grows lambda by 10x per solve until the residual reaches the tolerance, capped at 200 steps, and then bisects in log lambda for a fixed 60 iterations. For a straight-ish axis (residual about the weighted line already under the tolerance, very plausible for cross-track or altitude at the 10 ft default) all 200 expansions run and the answer is a lambda = 1e200 fit that is only an approximation of the least-squares line. The weighted LS line is an O(n) closed form and its residual can be compared with the tolerance first. The 60 bisection steps go about 8 past double precision, and a safeguarded secant on the smooth monotone residual(log lambda) would need about 8 solves.
- **Evidence given.** Standalone programs against build/libniceops_core.a, n = 6551 at 0.02 s (the demo track's own x, y, z in meters, tolerance = (10 ft)^2 * n = 60,861): within_tolerance takes 9.35, 9.44, 9.25 ms per axis (best of 5), single fit 0.232 ms, so about 40 solves each, 28 ms total for a geodetic run. On synthetic axes whose LS-line residual is below the tolerance: 27.4 to 29.3 ms each (200 solves, returned lambda = 1e+200). Accuracy of that path: for y = 80 sin(0.05 t) + noise, tolerance 1e12, returned fit vs the exact long-double LS line: max 6.9e-2 m, velocity error 2.9e-3 m/s, curvature 1e-194; the returned residual 9.74425e6 is below the true minimum 9.74939e6, which a straight line cannot be. Cause: cond(Q^T Q) is about 16 (n/pi)^4 = 3e14 for this n, so f = y - lambda Q gamma cancels at rounding level (lambda = 1e12..1e20 all give 3e-2..9e-2 m). Both errors are small next to a 3 m tolerance, so the accuracy is a footnote and the cost is the finding.
- **Scenario.** Every geodetic run pays about 28 ms in the spline (about 2 percent of a roughly 1.2 s run), and a level, straight track pays about 28 ms per straight axis on top.
- **Proposed change.** (1) Before the expansion, compute the weighted LS line and its residual; if tolerance >= that residual, return the line directly (fitted = line, curvature 0, lambda = infinity or the sentinel the test allows). (2) Replace the 60-step bisection with a safeguarded secant/Illinois on log lambda, keeping the current bracket and the `residual <= tolerance` guarantee. About +25 / -10 lines.
- **Expected impact.** About 24 of 28 ms on the default geodetic case and about 28 ms per straight axis, and an exactly straight line where the tolerance allows one. Against the roughly 1 s total this is 2 to 3 percent, only for WGS84 input; the demo case never calls the spline.
- **Conflicts with docs.** None. tests/test_smoothing_spline.cpp:214-221 ('a tolerance beyond the line's residual returns the line') and 169-183 (residual within 1e-6 relative of the bound) still describe the contract; the secant must keep the residual <= tolerance side. The geodetic README tables (0.085 dB etc.) would move by well under 1e-9 dB.

### trajectory-geodetic/B.r1.2: SmoothingSpline::within_tolerance runs 200 solves to lambda=1e200 on any near-straight axis, and throws a misleading 'not positive definite' error for tracks of about 40k samples or more

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort M, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: C×3**
- Locations: `src/smoothing_spline.cpp:340`, `src/smoothing_spline.cpp:246`, `src/trajectory.cpp:116`, `src/trajectory.cpp:290`
- **Claim.** When the tolerance is at or above the residual of the least-squares straight line, within_tolerance never gets `upper_residual >= tolerance`. The loop `while (upper_residual < tolerance && expansions < 200) { upper *= 10.0; ... }` (smoothing_spline.cpp:344-349) then multiplies lambda by 10 up to 1e200 with one O(n) pentadiagonal solve each, and returns fit(x,y,w,1e200). That is about 202 solves per axis where a closed-form line fit needs one. It happens on every straight or near-straight axis (a cruise-leg cross-track, say) under the default --path_smoothing 10 ft. Worse, R + lambda*Q'Q loses numerical positive-definiteness once R is negligible next to lambda*Q'Q. For n of about 40,000 rows this happens at lambda around 1e10-1e16, and solve_pentadiagonal returns false, which fit() reports as 'penalized system is not positive definite; check that x is strictly increasing and weights are positive' (a message that blames the input, which is fine). The whole trajectory is skipped.
- **Evidence given.** Straight line + 1 mm noise, tolerance 10 ft RMS (the geodetic reader's default), one axis, direct within_tolerance call:
n=3000  ok 11.8 ms  lambda=1e200
n=6500  ok 27.8 ms  lambda=1e200 (demo-track axes take ~10 ms each, lambda 1.8e4 / 1.3e5 / 8.3e4)
n=26000 ok 108 ms   lambda=1e200
n=40000, 52000, 65000, 80000, 104000: throw after 10-25 ms. The same at 10 Hz sampling.
with_smoothing sweep at n=40000: lambda 1e0..1e12 ok, 1e16..1e32 FAIL, 1e36 ok, 1e40..1e48 FAIL (erratic, i.e. rounding).
End to end: a 40,000-row (13 min at 50 Hz) near-straight geodetic CSV, default flags:
$ niceops -t long_straight.csv --gridstep 492 -> exit 1; log: `Skipping long_straight.csv: SmoothingSpline: penalized system is not positive definite; check that x is strictly increasing and weights are positive.` Nothing on the terminal.
Residual convergence towards the line is slow (relative change per decade still 7.6e-5 at lambda=1e10), so a 'residual stopped changing' cutoff would not fire before the failure; the exact line fit is what is needed. lambda=1e14 versus 1e200 fits differ by only 1.5e-8 m in position and 1e-14 m/s^2 in acceleration, so the fit at 1e200 was already round-off noise.
- **Scenario.** A 13-minute 50 Hz flight-test log (or an hour at 10 Hz) fed as a WGS84 track. Its cross-track axis is nearly straight, so the run dies with a message that points at the wrong cause. With shorter tracks the same axes silently cost 4-10x the solves they need.
- **Proposed change.** In within_tolerance, compute the weighted least-squares line's residual in closed form before bracketing. If `tolerance >= that residual`, return the line (fitted = a + b*x, curvature 0) with no solves; the header already documents this limit. For the remaining case, cap the upward search where lambda*p_diag dwarfs r_diag (about 1e12), or catch the PD failure during expansion and use the last good lambda instead of aborting. Reword the error so it does not blame x/weights when lambda is huge.
- **Expected impact.** Straight axes drop from about 202 solves to 0 (a few ms per track saved, larger on long tracks). Tracks of 40k rows and up stop failing on this path. Demo AC path and goldens do not use this code.

### spline-util/A.r1.1: within_tolerance throws a misleading 'not positive definite' error on valid long or irregular tracks whenever the tolerance exceeds the straight-line residual

- Finder's severity medium, category correctness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.95, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: C×3**
- Locations: `src/smoothing_spline.cpp:340`, `src/smoothing_spline.cpp:59`, `src/smoothing_spline.cpp:246`, `src/trajectory.cpp:116`
- **Claim.** When tolerance >= the residual of the weighted least-squares line, the upward search multiplies lambda by 10 up to 200 times (to 1e200). The unscaled LDL^T of R + lambda*P has condition number ~n^4 once lambda*P dominates R, so beyond roughly n ~ 25-30k uniform samples (or a single tiny time gap) a pivot goes non-positive at `if (!(value > 0.0) || !std::isfinite(value)) return false;` and fit() raises input_error('penalized system is not positive definite; check that x is strictly increasing and weights are positive.'). The tolerance form is documented to return the straight line in exactly this situation (smoothing_spline.hpp:47-49), and the condition is common: any axis that deviates from its line by less than the default 3.05 m RMS (level-flight altitude, cross-track in a frame aligned with the approach, a straight leg) takes this branch. The message blames x and weights, which are fine.
- **Evidence given.** Standalone driver linked against build/libniceops_core.a, within_tolerance(t, z, 3.048^2*n) on 50 Hz level flight (z = 65 m + 1 mm noise): n=20000 ok, n=25000 ok, n=30000 THROWS, 35000/40000/45000 THROWS. An exactly linear x(t) throws at n>=50000 too. With n=6551 level flight and one 1e-5 s gap in the time base: gap=1e-3 and 1e-4 ok, gap=1e-5 and 1e-6 THROW. End to end: a synthetic straight, level, 36000-row 50 Hz geodetic track (`niceops -t geo_long.csv ... --origin 39.8104276 -74.4263086 --frame_bearing 210`) exits 1 with stdout/stderr empty; log: '[error] Skipping .../geo_long.csv: SmoothingSpline: penalized system is not positive definite; check that x is strictly increasing and weights are positive.' The 20000-row prefix of the same file succeeds (exit 0). Related symptom in the bisection regime at large n: a curved 300000-sample track returns residual/tol = 0.799 (n=120000: 0.9977, n=60000: 0.9998), i.e. the noisy residual defeats the 'spend the whole budget' property that README documents. For calibration, at n=6551 the returned line at lambda=1e30 differs from the exact LS line by up to 0.147 m and its residual by 0.06% (60-digit Python Decimal reference).
- **Scenario.** A 10-minute (30000-row) 50 Hz geodetic recording of a straight-and-level leg, run with the default --path_smoothing 10, is rejected outright with an error that tells the user to check monotonic x, which they have already done. The tests only exercise this branch at n=20 (tests/test_smoothing_spline.cpp:214-221).
- **Proposed change.** Compute the weighted least-squares line's residual in closed form (O(n): weighted means, slope, sum of w r^2) before any solve. If tolerance >= that residual, return the exact line (fitted = line, curvature = 0, lambda = infinity) with no bracketing at all; otherwise bracket upward and treat a factorization failure as 'lambda too large' rather than as an input error (fall back to the line). Cap the expansion when the residual stops growing. Add tests at n=30000 level flight and with a 1e-5 s gap.
- **Expected impact.** Removes a spurious hard failure on long/irregular tracks, replaces up to 200 wasted O(n) solves with one O(n) pass, and makes the 'returns the line' contract exact instead of approximate to ~0.15 m.


## Plotting after a failed run

### main-cli/B.r1.0: Plotting after a failed single-trajectory run dereferences an empty std::optional

- Finder's severity medium, category correctness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.95, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: Rc C×2**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:2397`, `src/niceops.cpp:2554`, `src/niceops.cpp:2595`
- **Claim.** trajectory_holder is only assigned when process_trajectory returns. If the only trajectory throws, the catch at 2557 leaves the optional disengaged, and `else if (want_plots) draw_figures(*trajectory_holder, ...)` at 2597 then dereferences it. That is undefined behavior: the build is NDEBUG, so nothing checks it.
- **Evidence given.** Ran the shipped Release build in a scratch dir: `niceops -v nonexist.json -f figures/x.png --gridstep 492` gave exit=2, and no figure was written. log/niceops.log shows: `[error] Skipping tracks/S-76D/197421AC.csv: Unable to open vehicle configuration file: nonexist.json`, then `[info] 1 of 1 trajectories could not be processed.`, then `[error] Stopping: vector`. The last line is a length_error thrown from a Trajectory that was never constructed, and the exit code is 2 rather than the 1 that the comment at 2601 promises. Source: `std::optional<Trajectory> trajectory_holder;` (2397) ... `draw_figures(*trajectory_holder, integrated_levels, ...)` (2597).
- **Scenario.** A batch script runs `niceops -t bad_or_missing_vehicle.csv -f out.png`, or has a bad vehicle file or a database/track mismatch. Whether it prints 'Stopping: vector', crashes or draws garbage depends on heap contents. The real cause is buried in the log as a 'Skipping' line.
- **Proposed change.** Guard the call: `else if (want_plots && trajectory_holder)`, and otherwise log that plotting was skipped because the trajectory failed. Equivalently, skip plotting when failed_paths > 0. Add a CTest case (WILL_FAIL, or a check of the exit code) that runs a single failing trajectory with -f.
- **Expected impact.** Removes UB on an error path. The exit code becomes 1, as documented, instead of an accident-dependent 2 or a crash.

### main-cli/A.r1.4: draw_figures dereferences an empty std::optional when the only trajectory failed and figures were requested

- Finder's severity medium, category correctness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.95, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: Rc C×2**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:2397`, `src/niceops.cpp:2554`, `src/niceops.cpp:2581`
- **Claim.** trajectory_holder is assigned only when process_trajectory succeeds (2554). A failed trajectory is caught at 2557 and leaves it disengaged, but the plotting block at 2595-2599 calls draw_figures(*trajectory_holder, ...) whenever path_count == 1 and any of -p/-f/-F was given. Dereferencing a disengaged std::optional is undefined behavior; draw_figures then reads garbage from Trajectory::column().
- **Evidence given.** $ niceops -t nonexist.csv --gridstep 492 -f figures/x.png
exit=2, stderr empty; log: '[error] Skipping nonexist.csv: Unable to open CSV file' / '[info] 1 of 1 trajectories could not be processed.' / '[error] Stopping: vector' (a length_error from copying uninitialized vectors). Same with -F. The documented outcome (exit 1, 'Skipping ...') is replaced by exit 2 and a meaningless message; on another allocator/stdlib the same read can segfault. CI's GCC build would not diagnose it (no _GLIBCXX_ASSERTIONS).
- **Scenario.** A single-track run with -f/-F where the track has a header problem, the vehicle JSON is missing, or --trim leaves no states: the user gets 'Stopping: vector' and exit 2, or a crash, instead of the trajectory's real error.
- **Proposed change.** Guard the plotting block with 'if (failed_paths == 0)' (or trajectory_holder.has_value()); skip figures and keep exit code 1 when the trajectory failed.
- **Expected impact.** Removes UB from the error path and restores the documented 'Skipping' outcome and exit code.


## Dead code

### db-load/B.r1.3: Dead D-era contour-range code: Database::find_range, level_along_bearing, zero_range_level, the 7-argument level_at_observer overload and util/rootfind.hpp have no callers

### spline-util/A.r1.6: bisect() never terminates for tol = 0, has no bracket check, and its only caller Database::find_range is never called

- Finder's severity low, category simplification, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: C×6**
- Locations: `src/util/rootfind.hpp:12`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:2127`
- **Claim.** bisect() uses `tol` both as the bracket-width and the |f| stopping test. With tol <= 0 the interval collapses to two adjacent doubles, mid rounds to one of them, and `lo = mid` / `hi = mid` no longer shrinks the interval, so the loop spins forever. It also never evaluates f(hi), so a bracket without a sign change silently returns the upper (or lower) end. Its sole user is Database::find_range, which is declared in sphere_database.hpp:455 and defined at sphere_database.cpp:2127 but is referenced by nothing in src/, tests/, tools/ or the docs, so neither function is exercised by any test.
- **Evidence given.** Standalone driver: bisect(x-0.3, 0, 1, 1e-6) -> 0.29999923706054688 in 19 calls; bisect(5+x, 0, 100, 1e-3) -> 99.999619 (no sign change, returned silently); bisect(x-0.3, 0, 1, 0) was still running after 4 s and was killed. `grep -rn "find_range\|bisect(" src tests tools *.md` shows only the definitions and the single bisect call inside find_range.
- **Scenario.** Latent: anyone who wires find_range to a CLI contour option with a zero threshold hangs the run; a range_max that does not bracket the contour returns range_max without complaint (find_range's later check_level test only runs when zero_range_check is false).
- **Proposed change.** Either delete bisect() and find_range() (port leftovers with no caller or test), or, if kept for a planned contour feature, require tol > 0 (or loop a fixed 200 iterations), evaluate f(hi) and reject a non-bracketing interval, and add a test.
- **Expected impact.** Removes ~50 lines of untested, non-terminating code, or makes it safe before it gets a caller.
- **Conflicts with docs.** CLAUDE.md's port-fidelity rule protects D behaviors, and find_range mirrors a D function; deleting it is only safe if nothing in D's CLI reached it either (it does not in the current C++ CLI).
