### main-io-sel/B.r1.5: Dead `peak` variable in windowed_exposure (assigned in three places, never read)

- Finder's severity low, category simplification, effort S, finder's confidence 0.95, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: Rc V1 C×6**
- Locations: `src/niceops.cpp:317`, `src/niceops.cpp:350`, `src/util/sel.hpp:62`
- **Claim.** `std::size_t peak = 0;` is assigned at L324 and L350 but never read. It is a leftover of the walk-out-from-the-peak design, where the peak index seeded the walk; the outermost-crossing scans do not need it. Separately, `peak_range`'s `down` parameter is never passed by any caller.
- **Evidence given.** `clang++ -std=c++17 -fsyntax-only -Wall -Wextra ... src/niceops.cpp` -> `src/niceops.cpp:317:17: warning: variable 'peak' set but not used [-Wunused-but-set-variable]`. That is the only warning in the file. grep for `peak_range(` shows only tests/test_peak_range.cpp and sel.hpp call it, all with one argument.
- **Scenario.** Readers infer that the peak index feeds the window (the comment above still says 'so the bound has something to beat' and the path is described as peak-expanding). It does not, which hides how the outermost-crossing window is really found.
- **Proposed change.** Delete `peak` and its two assignments (net -4 LOC). Optionally drop the unused `down` parameter. Moot if the path is removed (finding 1).
- **Expected impact.** Small clarity gain; no runtime effect.
- **Verifier (context): confirmed, confidence 0.96.** The core claim is true and I reproduced it. In `windowed_exposure` (src/niceops.cpp), `peak` appears on exactly three lines: the declaration at L317 (`std::size_t peak = 0;`) and the assignments at L324 and L350 (`peak = it;`). No line reads it. The compiler agrees: `/usr/bin/clang++ -std=c++17 -fsyntax-only -Wall -Wextra ... src/niceops.cpp` gives `niceops.cpp:317:17: warning: variable 'peak' set but not used [-Wunused-but-set-variable]`. The reporter also has the history right. `git show f6afb16` shows `peak` was read by `expand_window(peak, nt, ...)`, which was the walk-out-from-the-peak design. `git show 9d6e986` shows the `expand_window(peak, ...)` call replaced by `ten_db_down_interval(nt, threshold, evaluate, certainly_below)`, which scans in from both ends and needs no peak index. That left `peak` as a dead leftover.

Nothing in the docs or the port-fidelity rule protects the variable. CLAUDE.md never mentions this local. It is not a D quirk, because it was added in f6afb16 as a C++ optimization and stopped being read in 9d6e986. The goldens and the byte-identical guarantee are unaffected. I copied src/niceops.cpp, removed the three lines, and compiled both versions with `clang++ -std=c++17 -O2 -DNDEBUG -c`. The two object files are byte-identical (`cmp` returned 0, both 870248 bytes), so the output effect is none-bitwise.

The reporter overstated some points:
- The net saving is -3 lines, not -4. The `if (level > best)` blocks keep their braces because `best = level;` is still inside them.
- The stated scenario is weak. The comment at L313-315 ("so the bound has something to beat") is about `best`, not `peak`, and it is still accurate. The L355-362 and L262-272 comments describe the outermost-crossing design correctly. The only misleading local artifact is the dead variable itself.
- The `down` parameter is not a leftover of this change. `peak_range(levels, double down = sel_window_db)` (sel.hpp L62-63) mirrors the D original, whose signature was `peak_range(double[] levels, double down = 10.0)` (`git show c74bab3:niceops.d`, L330). The D code never passed it either. Callers are `sound_exposure_level` (sel.hpp L157) and tests/test_peak_range.cpp, all with one argument. Nothing in tools/ or src/ passes it. Removing it is harmless but gains nothing and departs from the ported signature.

Severity is low and the value is marginal. The warning only appears with `-Wall -Wextra`, and CMakeLists.txt sets no warning flags, so nobody sees it in normal builds. It is a legitimate dead-code removal, but it is not worth its own commit.
  - Refined claim: In `windowed_exposure` (src/niceops.cpp), `std::size_t peak = 0;` (L317) and its assignments (L324, L350) are dead. Nothing has read `peak` since commit 9d6e986 replaced `expand_window(peak, ...)` with `ten_db_down_interval`. `peak_range`'s `down` parameter (sel.hpp L62-63) is also never passed, but that is the D original's signature, also never passed in D, so it is a harmless port artifact and not part of this leftover. The misleading-comment scenario does not hold: the surrounding comments describe `best` and the outermost-crossing window correctly.


## Duplication

### delaunay-exact/B.r1.4: Delaunay::nearest_vertex duplicates ConditionInterpolator::nearest

- Finder's severity low, category simplification, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/delaunay.cpp:422`, `src/delaunay.hpp:48`, `src/interpolator.cpp:151`, `tests/test_delaunay.cpp:218`
- **Claim.** Two identical linear nearest-point scans over the same vertex list exist. ConditionInterpolator already keeps `vertex_coordinates_` (a second copy of Delaunay::storage_'s real points) precisely so that nearest() works without a mesh, and its loop is the same arithmetic (delta = coord - query[j]; distance += delta*delta; strict <, first index wins).
- **Evidence given.** delaunay.cpp:426-441 vs interpolator.cpp:159-174: same loop body, same tie-breaking, both over the 2132 extended vertices. Delaunay::nearest_vertex is called only from interpolator.cpp:155 (`if (mesh_) return mesh_->nearest_vertex(query);`) and one unit test; no other src/tools user (grep). point_count() and dimension() are likewise test-only accessors.
- **Scenario.** Maintenance only: two copies of the fallback that must be kept in step, plus a duplicated coordinate array.
- **Proposed change.** Delete Delaunay::nearest_vertex (hpp 3 lines + cpp 21 lines), drop the `if (mesh_)` branch in ConditionInterpolator::nearest, and move the 'Nearest vertex' test to an interpolator test (which would also cover the previously untested fallback path).
- **Expected impact.** About -38 LOC net (-45 source, +7 test relocation); no runtime effect.

### refraction-profile/B.r1.6: LayeredAbsorption and EffectiveSoundSpeed carry the same height-grid construction and locate() twice

- Finder's severity low, category simplification, effort S, finder's confidence 0.8, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/atmosphere_profile.cpp:483`, `src/atmosphere_profile.cpp:520`, `src/refraction.cpp:293`, `src/refraction.cpp:330`
- **Claim.** Both classes tabulate a 1 m grid with the identical rule (`max_nodes = 20001`, `nodes_ = max(2, ceil(span) + 1)`, step widened past 20 km, `inv_step_ = 1/step_`) and the identical locate() (`u <= 0 ? 0 : min(size_t(u), nodes_ - 2)`, fraction = u - node, end cells extrapolate). Only one copy documents the extrapolation, and a fix to one (say, the round-off at the top edge) has to be repeated in the other.
- **Evidence given.** atmosphere_profile.cpp:487-496 and refraction.cpp:293-302 differ only in variable names; atmosphere_profile.cpp:520-525 and refraction.cpp:330-335 are the same five lines; the header comment at atmosphere_profile.hpp:190-192 is the only description of the extrapolation.
- **Scenario.** Maintenance drift between two tables that must agree on which node a height falls in (both are built from the same z_low/z_high in niceops.cpp:1057 and :1175).
- **Proposed change.** Introduce a small `UniformHeightGrid { z_low, step, inv_step, nodes; locate(z, node, fraction) }` in atmosphere_profile.hpp, constructed from (z_low, z_high), and have both classes hold one. Keep locate() arithmetic exactly as is so results stay bit-identical.
- **Expected impact.** About -20 lines and one place to document the edge behavior; no runtime effect (locate() is per state/observer, not per pair).

### main-cli/B.r1.5: The plt:: facade is a 15-function shim with one client, and -p can never display anything

- Finder's severity low, category simplification, effort M, finder's confidence 0.75, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/plotting.hpp:11`, `src/plotting.cpp:54`, `src/plotting_disabled.cpp:26`, `src/niceops.cpp:1799`, `src/niceops.cpp:2025`
- **Claim.** plotting.hpp mirrors matplotlib-d's call-by-call API (figure, contourf, tricontourf, three plot overloads, xlabel, ylabel, axis, colorbar, tight_layout, savefig, show, clear). The only client is draw_figures, plus one plt::available() at niceops.cpp:2582 (grep 'plt::' outside src/plotting* finds nothing else). Every new figure call has to be added to three files in sync, and plotting_disabled.cpp's 15 stubs are documented as never reached (lines 7-9). Separately, plotting.cpp:34 hard-wires Agg, which the docs call load-bearing for licensing, so `-p` (which draws everything and then calls plt::show(), niceops.cpp:1908) can never show a window.
- **Evidence given.** `niceops -S state/ci_state.csv --gridstep 492 -p` printed `UserWarning: FigureCanvasAgg is non-interactive, and thus cannot be shown`, exit 0, and produced no output file. It spent ~0.65 CPU-s more than a no-figure run (1.71 s user against ~1.0 s). `-p -f x.png` versus `-f x.png` differ by 0.03 s CPU (1.68 against 1.65), so the extra state figures built by -p are negligible. README.md:201 says -p 'Plot[s] the footprint with the flight track over it'. `import matplotlib.pyplot` versus `Figure` + `FigureCanvasAgg` was measured at 0.59 s versus 0.57 s user, so there is no import saving from bypassing pyplot.
- **Scenario.** Maintenance: adding a figure means editing plotting.hpp, plotting.cpp and plotting_disabled.cpp. User-facing: `-p` alone costs about 0.65 s and a stderr warning for nothing.
- **Proposed change.** Move draw_figures into plotting.cpp as two entry points, `plt::save_footprint(...)` and `plt::save_state_figures(...)`, taking plain vectors and paths, and inline the pyplot calls there. plotting.hpp then declares two functions plus available(), and plotting_disabled.cpp shrinks to three stubs. Drop plt::show() and the `plotting` parameter. Keep -p as a deprecated no-op that logs 'figures cannot be displayed; use -f/-F', or remove it. Validate by comparing the PNGs from the CI step byte-for-byte before and after.
- **Expected impact.** Net about -150 to -200 LOC across the four files (current total for the facade plus draw_figures is about 396 lines; the result is about 190), one place to edit per figure, and no misleading -p.

### db-eval/B.r1.7: niceops.cpp re-implements Database::group_blends for the packed-spectra table

- **Claim.** Both group trajectory states by their `blend.indices` with the same 'consecutive states nearly always share' shortcut plus a linear search; niceops.cpp additionally re-scans with std::find for every state to build packed_of_state. DirectionBounds already holds exactly that grouping (blend_of_state_) privately. Two copies of the grouping rule have to stay in step (a fixed_load_factor blend key, for example, is the indices only in both).
- **Evidence given.** niceops.cpp:1387-1412 (`packed_indices`, `last`, `found`, then a second loop with std::find) versus sphere_database.cpp:1799-1821. Costs: each is O(states x blends), 6551 x 52 on the demo, negligible; pack_spectra itself measured 0.4 ms per blend on AS350 (50 blends in 0.02 s), so nothing to gain in time.
- **Scenario.** Maintenance only: a change to how blends are keyed (say a fourth condition axis) must be made in two places.
- **Proposed change.** Have group_blends (or a small public free function) return `{std::vector<size_t> group_of_state, std::vector<std::array<...>> blends}`; niceops builds one PackedSpectra per group and `packed_of_state[it] = &packed[group_of_state[it]]`, and DirectionBounds reuses the same result.
- **Expected impact.** About -20 lines and one grouping rule; no runtime effect.

### pe-exact/A.r1.7: pe_compare.cpp and pe_field.cpp duplicate about 60 lines of setup and have already drifted

- Finder's severity low, category simplification, effort M, finder's confidence 0.7, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: recheck**
