- **Claim.** Both tools declare the same 15 CLI options, read and complete the atmosphere profile, build LayeredSoundSpeed and the effective-speed vector, construct the GroundModel and admittances, and fill a per-band PeSetup with identical field assignments and the same `seed + 1000u * b`. The header of pe_field.cpp (line 10) says its ground options are pe_compare's, but --porosity_rate is missing, so `--impedance_model variable_porosity` silently runs with alpha_e = 0 there and cannot be tuned; neither tool exposes layer_depth for delany_bazley_layer or PeSetup::domain_height, threads or layer_wavelengths.
- **Evidence given.** Side-by-side: pe_compare.cpp:41-81 vs pe_field.cpp:32-67 (options), :90-112 vs :77-89 (profile/layered/ground), :126-142 vs :94-110 (PeSetup fill loop). pe_compare sets ground.porosity_rate and ground.receiver_height (lines 107-110); pe_field sets neither (lines 86-89). Default bands also differ (four vs three).
- **Scenario.** A fix to one tool (a new PeSetup field, the seed rule, a ground option) is applied to one file only, as --porosity_rate already was; the two tools then disagree for the same command line.
- **Proposed change.** Move the shared options and setup into tools/pe_common.hpp (a `PlaneOptions` struct with `add_options(CLI::App&)` and `make_setups(const std::vector<double>& receiver_heights)` returning the layered profile, admittances and PeSetups); each tool keeps only its own output loop. Add --porosity_rate to pe_field through it. No CTest runs either tool today, so add one smoke test (tiny path, --samples 1, --ppw 10) that checks exit status and row count.
- **Expected impact.** About 60 fewer duplicated lines, one place to add options, and the tools get a smoke test that would also have caught the pe_field hang.

### trajectory-geodetic/B.r1.7: first_geodetic_position re-parses every AC track just to learn it is not geodetic (9.4 ms of a ~1 s run, once per path); Trajectory::frame() is dead and the lazy 'center on own first position' fallback only hides finding 1

- Finder's severity low, category simplification, effort S, finder's confidence 0.85, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/trajectory.cpp:357`, `src/niceops.cpp:2288`, `src/trajectory.hpp:161`, `src/trajectory.hpp:198`, `src/trajectory.cpp:181`, `src/trajectory.cpp:616`
- **Claim.** (1) For each path with no --origin, run() calls first_geodetic_position, which does `read_labeled_csv(filename)` on the whole file (trajectory.cpp:366) and then only inspects `csv.rows.front()`. For the default AC demo track that is a full 6551 x 27 parse that returns nullopt on the width test, and the Trajectory constructor then parses it a second time. The in-code comment (trajectory.cpp:359-362) says this 'does not show'; it is about 1% for one track but it is linear in the number of paths (84 shipped tracks in one invocation waste about 0.8 s). (2) `Trajectory::frame()` (trajectory.hpp:164-167) and the `frame_` member have no callers in src/, tests/ or tools/; run() keeps its own frame in `options.geodetic.frame` and never asks a Trajectory. That accessor belongs to the earlier 'adopt the first trajectory's frame' design that 'settled before anything loads' replaced. (3) With run() always supplying a frame whenever any track is geodetic, the constructor's fallback (`if (!frame) frame = LocalFrame(...)` at trajectory.cpp:181-190) is unreachable except through the bug in finding 1, where it converts a missing frame into a silent east/north projection.
- **Evidence given.** Timing program linked against build/libniceops_core.a on tracks/S-76D/197421AC.csv (CPU): read_labeled_csv 12.8 / 10.0 / 9.4 ms; first_geodetic_position -> nullopt 9.6 / 9.4 / 9.4 ms; Trajectory ctor 10-11 ms. The run's own log agrees: 'Reading vehicle configuration' 11:59:32.093 -> 'Parsed 6551 trajectory states.' 11:59:32.103. Replacing stringstream+stod by an in-place strtod row parser gives identical values and only saves 3.5 ms per pass (7.3 -> 3.8 ms), so the parser itself is not worth touching; the second parse is what to remove.
`grep -rnE '\.frame\(\)|->frame\(\)' src tests tools` finds no caller. Tests always build GeodeticInput with `input.frame` set (tests/test_trajectory.cpp:133-135, 507-508).
- **Scenario.** Batch runs over many AC tracks pay N extra full parses; maintainers carry an accessor and a fallback that nothing uses and that can mask a mis-settled frame.
- **Proposed change.** Give read_labeled_csv a `max_rows` argument (or a header-plus-first-row helper) and use it in first_geodetic_position; the result is unchanged. Delete `Trajectory::frame()` and `frame_`. Once finding 1 is fixed, make read_geodetic_states raise input_error when `geodetic.frame` is unset instead of self-centring, and drop the lazy block. Estimated net about -30 LOC and one full parse per path.
- **Expected impact.** About 9-10 ms (~1%) off each default run and about 0.8 s off an 84-track invocation; fewer ways for a missing frame to pass unnoticed.
- **Conflicts with docs.** trajectory.cpp:359-362 argues the double parse 'does not show' against a ~1 s database load; that holds for one track but not per-path scaling. Nothing in CLAUDE.md marks this as load-bearing.


## Test gaps

### refraction-profile/A.r1.2: Four wrong-way mutations in the linear-refraction code survive all 2659 assertions in [refraction],[shadow],[atmosphere_profile]; the curved plate branch of finish_ground has no test at all

- Finder's severity medium, category test-gap, effort S, finder's confidence 0.95, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/refraction.cpp:464`, `src/refraction.cpp:376`, `src/refraction.cpp:386`, `tests/test_refraction.cpp:275`
- **Claim.** Three mutants survive. (a) The plate branch of finish_ground with sin and cos swapped. (b) fit() widening a short span downward instead of upward. (c) The north-wind projection sign in fit(): weight = {1, hx, -hy}. No test reaches these because every wind test has wind_north = 0 or a purely linear profile. The widening test uses an exactly linear profile, where any window gives 0.02 /s. No unit test passes GroundGeometry::plate (grep of tests/ finds none). CTest covers plates only in uniform air, where finish_ground returns at 'if (!ray.curved) return;' before the plate code. (A fourth, dropping the 'direct.lit ?' guard, is an equivalent mutant because reflected_eigenray already returns exists=false past the boundary.)
- **Evidence given.** Mutated copies of refraction.cpp were linked with libniceops_core.a and tests/test_refraction.cpp, test_shadow_transition.cpp, test_shadow_attenuation.cpp and test_atmosphere_profile.cpp (build_mut.sh in /Users/evg5332/.claude/jobs/8792c8f6/tmp/scratch/r1_refr/mut). Baseline: 'All tests passed (2659 assertions in 39 test cases)'.
M1 (lines 464-465 swap std::sin/std::cos): All tests passed (2659 assertions in 39 test cases).
M3 (lines 378-380 -> 'z1 = z2 - min_fit_span; widened = moments(z1); m1 = &widened;'): All tests passed.
M4 (line 386 -> '{1.0, hx, -hy}'): All tests passed.
Controls that the harness can fail: dropping '- microphone_height' fails test_refraction.cpp:357; flipping hx,hy in direct_ray fails test_shadow_transition.cpp:317 and :489.
- **Scenario.** A refactor, or a fix to the reflected ray, flips the sign of the crosswind projection or the plate ground geometry. Every shipped track runs +x along 210 degrees, so real winds always have both local components, but no test would notice. The plate-microphone shadow results (GRAS on GR1425, the 2017 boards) would then be computed at the wrong grazing angle and distance, and CI would stay green.
- **Proposed change.** Add unit tests. (1) fit() on a profile with both wind_east and wind_north, both nonzero, and a non-unit (hx,hy), checked against brute-force least squares. (2) A short-span fit on a profile with a kink just above z1, which pins the upward widening. (3) finish_ground(GroundGeometry::plate) for a curved lit ray and a shadow ray: source_height = path_length*sin|arrival|, ground_distance = path_length*cos(arrival). (4) One CTest run with --refraction linear, a real wind and --plate_table compared against a stored result, so the curved plate path runs end to end.
- **Expected impact.** Closes the paths that carry the wind-direction and plate-geometry conventions with no coverage; would catch the sign and swap mutations above.

### spline-util/A.r1.4: Every SmoothingSpline test uses uniformly spaced knots, so four h[j] vs h[j+1] mutants of the system assembly pass the whole suite

- Finder's severity medium, category test-gap, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `tests/test_smoothing_spline.cpp:30`, `src/smoothing_spline.cpp:176`
- **Claim.** All 15 test cases build their knots with linspace(), for which h[j] == h[j+1], so any transposition of the two spacings in R, Q or the penalty bands is invisible. Production input is not uniform: fit_path fits the raw time column (trajectory.cpp:290-292), and the README/CLAUDE.md explicitly support sparse and unevenly spaced geodetic input. The one 'sparse' trajectory test resamples uniform data by stride.
- **Evidence given.** Copied src/smoothing_spline.cpp four times and compiled tests/test_smoothing_spline.cpp against each mutant with the project's Catch2 build (`./t_<m> "[spline]"`). Baseline: 'All tests passed (172 assertions in 15 test cases)'. Mutants, all of which also print 'All tests passed (172 assertions in 15 test cases)': M1 `r_off_[j] = h[j] / 6.0;` (was h[j+1]); M2 `c_[j] = 1.0 / h[j];` (was h[j+1]); M3 `r_diag_[j] = (h[j] + h[j]) / 3.0;`; M4 `b_[j] = -2.0 / h[j];`. A single added case on knots {0,0.3,1,1.1,2.5,2.6,4,7,7.2,9} that requires the first and second derivative to be continuous across every knot for lambda in {0, 1e-3, 1e-1} passes on the baseline (48 assertions) and fails on all four mutants.
- **Scenario.** A refactor of PenalizedSystem (it has already been restructured once for the lambda-independent precompute) that mixes up neighboring spacings would ship green and only distort geodetic tracks with irregular timestamps.
- **Proposed change.** Add a nonuniform-knot case (geometric or jittered spacing) to tests/test_smoothing_spline.cpp: C1/C2 continuity across knots, reproduction of a linear function at all lambda, and equality of the lambda=0 fit with an independently assembled natural spline. Also add the n=30000 level-flight and 1e-5 s gap cases from the within_tolerance finding.
- **Expected impact.** Closes a mutation gap on the only code path used for irregular timestamps.

### ground/A.r1.1: Plate-microphone azimuth mapping and outboard_azimuth have no test; a 90 degree or sign mutation passes the whole suite

- Finder's severity medium, category test-gap, effort M, finder's confidence 0.85, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:1596`, `src/niceops.cpp:1553`, `src/niceops.cpp:510`, `tests/test_plate_receiver.cpp:65`
- **Claim.** The plate-frame azimuth `atan2(separation.y, separation.x) - plate_mic_azimuth + 0.5*pi` is written out twice (sphere_database.cpp:1598-1599 and niceops.cpp:1554-1556, tied together only by a comment) and outboard_azimuth() picks the microphone side per observer. No test touches any of it: test_plate_receiver.cpp hands level_exact() an azimuth directly (line 73-74), grep finds no `plate_mic_azimuth`/`outboard` in tests/ or CMakeLists.txt, and the plate_windowed/plate_marched CTests (CMakeLists.txt:275-277) compare two runs that share the same mapping. Yet the mapping matters at the dB level.
- **Evidence given.** Demo grid, B407 fixed-load-factor database, variable_porosity 200, plates/gr1425_vp200.nc, `--gridstep 492`, shipped Release build. Changing only `--plate_mic_azimuth` from the default outboard to 0/90/180/270 degrees moves the level file by max 0.745/1.172/0.929/1.022 dB and mean 0.116/0.218/0.311/0.164 dB. The shipped tables are mirror-symmetric about the y axis (P_d(az) vs P_d(180-az): max relative difference 5.3e-15) but a 90 or 180 degree rotation changes P_d by up to 84% (measured 8.4e-01), so a dropped `+ 0.5*pi`, a flipped sign on plate_mic_azimuth, or an outboard_azimuth that returns the inboard direction would each move results by roughly 0.2-1.2 dB and go unnoticed. I derived the current formula against the test convention (atan2(-dy,-dx) = propagation direction source->microphone, microphone on +y) and it is correct as written; the gap is that nothing pins it.
- **Scenario.** A refactor of the azimuth expression at one of the two sites (the second is easy to miss), or of outboard_azimuth's degenerate branch for observers directly under the track (`nearest_distance > 1e-6`), silently rotates every plate microphone; all 53 CTest cases still pass.
- **Proposed change.** Move the expression into one function next to PlateReceiver (e.g. `plate_frame_azimuth(const Vec3& separation, double mic_azimuth)`), call it from both sites, and unit-test it: source directly inboard of an outboard-offset microphone gives pi/2; rotating geometry and mic azimuth by the same angle leaves PlateReceiver::level unchanged; outboard_azimuth returns the source-to-observer bearing away from the track and the documented right-of-track direction for an observer under it.
- **Expected impact.** Pins ~1 dB of plate-microphone sensitivity that is currently unguarded and removes a duplicated formula.

### db-eval/A.r1.6: No test pins the negative-EAA guard (excess_attenuation_is_a_loss) that the peak search and cull_range depend on

- Finder's severity low, category test-gap, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.hpp:613`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1759`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1849`
- **Claim.** Both cull_range() and direction_bounds() are upper bounds only because they drop the excess-attenuation term, which is valid only when no EAA factor is negative. The guard `return min_excess_factor_ >= 0.0;` makes them return 'unbounded' / unusable. The tests never build a database with a negative EAA (all fixtures use EAA = 0; the only `usable()` assertions are the two positive ones at test_sphere_database.cpp:186 and :868), so removing the guard breaks the exactness of the windowed path with no test failing.
- **Evidence given.** Mutation: in a scratch copy of the tree I changed the body of excess_attenuation_is_a_loss() to `return true;` and built niceops_unit_tests. `./build/niceops_unit_tests "[sphere_database],[spectral],[empty]"` -> `All tests passed (399008 assertions in 29 test cases)`. With the guard intact, a database with EAA = -20 correctly gives `direction_bounds usable=0; cull_range=inf` (neg.cpp), so this is a coverage gap only. grep of tests/ for min_excess_factor_ / excess_attenuation_is_a_loss finds nothing.
- **Scenario.** A refactor of the loader's eaa_min bookkeeping (it is computed over raw channel EAA before the non-finite-to-0 mapping, and NaN is skipped) or of the guard itself would silently make the windowed run inexact on any database with a negative EAA.
- **Proposed change.** Add a test that writes a small database with one negative EAA channel (write_fixed_load_factor_test_database already takes per-channel input to extend) and asserts `!db.direction_bounds(states).usable()` and `std::isinf(db.cull_range(blend, cutoff))`, plus the positive control.
- **Expected impact.** Pins a safety condition of the exactness claim.

### delaunay-exact/A.r1.4: The Delaunay and predicate tests cannot tell a Delaunay triangulation from any tiling, and in_sphere is unpinned above 3 dimensions

- Finder's severity low, category test-gap, effort M, finder's confidence 0.85, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `tests/test_delaunay.cpp:170`, `tests/test_predicates.cpp:90`
- **Claim.** test_delaunay.cpp checks only that located queries reconstruct and that the weights sum to 1. It never asserts the empty-circumsphere property, adjacency consistency or hull coverage. The dimension-generic claim ('a fourth operating parameter costs nothing') is exercised only by the random 4-D case, which passes for any valid tiling. in_sphere is pinned directly only in dimensions 2 and 3. There are no duplicate, NaN, coplanar, thin or hull-boundary cases, and each of those currently misbehaves (findings 1-4).
- **Evidence given.** Mutation runs (14 test cases, 3740 assertions in the baseline), scratch copies under scratch/r1_predel/mut:
  in_sphere always -1 for dim==4 (4-D cavity never grows) -> 'All tests passed'
  in_sphere always 0 for dim==4 -> 'All tests passed'
  exact-path orientation sign flipped for dim==4 -> 'All tests passed'
  enclosing scale 1000 -> 10 or 1.5 -> 'All tests passed'
Caught for comparison: in_sphere sign flipped for dim 4 or 2, dropped orientation normalization, lifted column rounded, exact difference dropping its low part, filter bound 0, cavity never grows or grows on the wrong sign in 3-D.
The predicates themselves are correct today. A differential fuzz against exact rational arithmetic (fuzz.py, seven generators including cube corners, ulp nudges, prisms and near-coplanar, dims 1-7, about 46000 cases) found 0 mismatches, including the in_sphere sign convention in every dimension. It confirms a differential test would pass now and would catch regressions.
- **Scenario.** A refactor of in_sphere or the cavity search for higher dimensions, or a change to the enclosing-simplex size, keeps the suite green while producing a non-Delaunay or holed mesh.
- **Proposed change.** 1. Add a differential predicate test on small integer coordinates for dims 1-5, using an exact int128 or Bareiss oracle, checking orientation and in_sphere including the on-sphere ties.
2. Add a Delaunay validity test through the public API: for random, lattice and prism inputs, take the indices from barycentric() at many queries and assert in_sphere(those points, every other input point) <= 0. Add a small Delaunay::simplices() accessor for tests if that is easier.
3. Add duplicate, coplanar, non-finite and thin-arc cases asserting the intended behavior once findings 1-3 are fixed.
- **Expected impact.** Turns four surviving mutants into failures and pins the dimension-generic claim.

### trajectory-geodetic/A.r1.7: Several trajectory-path behaviors can be mutated without any test failing

- Finder's severity low, category test-gap, effort S, finder's confidence 0.85, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/trajectory.cpp:602`, `src/trajectory.cpp:778`, `src/trajectory.cpp:357`, `tests/test_trajectory.cpp:547`, `tests/test_geodetic.cpp:55`
- **Claim.** Four load-bearing behaviors have no test that would notice their removal or breakage. (1) The constructor's `correct_vertical_speed_sign(states, filename)` call at line 602 is exercised by nothing: the vz tests call the free function directly, and the one Trajectory-level test asserts that the shipped S-76D and B205 tracks are NOT flipped. (2) watts_trim (crop rules, ixmax choice) is untested. (3) first_geodetic_position is tested only with the default unit (which is how finding 1 escaped). (4) to_geodetic is round-trip tested only in an unrotated frame, although `--latlon` with `--frame_bearing` depends on the rotated case.
- **Evidence given.** Loading all 84 shipped tracks (AW139, B205, S-76D) through the Trajectory constructor: 0 log lines `vz runs opposite`, so no shipped file reaches the correction. The tests/ tree references correct_vertical_speed_sign only at lines 553, 567, 583 and 594 of test_trajectory.cpp (direct calls) and never references watts_trim. The Newton inverse is in fact correct in rotated frames (round-trip 2e-9 m at bearings 0 and 210, latitudes 0-85 degrees), so (4) is a gap, not a bug.
- **Scenario.** Deleting line 602 makes every 2017-style positive-down file (most of the validation set) fly its descents as climbs; swapping `imin_z < imin_v` for `>` in watts_trim changes which part of the flight is integrated. `ctest` still passes 53/53.
- **Proposed change.** Add cases that write a small positive-down AC-format temp file and assert Trajectory flips vz; a constructed-trajectory watts_trim test; `first_geodetic_position` with `meters` on an `alt_m` file; and a `track_aligned` round trip through `to_geodetic`. Optionally a header/row-drop test for nan and empty fields (finding 3).
- **Expected impact.** Prevents silent regressions in the vz sign fix, the trim rule and the unit plumbing.

### pe-exact/A.r1.5: Tests would not notice the exact solutions losing absorption, a 5% error in the phase-screen spectrum, or wrong band weights; README's '0.06%' screen variance is seed luck

- Finder's severity low, category test-gap, effort S, finder's confidence 0.85, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: recheck**
- Locations: `tests/test_benchmark.cpp:51`, `src/exact_solutions.cpp:335`, `tests/test_pe_turbulence.cpp:55`, `tests/test_pe_turbulence.cpp:96`, `src/pe.cpp:78`, `README.md:744`
- **Claim.** Three load-bearing behaviors have no test that can fail. (1) `problem_at`/`pe_at` never set absorption, so upward_pressure/downward_pressure/homogeneous_pressure with nonzero absorption (complex k0 and complex l), which niceops_benchmark uses for every published 'exact_*' column, run in no test. (2) The screen variance test allows 8% and the mean-field test 0.07 on a ratio, so the spectrum constant can be off by about 5% unnoticed. (3) Band weights (F1) are unpinned. Also the README/CLAUDE.md statement that the variance agrees 'to 0.06%, less the 4% the finite column leaves out' does not hold: the 0.06% is what seeds 1..200 happen to give.
- **Evidence given.** Mutation 1: in a scratch copy of exact_solutions.cpp change wavenumber() to `return {2.0 * pi * p.frequency / p.sound_speed, 0.0};` -> `./bench_mut "[benchmark]"` prints `All tests passed (1326 assertions in 6 test cases)`. Mutation 2: in a scratch copy of pe.cpp change `0.033 * c2` to `0.0347 * c2` (+5.2% in screen variance) -> pe_phase_screen_variance and pe_turbulent_mean_field_decays both pass. Measured variance/(0.96*formula): 1.0006 with the test's 200 seeds but 1.0221 with 2000 seeds (scratch/pe_r1/var.cpp); the deterministic ensemble value sum(a_n^2)/2 for that grid is 0.9748 of the untruncated 0.7817 k^2 dx (gamma/4) L0^(5/3) formula (detvar.cpp), so the truncation is 2.5%, not 4%, and 200 seeds carry about 2% sampling noise. A test that would have caught mutation 1 would pass today: with absorption 5.4 dB/km (1 kHz) or 0.18 dB/km (100 Hz) on both sides, PE - exact over r >= 100 m is rms 0.010 dB / max 0.015 (case 2, 1 kHz, 500 m), rms 0.024 / 0.060 (case 2, 100 Hz, 1 km), rms 0.010 / 0.018 (case 3, 100 Hz), rms 0.009 / 0.012 (case 3, 1 kHz) (absorb.cpp).
- **Scenario.** A refactor of wavenumber(), of ell = (R/2k0^2)^(1/3) for complex k0, of the screen spectrum constants, or of the band offsets changes published benchmark or turbulence numbers with a green suite.
- **Proposed change.** Set absorption in problem_at/pe_at for one frequency in each of cases 1-3 (5.4 dB/km at 1 kHz), margin 0.1 dB. For the screens, either expose or recompute the deterministic variance sum(a_n^2)/2 for the test grid and pin it to 1e-9 against the formula times the truncation fraction, or raise the seed count and tighten to about 3%. Correct the README/CLAUDE.md sentence to the deterministic 0.975 figure. Add the band-average test from F1.
- **Expected impact.** Closes three silent-regression paths; adds about 1 s of test time for the absorption cases.

### main-io-sel/A.r1.6: Test gaps around the writers, the windowed skip path and the receiver azimuth: several load-bearing behaviors are pinned only by self-comparison

- Finder's severity low, category test-gap, effort M, finder's confidence 0.7, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `tests/test_peak_range.cpp:14`, `src/niceops.cpp:195`, `src/niceops.cpp:512`, `CMakeLists.txt:268`
- **Claim.** (a) ten_db_down_interval is unit-tested only with `certainly_below` always false (via peak_range); the lazy skip and windowed_exposure's seed/bound/scan wiring are exercised only by end-to-end windowed-vs-marched cmp tests on one track at --gridstep 492 (about 400 observers, of which CLAUDE.md says roughly 0.7% have a dip inside the window). A synthetic-history test with a sound fake bound (levels + slack) comparing lazy vs eager over random dips would pin the tie and dip rules directly. (b) outboard_azimuth's value is asserted nowhere: no ctest passes --plate_mic_azimuth and every plate comparison (mixed_receivers_are_their_subsets, plate_windowed_is_exact, refraction_uniform_plate_is_straight) runs the same function on both sides. The value matters: on the 8 mixed_flush microphones SEL moves 0.3-0.5 dB between 'outboard' and 180 degrees (e.g. 80.44 vs 80.84, 70.57 vs 70.91), so flipping the sign of `- 0.5*pi` or returning 0 would slip through. (c) write_band_history_file's content is compared only with itself (band_history_refracted_is_straight diffs refracted_i3 against straight_i3 through the same writer): `range / unit.to_meters`, column order, and reception_time = t + range/c are unpinned. I checked the documented property by hand: A-weighting the band columns and summing in energy reproduces level_dBA to 9e-4 dB (with rounded IEC constants) on every 200th row of straight_i0.csv, so a test of that is easy. (d) -a, --units/--grid/--gridstep on the CLI, and -D's absolute values are in no ctest; I verified DNL == SEL to 1.4e-14 dB by day and +10.000000000000043 dB at night, but nothing keeps that true. (e) iota_exclusive, read_observer_file and parallel_chunks (anonymous namespace / header) have no direct test at all, which is how findings 1-4 got through.
- **Evidence given.** `grep -rn 'parallel_chunks\|iota_exclusive\|read_observer_file\|outboard_azimuth\|windowed_exposure' tests/ CMakeLists.txt` returns nothing; `grep -n plate_mic_azimuth CMakeLists.txt tests/*.cpp` returns nothing. SEL vs azimuth table from running the mixed_flush case with `--plate_mic_azimuth outboard|0|90|180|270`.
- **Scenario.** Any of the mutations above (sign of the outboard offset, a units slip in the band-history writer, an off-by-one in the lazy end scan on a multi-lobed track) leaves all 53 ctest cases green.
- **Proposed change.** Move ten_db_down_interval/windowed_exposure-style logic behind a testable header (it is already templated), add the synthetic lazy-vs-eager test, a plate azimuth pin (one stored SEL per azimuth mode on a two-microphone file), a band-history content test (energy sum, units, column count), and a small `-D` daytime-equals-SEL ctest.
- **Expected impact.** Closes the gaps that let the writer and CLI defects in this list ship.

### main-cli/B.r1.7: CTest self_test also runs the whole demo case and rewrites the file regression_golden reads

- Finder's severity low, category test-gap, effort S, finder's confidence 0.7, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `CMakeLists.txt:250`, `src/niceops.cpp:2348`
- **Claim.** `niceops --self_test` does not return after Database::self_test (the D original did not either), so the CTest case `self_test` goes on to run the default demo and rewrites integrated/197421_LAE.csv. That is the input of regression_golden, whose FIXTURES_REQUIRED covers only demo_run (regression_default). self_test is not in the fixture, so under `ctest -j` it can truncate and rewrite the file while golden_compare is reading it. The comment at CMakeLists.txt:252-254 says the fixture exists to make `ctest -j` safe, but this writer is outside it.
- **Evidence given.** A scratch run of `niceops --self_test` ends with `Writing output to integrated/197421_LAE.csv` and leaves that file (276706 bytes). Stress test: 6 back-to-back demo runs writing integrated/197421_LAE.csv while golden_compare polled it in a loop gave `reads=1341 failed=1`, i.e. golden_compare fails when it reads during the rewrite window, which is a few ms per write. The two other tests that use the default -o are regression_default (CMakeLists.txt:250) and self_test (258); every other niceops CTest case passes -o.
- **Scenario.** `ctest -j` on a developer machine: self_test finishes its demo phase while regression_golden is diffing the file, giving a rare unexplained regression_golden failure. CI runs serially and so does not see it. It also spends about 30% of that test's CPU on a demo run that repeats regression_default.
- **Proposed change.** Change the self_test command to `niceops --self_test -o integrated/self_test_LAE.csv --gridstep 492`. It is CMake-only, needs no code change, and does not disturb the D-fidelity behavior of continuing after the self-test. Alternatively, put self_test into the same RESOURCE_LOCK or fixture as the demo.
- **Expected impact.** Removes a latent flake under parallel ctest, and shortens the self_test case by roughly a second of CPU.


## PE

### pe-exact/A.r1.0: solve_pe_bands band average puts full weight on both band edges: 0.44 dB rms (1.1 dB max) off the true band mean

- Finder's severity medium, category accuracy, effort S, finder's confidence 0.85, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: reviewer-measured at HEAD (section 10 C, P1)**
- Locations: `src/pe.cpp:421`, `src/pe.cpp:448`, `src/pe.hpp:106`
- **Claim.** The band level is documented as an energy average 'spread evenly in log frequency across the one-third-octave band', but the samples sit at k/(samples-1), so the two end samples are the band edges 2^(+-1/6), and all samples are weighted 1/samples. For the default 5 that gives the edges weight 0.2 each, where a uniform-in-log-f average gives them at most 0.125 (trapezoid) or 0 (midpoint). The band mean is therefore biased toward the edge frequencies, which matters wherever level varies across the band (interference nulls, shadow edges).
- **Evidence given.** pe.cpp:424 `const double offset = samples <= 1 ? 0.0 : (static_cast<double>(k) / (samples - 1) - 0.5) / 3.0;` and pe.cpp:454 `sum.relative_level[rcv][q] += std::pow(10.0, 0.1 * one.relative_level[rcv][q]) / samples;`. Measured with a standalone program linked to build/libniceops_core.a (scratch/pe_r1/band_real.cpp): still air, grass sigma_e 200, 500 Hz band, 100 m source, 1.2 m receiver, r = 100..400 m: solve_pe_band(samples=5) vs solve_pe_band(samples=41, edge weight only 1/41) differ by rms 0.438 dB, max 1.119 dB (e.g. r=110 m: 0.347 vs -0.772 dB; r=210 m: -1.615 vs -2.253). Quadrature study over 41 single-frequency marches (scratch/pe_r1/band.cpp), reference = 41-point trapezoid in log f, energy domain: `./band 500 100 1.2 800` -> current 5-sample rule rms 0.326 dB, max 1.270; five cell-centerd samples (offsets ((k+0.5)/5-0.5)/3) rms 0.028, max 0.110. `./band 250 30 1.2 600` -> 0.101/0.404 vs 0.009/0.035. In an upward-refraction shadow-edge case (linear lapse -0.018 /s, levels above -30 dB): 500 Hz, 30 m source rms 0.028/max 0.087 vs 0.002/0.006; 250 Hz, 100 m source 0.079/0.400 vs 0.008/0.062. Same 5 marches, roughly 10x smaller error.
- **Scenario.** Any niceops_pe_compare / niceops_pe_field run at the default --samples 5 (and solve_pe_band(...,5) in tests): every 'pe_<band>' CSV column carries this quadrature error in the lit/interference region, so rms comparisons of the ray models against the PE inherit up to about 0.3 dB rms of PE-side noise that is not physics. Shadow-edge calibration cases are affected far less (0.03-0.08 dB rms).
- **Proposed change.** Use cell-centerd sample frequencies: offset = ((k + 0.5) / samples - 0.5) / 3.0 for all samples >= 1 (samples == 1 still gives offset 0, so the special case can go). Keep the equal-weight energy sum. Add a test that compares solve_pe_band(setup, f, 5) with a 41-sample average on a lit interference case (e.g. 500 Hz, 100 m source, 1.2 m receiver, rigid or grass, r in 100..400 m) at about 0.1 dB; today no test pins the band weights at all (the two solve_pe_band tests only check a >20 dB drop and a 3 dB median).
- **Expected impact.** Band-level quadrature error falls about 10x at identical cost (0.33 -> 0.03 dB rms in the lit region). Stored PE runs and README/plot tables shift by 0.03-0.3 dB rms; the shadow-edge calibrations (ShadowTransition constants) should be re-scored with --no_pe-style reruns but were fitted to within 0.1-1 dB so are not expected to move materially.

### pe-exact/A.r1.1: PE default grid (20/10 points per wavelength) is not converged beyond about 6000 wavelengths in downward refraction, and the tools give no warning

- Finder's severity medium, category accuracy, effort S, finder's confidence 0.7, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: stale (fixed by split-step march)**
- Locations: `src/pe.cpp:135`, `src/pe.hpp:30`, `tools/pe_compare.cpp:72`, `tools/pe_field.cpp:62`
- **Claim.** Crank-Nicolson range error and the second-order vertical stencil's dispersion both accumulate with range measured in wavelengths. The docs quantify grid error only for the stored upward-refraction shadow cases (README: 'no level past 100 m by more than 0.7 dB') and, in the test comment, for benchmark case 2 to 1 km (0.3 dB at 20 ppw). Downward refraction to several km, the regime pe_compare is aimed at for downwind profiles, is worse by an order of magnitude, and the PE is the reference the ray models are scored against.
- **Evidence given.** Convergence study with the Airy profile c0/sqrt(1-2z/R) (exactly the test profile), impedance ground (sigma_e 200), reference (40 ppw, 20 range ppw), levels below -15 dB excluded (scratch/pe_r1/conv.cpp). `./conv 500 0.05 4000 5 1` (500 Hz, 0.05 /s, source 5 m, receiver 1 m): default (20,10) vs (40,20): r 500-1500 m rms 0.004 dB; 1500-3000 m rms 0.049, max 0.205; 3000-4000 m rms 0.483, max 1.618. `./conv 1000 0.03 6000 30 1.2` (1 kHz, 0.03 /s, 30 m source): 1500-3000 m rms 0.027, max 0.152; 3000-6000 m (8800-17600 wavelengths) rms 0.917, max 5.015 dB. The reference is itself short of converged: (40,10) vs (40,20) rms 0.262/max 1.27 and (30,15) rms 0.239/max 1.01 over 3-6 km, so true default-grid error is at least this large. Height resolution dominates ((20,20) vs (40,20): rms 0.656 over 3-6 km), range step second.
- **Scenario.** niceops_pe_compare --atmosphere <downwind profile> --bands 500 1000 --max_range 20000 (feet): the PE column beyond ~3 km is off by O(1) dB rms and up to 5 dB at 1 kHz, indistinguishable in the CSV/plots from the converged part; error metrics against the ray models then blame the wrong side.
- **Proposed change.** Cheapest: print a stderr warning (and a README line) when max_range / min wavelength exceeds about 5000 and the profile is not uniform, naming --ppw/--range_ppw as the remedy. Better: scale the default points_per_wavelength with max_range in wavelengths (or add a --converge option that reruns at 1.5x and reports the difference), and record the measured growth in the README next to the 0.7 dB statement. A 4th-order vertical stencil is the structural fix but is not needed to start.
- **Expected impact.** No change to results by default; users and the calibration harness learn when the PE reference stops being trustworthy. Raising the grid costs 2.25x (30,15) to 8x (40,20) run time for a 4x to 10x smaller error at long range.
- **Conflicts with docs.** README says doubling the grid resolution moves no level past 100 m by more than 0.7 dB; that was measured on the upward-refraction calibration cases. Here the same doubling moves downward-refraction levels by up to 5 dB at 3-6 km, 1 kHz. The 'do not coarsen the range step' guidance in CLAUDE.md is consistent with this but does not quantify the height-grid growth.

### pe-exact/A.r1.2: niceops_pe_field hangs and exhausts memory when --source_height is 0 (no --top)

- Finder's severity medium, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.95, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: R**
- Locations: `tools/pe_field.cpp:73`, `tools/pe_field.cpp:91`
- **Claim.** With a ground-level source and no --top, z_top = 2 z_s = 0 and dz = z_top / 150 = 0, so the loop `for (j = 0; j * dz <= z_top + 1e-9; j++) heights.push_back(j * dz)` never terminates and grows `heights` without bound.
- **Evidence given.** pe_field.cpp:74-75: `const double z_top = top > 0.0 ? top * unit.to_meters : 2.0 * z_s; const double dz = dz_out > 0.0 ? dz_out * unit.to_meters : z_top / 150.0;` and :92 `for (std::size_t j = 0; static_cast<double>(j) * dz <= z_top + 1e-9; j++)`. Reproduced: `cd $(runenv.sh pe_r1)/run; build/niceops_pe_field --atmosphere atmospheres/isa.csv --toward 270 --source_height 0 --max_range 1000 --bands 500 >/dev/null` -> `ps -o rss` shows 13,144,608 KB after 4 s and still allocating at 7 s (killed by hand). No error message; the process never reaches the PE.
- **Scenario.** Plotting the field of a ground-level source (a legitimate case for a field plot) without also passing --top. The same unbounded growth occurs for any tiny --dz relative to --top, since the height count is never checked.
- **Proposed change.** Reject non-positive --step/--top/--dz with CLI11 (`->check(CLI::PositiveNumber)`), fall back to a positive default top (for example max(2 z_s, 100 ft)) when z_s == 0, and cap the number of heights (say 100000) with a clear error before the loop. Compute the count as floor(z_top / dz) + 1 instead of iterating on a floating-point comparison.
- **Expected impact.** Turns a memory-exhausting hang into an immediate error or a sensible default.

### pe-exact/A.r1.3: PE columns are emitted, unflagged, for paths steeper than the PE's stated 30 degree validity; errors reach 5-16 dB

- Finder's severity low, category accuracy, effort S, finder's confidence 0.8, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: stale (fixed)**
- Locations: `tools/pe_compare.cpp:88`, `tools/pe_compare.cpp:160`, `tools/pe_field.cpp:116`, `src/pe.hpp:20`
- **Claim.** pe.hpp states the wide-angle equation is valid to about 30 degrees, yet pe_compare writes a PE level at every output range, the first of which defaults to max_range / 100, and neither the CSV nor tools/plot_pe_compare.py marks or masks the steep ones. Anyone computing an rms error of a ray model against the PE column over the whole file scores the ray model against a wrong reference at the near ranges.
- **Evidence given.** PE vs the Weyl-van der Pol two-ray field (the same reference test_pe.cpp uses, scratch/pe_r1/steep.cpp), 100 m source, 1.2 m receiver, grass sigma_e 200, still air. 250 Hz, elevation of the direct path 78.7/68.2/59.0/51.3/45.0/33.7/26.6 deg at r = 20/40/60/80/100/150/200 m: PE - exact = +1.07, +4.81, +15.69, -7.18, -3.17, +0.01, +0.15 dB. 1 kHz: +0.03, -7.28, -0.06, +10.73, -7.17, -0.27, -0.85 dB. Beyond about 35 degrees it agrees to a few hundredths of a dB, inside 50 degrees it is arbitrary. With the default step, `--max_range 8000` (ft) puts the first PE value at 24 m from a 328 ft source.
- **Scenario.** niceops_pe_compare --source_height 328 --receiver_height 3.937 --max_range 8000 (the README example): the first several rows carry PE values off by several dB; plot_pe_compare.py draws them.
- **Proposed change.** In pe_compare and pe_field, write NaN for the PE (or add a validity column) wherever atan((z_s + z_r) / r) exceeds about 35 degrees, and say so in the header comment and README. Optionally have solve_pe return this range as PeResult::valid_from so both tools and the plot script share it.
- **Expected impact.** Removes a few near-range garbage points from every comparison; no effect at the ranges the README tables use.

### pe-exact/A.r1.4: solve_pe validates too little, throws inside pool threads (process abort), and its own size guard runs after the out-of-bounds write it protects against

- Finder's severity low, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: partly reproduced, section 10 C, P5(b)**
- Locations: `src/pe.cpp:126`, `src/pe.cpp:195`, `src/pe.cpp:273`, `src/pe.cpp:378`, `src/pe.cpp:431`, `src/pe.cpp:454`, `src/util/parallel.hpp:16`, `tools/pe_compare.cpp:66`
- **Claim.** (a) solve_pe's exceptions (invalid_argument, length_error) are thrown inside parallel_chunks workers, so any bad input aborts the process instead of reaching a handler; parallel.hpp still says 'nothing here throws'. (b) The `m < 3` guard at pe.cpp:202 comes after `std::vector<complex> main(m)` (195) and `main[0] += ...` (198), so m == 0 dereferences a null pointer before the check can throw. (c) Missing checks: samples <= 0 (per_band = max(samples,1) but the divisor is `samples`), points_per_wavelength / range_points_per_wavelength <= 0 or absurdly small, output_step <= 0 (line 378 loops forever pushing results), negative receiver heights (line 275 casts a negative double to size_t, undefined). The CLI adds no range checks.
- **Evidence given.** Observed with the shipped build (run dir from runenv.sh, isa.csv, source 300 ft, receiver 4 ft): `--max_range 0` -> `libc++abi: terminating due to uncaught exception of type std::invalid_argument: solve_pe: frequency, range and receivers are required` (three threads, abort). `--ppw -20` -> uncaught std::length_error: vector (abort). `--bands 0` -> uncaught runtime_error from ground_impedance (abort). `--samples 0 --max_range 300` -> stderr 'PE: 1 bands x 0 frequencies' and the pe_500 column is `inf` on every row, exit 0. `--range_ppw 0` -> header only, exit 0. `--ppw 0.01` -> pe column -3000 dB on every row, exit 0. `--receiver_height -4` -> pe column -3000/-2826 dB, exit 0 (on x86 the size_t cast of a negative double is UB and typically selects the top row). ASan/UBSan build of pe.cpp with ppw = 0.002 (n = 1, m = 0): `runtime error: reference binding to null pointer of type 'std::complex<double>'` at `main[0] +=` then SEGV, before the `m < 3` throw.
- **Scenario.** A mistyped or out-of-range option to niceops_pe_compare / niceops_pe_field either aborts with a core dump from a worker thread or, worse, exits 0 with inf / -3000 dB in the CSV that a plotting script will happily draw.
- **Proposed change.** Validate every PeSetup in solve_pe_bands on the calling thread before dispatch (frequency, max_range, output_step, both ppw > 0, receiver heights >= 0, samples >= 1, z ascending, n large enough) and move the `m < 3` check ahead of line 195. Make parallel_chunks capture the first exception with std::exception_ptr and rethrow after joining (and fix the comment), and wrap the tools' main in try/catch that prints the message. Add `->check(CLI::PositiveNumber)` to --samples/--ppw/--range_ppw/--max_range/--step and `->check(CLI::NonNegativeNumber)` to the heights.
- **Expected impact.** Bad input gives a one-line error and a nonzero exit instead of aborts or plausible-looking junk.

### pe-exact/A.r1.6: niceops_core uses std::thread (solve_pe_bands) but only some targets link Threads::Threads

- Finder's severity low, category build, effort S, finder's confidence 0.4, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: Rc**
- Locations: `CMakeLists.txt:203`, `CMakeLists.txt:232`, `src/pe.cpp:431`
- **Claim.** pe.cpp (inside niceops_core) instantiates parallel_chunks and so std::thread. Only `niceops` and `niceops_benchmark` list Threads::Threads; niceops_unit_tests (which runs solve_pe_band in test_pe.cpp and test_pe_turbulence.cpp), niceops_pe_compare and niceops_pe_field link niceops_core alone, and niceops_core does not propagate the dependency.
- **Evidence given.** `grep -n Threads CMakeLists.txt` returns only lines 203, 204 and 245. CI is ubuntu-latest (glibc >= 2.34, where pthread is in libc), so it cannot show this. On glibc < 2.34 with GCC/libstdc++ an executable that does not link -pthread sees `__gthread_active_p() == false` and std::thread throws std::system_error 'Enable multithreading to use std::thread: Operation not permitted', unless another shared library (e.g. HDF5 via netCDF) happens to pull in libpthread. This is the reason `niceops` links Threads::Threads explicitly. Not reproducible on this macOS host.
- **Scenario.** Building the tools or the unit tests on an older Linux distribution (RHEL 8, Ubuntu 20.04) as the README invites: the PE tests or pe_compare fail at run time with a system_error.
- **Proposed change.** Add `find_package(Threads REQUIRED)` before niceops_core and `target_link_libraries(niceops_core PUBLIC Threads::Threads)`; then the per-target additions become redundant.
- **Expected impact.** Removes a latent link/run failure on older glibc; no effect elsewhere.


## Numerical edge cases

### db-eval/A.r1.5: Spherical spreading is extrapolated inward without a floor: levels grow as 1/r inside the sphere and are +inf at r = 0

- Finder's severity low, category accuracy, effort S, finder's confidence 0.5, output effect: changes-goldens. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:1343`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1415`
- **Claim.** `spreading_loss = 20.0 * std::log10(range * inv_sphere_radius_)` (and its eval_spectrum twin) goes negative for range < R and to -inf at range = 0, so the ground level exceeds the sphere-surface level without limit. The excess-attenuation term reverses sign inside the sphere too. Everything downstream of eval already treats r <= R as special (windowed_exposure evaluates rather than trusts the bound there), but the model itself has no near-field cap. Both asserts that would have flagged +inf are compiled out (NDEBUG, known).
- **Evidence given.** An observer exactly coincident with a hovering source (hover_e.csv, observer at (0,0,100) ft): output row `0,0,inf`, exit code 0. One foot below: 143.05 dB; 10 ft below: 123.03 dB (the 100 ft ring is ~103 dB). On the demo case 28 of 14400 grid observers have their closest approach inside the 30.48 m sphere (lowest track height is 15.8 m), with up to 4.41 dB of extrapolated spreading gain over the sphere-surface value baked into golden/197421_LAE.csv (closest cases x=200/225, y=0: range 18.3 m).
- **Scenario.** Heliport/vertiport or pad observers near a landing or takeoff; a WGS84 waypoint track that touches down on an observer. The SEL of those observers is dominated by a 1/r extrapolation of a compact-source model well inside its validity radius, and is inf for an exact overlap.
- **Proposed change.** At minimum, guard range <= 0 (clamp to a small epsilon or raise a clear error) so the output never contains inf. Optionally cap the spreading gain at the sphere surface (use max(range, R) in the spreading term), which is a modeling decision: it changes those 28 golden cells by up to 4.4 dB and would need the goldens regenerated and an explicit statement under the port-fidelity rule. State the current behavior in the README either way.
- **Expected impact.** Removes inf/absurd SEL for near-coincident observers. Any real floor changes results for observers inside the sphere radius.
- **Conflicts with docs.** Port fidelity (D behavior) applies to the floor. The r = 0 guard alone does not change any golden.

### db-load/A.r1.5: Level is +inf at exactly zero range (Level(dB) = inf in the output CSV), and there is no floor inside the source sphere

- Finder's severity low, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:1344`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:2062`
- **Claim.** eval() computes spreading_loss = 20*log10(range * inv_sphere_radius_) with no lower bound on range, and level_from_geometry passes range = sqrt(squared_range) straight in (its `range > 0 ? 1/range : 0` guard only keeps theta finite). At range == 0 the level is +inf; the `assert(std::isfinite(...))` lines that would catch it are compiled out in the shipped Release build. Nothing downstream filters it (the only isfinite in niceops.cpp is the evaluated-count test), so the observer's SEL is inf.
- **Evidence given.** r1_db/zerorange.cpp (S-76D_M3.nod, source at (100,50,0), observer moved up the z axis): 0 m: inf dB; 0.001 m: 175.4; 1 m: 115.4; 5 m: 101.4; 15 m: 91.8; 30.48 m: 85.6; 60 m: 79.6.
End to end with the shipped build (runenv dir): observer file rows 'id,x,y,z' copied verbatim from row 3002 of tracks/S-76D/197421AC.csv (so the state and the observer convert to the identical position), niceops -t tracks/S-76D/197421AC.csv -d databases/S-76D_M3.nod -v databases/S-76D.json -b obs.csv: exit 0 and the output row is '-2462.3183580700002,156.89971298,inf'; the same point 10 ft higher gives 103.0 dB.
- **Scenario.** An observer file that includes a point the aircraft passes through (an onboard or pad microphone, a synthetic test track, a grid node at a touchdown point at z = 0): the row is inf, and any run-wide average/maximum written by the metrics file (-m) is infinite too.
- **Proposed change.** Floor the range used for spreading at a small positive value (or at sphere_radius_, if a measured case shows the 1/r extrapolation inside the source sphere is wrong; the D original had neither, so that stronger change needs the measurement the port-fidelity rule asks for). The minimal change is to guard exact zero only, and to warn once per run when any observer lies within the sphere radius of a state.
- **Expected impact.** Removes an inf that poisons the whole-run metrics; the in-sphere 1/r extrapolation remains a modeling question.
- **Conflicts with docs.** The floor inside the sphere would depart from the D original's behavior; only the exact-zero guard is unconditional.

### db-eval/A.r1.7: pack_spectra shifts a uint64_t by band >= 64 for databases with more than 64 bands

- Finder's severity low, category correctness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.6, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:1375`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1458`
- **Claim.** pack_spectra() sets `packed.empty_bands[...] |= std::uint64_t {1} << band` for every empty band in [first_band, last_band). The header states that databases with more than 64 bands skip the fast path, and eval_spectrum() does guard on `n_bands <= 64`, but pack_spectra() itself has no such guard. With more than 64 bands and a gated (empty) entry at band >= 64 the shift count is >= the type width, which is undefined behavior (x86 and AArch64 wrap the count, so the harmless wrong bit lands in a mask that is then never read). The packed values for such a database (cells x 4 x bands doubles per blend) are also built for nothing.
- **Evidence given.** Code reading only: `packed.last_band = std::min(last_band, n_bands)` at l.1367 is n_bands for the default last_band, and l.1379-1380 shift by `band` without a `band < 64` test. No shipped database has more than 64 bands (largest spectral ones carry 26 third-octave bands), so I did not execute the path.
- **Scenario.** A narrow-band spectral database (e.g. 1/12-octave, > 64 bands) with ambient-gated directions run with --spectral (niceops.cpp:1403 packs for every blend unconditionally). UBSan -fsanitize=shift would flag it, and a compiler that exploits the UB is permitted to do anything.
- **Proposed change.** Skip the mask (and the packing itself, so the memory is not spent) when n_bands > 64: add `band < 64` to the condition, or return an empty PackedSpectra so eval_spectrum's fallback loop is used.
- **Expected impact.** Removes UB and wasted memory for wide-band databases. No effect on shipped ones.

### db-eval/B.r1.5: pack_spectra shifts a 64-bit mask by band index without checking band < 64 (undefined behavior for databases with more than 64 bands)

- Finder's severity low, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.85, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/sphere_database.cpp:1358`, `src/sphere_database.cpp:1458`, `src/sphere_database.hpp:383`, `src/niceops.cpp:1403`
- **Claim.** The header says databases with more than 64 bands 'skip the fast path', and eval_spectrum does (`n_bands <= 64`), but pack_spectra still runs for every spectral run (niceops.cpp:1403) and executes `packed.empty_bands[c * n_slots + k] |= std::uint64_t {1} << band;` for every band in [first_band, last_band) with v <= empty_level_db. With n_bands > 64 and any floor-valued entry in band >= 64 that shift count is >= the type width, which is undefined behavior (flagged by -fsanitize=shift; on arm64/x86 it silently sets band mod 64, which is harmless only because the masks are then never read).
- **Evidence given.** Code as quoted; sphere_database.cpp:1379-1380 `if (band >= packed.first_band && band < packed.last_band && v <= empty_level_db) packed.empty_bands[c * n_slots + k] |= std::uint64_t {1} << band;` has no `band < 64` term, while sphere_database.cpp:1458 guards only the reader. Shipped databases have 31 bands, so nothing trips today; not reproduced under a sanitizer (would need a >64-band database, which write_sphere() can produce).
- **Scenario.** panam builds a 1/12-octave or 100-band database with gated (-inf -> -1000 dB floor) entries above band 63; a sanitizer build of the tests or CI reports the shift.
- **Proposed change.** Add `band < 64 &&` to that condition (or skip the mask allocation when n_bands > 64), and add a write_sphere/pack_spectra case with 70 bands and one floored entry.
- **Expected impact.** Removes a UB path; no effect on current databases.

### delaunay-exact/A.r1.2: The finite enclosing simplex (1000x extent) makes real simplices disappear for point sets thinner than about 2e-4 of their extent

- Finder's severity low, category accuracy, effort S, finder's confidence 0.8, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/delaunay.cpp:45`, `src/delaunay.cpp:339`
- **Claim.** The artificial vertices sit 1000*max(1, extent) away. A hull facet of circumradius r survives Bowyer-Watson only if a sphere through it excludes every artificial vertex, which needs the next point to be more than roughly r^2/(2*scale) inside. For sets that are near-planar in normalized coordinates the hull facets are swallowed by the artificial vertices' huge spheres. The real region then has holes, or is empty, and barycentric() returns false, so the caller silently uses the nearest sphere. The class is advertised as a general Delaunay, and the outcome is a cliff rather than a gradual loss.
- **Evidence given.** 2-D arc of 24 points, thickness t relative to unit extent, coverage of queries that are inside the true convex hull:
$ ./thin
  t=1e-01, 1e-02, 3e-03, 1e-03, 3e-04: 22 real simplices, 100.0% located
  t=1e-04: real simplices 0, located 0 of 95379 (0.0%)
  t=1e-05: real simplices 0, located 0 (0.0%)
3-D: 40 points at z in [0, 1e-6] give 16 real simplices, and 45 of 46 real-region boundary facets fail the convexity check (./validate2 in_sliver3.txt), with 0 of 20000 in-hull queries located.
Raising the scale to 1e6*extent or 1e9*extent fixes both (thin_sc: 100% located at 1e-4 and 1e-5). On the real S-76D_M3 database, scale 1e6 gives 10049 simplices, 0 violations and 0 hull notches. Indices and weight bits are byte-identical to the current build for all 6551 demo states plus 20000 random queries (cmp_base vs cmp_1e6, cmp says IDENTICAL).
The unit suite does not notice: mutating the scale to 10*extent passes all 14 test cases.
- **Scenario.** A database whose conditions are nearly affinely dependent after normalization, for example gamma a near-exact function of mu across all spheres. Interpolation silently degrades to nearest-sphere with no diagnostic.
- **Proposed change.** Raise the enclosing scale to 1e6*extent or more. Exactness is unaffected, since the coordinates remain normal doubles far below overflow. Optionally add a test with a 1e-4-thick arc, in 2-D and 3-D, asserting that all in-hull queries locate.
- **Expected impact.** Removes a robustness cliff at negligible cost. Shipped databases are bit-identical, as measured above.

### delaunay-exact/A.r1.3: Delaunay::barycentric answers 'inside' or 'outside' for a query on the hull boundary depending on the previous query

- Finder's severity low, category correctness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.8, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/delaunay.cpp:139`, `src/delaunay.cpp:339`, `src/interpolator.hpp:46`
- **Claim.** locate() stops at the first simplex where no facet strictly separates the query. A query lying exactly on a hull facet belongs to both the real simplex and the artificial simplex across that facet, so the walk ends in whichever it reaches first, which depends on hint_ (the previous located simplex). If it ends in an artificial simplex, barycentric() returns false even though the point is in the closed hull. The same query therefore gets interpolation or the nearest-sphere fallback according to history. The 0.1 domain extension in ConditionInterpolator hides this for the shipped databases, because the unit cube is strictly interior. It is exposed by extend_domain_by = 0 (a public parameter) and by anyone using Delaunay directly.
- **Evidence given.** S-76D_M3 conditions with no extension (./hullhist2):
  queries ON the mu=0 hull face: after an interior query 5000/5000 report inside; after an outside query 0/5000 do; 5000 differ.
Lattice 5x5x5: a query on a hull face, (4,2,2), reports outside from 40 of 616 starting simplices; the hull vertex (4,4,4) from 122 of 616 (./hullq lat3.txt 4 2 2).
With the shipped extension, all 20 databases show 0.000% fallback for uniform queries and for queries snapped to unit-cube faces with 6 random hints each (./cov2 on real_*.txt).
- **Scenario.** Anyone builds ConditionInterpolator with extend_domain_by=0, or reuses Delaunay for another parameter set. Hover-like queries at the envelope edge flip between interpolated and nearest-sphere levels depending on the preceding trajectory state.
- **Proposed change.** In Delaunay::barycentric, when the located simplex has an artificial vertex, test the neighbors across its facets that contain only real vertices. Accept a real simplex that contains the query with orientation >= 0 on every facet. Or state in delaunay.hpp that queries within the hull boundary are unspecified and require extension.
- **Expected impact.** Makes the API deterministic in the query alone. No effect at present, because the extension covers the cube.

### trajectory-geodetic/A.r1.5: Projection has no longitude wrap and no distance sanity check, so an antimeridian crossing or a sign-flipped --origin yields nonsense with no error

- Finder's severity medium, category correctness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.8, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: R**
- Locations: `src/geodetic.cpp:108`, `src/trajectory.cpp:173`, `src/niceops.cpp:2276`
- **Claim.** to_local computes `lon -= reference_.longitude * deg_to_rad` without wrapping into (-pi, pi]. The only longitude guard, `std::fabs(lon) > 10.0`, runs before that subtraction and only rejects absurd inputs. Callers check |lon| <= 180 only. A track that crosses 180 degrees, or a wrong-sign --origin, therefore feeds a delta near +/-2pi (or ~150 degrees) into the polyconic formula, which returns finite garbage. There is no error and no check that positions land within a plausible distance of the origin. The header promises 'Throws for a position outside the projection's domain'.
- **Evidence given.** Reference lon 179.99, lat 40: a point at -179.99 (1.7 km east across the antimeridian) projects to (5,947,928.6, 12,361,454.4) m. A frame at (39.8, +74.4) receiving a point at (39.8, -74.4) returns (-7,633,660, 8,367,008) m. A 1 Hz track at lat -17 crossing 180 at 30 m/s through Trajectory: x=1800 m at t=59 s, then x=-2.0e7 m, y=-2.6e7 m with mu=95,578 (0.161 at the start). The reader raises no error. Projection accuracy elsewhere is fine: within +/-3 km over 3170 points across hemispheres, longitudes and rotations, the worst deviation from exact ECEF-derived ENU is 0.6 mm.
- **Scenario.** Operations near the date line (Fiji, Aleutians, Chatham Is.), or a typo in --origin's longitude sign. Output is meaningless (levels at -inf or the floor), the run exits 0, and the cause is invisible.
- **Proposed change.** Wrap the longitude difference: `lon = std::remainder(lon - ref_lon, 2*pi)` (or the equivalent add/subtract of 2pi). Also raise input_error, or at least warn, when a projected position lies more than a stated distance (say 200 km) from the frame origin. That catches the flipped-sign origin and a wrong --origin.
- **Expected impact.** One-line fix for the antimeridian, and turns a silent absurd-frame run into a clear error for a mistyped origin.

### trajectory-geodetic/B.r1.5: LocalFrame::to_local does not wrap the longitude difference, so positions across the antimeridian project to thousands of kilometers away with no error

- Finder's severity low, category correctness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: R**
- Locations: `src/geodetic.cpp:108`
- **Claim.** to_local subtracts the reference longitude in radians (`lon -= reference_.longitude * deg_to_rad;`, line 123) and feeds the raw difference to `sin(lon*sin(lat))`. The only domain test is `std::fabs(lon) > 10.0` on the pre-subtraction longitude (line 114), so a difference of up to 2*pi passes. A reference at 179.99 deg E and a point at -179.99 (0.02 deg, 1.7 km apart) gives a difference of -6.28 rad and coordinates in the thousands of kilometers. A track that crosses 180 deg, or an --origin on one side of it with a track on the other, is silently mis-projected. Nothing checks the projected size.
- **Evidence given.** cxxlink program calling LocalFrame::to_local:
lat 39.8, ref lon 179.99: point 180.00 -> x = 856.4 m (correct); point -179.99 -> x = 5,908,875 m, y = 12,549,410 m.
lat -17.7 (Fiji): point -179.99 -> x = -18,851,148 m, y = -26,647,018 m.
lat 64.0: point -179.99 -> x = 1,853,318 m, y = 610,274 m.
Reference -179.99 and point 179.99 (1.7 km apart) -> x = -5,908,875, y = 12,549,410 m.
- **Scenario.** Operations near Fiji, the Aleutians, Chukotka or Chatham Islands with `--origin` just on the other side of the antimeridian from the track. Rare, but the failure is unbounded and silent.
- **Proposed change.** Wrap the difference into [-pi, pi]: `lon = std::remainder(lon - reference_.longitude * deg_to_rad, 2.0 * pi);`. std::remainder is exact and returns its argument unchanged when |x| < pi, so every same-side input is bit-identical. Add a test at 179.99/-179.99. Optionally normalize to_geodetic's returned longitude into (-180, 180].
- **Expected impact.** Correct projection across 180 deg; bit-identical for all current inputs and the goldens.

### refraction-profile/A.r1.1: reflected_eigenray returns a root that is not an eigenray when the receiver is above the source under downward refraction

- Finder's severity low, category correctness, effort M, finder's confidence 0.9, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/refraction.cpp:141`, `src/refraction.cpp:171`, `src/refraction.cpp:241`, `src/refraction.cpp:453`
- **Claim.** legs() assumes the reflected ray reaches z_r on its first, ascending crossing: from_ground = kappa_r/(d0+d_r). When z_r > z_s and the gradient is positive, launch angles with rho = h_s/cos(theta) < h_r never reach z_r. There v<0 and d() returns sqrt(max(v,0)) = 0, so the clamped miss() is a continuation of the range function that is not monotonic. The secant warm start and the Illinois bracket then accept a zero of that continuation. The result is exists=true with a launch angle whose traced ray ends at the wrong height, and with a wrong grazing angle. The claim that range is monotonic in launch angle 'checked across both gradient signs' holds only for z_r <= z_s.
- **Evidence given.** Fuzz (fuzz2.cpp): random a in +-[3e-4, 0.1], z_s in [0.5,1000] m, z_r in [0.5,100] m, r in [30,5000] m, trace of the returned launch by RK4 with one bounce. Counts by class: a<0, z_r<=z_s: ok=1727 wrong=0; a<0, z_r>z_s: ok=865 wrong=0; a>0, z_r<=z_s: ok=1949 wrong=0; a>0, z_r>z_s: ok=912 wrong=117.
Concrete case (case1.cpp): a=0.01, z_s=2, z_r=3, r=800 m. Code: launch=-0.003366, grazing=0.011357. Its traced ray ends at z=1.8954 m, not 3.0000. The true first-crossing root, by scanning launch angles and bisecting, is launch=-0.008834, grazing=0.013989, so grazing is 19% low. Through finish_ground the equivalent geometry is h'=6.09 m against 8.19 m. Delany-Bazley grass, receiver 3 m, excess_ground_attenuation per band, code minus true: -1.98 dB at 200 Hz, -3.05 at 250 Hz, -2.39 at 1 kHz, -2.01 at 1.6 kHz, +7.95 at 5 kHz, -9.37 at 8 kHz. At r=300 the same inputs are exact; at r=2000 the code lands on a valid over-the-top root. Whether it is right therefore depends on range, and nothing signals when it is not.
- **Scenario.** Reachable when the microphone height passed to finish_ground exceeds the source height: --receiver_height larger than the aircraft's hub height, or a per-observer height column (id,x,y,z,height) for a mast microphone, with downwind (positive-gradient) paths, on the low states of a takeoff or landing. The shipped demo track never goes below 51.8 ft (15.8 m), so nothing shipped hits it. When it does, the ground model gets a source height and ground distance that do not correspond to any ray.
- **Proposed change.** Make the receiver leg well-defined: where v<0 for the receiver height (rho < h_r), do not clamp. Either return a NaN residual there so the bracket ends at the launch angle where rho = h_r, or use the descending-branch formula from_ground = d0 + d_r (as to_ground already does for theta>=0). Then either solve on that branch or report exists=false. Also verify a returned root by checking rho >= h_r. In finish_ground, do not silently substitute grazing=0 when a lit ray has no reflected partner; fall back to the direct ray's geometry or log it. Add a test with z_r > z_s, a>0 checked against trace().
- **Expected impact.** Removes wrong ground geometry (2-3 dB in mid bands in the example, 9 dB in single bands) for a rarely used but legal configuration, and makes the documented monotonicity claim true.
- **Conflicts with docs.** CLAUDE.md, refraction section: 'The reflected ray is a 1-D solve on launch angle, along which the range is monotonic (checked across both gradient signs).' Also src/refraction.hpp lines 9-12 and the existing reflected_eigenray test, which only covers z_s=150 > z_r=1.2. README's 'one direct and one reflected ray' limitation says arrivals are omitted; this is a different fault, a non-ray returned as the reflected ray.

### refraction-profile/A.r1.3: EffectiveSoundSpeed samples the profile on a fixed 1 m grid, so sub-meter structure of a log-law wind is lost in the fitted gradient

- Finder's severity low, category accuracy, effort M, finder's confidence 0.6, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/refraction.cpp:293`, `src/refraction.cpp:330`
- **Claim.** The moments are exact integrals of the piecewise-linear interpolant between 1 m nodes, not of the profile itself. Any profile level that does not sit on an integer height is smoothed. Log-law winds are steepest in the lowest meter, so the first cell (0 to 1 m) carries a chord that underestimates the true mean wind there. The path fit is least squares from the receiver height upward, so microphones at or below about 0.5 m, the flush 3 mm boards of the 2017 tests, see the largest error.
- **Evidence given.** table_res.cpp and table_res2.cpp compare EffectiveSoundSpeed::fit with brute-force least squares (4e5 quadrature points) on the true profile. A dense neutral log law (u*=0.4, z0=0.03, levels 0.05 m up): upwind gradient error for receiver 0.2 m is -13.8% with source 5 or 10 m, -6.8% at 20 m, -2.6% at 50 m, -1.2% at 100 m; for receiver 1.2 m it is <= 0.8%. On the shipped atmospheres/example_windy.csv (levels at 0, 1.5, 3, 6 m), --frame_bearing 210, upwind: gradient errors of -2.6% (receiver 3 mm, source 5 m) to -5.7% (1.2 m), and shadow_boundary shifts of -1.3 to -7 m on 99-1620 m. Immaterial next to the diffraction length of 100-400 m.
- **Scenario.** Users with a dense surface-layer sounding (a mast with levels at 0.1-2 m) and flush microphones near a low source get an equivalent gradient up to about 14% low, so a shadow boundary about 7% too near. The empirical boundary constants (0.363, 0.75, 1.60/1.90) were calibrated against this table, so part of the error is baked into them.
- **Proposed change.** Either document the 1 m sampling in the header and README (the fit is not exact for sub-meter structure), or put the profile's own levels in the table as extra nodes, or subdivide below 2 m. Rerun the day-284 calibration afterward, since the fitted constants may shift slightly.
- **Expected impact.** Removes an error of up to a few percent (at most 14% for sub-0.5 m receivers) in gradient and about half that in the shadow boundary. Small for the shipped profile.
- **Conflicts with docs.** README 'The fit is least squares over the heights the path spans' implies the profile's own least-squares line; the code fits the 1 m interpolant. refraction.hpp calls the table 'exact for f linear across' a cell, which is true of the interpolant but not of the profile.

### ground/A.r1.0: Tabulated point-receiver ground effect aborts for receiver_height == 0 (a documented, supported value)

- Finder's severity medium, category correctness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.95, output effect: changes-behavior. **Evidence: stale (fixed)**
- Locations: `src/ground_effect.cpp:378`, `src/ground_effect.cpp:297`, `src/niceops.cpp:1119`, `tests/test_ground_effect.cpp:370`
- **Claim.** GroundTable's constructor evaluates a node at s = 0 (hs = 0 exactly). With a microphone at height 0, cos_grazing is exactly 0 there, the plane-wave Q is -1, and for every band whose |w| exceeds the 500 cutoff the boundary-loss term is dropped, so Q = -1 and magnitude = |1+Q|^2 = 0 exactly. That trips the hard input_error at line 297, which propagates out of the table constructor and kills the whole trajectory. The exact path never hits it because real states have hs > 0.
- **Evidence given.** Repro (scratch copy of the repo root, shipped Release build): `niceops -d databases/B407_fixed_load_factor.nod -v databases/B407.json -t tracks/S-76D/197421AC.csv --ground_effect --receiver_height 0 --gridstep 492 -o integrated/rh0.csv` -> exit=1, 0 bytes on stdout and 0 bytes on stderr, no output file. Only log/niceops.log says: `Skipping tracks/S-76D/197421AC.csv: Excess ground attenuation came out non-positive (0) at 10000 Hz for a source 0 m up and 15.8489 m away. Check the flow resistance (225 kPa s/m^2) and receiver height (0 m).` The same command with `--exact_ground` exits 0 and writes integrated/rh0_exact.csv (9219 bytes). A standalone program replaying the table's node grid (641 s-nodes x 165 r-nodes, hr = 0) fails at 117 nodes, all with is = 0: `hr=0 table nodes: 105765, failing 117; failing s-indices: is=0 (x117)`. GroundTable(hr=1.2) works. The message's advice is wrong for this cause. An observer file with a fifth-column height of 0 and no --plate_table takes the same path (niceops.cpp:841-856 makes it a point receiver at height 0).
- **Scenario.** A user models flush microphones as point receivers on the surface (ground_effect.hpp:96-99 and the ground_gain_bounds tests use receiver_height 0.0 for exactly this) with `--ground_effect --receiver_height 0`, or runs a test-array file whose height column contains 0 without --plate_table. Default (tabulated) mode aborts the run with nothing on the terminal but exit status 1; only --exact_ground (3-4x slower, meant for checking) works.
- **Proposed change.** Make the s = 0 node representable: in excess_ground_attenuation, floor `magnitude` at a tiny positive value when it is non-positive only within rounding (e.g. `magnitude <= 0` and `|1+Q|` small) and keep input_error for NaN; or build the table's first s-node at a small positive elevation. Add a GroundTable test at receiver_height 0 (and one tiny value such as 1e-6) against the exact model for hs >= 1 m. Independently, have run() print input_error text to stderr so a user-facing refusal is not silent.
- **Expected impact.** The default fast path works for flush point receivers; no change for hr > 0.
- **Conflicts with docs.** src/ground_effect.hpp:96-99 says receiver_height 0 puts the microphone on the surface and the model tends to +6 dB; README 'Receivers' presents height 0 as a supported microphone. Neither says the tabulated path cannot do it.

### ground/A.r1.3: PlateReceiver::load trusts netCDF variable shapes and the microphone offset direction

- Finder's severity low, category robustness, effort S, finder's confidence 0.8, output effect: none-bitwise. **Evidence: S**
- Locations: `src/plate_receiver.cpp:24`, `src/plate_receiver.cpp:92`, `src/plate_receiver.cpp:96`, `src/plate_receiver.cpp:133`
- **Claim.** variable() sizes a buffer from the dimension lengths and then calls nc_get_var_double, which copies the variable's whole extent whatever the buffer holds; the variable's own dimensions are never checked against the ones the reorder loop assumes (band, sub, elevation, azimuth). Separately, the azimuth mapping assumes the microphone lies on the plate's +y axis, but load() reads mic_x_m and mic_y_m and keeps only their hypot (line 92), and bound() never compares the table's recorded `sound_speed_mps` (342.9 in both shipped tables) with the run's.
- **Evidence given.** Crafted table (2 bands x 5 sub x 3 elevations x 4 azimuths, all names and attributes present) whose P_d_real carries 4,000,000 values on a separate dimension: `PlateReceiver::load("badplate.nc")` writes 32 MB into a 120-value buffer and the process dies with exit status 133 (SIGTRAP) and no message; no exception is raised. Reading nc attributes of the shipped tables shows mic_x_m = 0, mic_y_m = 0.15 (so valid tables are fine); a table built with the microphone on x would load and rotate every azimuth by 90 degrees, which the plate_mic_azimuth measurement in the test-gap finding shows is worth up to ~1.2 dB.
- **Scenario.** A table from a newer panam writer with a transposed or extra dimension, a truncated copy, or a table built with the microphone on +x is loaded: at best garbage levels with no diagnostic, at worst heap corruption.
- **Proposed change.** In variable(), call nc_inq_varndims/nc_inq_vardimid (or nc_inq_varlen) and refuse a variable whose length differs from the expected size; require |mic_x_m| tiny relative to mic_y_m (or store the angle and rotate by it); warn when |sound_speed_mps - run sound speed| exceeds ~1%.
- **Expected impact.** Malformed or differently oriented tables fail with a message instead of corrupting memory or silently rotating.
